# Component Red-Team & DX Hardening — tg-mini-app-uikit

This file is an **ExecPlan**: a self-contained, living work plan for hardening
every component family of the `tg-mini-app-uikit` design system. It follows the
OpenAI Codex `PLANS.md` convention for multi-hour / long-running problem
solving — read it top to bottom before changing component code, and keep the
living sections (`Progress`, `Surprises & Discoveries`, `Decision Log`,
`Outcomes & Retrospective`) updated as work proceeds.

> The previous occupant of `plans.md` (the Surface Composer demo design plan)
> was explicitly authorised for replacement. This audit-driven plan now
> supersedes it as the single active `plans.md` effort.

## How this plan was produced

Generated on **2026-06-21** by a deterministic multi-agent audit (45 subagents):

1. **Dimensions** — a shared problem-dimension lens (Telegram runtime, a11y,
   API ergonomics, state, types, perf, theming, narrow-WebView/i18n, gesture,
   robustness, safety) was localised per component family.
2. **Red-team** — one adversarial auditor per family read its real source +
   tests/stories + consumer usage (`examples/surface-composer`,
   `examples/trailhead`) and logged evidence-backed findings with `file:line`.
3. **DX-searcher** — a second pass per family proposed developer-experience
   improvements (defaults, API shape, types, a11y-by-default, docs, perf).
4. **Problem-solver** — a synthesis pass consolidated everything into the
   cross-cutting themes, global priorities, and sequencing below.

High- and critical-severity findings additionally went through an adversarial
**verification** pass (a fresh agent tried to *refute* each one against the
code). Each such finding carries a `Verify:` verdict. Medium/low findings are
unverified — treat as leads, confirm before acting.

## Agent Operating Contract

A coding agent executing this plan must:

- read the whole file before touching component code;
- work the **Execution Sequencing** milestones in order — shared `internal/`
  hooks before the composites that depend on them;
- treat each finding/DX item as a task: tick its `- [ ]` box only when the
  *Done when* acceptance check passes (test + visual/a11y where relevant);
- confirm a `Verify: refuted/unclear` finding against the code before spending
  effort on it, and re-confirm every unverified medium/low finding;
- prefer TDD: write the failing behavior/a11y test named in *Done when* first;
- keep changes token-driven (semantic CSS vars, no raw colors), SSR-safe, and
  regression-gated (Playwright visual + axe, Storybook coverage);
- update `Progress`, `Surprises & Discoveries`, and `Decision Log` as work lands;
- never bundle unrelated refactors into a finding's fix.

## Problem Dimensions (the lens)

Every finding maps to one of these axes. A dimension matters only when changing
its value changes behavior, a11y outcome, contract, side effect, or visible state.

- **D-TG Telegram runtime** — safe-area insets, viewport resize/expand, theme CSS vars, haptics, native MainButton/BackButton, CloudStorage, invoices, WebApp events, runtime-absent fallback, SSR import safety
- **D-A11Y Accessibility** — keyboard operability, focus trap/restore, ARIA role/name/state, live-region announcements, prefers-reduced-motion, ≥44px touch targets, token contrast
- **D-API API ergonomics** — controlled vs uncontrolled, prop naming, ref forwarding, composition/asChild escape hatches, sane defaults, prop explosion, footguns
- **D-STATE State model** — loading/empty/error/disabled/readonly, async boundaries, edge values (0, negative, huge, overflow, empty), controlled-value drift
- **D-TYPES TypeScript surface** — exported prop/types quality, generics & inference, discriminated unions, event typings, stray any, JSDoc
- **D-PERF Performance** — needless re-renders, missing memo/virtualization, layout thrash, bundle weight, heavy deps, tree-shaking
- **D-THEME Tokens / theming** — semantic tokens vs raw colors/hardcoded px, density/radius/accent/type-scale/motion knobs, dark+light correctness
- **D-RESP Narrow WebView / i18n** — overflow/truncation, long text & i18n length, RTL, dynamic text scaling
- **D-GESTURE Touch / gesture** — drag/swipe/long-press correctness, scroll-lock conflicts, pull-to-refresh, pointer cancel, Telegram vertical-swipe-minimize guard
- **D-EDGE Robustness** — null/empty/missing data, duplicate keys, rapid taps, unmount-during-async, races, concurrency
- **D-SEC Safety** — file-input/image src handling, dangerouslySetInnerHTML, spoiler/PII exposure

## Executive Summary

The kit is a feature-rich, design-token-driven Telegram Mini App library whose surface is broad and visually mature, but whose foundations carry systemic correctness, accessibility, and Telegram-runtime gaps that will bite any real consumer. The single most dangerous defect is in the shared internal layer: INT-001's tkShouldCommit treats any flick >0.5px/ms as a commit irrespective of distance or element size, so micro-flicks close sheets and fire destructive SwipeCell full-swipe actions (compounded by GES-007 defaulting fullSwipe=true) — a data-loss footgun reachable from many composites. Accessibility is the most pervasive weak spot: a 44px touch-target shortfall recurs in nearly every family (BTN-004, CTL-004, CRD-003, INP-009, SVC-006, FBK-009, OVL-008, TCRD-005), single-select groups are mis-modeled as independent aria-pressed toggles (BTN-002, CTL-001, NAV-002, FBK-004, PTN-002), and loading/async/navigation state is silent to assistive tech across FBK-001, INP-008, LST-004, GES-005, NAV2-001/002, FRM-004. The controlled/uncontrolled contract is inconsistent and at root broken (INT-004 ignores changing defaultValue and drops state on controlled-flips), surfacing as auto-select-first footguns (INP-001, CTL-002) and missing controlled modes (CRS-002, NAV2-007, CHT-002, NAV-005). Telegram-runtime correctness is undermined by module-global state that desyncs across duplicate bundles (INT-005, FND-003, FND-004), missing touch-action arbitration that fights vertical-swipe-to-minimize (INT-003, NAV2-004, CTL-011), and a "Telegram native" MainButton that binds nothing native (BTN-007). Theming has real escape leaks: hardcoded #fff and raw hex bypass --tk-on-accent/tokens across CTL-003, DSP-009, FBK-008, GES-008, LST-012, PTN-001, TCRD-006, ONB-008, and TYP-001 means a bare .tk root renders unstyled. Reduced-motion is honored only inside a .tk scope, so isolated atoms animate against user preference (SVC-005, CRD-004, LST-008, FBK-012, DSP-005). DX-wise the kit lacks a regression net where it is most stateful (FRM-010, INT-011 zero tests), and TypeScript is silently erased in several polymorphic impls (BTN-010, SVC-010, LST-002, NAV2-006). Net: the component catalog is shippable for happy-path demos but not yet safe for production Mini Apps until the shared gesture/state/runtime primitives and the cross-cutting a11y/token/motion contracts are hardened.

## Cross-Cutting Themes

Systemic issues that recur across families — fix these once at the foundation
layer instead of N times.

### `CC-01` · 🔴 CRITICAL · effort M — Destructive gesture commit heuristic and full-swipe defaults can fire actions/close overlays on micro-flicks

- **Affects:** INT, GES, OVL, NAV2
- **Recommendation:** Rewrite tkShouldCommit (INT-001) into a named, distance- AND size-aware policy (commit only when offset exceeds a fraction of element extent OR velocity is paired with sufficient travel), expose it as overridable per INT-DX-004, then flip SwipeCell fullSwipe default to false with a destructive-safe confirm step (GES-007/GES-DX-003). Make every overlay/sheet dismiss and SwipeCell action route through this one policy so micro-flicks can no longer trigger destructive actions.

### `CC-02` · 🟠 HIGH · effort L — Module-global mutable state in shared primitives/runtime desyncs across duplicate bundles or skipped cleanup

- **Affects:** INT, FND
- **Recommendation:** Consolidate the overlay z-stack, scroll-lock counter, vertical-swipe-guard refcount (INT-005), Telegram theme-mirror writes (FND-003) and back-button want-count/queue (FND-004) onto a single context/registry keyed to the TKProvider root instead of module globals; guard against React StrictMode double-invoke and always reverse on unmount. This removes a whole class of 'second provider/duplicate package silently breaks Back, scroll-lock, or theming' bugs.

### `CC-03` · 🟠 HIGH · effort M — 44px minimum touch target violated across nearly every interactive family

- **Affects:** BTN, CTL, CRD, INP, SVC, FBK, OVL, TCRD, FRM
- **Recommendation:** Add a single minTarget primitive (SVC-DX-005: minTarget?: number|false default 44 injecting min-width/min-height + invisible hit-slop) on TKTappable and route Button/IconButton, control thumbs/checkboxes, CardChip, input clear/reveal, feedback CTAs, toast actions, favorite toggles and calendar day cells through it. Covers BTN-004, CTL-004, CRD-003, INP-009, SVC-006, FBK-009, OVL-008, TCRD-005, FRM-007.

### `CC-04` · 🟠 HIGH · effort L — Single-select groups mis-modeled as independent aria-pressed toggle buttons with no group role/name

- **Affects:** BTN, CTL, NAV, FBK, PTN
- **Recommendation:** Provide one shared roving-focus + group-semantics primitive (INT-DX-003 useRovingFocus) and remodel InlineButtons (BTN-002/003), Rating (CTL-001/CTL-DX-008), Segmented (NAV-002), Tabbar/TabView (NAV-001), interactive Bars (FBK-004) and SlotPicker (PTN-002) onto correct radiogroup/tablist/single-toggle semantics with a labelled group and positional total. Fixes the roving-index seed/re-sync bugs (BTN-003, NAV-004, NAV-008) in one place.

### `CC-05` · 🟠 HIGH · effort M — Loading / async / navigation / error state is silent to assistive tech (no role/status/aria-live)

- **Affects:** FBK, INP, LST, GES, NAV2, FRM, BTN, CRS
- **Recommendation:** Ship a tiny useTKBusyAnnounce / aria-live='polite' status primitive (BTN-DX-008) and wire it into AsyncBoundary (FBK-001), field errors as role=alert (INP-008), InfiniteList loading/new-content (LST-004), PullToRefresh (GES-005), nav push/pop announcements (NAV2-002), PIN progress/error (FRM-004), busy buttons (BTN-009) and carousel live region (CRS-005). One owned live region instead of per-component silence.

### `CC-06` · 🟠 HIGH · effort M — Broken/inconsistent controlled-uncontrolled contract: auto-select-first footguns and missing controlled modes

- **Affects:** INT, INP, CTL, CRS, NAV, NAV2, CHT
- **Recommendation:** Fix useControllable to honor a changing defaultValue and not drop internal state on controlled->uncontrolled flips (INT-004), add the options-object overload that warns on misuse (INT-DX-007), then route every group/select through it. Remove auto-select-first so 'nothing selected' is representable (INP-001, CTL-002), and add controlled value/onChange where it is missing (CRS-002, NAV-005, NAV2-007, CHT-002).

### `CC-07` · 🟠 HIGH · effort S — Disabled polymorphic anchors still navigate / activate (as='a' + disabled is not inert)

- **Affects:** BTN, SVC, LST, CRD
- **Recommendation:** Centralize disabled handling in the polymorphic/Tappable base so as='a' disabled drops href, sets aria-disabled, removes from tab order and swallows onClick/keyboard activation (BTN-001, SVC-001). Have TKButton/TKCell/TKCard interactive paths consume that one helper instead of each re-deriving press styling without operability (CRD-007, CRD-010).

### `CC-08` · 🟠 HIGH · effort M — Hardcoded #fff / raw hex bypass --tk-on-accent and theme tokens, breaking light/custom accents

- **Affects:** CTL, DSP, FBK, GES, LST, PTN, TCRD, ONB
- **Recommendation:** Sweep every literal #fff / rgba(255,255,255) / raw palette to var(--tk-on-accent, #fff) or the appropriate semantic token: Switch/slider thumbs (CTL-003), avatar/counter ink (DSP-009), timeline node (FBK-008), swipe labels (GES-008), list icon chips (LST-012), XPHeader (PTN-001), banner card (TCRD-006), confetti palette (ONB-008). Mechanical but breaks real contrast on light-accent Telegram themes today.

### `CC-10` · 🟠 HIGH · effort M — Missing touch-action / vertical-swipe-guard arbitration fights native scroll and swipe-to-minimize

- **Affects:** INT, NAV2, CTL, LAY, ONB, FRM
- **Recommendation:** Make useDragGesture return the correct touch-action for its axis (INT-DX-002) and have horizontal swipe-back/sliders set touch-action:pan-y while vertical sheets set pan-x. Expose disableVerticalSwipes on TKPage (LAY-DX-005) and wire the guard for nav-back (INT-003, NAV2-004), sliders (CTL-011), calendar/onboarding scroll capture (FRM-006, ONB-005). Removes gesture conflicts with Telegram's minimize gesture.

### `CC-17` · 🟠 HIGH · effort M — Hidden/secret content stays in DOM and is reachable by AT, copy tools and scrapers before reveal

- **Affects:** DSP, INP, FND, OVL
- **Recommendation:** Make TKSpoiler genuinely hide content (not just CSS blur) until revealed, with a labelled reversible API (DSP-003/DSP-004/DSP-DX-003); validate dropped files against the accept allowlist (INP-005); validate openLink/openInvoice URL schemes (FND-007); mark overlay backgrounds inert/aria-hidden (OVL-006). Closes the safety/PII leak set.

### `CC-18` · 🟠 HIGH · effort L — No tests or stories where the kit is most stateful (forms, internal gesture heuristics, polymorphic logic)

- **Affects:** FRM, INT, BTN, INP, CRS, NAV2
- **Recommendation:** Stand up a regression net before refactoring the shared layer: unit tests for the internal primitives' pure commit/velocity/roving heuristics (INT-011) and the forms family state/caret/locale logic (FRM-010), plus copy-pasteable controlled-by-default stories per family. This is the safety harness the foundations-first milestones depend on.

### `CC-09` · 🟡 MEDIUM · effort M — Reduced-motion honored only under the .tk scope; isolated atoms ignore prefers-reduced-motion

- **Affects:** SVC, CRD, LST, FBK, DSP, OVL, ONB, INP
- **Recommendation:** Move the reduced-motion gate off the .tk-scoped CSS and into a shared useReducedMotion hook plus a self-contained @media block emitted per animated atom, and expose reduceMotion?: boolean|'auto' on TKProvider (FND-DX-002) reacting to OS changes live. Fixes press-scale (SVC-005, CRD-004), accordion (LST-008), skeleton/timeline (FBK-012), spoiler (DSP-005) and OTP caret (INP-002) in isolation.

### `CC-11` · 🟡 MEDIUM · effort M — Array-index React keys remount/flash content on reorder, removal, or window shift

- **Affects:** DSP, CRS, LST, NAV, PTN
- **Recommendation:** Add a getKey?: (item,index)=>string|number escape hatch to data-driven lists and key by stable identity: AvatarStack (DSP-007), Carousel slides (CRS-008), VirtualList rows (LST-005, LST-DX-003), nav per-index ref arrays (NAV-007/008), PaymentSummary/SlotPicker rows (PTN-008, PTN-010). Stops wrong-image flashes and full-row remounts.

### `CC-12` · 🟡 MEDIUM · effort M — Polymorphic/impl components erase TypeScript via Record<string,unknown> and ref casts to never

- **Affects:** BTN, SVC, LST, NAV2, CRD
- **Recommendation:** Introduce one shared TKPolymorphicProps<T,Own> helper and type all impls against it, dropping the Record<string,unknown> bodies and hand-written forwardRef casts (BTN-010, SVC-010, LST-002, NAV2-006). Restores prop type-checking and ref typing across the polymorphic family in one pass.

### `CC-13` · 🟡 MEDIUM · effort M — No ref forwarding / className / native-prop escape hatch on many display, layout, feedback and typography components

- **Affects:** DSP, FBK, LAY, TYP, CRD, TCRD, CRS
- **Recommendation:** Adopt one convention repo-wide: forwardRef + accept className + spread ...rest onto the root node. Apply to display atoms (DSP-008), feedback (FBK-011), layout (LAY-006), typography (TYP-004/TYP-005), cards (CRD-001), template cards (TCRD-010) and carousel (CRS-011). Unblocks composition/measurement without per-component bespoke work.

### `CC-14` · 🟡 MEDIUM · effort S — Numeric edge values (empty/zero/NaN/negative) produce -Infinity, NaN%, or divide-by-zero in charts and geometry

- **Affects:** TCRD, FBK, CTL, CRS, PTN, DSP, ONB, LAY
- **Recommendation:** Add a shared clamp/normalize helper and guard every reduce(Math.max)/divide path: StatTile empty & all-zero bars (TCRD-001/002), Bars Math.max spread (FBK-006), slider step<=0 (CTL-009), carousel zero-slide (CRS-007), SlotPicker/xp out-of-range (PTN-005), counter max NaN (DSP-006), confetti count (ONB-010), tkSafePad negative/NaN (LAY-009). Make these unbreakable by construction.

### `CC-15` · 🟡 MEDIUM · effort L — Shared field-chrome contract (label/hint/error/required + group semantics) is duplicated or absent across controls and inputs

- **Affects:** CTL, INP, FRM, CHT
- **Recommendation:** Export one TKFieldProps<V> base (INP-DX-001) and a useTKFieldValidation hook, then attach label/hint/error/required and labelled group roles uniformly to controls (CTL-DX-001/003), inputs, forms (name plumbing FRM-DX-001) and the chat composer label (CHT-DX-004). Removes the inconsistent onChange/error/disabled coverage across siblings (INP-007).

### `CC-16` · ⚪ LOW · effort S — Missing/inconsistent haptics across interactive composites despite an established house pattern

- **Affects:** CRS, NAV, GES, ONB, PTN
- **Recommendation:** Make useOptionalHaptics on-by-default-but-overridable and fire selection()/notification() consistently: carousel swipe & dots (CRS-006/CRS-DX-004), CategoryTabs/PageDots/Steps (NAV-006), long-press (GES-009), onboarding advance/finish (ONB-006/ONB-DX-009), slot picker & leaderboard taps (PTN-DX-002).

## Global Priorities

Ordered buckets across the whole kit. `P0` = correctness / a11y / Telegram-runtime
breakage that bites consumers today; `P1` = important DX + robustness; `P2` = polish.

### P0 — do first

- [x] INT-001 — micro-flick commit heuristic fires destructive full-swipe and closes sheets; core gesture-safety defect (CC-01)  
  ↳ _critical · tkShouldCommit treats any flick >0.5px/ms as a commit regardless of distance OR size — micro-flicks close sheets and trigger destructive full-swipe_
- [x] GES-007 — SwipeCell fullSwipe=true auto-fires the FIRST action on over-swipe with no confirmation; data loss (CC-01)  
  ↳ _high · SwipeCell defaults fullSwipe=true — an over-swipe auto-fires the FIRST action with no confirmation_
- [x] INT-005 — overlay/scroll-lock/swipe-guard module globals desync across duplicate bundles, breaking layering and scroll lock (CC-02)  
  ↳ _high · useOverlayLayer / useScrollLock / useVerticalSwipeGuard share unguarded module globals that desync across a duplicated bundle or a skipped cleanup_
- [x] INT-004 — useControllable drops state on controlled-flip and ignores changing defaultValue; root of the kit's state-drift bugs (CC-06)  
  ↳ _high · useControllable ignores a changing defaultValue and silently drops internal state when switching controlled→uncontrolled_
- [x] FND-002 — motionSpeed=0 poisons every motion token via calc(.../0), breaking all animation app-wide  
  ↳ _high · motionSpeed={0} poisons every motion token via calc(.../0)_
- [x] FND-003 — Telegram theme mirroring writes global <html> vars and never cleans up; multiple providers fight (CC-02)  
  ↳ _high · telegram theme mirroring writes global <html> vars and never cleans up; multiple providers fight_
- [x] FND-001 — useShare.shareToStory promise hangs forever with no story-result event; permanent await leak  
  ↳ _high · useShare.shareToStory promise hangs forever when no story-result event fires_
- [x] TYP-001 — bare .tk renders unstyled/transparent because base paints from tokens only defined under data-theme  
  ↳ _high · Base `.tk` paints color/background from tokens that only exist under data-theme/.tk-tg — bare `.tk` renders unstyled/transparent_
- [x] INP-005 — TKFileInput dropZone bypasses the accept allowlist, committing any dropped file type (CC-17)  
  ↳ _high · TKFileInput dropZone bypasses the `accept` allowlist — dropped files of any type are committed_

  ↳ _high · TKSpoiler only blurs and disables selection in CSS — the hidden text stays in the DOM and is read by screen readers, copy tools and page scrapers before reveal_

  ↳ _high · Uncontrolled TKSelect silently auto-selects the first option and never fires onChange — placeholder never shows, parent state desyncs_

  ↳ _high · Uncontrolled RadioGroup auto-selects the first option, so 'nothing selected' is impossible_

## Execution Sequencing

Foundations first: shared `internal/` primitives before the composites that lean
on them, so a fix lands once and propagates.

### Milestone 1: M0 — Safety net before touching shared code

- **Why:** Every later milestone refactors primitives that many composites depend on; without tests/stories the kit is the most stateful exactly where it has zero coverage. Lock behavior first so foundations work is verifiable.
- **Includes:** `INT-011`, `FRM-010`, `INT-DX-008`, `BTN-DX-007`, `CTL-DX-006`, `INP-DX-008`, `CC-18`

### Milestone 2: M1 — Internal primitives: gesture safety, controllable, roving focus, layering

- **Why:** These are the shared hooks under gestures, overlays, nav, forms and every group control. Fixing the commit heuristic, the controllable contract, touch-action arbitration, roving focus and the module-global layering registry unblocks and de-risks all component-level milestones.
- **Includes:** `INT-001`, `INT-DX-004`, `INT-004`, `INT-DX-007`, `INT-002`, `INT-005`, `INT-003`, `INT-DX-002`, `INT-DX-003`, `INT-DX-001`, `INT-DX-005`, `INT-006`, `INT-007`, `INT-010`, `CC-01`, `CC-02`, `CC-06`, `CC-10`

### Milestone 3: M2 — Foundation/runtime: provider, theming base, motion, Telegram correctness

- **Why:** Provider-level defects (unstyled bare .tk, motion divide-by-zero, leaking global theme mirror, env masking) affect everything rendered. Land the token base, reduce-motion path and runtime hardening before component sweeps so the cross-cutting token/motion fixes have a correct substrate.
- **Includes:** `TYP-001`, `FND-002`, `FND-003`, `FND-001`, `FND-004`, `FND-009`, `FND-007`, `FND-006`, `FND-008`, `FND-DX-002`, `FND-DX-001`, `FND-DX-005`, `TYP-009`, `CC-09`

### Milestone 4: M3 — Cross-cutting a11y primitives: 44px targets, group semantics, live announcements, disabled anchors

- **Why:** With roving focus and the live-region primitive in place, sweep the recurring a11y defects in one coordinated pass using shared helpers (minTarget, useTKBusyAnnounce, polymorphic disabled). Doing this as a theme avoids per-component drift.
- **Includes:** `SVC-DX-005`, `BTN-DX-008`, `CC-03`, `CC-04`, `CC-05`, `CC-07`, `BTN-004`, `CTL-004`, `CRD-003`, `INP-009`, `SVC-006`, `FBK-009`, `OVL-008`, `BTN-001`, `SVC-001`, `BTN-002`, `NAV-002`, `CTL-001`, `FBK-001`, `LST-004`, `GES-005`, `FRM-004`

### Milestone 5: M4 — State & field contract: kill auto-select-first, ship TKFieldProps, controlled modes

- **Why:** Building on the fixed useControllable, remove the auto-select-first footguns, add controlled value/onChange where missing, and standardize the label/hint/error/required field chrome across controls, inputs, forms and chat.
- **Includes:** `INP-001`, `CTL-002`, `PTN-004`, `NAV-005`, `NAV2-007`, `CRS-002`, `CHT-002`, `INP-DX-001`, `INP-DX-002`, `CTL-DX-001`, `CTL-DX-003`, `FRM-DX-001`, `LST-003`, `CC-15`

### Milestone 6: M5 — Numeric robustness, stable keys, perf hot-paths

- **Why:** Mechanical correctness sweep: clamp/normalize numeric edges, add getKey identity to data-driven lists, and fix the scroll/render hot-paths. Low-risk now that the primitives and tests exist.
- **Includes:** `TCRD-001`, `TCRD-002`, `FBK-006`, `CTL-009`, `CRS-007`, `PTN-005`, `DSP-006`, `ONB-010`, `LAY-009`, `NAV-004`, `LST-DX-003`, `LST-005`, `DSP-007`, `NAV-007`, `CRS-008`, `PTN-008`, `LAY-001`, `CHT-005`, `DSP-011`, `CC-11`, `CC-14`

### Milestone 7: M6 — Token & motion sweep, escape hatches, polymorphic typing

- **Why:** On the corrected token/motion substrate from M2, replace every hardcoded #fff/raw hex with semantic tokens, add the forwardRef+className+rest escape-hatch convention, and restore typing on the polymorphic impls.
- **Includes:** `CTL-003`, `DSP-009`, `FBK-008`, `GES-008`, `LST-012`, `PTN-001`, `TCRD-006`, `ONB-008`, `CC-08`, `CC-13`, `CC-12`, `LST-002`, `BTN-010`, `SVC-010`, `NAV2-006`, `DSP-008`, `FBK-011`, `LAY-006`, `TYP-004`

### Milestone 8: M7 — Composite-specific correctness: carousel, overlays, nav-shell, gestures, onboarding, chat

- **Why:** Remaining per-family defects that aren't pure cross-cutting themes — carousel snap/resize, overlay timer/inert/toast bounds, nav-shell focus/controlled stack, gesture ergonomics, onboarding dismissal, chat IME — each consuming the now-hardened primitives.
- **Includes:** `CRS-001`, `CRS-003`, `CRS-004`, `CRS-005`, `OVL-001`, `OVL-002`, `OVL-003`, `OVL-006`, `OVL-007`, `NAV2-001`, `NAV2-003`, `NAV2-004`, `NAV2-002`, `NAV2-009`, `GES-007`, `GES-001`, `GES-002`, `GES-004`, `GES-011`, `ONB-001`, `ONB-002`, `ONB-003`, `ONB-004`, `CHT-004`, `CHT-001`, `CHT-003`, `LAY-002`, `LAY-003`

### Milestone 9: M8 — DX surface & docs: batteries-included APIs, stories/MDX, haptics consistency, polish

- **Why:** With correctness and a11y solid, ship the higher-leverage DX APIs (TKApp, asChild, data-driven overloads, imperative toast/carousel handles), consistent haptics, and the copy-pasteable story/MDX recipes that make the kit safe to adopt. Remaining P2 polish lands here.
- **Includes:** `FND-DX-007`, `OVL-DX-002`, `OVL-DX-004`, `CRS-DX-006`, `NAV-DX-002`, `LST-DX-002`, `TCRD-DX-001`, `TYP-DX-001`, `CC-16`, `CRS-006`, `NAV-006`, `GES-009`, `ONB-006`, `PTN-DX-002`, `BTN-DX-001`, `BTN-DX-003`, `TYP-DX-002`, `FBK-DX-08`

## Per-Component Findings & Tasks

Each item is a task. Findings are ordered by severity. `Verify:` shows the
adversarial re-check verdict for high/critical findings.

### Buttons — Button, IconButton, InlineButtons, MainButton (Telegram native), Spinner

`BTN` · `packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons` · 10 findings (0🔴 4🟠 5🟡 1⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility (role/name/state, keyboard, touch target) · `D-STATE` State model (loading/disabled/controlled drift, async) · `D-TG` Telegram runtime (native MainButton, safe-area, haptics) · `D-API` API ergonomics (props, escape hatches, footguns) · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView / i18n (overflow, truncation, long text) · `D-TYPES` TypeScript surface quality

#### Findings

- [x] **`BTN-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Disabled polymorphic TKButton (as="a") is still keyboard-activatable and navigates
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/button.tsx:57-86`
  - **Evidence:** `{...(Tag === "button" ? { type: "button", disabled: blocked } : { "aria-disabled": blocked || undefined })}` then `{...rest}` (which carries `href`) with style `pointerEvents: blocked ? "none" : undefined`
  - **Impact:** A Mini App rendering `<TKButton as="a" href=... disabled>` (or `loading`) gets a link that only blocks the mouse: it stays in the tab order and Enter still follows the href. Users navigate away mid-async / from a disabled CTA, and screen readers announce it as an enabled link. pointerEvents:none is not a real disable for anchors.
  - **Fix:** For non-button tags when blocked: drop `href` from the spread, add `tabIndex={-1}` and `role="link"`/`aria-disabled`, and gate the keydown/click. Better: when `as` is set and blocked, render the anchor without href and prevent default on click/keydown, or document that consumers must not pass href while disabled.
  - **Done when:** Render `<TKButton as="a" href="/x" disabled>`; Tab focuses it? should be no; pressing Enter does not navigate; axe reports no actionable disabled control.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — button.tsx:57-86 — when as!="button" it only sets aria-disabled and pointerEvents:none; href flows in via {...rest} with no tabIndex=-1, href removal, or key handler, so a disabled/loading anchor stays focusable and Enter still navigates.
- [x] **`BTN-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — InlineButtons is a single-select group exposed as independent toggle buttons (wrong ARIA semantics)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/inline-buttons.tsx:45-76`
  - **Evidence:** `<div role="group" ...>` with each item rendered as `<button ... aria-pressed={selected} ...>` where `selected = item.selected ?? active === item.id` and selecting one clears the others via `active`
  - **Impact:** This is a segmented single-select control (radio/tab semantics) but it announces N independent toggle buttons in a generic group. VoiceOver/TalkBack users hear 'pressed/not pressed' with no 'X of N' position and no mutual-exclusion cue, so the single-select nature is invisible to AT — a real comprehension failure in a Mini App tab/segment switcher.
  - **Fix:** Use `role="radiogroup"` on the container (or `tablist`) and `role="radio"`/`aria-checked` (or `role="tab"`/`aria-selected`) on items, keeping the existing roving tabindex. Expose a prop if some consumers genuinely want multi-toggle.
  - **Done when:** Screen reader announces the container as a radio/tab group and items as 'radio, checked, 2 of 4'; axe has no role/state mismatch.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — inline-buttons.tsx:45-76 — a single-select control (selecting one clears others via `active`) is exposed as role="group" with independent aria-pressed toggle buttons; no tablist/tab or radiogroup/radio semantics convey the mutual exclusion to AT.
- [x] **`BTN-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — InlineButtons roving focus index seeds from item 0 and never re-syncs to selection or item changes
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/inline-buttons.tsx:42-67`
  - **Evidence:** `const [focusIdx, setFocusIdx] = useState(() => tkTabbableIndex(0, items.length, disabledAt));` — initial arg is hard-coded `0`, and there is no effect recomputing focusIdx when `items`, `value`, or disabled flags change; `refs.current` is also never trimmed when the list shrinks
  - **Impact:** tabIndex=0 (the keyboard entry point) lands on the first enabled item, not the selected one — so Tab into a segmented control focuses the wrong segment. If a consumer later changes `items` (reorder/remove) or the controlled `value`, focusIdx can point at a now-disabled or out-of-range button, leaving the group with no tabbable child (keyboard trap-out) or a stale ref. Common in dynamic Mini App filters.
  - **Fix:** Seed focusIdx from the active/selected index, and add an effect (or derive during render) that clamps focusIdx to a valid enabled index whenever items.length/active/disabled change; truncate refs.current to items.length.
  - **Done when:** With a selected item at index 2, Tab focuses index 2; removing items below the focused index keeps a valid enabled tabbable item; no console ref warning on shrink.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — inline-buttons.tsx:42 seeds focusIdx with tkTabbableIndex(0,...) (hardcoded 0, not the selected index), and there is no useEffect/findIndex re-syncing focusIdx to value/items/disabled changes nor trimming refs.current — so Tab lands on the first enabled item and stale indices are possible on dynamic lists.
- [x] **`BTN-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Every button variant ships below the 44px minimum touch target
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/shared.ts:7-13`
  - **Evidence:** `BTN_SIZES = { sm: { h: 34, ... }, md: { h: 44, ... }, lg: { h: 52 } }` and `ICON_BTN_SIZES = { sm: 32, md: 40, lg: 48 }`; InlineButtons uses `height = size === "sm" ? 34 : 40` (inline-buttons.tsx:38)
  - **Impact:** Default IconButton (md=40), InlineButtons (md=40), and all `sm` variants (34/32) are under the 44x44 CSS-px tap target Apple/WCAG 2.5.5 recommend. In a phone-only Telegram WebView these are the primary tap controls; small fingers mis-tap, hurting any team that adopts the defaults as-is.
  - **Fix:** Either bump md to >=44 (IconButton/InlineButtons) or keep the visual size but expand the hit area with a transparent ::before / padding so the actual pointer target is >=44px. Document target sizes per size token.
  - **Done when:** Computed pointer hit-area for default IconButton and InlineButtons items is >=44x44px (measure ::before or padding); visual size may stay smaller.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — shared.ts:13 ICON_BTN_SIZES md=40/sm=32 and inline-buttons.tsx:38 height md=40/sm=34 are below 44px, with no hit-slop expansion (tokens.css .tk-press has none). Title overstates (Button md=44/lg=52 meet WCAG), but the substantive defect — default IconButton/InlineButtons and all sm variants under 44px — reproduces.
- [x] **`BTN-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — IconButton silently drops most native handlers/attributes (no onKeyDown, title, name, type, onMouseEnter…)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/icon-button.tsx:27-40`
  - **Evidence:** props are destructured to a fixed set and forwarded via `{...tkDomProps(dom)}`; `tkDomProps` (internal/dom.ts:54-67) returns only id/data-testid/onPointerDown/Up/Cancel/onFocus/onBlur/aria-*
  - **Impact:** Consumers can't attach `onKeyDown`, `onMouseEnter` (tooltip triggers), `title`, `name`, `value`, `form`, or `type="submit"` to an IconButton — and there's no `asChild`/ref-to-spread escape hatch. A Mini App that needs an icon submit button or a hover tooltip has to wrap it or drop the component, a real workaround tax that's easy to miss because the props just vanish with no type error on excess (extra keys land in `...dom` and are discarded).
  - **Fix:** Either spread a vetted-but-broader native prop set, accept a `buttonProps`/rest passthrough, or expose `asChild`. At minimum forward onKeyDown, onMouseEnter/Leave, title, name, value, type, form; document the allow-list.
  - **Done when:** `<TKIconButton onKeyDown={fn} title="x" type="submit" />` reaches the DOM `<button>`; onKeyDown fires and the button submits its form.
- [x] **`BTN-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-RESP` — IconButton numeric badge has no overflow cap and a fixed-height pill — large counts overflow
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/icon-button.tsx:58-79`
  - **Evidence:** `{typeof badge === "number" ? <span style={{ minWidth: 17, height: 17, padding: "0 4px", fontSize: "var(--tk-fz-caption2)" ... }}>{badge}</span>`
  - **Impact:** `badge={1280}` renders the raw number in a 17px pill: it grows wide and/or clips, and there's no `99+` cap and no zero/negative guard (`badge={0}` still renders a '0' bubble because the relative-position check is `badge !== false`). A notifications IconButton in a narrow header looks broken on real unread counts.
  - **Fix:** Clamp display to `badge > 99 ? "99+" : badge`, hide the bubble when `badge <= 0`, and let height/line-height accommodate two glyphs. Add a `maxBadge` prop if needed.
  - **Done when:** `badge={0}` renders no bubble; `badge={1280}` shows '99+' within the pill without clipping; snapshot stable.
- [ ] **`BTN-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — TKMainButton labelled 'Telegram native' has no safe-area inset and no native MainButton binding
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/main-button.tsx:72-93`
  - **Evidence:** fixed `height: 52` with no `paddingBottom`/`env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` and no use of `useMainButton`/`@tg-mini-app/telegram` button API; the doc comment only says 'Telegram-style bottom action button'
  - **Impact:** Despite the family naming it 'MainButton (Telegram native)', this is a styled DOM `<button>` fallback. Teams who place it as the bottom CTA on an iPhone with a home indicator get it crammed against the safe-area edge (or behind the indicator) unless they remember the external wrapper, and they may assume it auto-binds the real Telegram MainButton (it doesn't — see PrimaryActionBar.tsx which must gate it behind runtimeMode and call useMainButton separately).
  - **Fix:** Add a `safeArea`/bottom-inset option (or apply `padding-bottom: env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` when used as a docked bar), and document clearly that native binding is via `useMainButton`, this is the DOM fallback only.
  - **Done when:** In a viewport with a simulated bottom inset the button content clears the safe area; docs/types state the native-vs-fallback split explicitly.
- [ ] **`BTN-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-STATE` — MainButton fires success haptics and runs its state machine only in auto mode — controlled status is inconsistent
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/main-button.tsx:44-58`
  - **Evidence:** `if (status !== undefined) return;` short-circuits before `haptics.notification("success")` and the loading/success/idle transitions; haptics live only inside the auto (`result.then`) branch
  - **Impact:** A team that controls `status` (the documented controlled path) gets no success haptic, no auto-revert, and on a rejected onClick no error feedback at all (`() => mounted.current && setAuto("idle")` swallows it without `haptics.notification("error")`). Behaviour silently diverges between the two supported modes, so haptic feedback depends on which API you happen to use.
  - **Fix:** Fire success/error haptics on status transitions regardless of controlled vs auto (e.g. an effect keyed on `state`), and surface an error path (haptic + optional onError) for rejected promises instead of a silent idle.
  - **Done when:** Controlled `status="success"` triggers the success haptic once; a rejected auto onClick fires an error haptic and returns to idle.
- [x] **`BTN-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — Loading buttons set aria-busy but provide no accessible busy/done announcement
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/button.tsx:63-111`
  - **Evidence:** `aria-busy={loading || undefined}` with the spinner swapped in via `visibility:hidden` measuring layer; no `aria-live`/`role="status"` and the standalone `<TKSpinner>` (spinner.tsx:8-21) is an unlabeled `<span>` with no `role`/`aria-hidden`
  - **Impact:** Toggling `loading` (or MainButton loading/success) changes nothing a screen reader announces — `aria-busy` on a focused control is weakly/inconsistently surfaced, and there is no 'Loading…'/'Done' live update. Blind users get no feedback that an async action is in flight or finished. Exported TKSpinner used alone is announced as empty content.
  - **Fix:** Add an off-screen `role="status" aria-live="polite"` text (e.g. locale.loading/done) that updates on the loading/success transitions, and give TKSpinner an `aria-hidden` default plus an optional `label`/role=status when used standalone.
  - **Done when:** Toggling loading announces a polite 'Loading…' and success announces 'Done'; standalone decorative spinner is aria-hidden by default.
- [x] **`BTN-010`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-TYPES` — TKButton polymorphic implementation discards typing (Record<string, unknown>, ref cast to never)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/button.tsx:35-60`
  - **Evidence:** impl param typed `TKButtonOwnProps & { as?: ElementType } & Record<string, unknown>` and `ref={ref as never}`, with the public type asserted via `as <T ...>(props) => ReactElement`
  - **Impact:** Inside the component all forwarded `...rest` props are untyped, so element-specific attribute typos (e.g. `hreff`) aren't caught and the `as never` ref cast hides any ref-type mismatch. The double cast also means generic inference rests entirely on the hand-written overload — fragile under refactor and harder for consumers to get precise autocompletion for `as`-specific props.
  - **Fix:** Type the impl against `TKButtonProps<ElementType>` (or a constrained generic) instead of `Record<string, unknown>`, and forward the ref through a properly typed `ForwardedRef<Element>` without `as never` where avoidable.
  - **Done when:** `typecheck` passes with the impl typed without `Record<string, unknown>`/`as never`; passing an invalid attribute for the chosen `as` is a type error.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`BTN-DX-001`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** There is no single component that bridges the native Telegram MainButton (`useMainButton` in @tg-mini-app/telegram, packages/telegram/src/buttons.ts:180) and the in-DOM fallback (`TKMainButton`, packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/main-button.tsx:23). Every consumer hand-writes the same ~20-line wrapper: examples/trailhead/src/components/PrimaryAction.tsx and examples/surface-composer/src/surface/PrimaryActionBar.tsx both reinvent a `NativeMainButton` + `useHasNativeChrome()` branch + `TKBottomBar` fallback. This is the #1 friction for the most important button in the genre, and it forces the consumer to know which package owns which API.
  - **Proposal:** Ship one declarative `<TKActionButton>` that internally calls `useMainButton({text,loading,disabled,onClick})` when `useHasNativeChrome()` is true and renders `<TKBottomBar><TKMainButton/></TKBottomBar>` otherwise. The common case becomes one line: `<TKActionButton label={`Pay ${total}`} loading={paying} onClick={pay} active />`. Keep an `active` prop so mounted-but-hidden panels don't fight over the single native button (the exact rule both demos hand-code today). This collapses both demo wrappers into a single import and removes the cross-package knowledge requirement.
- [ ] **`BTN-DX-002`** · api · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** `TKMainButton` and the native `useMainButton` have divergent, non-mappable props. The in-DOM one exposes `status`/`successLabel`/`successDuration` and an auto promise-driven state machine (main-button.tsx:9-20) while the native one exposes `loading`/`color`/`textColor`/`shine`/`secondary` (buttons.ts:67-79) with NO success state. A developer who learns one cannot transfer the knowledge, and the auto 'success' affordance silently vanishes when the app runs inside Telegram — the same `<TKMainButton>` JSX behaves differently per environment.
  - **Proposal:** Unify on the native vocabulary as the source of truth: give `TKMainButton` a `loading?: boolean` prop (currently only `status`) so the controlled API matches `useMainButton`, and surface a shared `TKMainButtonState = {label, loading, disabled, onClick, success?}` type both the component and the hook accept. The auto success/promise machine becomes opt-in via `autoSuccess` rather than the implicit default, so the in-DOM and native paths render identically. Aligns the two surfaces so BTN-DX-001's bridge is trivial.
- [ ] **`BTN-DX-003`** · api · effort L
  - **Pain:** `TKInlineButtons` requires consumers to hand-roll an `items` array of `{id,label,icon,...}` objects (inline-buttons.tsx:5-13), which is verbose and order-coupled. There is no JSX child API, so you cannot conditionally render an option, map with keys naturally, or co-locate an option's content with its handler. Compared to the compound-component pattern users expect from a segmented control, the array API is a closed box.
  - **Proposal:** Add a compound/children API alongside the array prop: `<TKInlineButtons value={tab} onChange={setTab}><TKInlineButtons.Item id="day" icon="sun">Day</TKInlineButtons.Item>...</TKInlineButtons>`. Keep `items` as the shorthand. The compound children let the parent own selection/roving (fixing the ergonomics around BTN-002/BTN-003 at the API layer) while giving consumers natural JSX composition and conditional rendering. Map children to the same internal model so behavior is identical.
- [ ] **`BTN-DX-004`** · defaults · effort S · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** `IconButton` makes the accessible name optional (`label?: string`, icon-button.tsx:21-22) even though an icon-only button has no visible text, so it is trivially easy to ship an unlabelled, screen-reader-invisible control — and nothing warns. The defaults invert the safe path: the inaccessible button is the zero-config one.
  - **Proposal:** Make `label` required in the type (`label: string`) so TypeScript blocks the inaccessible case at the call site, with a dev-only `console.error` runtime guard for JS consumers. Provide an explicit escape hatch (`aria-labelledby` / `aria-hidden` + a documented `decorative` prop) for the rare label-elsewhere case. a11y-by-default: the common path can no longer ship without a name.
- [ ] **`BTN-DX-005`** · types · effort M
  - **Pain:** `TKButton`'s polymorphic implementation throws away typing: the impl signature is `TKButtonOwnProps & {as?: ElementType} & Record<string,unknown>` with `ref as never` (button.tsx:50,60,124-126). The exported cast restores SOME inference, but the open `Record<string,unknown>` means typos in native props (e.g. `hreff` on `as="a"`) are NOT caught, and autocomplete for element-specific attributes is unreliable. The escape hatch silently degrades type safety exactly where polymorphism makes mistakes likely.
  - **Proposal:** Type the impl against the public `TKPolymorphicProps<T, TKButtonOwnProps>` (polymorphic.ts:7) instead of `Record<string,unknown>`, and add a properly-typed `ref` via `ForwardedRef<ElementRef<T>>`. Then `<TKButton as="a" hreff="/x">` becomes a compile error and `href`/`target`/`download` autocomplete. Inference 'just works' for the polymorphic case the kit advertises in its own doc comment (button.tsx:123).
- [ ] **`BTN-DX-006`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** `TKButton` has no `asChild` escape hatch. Polymorphism via `as` works for plain tags, but the moment a consumer needs to wrap a router `<Link>` (react-router/Next) they must either lose the styling or re-implement the visual recipe, because `as={Link}` collides with Link's own prop typing and the discarded generics (BTN-DX-005). This is the single most common real-world need — a styled button that is actually a client-side link.
  - **Proposal:** Add `asChild?: boolean` (Radix Slot pattern): `<TKButton asChild variant="filled"><Link to="/trips">Trips</Link></TKButton>` merges the button's className/style/handlers onto the single child element. Document `as` for tags, `asChild` for foreign components. Gives a clean, fully-typed composition path that the polymorphic `as` cannot cover, and sidesteps the ref/typing issues for router links.
- [ ] **`BTN-DX-007`** · docs · effort S
  - **Pain:** Storybook coverage is thin and groups unrelated atoms: the entire MainButton state machine plus Spinner share one story `MainButtonAndSpinner` (packages/uikit/storybook/atoms/buttons.stories.tsx:70), and there is no story for the polymorphic `as="a"` link, the `asChild` router case, the loading/busy announcement, the badge-overflow behavior, or the native-vs-fallback MainButton split. A developer discovering this family in Storybook cannot copy-paste the patterns that actually bite (link buttons, loading flows, the native bridge).
  - **Proposal:** Add focused, copy-pasteable stories: `LinkButton` (`as="a"` + the asChild router pattern), `LoadingFlow` (promise-driven auto success + controlled `loading`), `NativeMainButtonBridge` (the BTN-DX-001 component inside and outside Telegram chrome), and `IconButtonBadges` (dot, counter, overflow cap). Each story's source doubles as the recipe. Closes the discovery gap that pushes developers to read the source.
- [x] **`BTN-DX-008`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** Loading buttons set `aria-busy` but give no live announcement, so a screen-reader user pressing 'Pay' hears nothing while the request runs and nothing on success (button.tsx:63, main-button.tsx:70). The kit already knows the success moment (it fires `haptics.notification('success')` at main-button.tsx:53) but never announces it. There is also no shared, tokenized loading affordance hook — every component re-derives spinner sizing from `s.h` (button.tsx:110).
  - **Proposal:** Add a tiny `useTKBusyAnnounce(state, {loadingText, doneText})` that owns an `aria-live="polite"` region and the locale-aware default strings (the kit already has `locale.done`, main-button.tsx:29). `TKButton`/`TKMainButton` call it when `loading`/`status` changes, so pressing a loading button announces 'Loading…' then 'Done' for free. Ships accessible busy/done announcements by default and gives consumers a reusable primitive for their own async buttons.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

COVERAGE GAP: there are NO test or story files for this family — no *.test.tsx / *.stories.tsx exist under packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons (verified via find/grep). So none of the above (loading layout stability, roving keyboard, controlled MainButton cycle, disabled-anchor, badge overflow, touch targets) is regression-guarded by the kit's own Playwright/Storybook a11y gates; consumers are the de-facto test suite. ASSUMPTIONS: (1) BTN-004 uses the 44x44 CSS-px guideline (WCAG 2.5.5 AAA / Apple HIG); product may intentionally accept 40px md but the sm sizes (32/34) are hard to defend. (2) BTN-001 anchor behaviour inferred from standard DOM semantics (aria-disabled + pointer-events:none does not block keyboard activation of anchors) — not executed in a browser here. (3) Haptics/runtime-absent fallback was confirmed safe in packages/telegram/src/provider.tsx:113-130 (optional chaining, no-op outside Telegram), so SSR/runtime-absent import safety of the buttons themselves is fine — no finding there. (4) The family barrel resolves correctly via src/atoms/buttons.tsx (sibling to the directory); the directory itself has no index.ts but that is by design and not a bug. NOT RAISED (checked, acceptable): Spinner keeps animating under prefers-reduced-motion because the reduce media block (tokens.css:332-342) deliberately only neutralizes --tk-t* and skel/pulse, leaving informational spinners spinning — defensible per the inline comment. Button's loading measuring-layer (visibility:hidden clone) correctly keeps width stable. mergeRefs/tkZ in internal/dom.ts are unused by this family.

---

### Controls — Checkbox, Switch, RadioGroup, Chips, Rating, StepperRating, Stepper, Sliders, Selection

`CTL` · `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls` · 12 findings (0🔴 4🟠 7🟡 1⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility (keyboard, ARIA semantics, touch-target, contrast) · `D-STATE` State model & edge values · `D-TG` Telegram runtime / WebView fitness · `D-THEME` Tokens & theming correctness · `D-API` API ergonomics & footguns · `D-TYPES` TypeScript surface quality · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView / i18n / RTL · `D-EDGE` Robustness (empty data, rapid taps, races)

#### Findings

- [x] **`CTL-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Rating uses aria-pressed toggle buttons with no keyboard model and keyboard cannot reach half values
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/rating.tsx:23-45`
  - **Evidence:** <div role="group" aria-label={locale.rating} ...> ... <button aria-pressed={!readonly && v >= i + 1} tabIndex={readonly ? -1 : 0} onClick={... e.clientX - r.left < r.width / 2 && e.clientX > 0 ? i + 0.5 : i + 1}> — there is no onKeyDown anywhere in the component.
  - **Impact:** A Mini App rating input is announced as a row of independent toggle buttons (not a single rating), forces Tab-per-star, and with allowHalf the half-star branch depends on e.clientX>0 so every keyboard Enter/Space resolves to a full star — half values are unreachable without a mouse, which doesn't exist on touch either. Screen-reader and keyboard users get a broken, mislabeled control.
  - **Fix:** Model it as role="radiogroup" (or a single role="slider" with aria-valuemin/max/now/text) with one tab stop and ArrowLeft/Right roving via tkRovingNext; drive selection from the model, not clientX; keep clientX only for pointer half-detection and add Shift+Arrow (or a half step) to reach .5 values from the keyboard.
  - **Done when:** Keyboard-only: Tab lands once on the rating, Arrow keys change the value (including reaching X.5 when allowHalf), and a screen reader announces a single rating control with current value — covered by a Testing Library keyboard test plus an axe check.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — rating.tsx has no onKeyDown anywhere; it is a row of aria-pressed toggle buttons in a role=group, and the allowHalf onClick (line 43) gates the half branch on `e.clientX > 0`, so synthetic keyboard activation (clientX===0) always resolves to i+1 — half values unreachable by keyboard.
- [x] **`CTL-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — Uncontrolled RadioGroup auto-selects the first option, so 'nothing selected' is impossible
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/radio-group.tsx:18-19`
  - **Evidence:** const firstEnabled = items.find((item) => !item.disabled);\n const [val, setVal] = useControllable(value, defaultValue ?? firstEnabled?.value ?? "", onChange);
  - **Impact:** A team rendering <TKRadioGroup options={...} /> for an optional question gets the first radio pre-checked and never receives an onChange for it, so their form state silently disagrees with the UI (they think nothing is chosen; the user sees option 1 filled). There is no way to start blank without going fully controlled.
  - **Fix:** Default the uncontrolled value to "" (no selection) and only pre-select when defaultValue is explicitly provided; keep the WAI-ARIA 'first arrow press selects' behavior via roving instead of pre-selecting on mount.
  - **Done when:** Mounting an uncontrolled TKRadioGroup with no defaultValue renders zero radios with aria-checked=true; supplying defaultValue selects exactly that option.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — radio-group.tsx:18-19 seeds the uncontrolled value to `firstEnabled?.value` when no defaultValue is given, so the first radio renders aria-checked/filled while onChange never fires for it — no way to start blank without going fully controlled.
- [x] **`CTL-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-THEME` — Switch knob and slider thumbs hardcode #fff + rgba shadows instead of tokens
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/switch.tsx:51-52`
  - **Evidence:** background: "#fff",\n boxShadow: "0 2px 6px rgba(0,0,0,.22)", — mirrored in sliders.tsx:98-99 and 270-271 (background:"#fff"; boxShadow:"0 2px 8px rgba(0,0,0,.25)").
  - **Impact:** On a light Telegram theme the white knob sits on a light --tk-surface-3 off-track / light slider track with almost no contrast, and the fixed black drop-shadow ignores dark mode. These knobs are invisible to the accent/density/theme knobs the rest of the kit exposes, so a Mini App matching Telegram's theme-params can't restyle them and fails low-vision contrast on the off-state.
  - **Fix:** Replace #fff with a semantic token (e.g. --tk-thumb / --tk-on-surface) and the raw shadow with --tk-shadow-sm or an elevation token, defined per light/dark in tokens.css; add a subtle inset ring for the off-state so the knob is distinguishable on light tracks.
  - **Done when:** Switching tokens.css [data-theme=light]/[dark] visibly restyles the knob/thumb; an automated contrast check on the off-state knob vs track passes >=3:1, and no raw #fff/rgba color literals remain in the controls directory.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — Hardcoded non-token values confirmed: switch.tsx:51-52 (background '#fff', boxShadow rgba(0,0,0,.22)) and sliders.tsx:98-99 and 270-271 ('#fff' + rgba(0,0,0,.25)); these ignore theme/accent tokens used elsewhere.
- [x] **`CTL-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Interactive touch targets fall well under the 44px Telegram minimum
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/switch.tsx:27-28`
  - **Evidence:** const W = small ? 42 : 51; const H = small ? 26 : 31; — the standalone (label-less) Switch button has padding:0 so its hit area is 51x31. Rating buttons are size 26 + padding 2 (~30px, rating.tsx:50-58); the chip remove button is width/height 22 (chips.tsx:118-120); stepper buttons are 34x34 (stepper.tsx:57-58).
  - **Impact:** In a narrow finger-driven Telegram WebView these are mis-tap magnets: a 31px-tall standalone switch, 30px rating stars, a 22px chip dismiss button and 34px steppers all miss the 44px target guideline, causing accidental toggles/removals and failing the project's own a11y gate intentions.
  - **Fix:** Keep the visual size but expand the hit area to >=44px via padding or a transparent ::before/inset overlay on the button (the label-less switch, rating stars, chip remove, and stepper buttons); document min target in the family contract.
  - **Done when:** getBoundingClientRect (or a Playwright bounding-box assertion) reports >=44px in both axes for the standalone switch button, each rating star, the chip remove button, and the stepper +/- buttons.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — All cited dimensions verified and under 44px: standalone switch 51x31 (padding:0, H=31), rating star ~30px (icon 26 + padding 2), chip remove button 22x22 (chips.tsx:118-120), stepper buttons 34x34 (stepper.tsx:56-57); .tk-press adds no hit-area padding.
- [x] **`CTL-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — Sliders render an unlabeled role=slider when `label` is omitted; range thumbs are indistinguishable
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/sliders.tsx:79-90`
  - **Evidence:** aria-label={label} on both range thumbs (sliders.tsx:84) and on the single track (sliders.tsx:207); label is an optional prop with no default and no fallback to aria-labelledby/value.
  - **Impact:** Omitting label (easy to do — it's optional) yields a slider with no accessible name, an axe violation. For range mode both thumbs share the identical label, so a screen reader announces two indistinguishable 'slider' handles, leaving low-vision users unable to tell min from max thumb.
  - **Fix:** Require a name (label or aria-labelledby) or derive one; in range mode suffix per-thumb labels (e.g. `${label} minimum` / `${label} maximum` via locale strings) so each thumb is uniquely named.
  - **Done when:** A slider without label triggers a dev warning or type error; axe reports no 'aria-input-field-name' violation; the two range thumbs expose distinct accessible names.
- [x] **`CTL-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-STATE` — Stepper `editable` accepts NaN/empty/out-of-range drafts until blur and shows the wrong mobile keyboard
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/stepper.tsx:83-101`
  - **Evidence:** value={draft ?? String(v)} ... onChange={(e) => setDraft(e.target.value)} onBlur={() => { if (draft != null) { const parsed = Number(draft); if (Number.isFinite(parsed)) setV(...) } setDraft(null); }} — no inputMode/enterKeyHint, and during editing the typed string is held unvalidated.
  - **Impact:** A quantity stepper lets the user type '999' or '-5' or clear the field and only snaps on blur, so any logic reading onChange mid-edit (live totals, price, validation) sees nothing while the field shows an invalid value; clearing then blurring restores the old value with no feedback. Without inputMode='numeric' the mobile soft keyboard opens to the full QWERTY/text keyboard on many Telegram WebViews.
  - **Fix:** Add inputMode="numeric" enterKeyHint="done"; clamp/validate on each change (or at least surface that the draft is invalid) and treat an empty/NaN blur as min rather than silently reverting; consider firing onChange on commit consistently.
  - **Done when:** On a mobile WebView the numeric keypad appears; typing an out-of-range or non-numeric draft is clamped/rejected predictably and onChange reflects the committed value; clearing the field commits min, not the stale previous value.
- [ ] **`CTL-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TYPES` — ChipGroup and Slider expose untyped unions instead of discriminated props
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/chips.tsx:155-163`
  - **Evidence:** multi?: boolean; value?: string | string[]; defaultValue?: string | string[]; onChange?: (value: string | string[]) => void; — onChange is always the union regardless of multi. Likewise TKSliderProps (sliders.tsx:7-26) bundles value/onChange and rangeValue/onRangeChange/defaultRange in one flat interface with no `range` discriminant.
  - **Impact:** Consumers of ChipGroup must hand-narrow string|string[] in every onChange handler even when multi is statically false/true, and TS won't catch passing value to a range slider or rangeValue to a single one. This is a daily friction footgun and lets invalid prop combinations compile.
  - **Fix:** Make TKChipGroup generic/overloaded on multi (multi:true => string[], else string) and split TKSliderProps into a discriminated union on `range` (single: value/onChange; range:true: rangeValue/onRangeChange) so the compiler enforces the right value shape and callback.
  - **Done when:** A multi ChipGroup's onChange parameter infers string[] (single infers string) with no manual narrowing, and passing value to a range slider (or rangeValue to single) is a type error.
- [ ] **`CTL-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-RESP` — Rating fill, slider geometry and value tooltip are left-anchored and break under RTL
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/rating.tsx:62-66`
  - **Evidence:** <span style={{ position: "absolute", inset: 0, width: "50%", overflow: "hidden", ...}} data-tk-half-star> — half-star always fills the left 50%. Sliders compute left:`calc(${pct}% - 14px)` and the tooltip left:`calc(${pct}% - 18px)` (sliders.tsx:266, 282) with no logical-property/dir handling.
  - **Impact:** In an RTL Telegram locale (Arabic/Hebrew/Persian) the half-star fills the wrong half and the slider thumb/fill/tooltip read left-to-right against the document direction, so a 20% value renders at the visual 80% position. Mini Apps shipping RTL get a visually inverted, confusing control.
  - **Fix:** Detect direction (CSS logical properties / inset-inline-start, or a dir-aware pct) and mirror the half-star fill and slider geometry under RTL; gate on document/computed direction rather than hardcoding left.
  - **Done when:** Rendering inside dir="rtl" mirrors the half-star fill and positions the slider thumb/fill/tooltip from the inline-start edge so a given value maps to the correct visual position.
- [x] **`CTL-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-STATE` — Sliders divide by `step` and `(max-min)` with no guard against step<=0
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/sliders.tsx:52`
  - **Evidence:** const snapped = Number(Math.min(max, Math.max(min, min + Math.round((raw - min) / step) * step)).toFixed(4)); — single slider mirrors this at sliders.tsx:168; step defaults to 1 but is consumer-controllable with no validation.
  - **Impact:** A consumer passing step={0} (or a negative step) produces Math.round(x/0) => NaN, and aria-valuenow/value become NaN, freezing the thumb and emitting NaN through onChange into their form state. max===min is guarded for pct but step=0 is not, so a config typo silently corrupts the control.
  - **Fix:** Clamp step to a positive minimum (e.g. step = step > 0 ? step : 1) at the top of each impl, and guard the snap so a degenerate range returns min; optionally warn in dev.
  - **Done when:** Rendering a slider with step={0} or step<0 still moves in sane increments and never emits NaN via onChange or aria-valuenow.
- [x] **`CTL-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — RadioGroup, ChipGroup, Rating, Stepper and Slider drop ref and group-level aria/disabled passthrough
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/radio-group.tsx:7-16`
  - **Evidence:** export interface TKRadioGroupProps { options; value; defaultValue; onChange; disabled; testId } — no ref, no aria-label/aria-labelledby; the container is <div role="radiogroup" data-testid={testId} ...> with no labelling. ChipGroup container (chips.tsx:183) is a bare <div> with no role/aria; Rating/Stepper/Slider are plain functions (no forwardRef).
  - **Impact:** A radiogroup with no accessible name is an axe violation, and there's no prop to label it; ChipGroup announces as a generic group with no name or multiselect semantics. None of these group/value controls forward a ref, so consumers can't focus, scroll-into-view, or measure them, forcing wrapper-div workarounds and breaking composition with form libraries.
  - **Fix:** Add aria-label/aria-labelledby (and an aria-orientation) to RadioGroup and ChipGroup containers, give ChipGroup role/aria-multiselectable semantics, and forwardRef the group root on RadioGroup/ChipGroup/Rating/Stepper/Slider (consistent with Checkbox/Switch/Chip which already do).
  - **Done when:** RadioGroup/ChipGroup accept and apply aria-label(/labelledby) with no axe name violation, and a forwarded ref on each group control resolves to its root DOM node.
- [x] **`CTL-011`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — Slider track captures the pointer with touchAction:none, conflicting with Telegram vertical-swipe-to-minimize
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/sliders.tsx:110-123`
  - **Evidence:** onPointerDown={(e) => { ... e.currentTarget.setPointerCapture(e.pointerId); ... }} ... style={{ ... touchAction: "none", ... }} — single slider mirrors this (sliders.tsx:214-228). There is no vertical-swipe guard or disableVerticalSwipes coordination.
  - **Impact:** touchAction:none plus pointer capture means a touch that starts on the slider swallows vertical movement; in a Telegram WebView a downward drag begun on the slider can fight the native swipe-to-minimize gesture, and a horizontal drag that strays vertically still drags the thumb. Mini Apps embedding a slider near the top of the sheet get janky gesture conflicts the kit doesn't help mediate (other gesture components in the repo, e.g. pull-to-refresh, explicitly coordinate this).
  - **Fix:** Constrain to horizontal intent (touchAction: 'pan-y' until a horizontal drag is detected, or only capture after horizontal threshold) and/or call the Telegram disableVerticalSwipes guard while dragging, releasing on pointerup — mirroring the gesture coordination already used elsewhere in the kit.
  - **Done when:** Starting a vertical swipe on the slider still lets the WebView/sheet handle it (no thumb jump), while a clear horizontal drag moves the thumb; verified in a Playwright touch-drag test.
- [ ] **`CTL-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-EDGE` — Position-indexed groups have no empty-list / out-of-range guard
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/controls/radio-group.tsx:23`
  - **Evidence:** const tabbable = tkTabbableIndex(items.findIndex((item) => item.value === val), items.length, disabledAt); — with an empty options array findIndex returns -1 and tkTabbableIndex falls back to 0; ChipGroup seeds focusIdx the same way (chips.tsx:175) and Rating maps Array.from({length:max}) with no max<=0 guard (rating.tsx:25).
  - **Impact:** Passing options={[]} (a real async/empty-data state) renders an empty radiogroup whose computed tabbable index is 0 with no item, and Rating with max={0} or a negative max renders nothing or throws on Array.from; teams hitting a loading/empty branch get subtle dead controls or runtime edge cases instead of a clean empty render.
  - **Fix:** Early-return a benign empty state when items.length===0 (and clamp Rating max to >=0), so groups render nothing focusable rather than computing indices against an empty list.
  - **Done when:** Rendering RadioGroup/ChipGroup with [] and Rating with max<=0 produces a stable empty control with no console error and no stray tabbable index.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`CTL-DX-001`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** Every input atom (input.tsx, textarea.tsx, choices.tsx, native-input.tsx) wraps itself in TKFormField and gets label/hint/error/required + aria-invalid/aria-describedby wiring for free, but NOT ONE control atom does. To label a Switch, RadioGroup, Slider, Rating or Stepper with a description and validation error, a consumer must hand-roll a label element, generate ids, and wire aria-describedby themselves — Profile.tsx:131 falls back to a bare `ariaLabel` string, PlatformLab.tsx:103 hand-builds a `<Row label>` wrapper around every control. There is no way to show a field-level error on a RadioGroup/ChipGroup/Slider at all.
  - **Proposal:** Add the existing `label?: ReactNode; hint?: ReactNode; error?: ReactNode; required?: boolean` quartet (same names as TKInput) to every control and render through TKFormField, auto-generating an id with useId() and threading htmlFor/aria-describedby/aria-invalid into the control's focusable element. Common case becomes one line: `<TKSlider label="Radius" hint="px" value={r} onChange={setR} />` renders the caption-styled label, optional hint, and an inline red error — and `error` flips aria-invalid automatically so a consumer cannot ship a slider/radio with a visible error that screen readers can't see. Keep the bare `aria-label`/`ariaLabel` path for standalone (label-less) usage.
- [ ] **`CTL-DX-002`** · types · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** TKChipGroup (chips.tsx:155-163) types `value`/`defaultValue`/`onChange` against the untyped union `string | string[]` regardless of `multi`. Consumers must re-narrow at the call site — Feed.tsx:278 is forced to write `onChange={(value) => onDifficulty(Array.isArray(value) ? value : [value])}` even though it always passes `multi`. Single-select consumers get a `string[]` in their handler's type and have to defensively cast. TKSlider (sliders.tsx:7-25) has the same shape: `value`/`onChange` (single) and `rangeValue`/`onRangeChange` (range) coexist on one flat interface gated only by a boolean `range`, so nothing stops `<TKSlider range onChange={...} />` (wrong handler) from type-checking.
  - **Proposal:** Make `multi`/`range` discriminants drive inference via overloaded prop types. ChipGroup: `type TKChipGroupProps = (TKChipBase & { multi: true; value?: string[]; defaultValue?: string[]; onChange?: (v: string[]) => void }) | (TKChipBase & { multi?: false; value?: string; defaultValue?: string; onChange?: (v: string) => void })`. Now `multi` infers `string[]` everywhere and Feed.tsx drops the Array.isArray dance. Same discriminated split for Slider so `range` exposes only `rangeValue`/`defaultRange`/`onRangeChange` and single exposes only `value`/`onChange` — passing the wrong handler becomes a compile error.
- [ ] **`CTL-DX-003`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** Group controls are inconsistent and under-labeled by default. TKRadioGroup (radio-group.tsx:25) emits `role=radiogroup` with no aria-label/aria-labelledby hook; TKChipGroup (chips.tsx:183) renders a plain `<div>` with no group role at all, so a screen reader announces N disconnected toggle buttons with no group name; TKRating (rating.tsx:24) hardcodes `aria-label={locale.rating}` so two ratings on one screen ('Service', 'Delivery') are indistinguishable. There is no 44px-target or labeling story a consumer can opt into — they'd have to wrap and relabel manually.
  - **Proposal:** Give every group a real group semantic that's labeled by CTL-DX-001's `label`: RadioGroup keeps role=radiogroup, ChipGroup gains role=group (or role=listbox+aria-multiselectable for multi), Rating's group label comes from `label` instead of the generic locale string — all wired via aria-labelledby to the FormField label id. Where no `label` is given, surface a dev-only `console.warn` (stripped in prod) naming the component, e.g. 'TKRadioGroup: pass `label` or `aria-label` so the group is announced.' This makes the accessible-by-default path the one-line path and makes the inaccessible path loud in dev.
- [ ] **`CTL-DX-004`** · composition · effort L
  - **Pain:** The selection components are closed: TKRadioGroup, TKChipGroup, TKRating and TKStepper render their own buttons/inputs with fully hardcoded inline styles and expose no way to render a custom item, swap the control element, or attach to a real form. ContextChip.tsx has to re-skin TKChip via a `style` prop override (padding/gap/fontSize) because there's no variant/asChild hook; anyone wanting a card-style radio, an emoji rating, or a labeled chip with a count badge has to abandon the component and reimplement roving focus and aria from scratch. None of these controls render a hidden native input, so they can't participate in an uncontrolled `<form>` submit / FormData.
  - **Proposal:** Add a `renderItem?` render-prop to the group controls (RadioGroup/ChipGroup/Rating) — `renderItem?(item, state: { selected; disabled; focused }): ReactNode` — keeping roving focus, aria and keyboard in the kit while letting consumers own the visuals (ContextChip stops fighting `style`). For form participation, add an optional `name?: string` that emits a visually-hidden native `<input type="radio|checkbox|hidden">` mirroring the value so these controls submit in a plain form and reset with it. Provide a `TKChip` `size?: 'sm' | 'md'` token-driven variant so the common re-skin is a prop, not a style splat.
- [ ] **`CTL-DX-005`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKStepper `editable` (stepper.tsx:84-114) ships a poor default UX: `type="number"` with no `inputMode`/`pattern`, so iOS Telegram opens the full punctuation keyboard instead of the digit pad; the draft accepts NaN/empty/out-of-range until blur with no `enterKeyHint`; and there is no formatting hook for currency/units. The common 'quantity 1..99' case is fine, but the moment a consumer wants '$' or a thousands separator they must abandon `editable`. Sliders likewise have no value formatter — only a raw `suffix` string, so a 1500 → '1.5k' or '$1,500' label is impossible without forking.
  - **Proposal:** Default Stepper's editable input to `inputMode="numeric"` + `enterKeyHint="done"` and constrain the draft on input (allow only digits/sign, clamp to [min,max] live, reject NaN before blur). Add a shared `format?: (value: number) => string` (and optional `parse?`) to both Stepper and Slider so the displayed number and the slider tooltip/aria-valuetext route through it — `<TKSlider format={px} />`, `<TKStepper format={usd} />`. This makes the correct mobile keyboard and localized display the default-with-one-prop instead of a fork.
- [ ] **`CTL-DX-006`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** There is no Storybook/MDX or copy-pasteable usage for this entire family — the only references are the two example apps, which exercise a narrow slice (readonly Rating, single Switch, multi ChipGroup) and silently model anti-patterns: PlatformLab.tsx hand-wraps every Slider in a bespoke `<Row label>` because the kit has no labeled-control recipe, and the controlled-vs-uncontrolled contract (when to pass `value` vs `defaultValue`, why RadioGroup auto-selects index 0) is undocumented, which is exactly what bites consumers in CTL-002. A developer's first question — 'how do I show a required radio group with an error?' — has no answer in the repo.
  - **Proposal:** Add a `controls.stories.tsx` (or MDX) per component with: the one-line happy path, a controlled example, a labeled+error+required example (post CTL-DX-001), a disabled example, and a 'standalone, no visible label' example showing `aria-label`. Include a short 'Controlled vs uncontrolled' note and an explicit 'allow no initial selection' RadioGroup recipe. Mirror the existing inputs stories structure so the family is discoverable and the accessible patterns are the copy-paste defaults.
- [ ] **`CTL-DX-007`** · api · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** Group APIs are split and inconsistent on the smallest details, raising the learning cost. RadioGroup/ChipGroup take `options`/`items` (two different prop names for the same TKOption[] concept), expose `testId` but drop ref + group-level `aria-*`/`disabled` passthrough (they don't extend TKDomProps the way Checkbox/Switch/Chip do), and only Stepper/Slider/Rating support `disabled` while RadioGroup supports it but ChipGroup has no group-level `disabled` at all. A consumer can't disable a whole ChipGroup in one prop, can't ref it, and has to remember 'items here, options there'.
  - **Proposal:** Unify the group surface: settle on a single prop name (`options: TKOption[]`) across RadioGroup and ChipGroup with the other kept as a deprecated alias for one minor; have all controls extend TKDomProps (id, aria-label/labelledby/describedby, testId) and forwardRef to the group root; and add a group-level `disabled?: boolean` to ChipGroup that cascades to every chip (matching RadioGroup/Slider). Net effect: `<TKChipGroup options={x} disabled aria-label="Filters" ref={r} />` works the same shape as every sibling control.
- [ ] **`CTL-DX-008`** · a11y · effort L · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** TKRating's interactive mode (rating.tsx:30-72) is a row of `aria-pressed` toggle buttons with no roving tabindex, no arrow-key model, and half-values reachable only by a left/right mouse-click hit-test (e.clientX vs rect midpoint) — so keyboard and touch users cannot enter 3.5, and a screen reader hears five independent press buttons rather than one rating. This is the most common 'leave a rating' interaction in a Mini App and today the accessible path doesn't exist, forcing any conscientious consumer to rebuild it.
  - **Proposal:** Re-model interactive Rating as a single `role=slider` (aria-valuemin=0, aria-valuemax=max, aria-valuenow, aria-valuetext='3.5 of 5 stars') with Arrow keys stepping by `allowHalf ? 0.5 : 1`, Home/End jumping to 0/max, reusing the same keyboard core as TKSlider so half-values are keyboard-reachable by construction. Keep `readonly` as `role=img` with an aria-label of the value (no focusable buttons). This makes 'a keyboard user can give a 3.5-star rating' true by default instead of impossible.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

COVERAGE GAPS: There are zero *.test.tsx and zero *.stories.tsx files for the entire controls family (verified via find/grep across packages/uikit/src) — so none of the keyboard, ARIA, controlled/uncontrolled, RTL, or edge-value behaviors above are guarded by the project's Storybook/Playwright a11y+visual gates. This is the single biggest latent risk: every finding here is currently unprotected against regression.\n\nVERIFIED-SAFE (not flagged): (1) Haptics are correct — useOptionalHaptics (packages/telegram/src/provider.tsx:113-130) is default-off, optionally-chained against wa?.HapticFeedback, and safe to call with no provider/runtime, so SSR/runtime-absent is fine. (2) prefers-reduced-motion is handled globally — tokens.css:332-337 collapses --tk-t1/--tk-t2 to 1ms and gates .tk-pop behind no-preference, so the inline transitions in these controls respect reduced motion. (3) No dangerouslySetInnerHTML, file inputs, or untrusted src handling anywhere in the family, so D-SEC has no findings. (4) useControllable (internal/useControllable.ts) is a correct controlled/uncontrolled bridge; controlled-mode drift is not an issue except for the RadioGroup default-selection in CTL-002.\n\nASSUMPTIONS: CTL-004 target sizes are read from the inline style literals; actual rendered hit area was not measured in a browser (no Playwright run performed in this audit). CTL-011's gesture conflict is inferred from touchAction:none + setPointerCapture against the repo's documented Telegram vertical-swipe behavior (MEMORY notes pull-to-refresh/gesture work) rather than reproduced live. CTL-008 RTL behavior is inferred from the hardcoded left/width:50% geometry; no RTL render was executed.\n\nCONSUMER CONTEXT: examples/trailhead (PlatformLab.tsx) and surface-composer (BuyerTrustStrip/SurfaceSlots) use TKRating mostly in readonly+allowHalf display mode (which sidesteps CTL-001's keyboard gap) and TKSlider with an explicit label, so the demos happen to avoid the worst paths — meaning external Mini App teams using the interactive/unlabeled variants are the ones who will hit these first.

---

### Display atoms — Avatar, Badges, Blockquote, Image, Spoiler

`DSP` · `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display` · 12 findings (0🔴 3🟠 7🟡 2⚪) · 9 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility · `D-SEC` Safety / PII exposure · `D-STATE` State model & edge values · `D-TG` Telegram runtime & scope coupling · `D-API` API ergonomics · `D-THEME` Tokens / theming correctness · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView / i18n / RTL · `D-PERF` Performance / CLS

#### Findings

- [x] **`DSP-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Avatar photo defaults to alt="" and leaves initials text under the image, so screen readers announce stale initials (or nothing) for a person's photo
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/avatar.tsx:18-78`
