  - **Evidence:** `alt = ""` default plus render order: `{initials}` is a text node and the photo is `<img src={src} alt={alt} ... style={{ position:"absolute", inset:0 }} />`. With a photo present and no alt, the image is decorative (alt="") while the initials text node stays in the DOM behind it.
  - **Impact:** A consumer rendering `<TKAvatar src={user.photo} initials="JD" />` (the documented fallback pattern) produces an avatar that a screen reader reads as 'JD' even though a real photo is shown, or as nothing when initials are empty. There is no way to give the photo an accessible name without also seeing the alt text, and the initials are never hidden from assistive tech.
  - **Fix:** When a photo is showing, hide the initials from AT (wrap them in `aria-hidden`) and surface a real name: derive the avatar's accessible name from `alt` (fall back to `initials`) on the container, and set the inner `<img>` `alt=""`/`aria-hidden`. Treat the whole atom as a single labelled `img` role rather than two competing text sources.
  - **Done when:** With `<TKAvatar src=... initials="JD" alt="Jane Doe" />`, an accessibility-tree snapshot exposes exactly one node named 'Jane Doe' and no 'JD' text; with only `initials` and no src, the name is 'JD'; with a loaded photo and no alt, no stale initials are announced.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — avatar.tsx:18 defaults alt=""; lines 69-78 render the {initials} text node then an absolutely-positioned <img alt={alt}>. With a photo + empty alt, the image is decorative while the initials text node stays in the DOM/accessibility tree with no aria-hidden — exactly the claimed stale-announcement defect. No CSS hides the initials.
- [x] **`DSP-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Avatar presence status (online/offline) is purely visual — no role/label, invisible to screen readers
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/avatar.tsx:21-47`
  - **Evidence:** The presence dot renders as `<span data-tk-avatar-status style={{...background: status==="online" ? "var(--tk-green)" : "var(--tk-text-3)"}} />` with no `role`, `aria-label`, or text alternative; the custom-node branch is also an unlabelled `<span>`.
  - **Impact:** A Mini App showing who is online (chat lists, member rosters) conveys that state only through a green/gray dot. Screen-reader and low-vision users get no signal, and online vs offline differ only by color — a WCAG 1.4.1 (use of color) failure baked into the kit.
  - **Fix:** Add a visually-hidden text alternative or `role="img" aria-label` to the status dot driven by a localizable string (e.g. add `online`/`offline` to TKLocale), so presence is announced; keep it optional via an `aria-hidden` escape hatch for the custom node.
  - **Done when:** `<TKAvatar status="online" />` exposes an accessible 'Online' (localized) text alternative in the a11y tree; toggling to 'offline' changes the announced text, not just the color.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — avatar.tsx:24-41 — the presence dot is a bare <span data-tk-avatar-status> with no role/aria-label/text, and online vs offline differ only by background color (var(--tk-green) vs var(--tk-text-3)). The custom-node branch (line 40) is also unlabelled, so the state is invisible to AT (WCAG 1.4.1).
- [x] **`DSP-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-SEC` — TKSpoiler only blurs and disables selection in CSS — the hidden text stays in the DOM and is read by screen readers, copy tools and page scrapers before reveal
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/spoiler.tsx:31-44`
  - **Evidence:** Hidden content is rendered as `<span aria-hidden={open ? undefined : "true"} style={{ filter: open ? "none" : "blur(6px)", ... userSelect: open ? undefined : "none" }}>{children}</span>` — `aria-hidden` plus a blur filter and `user-select:none`; the actual text remains in the DOM at all times.
  - **Impact:** A team using TKSpoiler to hide a spoiler/PII/2FA code believes it is concealed, but the plaintext is present in the DOM and accessibility tree path before reveal: it leaks via DevTools, 'select all', browser reader mode, OCR-free copy, and any DOM-reading extension. `aria-hidden` also means even when revealed=true the toggle path can leave it momentarily mis-announced. This is a security-relevant gap given the component's stated purpose.
  - **Fix:** Document explicitly that TKSpoiler is presentational-only and must not be used for secrets; for true concealment, gate the children behind the `open` state (don't render or render a masked placeholder until revealed) so the sensitive string is absent from the DOM until the user reveals it.
  - **Done when:** Either (a) docs/JSDoc warn it is visual-only and not for PII, and a guarded variant renders no child text until revealed, verified by querying the DOM for the secret string while `revealed=false` and getting no match; or (b) the default no longer ships secret text in the DOM pre-reveal.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — spoiler.tsx:32-44 — {children} is always rendered into the DOM and only blurred via CSS filter + aria-hidden/user-select:none when closed, so the plaintext is present in the DOM at all times and leaks via DevTools, reader mode, and scrapers before reveal. The 'momentary mis-announce on reveal' aside is speculative, but the core DOM-leak defect is reproduced.
- [x] **`DSP-004`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-STATE` — TKSpoiler reveal is irreversible and the controlled API can't re-hide it
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/spoiler.tsx:22-29`
  - **Evidence:** `onClick={() => !open && setOpen(true)}` and the keydown handler only ever call `setOpen(true)`; there is no path that sets it back to false. The element drops `role="button"`/`tabIndex` once open, so it can never be toggled closed by the user.
  - **Impact:** A consumer passing `revealed={false}` expecting a toggle (e.g. 'tap to hide again' or programmatic re-blur after navigation) finds it impossible from interaction; `onRevealChange` only ever fires `true`. Lists that reuse spoiler nodes across virtualized rows can show previously-revealed content because the atom has no re-hide story.
  - **Fix:** Support toggling: keep it focusable/labelled while open and call `setOpen(!open)`, or at minimum honor a controlled `revealed={false}` to re-blur and document the one-way default. Ensure `onRevealChange` can emit `false`.
  - **Done when:** Driving `revealed` from true→false re-blurs the content and restores the button affordance; an uncontrolled toggle variant flips both directions and emits both `true` and `false` through `onRevealChange`.
- [x] **`DSP-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — Spoiler focus ring and reduced-motion collapse only exist inside the .tk root — atoms rendered outside TKThemeProvider lose keyboard focus visibility and ignore prefers-reduced-motion
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/spoiler.tsx:36-40`
  - **Evidence:** Spoiler sets inline `transition: filter var(--tk-t2)...` and is keyboard-focusable (`tabIndex={0}`), but the focus ring is scoped `.tk :focus-visible { outline: ... }` (tokens.css:329) and the reduced-motion override only fires under `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce){ .tk { --tk-t2:1ms } ... }` (tokens.css:332-341). Both require the `.tk` class that only TKThemeProvider adds (theme.tsx:147).
  - **Impact:** Mini App teams routinely drop a single atom into existing markup without wrapping it in TKThemeProvider. There, the focusable Spoiler shows no visible focus outline (keyboard a11y failure) and its blur transition still animates for users who asked for reduced motion. The coupling is invisible until QA on a real device.
  - **Fix:** Make focusable display atoms self-sufficient: render a component-local `:focus-visible` outline (or inline focus style) and respect reduced motion without depending on the `.tk` scope (e.g. gate inline transitions behind a `prefers-reduced-motion` check or a token that defaults safely). Alternatively document that focusable atoms require a TKThemeProvider ancestor.
  - **Done when:** Rendering `<TKSpoiler>` with no TKThemeProvider ancestor still shows a focus ring on Tab and renders no blur transition under an emulated `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (verified via Playwright a11y/visual on a bare mount).
- [x] **`DSP-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-STATE` — TKCounter max only clamps numeric values, silently ignored for string/ReactNode values and unsafe for negative/NaN
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/badges.tsx:75-86`
  - **Evidence:** `value: ReactNode` with `const shown = typeof value === "number" && max != null && value > max ? \`${max}+\` : value;`
  - **Impact:** Counts from an API often arrive as strings; `<TKCounter value={"1000"} max={99} />` renders the full '1000' and overflows the pill in a narrow WebView because the string bypasses the guard. Negative values (e.g. a decrement race) render as '-5' with no clamping, and there is no `0`-hides behavior, so badges flash '0'.
  - **Fix:** Coerce/parse numeric-looking values before comparing (or type `value` as `number | string` and document), clamp at 0 (or add a `showZero` prop), and apply `max+` to parsed numbers regardless of string vs number input.
  - **Done when:** `value="1000" max={99}` renders '99+'; negative values render '0' (or are clamped per prop); `value={0}` follows the documented zero behavior — covered by unit tests.
- [x] **`DSP-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — AvatarStack uses array index as React key — reordering/removing avatars causes wrong-image flashes and broken transitions
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/avatar.tsx:105-118`
  - **Evidence:** `{shown.map((a, i) => ( <span key={i} ... ><TKAvatar initials={a.initials} src={a.src} ... /></span> ))}`
  - **Impact:** A live presence/member stack that reorders or removes a participant reuses DOM nodes by position, so the lazy-loaded `<img>` and `failed` state from the previous occupant linger — users see the wrong face momentarily, and image error states stick to the wrong person. Classic index-key reconciliation bug in a component explicitly built for changing rosters.
  - **Fix:** Key by a stable identity from the item (add an optional `id`/`key` to TKAvatarStackItem and require/encourage it), falling back to a composite of src+initials only when no id is provided.
  - **Done when:** Removing the first avatar from a 4-item stack keeps remaining avatars' images stable (no flash/re-fetch), verified by a reorder test asserting each rendered img src tracks its item identity.
- [x] **`DSP-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — Display atoms don't forward refs or accept className/standard DOM props, and TKAvatar/TKBlockquote/TKImg take no style on the inner node — limited composition escape hatches
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/avatar.tsx:18-18`
  - **Evidence:** `export function TKAvatar({ initials, size, tone, src, alt, status, shape, testId }: TKAvatarProps)` — no `ref`, no `className`, no `style`, no rest-spread; TKBadge/TKBlockquote accept `style` but no `className`/`ref`; only TKImage takes `className`.
  - **Impact:** Consumers can't attach a ref to measure/scroll-into-view an avatar, can't add a utility class, and can't wire a tooltip/anchor or `onClick` without an extra wrapper. Inconsistent surface (some atoms take style, some don't; only Image takes className) forces per-component workarounds and prevents `asChild`-style composition that Mini App layouts frequently need.
  - **Fix:** Standardize the surface across the family: forward `ref`, accept `className`, and spread remaining DOM props (or an explicit `style`) on the root of every atom; document the consistent contract.
  - **Done when:** `<TKAvatar ref={r} className="x" onClick={fn} />` attaches the ref to the root element, applies the class, and fires onClick; the same `ref`/`className` contract holds for Badge, Dot, Counter, Blockquote, Img/Image, Spoiler.
- [x] **`DSP-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-THEME` — Avatar and Counter hardcode #fff for text instead of the --tk-on-accent token, breaking custom/light-accent themes
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/avatar.tsx:63`
  - **Evidence:** TKAvatar sets `color: "#fff"` (avatar.tsx:63) over `background: tone || "var(--tk-accent-grad)"`, and TKCounter sets `color: "#fff"` (badges.tsx:100) over `background: map[tone]`, while TKBadge correctly uses `var(--tk-on-accent)`.
  - **Impact:** A Mini App that sets a pale brand accent (Telegram lets users pick light themes / pale `tg-theme-button-color`) gets white initials on a near-white gradient — unreadable — because the atoms ignore the `--tk-on-accent` token that exists precisely to solve this. Inconsistency with TKBadge means the same kit contradicts itself.
  - **Fix:** Replace the literal `#fff` in TKAvatar and TKCounter with `var(--tk-on-accent)` (and for a custom `tone`, document that the consumer owns the contrast or expose an ink prop).
  - **Done when:** With a pale accent theme, avatar initials and counter text use `--tk-on-accent` and remain ≥ WCAG AA contrast; no raw `#fff` remains in the display atoms (grep clean).
- [x] **`DSP-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-RESP` — Badge has no max-width/overflow handling and presence dot uses LTR-fixed corner — long i18n labels overflow and RTL presence sits on the wrong side
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/badges.tsx:25-43`
  - **Evidence:** TKBadge root has `display:inline-flex; padding:"3px 9px"` with no `max-width`, `overflow`, `white-space`, or `text-overflow`. TKAvatar status dot is pinned `right:0, bottom:0` with `transform:"translate(20%,20%)"` (avatar.tsx:28-36) with no logical-property/RTL handling.
  - **Impact:** Translated labels (German/Russian compounds) or long status text push the badge past the row in a ~360px Telegram WebView with no ellipsis. In RTL locales the avatar presence dot stays bottom-right instead of mirroring to bottom-left, landing over content and looking broken. Both are predictable i18n/RTL regressions for international Mini Apps.
  - **Fix:** Give TKBadge a sane `max-width`/`overflow:hidden`/`text-overflow:ellipsis` option (or document single-line intent and clamp), and position the avatar status dot with logical insets (`inset-inline-end`) so it mirrors under `dir="rtl"`.
  - **Done when:** A 40-char badge label ellipsizes within a 360px container instead of overflowing; under `dir="rtl"` the avatar presence dot renders on the bottom-leading corner (visual snapshot).
- [x] **`DSP-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-PERF` — TKAvatar re-renders itself recursively whenever status is set, doubling the component tree and the src-reset effect
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/avatar.tsx:21-48`
  - **Evidence:** `if (status != null) { ... return ( <span ...><TKAvatar initials={initials} size={size} tone={tone} src={src} alt={alt} shape={shape} /> {dot} </span> ); }` — the status branch mounts a second TKAvatar instance that re-runs `useState(failed)` + `useEffect(() => setFailed(false), [src])`.
  - **Impact:** Every avatar with a presence dot (chat lists, rosters — exactly the high-count case) instantiates two avatar components and two effects per item, adding avoidable render/effect overhead in long virtualized lists on low-end Telegram Android devices. The recursion also makes the failed-image state live in the inner instance, complicating any future memoization.
  - **Fix:** Refactor to a single render: compute the avatar body once and overlay the dot as a sibling within one component, avoiding the recursive self-render and duplicate effect. Consider `React.memo` for stack items.
  - **Done when:** Rendering `<TKAvatar status="online" />` mounts a single TKAvatar instance (one src-reset effect) per React profiler/commit, with no behavioral change to the dot.
- [ ] **`DSP-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-STATE` — TKImage error state has no retry and no accessible distinction from the empty placeholder; blur-up placeholder lacks decoding hints
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/display/image.tsx:61-76`
  - **Evidence:** `const [state, setState] = useState(src ? "loading" : "error")` and on error it renders the same `tk-img-ph` div as the no-src case with `role={alt ? "img" : undefined} aria-label={alt || undefined}` and `{fallbackLabel ?? locale.image}`. There is no path back to 'loading' except a `src` change, and no `onRetry`.
  - **Impact:** When a CDN image 404s, the consumer gets a placeholder identical to 'no image provided' with no way to retry without changing the `src` reference, and if `alt` is empty the failed media has no accessible name at all — a feed of broken images all read as the generic 'image' label. Transient network failures (common on mobile) are unrecoverable from the component side.
  - **Fix:** Expose an imperative/`key`-based retry or an `onRetry`/`retryKey` prop, differentiate the error placeholder (distinct label/state flag) from the empty one, and add `decoding="async"`/explicit dimensions to reduce decode jank; ensure failed images still carry the alt as accessible name.
  - **Done when:** After an image error, providing a retry (prop or key bump) returns the component to 'loading' and re-attempts the fetch; the error placeholder is distinguishable (by test id/aria) from the no-src placeholder and exposes `alt` when provided.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`DSP-DX-001`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** The most common avatar case — a person with a photo that falls back to initials — forces the consumer to juggle four props (initials, src, alt, tone) and still ships a stale-initials a11y bug (avatar.tsx:18, alt defaults to ""). Initials must also be derived by hand at every call site: BuyerTrustStrip.tsx and GuideDirectory.tsx (line 163) both pre-compute {initials, tone}. The kit ships no helper, so every app reinvents 'first letters of name, uppercase, max 2'.
  - **Proposal:** Make the person case one line by accepting `name` and deriving the rest: <TKAvatar name="Ada Lovelace" src={url} />. When `name` is set, default `alt` to `name` (fixes the photo-alt case by construction), auto-derive initials via an exported `tkInitials(name)` util (first letter of first two words, locale-aware uppercase), and seed a deterministic `tone` from a hash of `name` so stacks get stable distinct colors for free. Keep initials/alt/tone as explicit overrides; `initials` still serves non-person avatars (emoji/place).
- [ ] **`DSP-DX-002`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** Every interactive atom in the kit is polymorphic + ref-forwarding + className-accepting (buttons/button.tsx uses TKPolymorphicProps, forwardRef, className), but all five display atoms are plain functions that swallow refs and reject className/standard DOM props (avatar.tsx:18, blockquote.tsx:13, badges.tsx:22). A consumer can't attach a tooltip ref to TKBadge, add a utility class, set a data-attr for analytics, or pass title/onClick — they must wrap each atom in an extra <span>, which is exactly what SurfaceSlots.tsx and the avatar-stack shadow span already do. This inconsistency bites within minutes of real composition (builds on DSP-008).
  - **Proposal:** Adopt the kit's existing convention on display atoms: accept `className` and spread `...rest: HTMLAttributes` onto the root, and wrap each in `forwardRef` so refs land on the rendered element. Reuse TKPolymorphicProps where an `as`/`asChild` root makes sense (e.g. TKBadge as a link/button). Concretely: `export const TKBadge = forwardRef<HTMLSpanElement, TKBadgeProps>(...)` with `interface TKBadgeProps extends HTMLAttributes<HTMLSpanElement>`. Removes the wrapper-span tax and makes display atoms feel like the rest of the kit.
- [ ] **`DSP-DX-003`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKSpoiler's a11y-by-default is impossible to get right today: hidden text stays in the DOM and is read by screen readers/scrapers before reveal (DSP-003), and there is no way to label what is hidden, so a screen-reader user just hears the secret text followed by 'Show hidden content' button (spoiler.tsx:20-21). The reveal is also one-way (DSP-004). Consumers have no prop to make any of this correct — the inaccessible path is the only path.
  - **Proposal:** Make the accessible spoiler the default and give it a real API: add `label` (e.g. 'Spoiler: episode ending') used as the button's accessible name, render children with `aria-hidden` AND visually-hidden/clipped (or gate them behind `hidden` until revealed) so SR/scrapers can't read them pre-reveal, and announce reveal via the existing button semantics. Add `rehideAfter?: number` plus support for the controlled `revealed={false}` round-trip so it can re-hide. Signature: <TKSpoiler label="Plot twist" rehideAfter={5000}>…</TKSpoiler>. A11y-correct becomes the zero-config behavior.
- [ ] **`DSP-DX-004`** · api · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** TKAvatar's presence API is stringly-typed and overloaded: `status?: "online" | "offline" | ReactNode` (avatar.tsx:12) mixes a semantic enum with an arbitrary node, which is why presence has no accessible label (DSP-002) and why the component recurses into itself to render the dot (avatar.tsx:42-47, the DSP-011 double-tree). Consumers can't express 'away/busy' without dropping to a raw node and losing all built-in styling, and there's no obvious place to attach a label.
  - **Proposal:** Split presence into a typed, labelled prop: `presence?: { status: "online" | "offline" | "away" | "busy"; label?: string }` (or a `presence` enum + `presenceLabel`). Render the dot inline in one pass (kill the self-recursion) with a visually-hidden label so SR announces 'Ada, online'. Keep a `badge?: ReactNode` slot for the genuinely-custom corner node, separating 'semantic presence' from 'arbitrary overlay'. Typed enum gives autocomplete, the label closes DSP-002, and the flattened render closes DSP-011.
- [ ] **`DSP-DX-005`** · api · effort S · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** TKCounter's `value: ReactNode` invites misuse: the `max` clamp silently no-ops for strings/ReactNode and is unsafe for negative/NaN (badges.tsx:85, DSP-006), and there is no zero-hiding, so the universal notification-badge pattern ('show count, hide at 0, cap at 99+') is hand-rolled by every caller. The type says 'any node' but the only well-supported shape is a number.
  - **Proposal:** Tighten to the real contract and bake in the common rules: `value: number` with `max?: number` (default 99) clamping safely (floor at 0, ignore NaN, render `${max}+` above cap) and `showZero?: boolean` (default false → renders nothing at 0, so callers can drop their `count > 0 &&` guards). For the rare label-badge case, point consumers at TKBadge instead. One-line common case: <TKCounter value={unread} /> just works and never overflows.
- [ ] **`DSP-DX-006`** · a11y · effort S
  - **Pain:** Display atoms silently theme onto #fff and assume the .tk root, so a consumer can ship something invisible without warning: Avatar and Counter hardcode `color: "#fff"` instead of --tk-on-accent (avatar.tsx:62, badges.tsx:100 — DSP-009), and Spoiler's focus ring + reduced-motion handling only exist inside .tk so atoms rendered outside TKThemeProvider lose keyboard focus visibility (spoiler.tsx, DSP-005). The failure is invisible at author time — it only shows on a light/custom accent or outside the provider.
  - **Proposal:** Default to tokens so correctness survives theming: swap both #fff to `var(--tk-on-accent, #fff)` (token first, #fff as the literal fallback), and inline the spoiler's `:focus-visible` outline + `@media (prefers-reduced-motion)` transition-collapse as component styles (or a tiny injected style block) so they don't depend on the .tk ancestor. Optionally dev-warn once if TKThemeProvider context is missing. Consumers get correct contrast and focus visibility without reading the theming docs.
- [ ] **`DSP-DX-007`** · perf · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKImage owns the trickiest perf-sensitive media pattern (blur-up, lazy, fallback) but leaves the highest-leverage perf hints to the consumer, who usually omits them: no `decoding="async"` / `fetchPriority` on the real or placeholder img, no `width`/`height` to reserve layout (only aspect-ratio), and the error state has no retry and is visually identical to the empty placeholder (image.tsx:64-76, 110-132 — DSP-012). SurfaceSlots.tsx:99 uses it for the hero 16:9 media, exactly where a missing fetch priority hurts LCP.
  - **Proposal:** Make TKImage fast and resilient by default: emit `decoding="async"` on both imgs and `fetchPriority="high"` when an opt-in `priority` prop is set (LCP hero), forward optional `width`/`height` for CLS-free reservation, and add a built-in retry — an accessible 'Couldn't load — retry' affordance in the error state (distinct copy + role from the empty `fallbackLabel` placeholder, with `onError` still firing). Hero usage becomes <TKImage src priority alt … /> and degrades gracefully.
- [ ] **`DSP-DX-008`** · api · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** TKAvatarStack only takes a flat `TKAvatarStackItem[]` (avatar.tsx:91) that omits `name`/`status` and keys by array index (DSP-007 wrong-image flashes on reorder). Real call sites already fight this: GuideDirectory.tsx:163 maps domain people into the item shape by hand, and the missing stable id is *in the data* (people have ids) but the component can't use it. There's also no '+N' affordance to expand or a way to label the group for SR.
  - **Proposal:** Let the stack consume domain data directly with a `getKey`/`id` so React keys are stable, and pass through the richer avatar props: `items` accept `{ id?, name?, src?, tone?, status? }` (reuse DSP-DX-001's `name` derivation), key by `id ?? src ?? name`, and add `aria-label` for the group plus an optional `onOverflowClick` so '+N' is actionable. Signature: <TKAvatarStack items={people} getKey={p => p.id} max={4} aria-label="5 guides" />. Fixes reorder flashing and removes the hand-mapping boilerplate.
- [ ] **`DSP-DX-009`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** These five atoms carry the kit's subtlest correctness rules — spoiler reveal semantics, avatar photo-vs-initials a11y, image blur-up/fallback, counter clamping — yet there's no Storybook/docs surface demonstrating the right way. Consumers learn by copying from examples (BuyerTrustStrip, GuideDirectory, SurfaceSlots), which means they also copy the index-key avatar stack and the alt="" photo. The correct pattern is undiscoverable without reading source.
  - **Proposal:** Add a Display Storybook page with copy-pasteable, a11y-correct recipes: 'Person avatar (name + photo + presence)', 'Avatar stack from domain data with getKey', 'Spoiler with label + re-hide', 'Image: hero priority, blur-up, retry-on-error', 'Counter: unread badge, hide-at-zero, 99+'. Include an a11y note per story and a 'Common mistakes' callout (alt="" on a photo, index keys, status without label) so the green path is the path of least resistance. Pairs with the new defaults so docs and code agree.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

COVERAGE GAP (high-signal): there are NO test or stories files anywhere in packages/uikit/src (confirmed via find for *.test.tsx/*.test.ts/*.stories.tsx — zero matches), so this entire display family ships with no unit, visual, or a11y coverage. The repo memory references a 'broken e2e gate'; combined with no atom-level tests, the findings above (alt/initials a11y, spoiler PII, counter clamping, index keys) would not be caught by CI. Recommend at minimum Playwright a11y snapshots for Avatar(status), Spoiler(focus+reveal), Image(error) and a unit test for Counter clamping.\n\nASSUMPTIONS / not independently device-verified: (1) DSP-005 assumes consumers sometimes render atoms outside TKThemeProvider — true for piecemeal adoption but the examples wrap content in the provider, so impact is conditional. (2) Soft 'gray' badge in real-TMA theme pairs --tk-text-2 (=tg-theme-subtitle-text-color fallback) on --tk-surface-3 (12% text mix); the documented AA tuning is verified for the brand inks but the neutral gray-on-gray pairing under arbitrary Telegram theme colors is unverified — flagged as a lower-confidence theming risk rather than a hard finding. (3) DSP-001 layering (image absolutely positioned over the initials text node) is read from the source; the exact SR announcement order was reasoned from DOM structure, not run through a live screen reader. (4) DSP-003 'select all'/scraper leakage is inferred from `user-select:none` being CSS-only with text present in the DOM. (5) The free-form Avatar `tone: string` (e.g. examples pass `hsl(...)` and the trailhead demo passes raw HSL) is an intentional escape hatch but is a contrast/dark-mode footgun worth documenting; folded into DSP-009 rather than listed separately to avoid padding. TKDot and TKImg are low-risk and were reviewed but produced no standalone findings.

---

### Inputs — Input, Textarea, Search, Select, OTP, SelectOtp, FileInput, FileSearch, Choices, Selectable, FormField, base

`INP` · `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs` · 12 findings (0🔴 5🟠 5🟡 2⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility (ARIA semantics, live regions, touch targets, reduced-motion) · `D-STATE` State model (controlled/uncontrolled, defaults, drift, error/disabled coverage) · `D-API` API ergonomics & consistency · `D-TG` Telegram runtime (SMS one-time-code autofill, narrow WebView keyboard, safe-area) · `D-SEC` Safety (file drop validation, image src, untrusted data) · `D-PERF` Performance (ref churn, re-renders, large option lists) · `D-EDGE` Robustness (empty lists, races, unmount-during-async, controlled flips)

#### Findings

- [x] **`INP-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — Uncontrolled TKSelect silently auto-selects the first option and never fires onChange — placeholder never shows, parent state desyncs
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/select.tsx:32-33`
  - **Evidence:** const firstEnabled = allItems.find((item) => !item.disabled); const [val, setVal] = useControllable(value, defaultValue ?? firstEnabled?.value ?? "", onChange);
  - **Impact:** A team rendering <TKSelect options={...} placeholder="Choose…" onChange={setX}/> uncontrolled gets the first option pre-selected visually, the placeholder is never shown, and onChange never fires for that initial value — so their form state stays empty/undefined while the UI says an option is chosen. This is a classic 'why is my form submitting nothing / wrong default' footgun and diverges from native <select> and from TKMultiselect (which defaults to []).
  - **Fix:** Default to empty string so the placeholder renders, matching native semantics: useControllable(value, defaultValue ?? ""). If an auto-first behavior is wanted, make it opt-in via a `defaultFirst` prop AND fire onChange(initialValue) once on mount so controlled parents stay in sync.
  - **Done when:** Render uncontrolled TKSelect with options and a placeholder and no defaultValue: the trigger shows the placeholder text (text-3 color) and the value is unselected until the user picks; a spy onChange is not called on mount. Existing defaultValue behavior unchanged.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — select.tsx:33 passes `defaultValue ?? firstEnabled?.value ?? ""` into useControllable, which (useControllable.ts:12,19-22) seeds internal state without calling onChange. Uncontrolled with no defaultValue pre-selects the first enabled option, so `selected` is truthy and the button (select.tsx:161) shows its label instead of the placeholder while onChange never fires for that initial value.
- [x] **`INP-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — OTP blinking-caret animation ignores prefers-reduced-motion and uses a hardcoded 900ms (not the motion token)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/otp.tsx:95-102`
  - **Evidence:** animation: "tk-fade-in calc(900ms / var(--tk-ms)) ease-in-out infinite alternate",
  - **Impact:** The reduced-motion media query in tokens.css only zeroes --tk-t1/t2/t3 and stops .tk-skel/.tk-pulse; this inline `infinite` animation is keyed off a literal 900ms, so a Mini App user with 'Reduce Motion' on (common accessibility setting) gets a permanently pulsing caret during code entry. Telegram/iOS users who enable reduced motion expect this to stop; it is an a11y regression and a battery/CPU cost on a hot auth screen.
  - **Fix:** Move the caret animation to a CSS class (e.g. .tk-otp-caret) defined under @media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference), and set the duration from a token. Under reduced motion render the caret static (solid bar) instead of animated.
  - **Done when:** With emulated prefers-reduced-motion: reduce, the OTP active-cell caret has no running animation (getAnimations() empty or animationName none) and remains visible as a static bar; with no-preference it blinks.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — The caret at otp.tsx:101 animates `tk-fade-in calc(900ms / var(--tk-ms)) ... infinite alternate`, keyed off a literal 900ms; the reduced-motion block at tokens.css:332-342 only zeroes --tk-t1/2/3 and stops .tk-skel/.tk-pulse, so this infinite animation keeps running with Reduce Motion on.
- [x] **`INP-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-TG` — Atom TKOTP lacks SMS one-time-code autofill (no autoComplete / name / autocomplete=one-time-code) that the PIN composite already wires
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/otp.tsx:53-62`
  - **Evidence:** <input ref={mergeRefs(ref, forwardedRef)} value={v} onChange={(e) => setV(...)} inputMode="numeric" aria-label={locale.oneTimeCode} style={{ position: "absolute", opacity: 0, pointerEvents: "none", width: 1, height: 1 }} />
  - **Impact:** The hidden input has pointerEvents:none and no autoComplete="one-time-code" and no name, so iOS/Telegram cannot offer SMS code autofill — the #1 reason to use an OTP field in a Mini App. src/composites/forms/pin.tsx already sets autoComplete="one-time-code" (line 112), so the atom is strictly worse than the sibling and teams must drop down to PIN or hack it.
  - **Fix:** Add autoComplete="one-time-code", a forwardable `name` prop, and remove pointerEvents:"none" (keep it visually hidden but focusable/autofillable). Mirror the PIN composite's approach.
  - **Done when:** The rendered OTP input has autoComplete="one-time-code"; a programmatic value set by autofill (fireEvent.change) populates the cells and fires onChange/onComplete. A `name` prop appears on the input for form submission.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — The hidden OTP input (otp.tsx:53-62) has pointerEvents:none and no autoComplete/name, while the sibling PIN composite sets autoComplete="one-time-code" (pin.tsx:112), so the atom cannot offer iOS/Telegram SMS code autofill.
- [x] **`INP-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — TKSelect renders an editable filter <input> as a direct child of role=listbox — invalid combobox/listbox ARIA
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/select.tsx:176-251`
  - **Evidence:** <div role="listbox" id={listId} aria-labelledby={labelId} aria-hidden={!open} ...> {searchable && open ? (<div ...><input ref={searchRef} ... aria-controls={listId} .../></div>) : null} {items.map(...<button role="option" .../>)}
  - **Impact:** A listbox may only contain option/group elements; embedding a textbox inside it makes the searchable combobox semantically invalid. Screen readers (VoiceOver in the Telegram iOS WebView) can mis-announce the filter or the option count, and the input's aria-controls pointing back at its own containing listbox is circular. Teams relying on a11y audits (the repo gates on Playwright a11y) will fail axe 'aria-required-children'.
  - **Fix:** Use the APG editable-combobox pattern: render the filter input in the trigger row (or a header outside the listbox), give it role=combobox/textbox, and reference the listbox via aria-controls/aria-owns. Keep only role=option/group children inside role=listbox.
  - **Done when:** axe-core reports no aria-required-children / aria-required-parent violation on the searchable TKSelect open state; the filter input is not a DOM descendant of the element with role=listbox.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — select.tsx:176 is role="listbox" and, when searchable+open, renders an editable <input> (select.tsx:214) as a direct descendant with aria-controls pointing back at its own listId (select.tsx:219) — invalid listbox children plus a circular aria-controls that fails axe aria-required-children.
- [x] **`INP-005`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-SEC` — TKFileInput dropZone bypasses the `accept` allowlist — dropped files of any type are committed
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/file-input.tsx:81-90`
  - **Evidence:** onDrop={dropZone ? (event) => { event.preventDefault(); setDragOver(false); if (disabled) return; const dropped = Array.from(event.dataTransfer?.files ?? []); if (dropped.length) commit(multiple ? dropped : dropped.slice(0, 1)); } : undefined}
  - **Impact:** `accept` is honored by the native picker (input accept=…) but the drag-drop path commits dataTransfer.files raw. A consumer building an avatar uploader with accept="image/*" gets arbitrary files (PDFs, executables, 200MB videos) handed to onFilesChange on drop, and the component even builds a preview object URL. Backends trusting the client's accept contract get unexpected payloads; it's an inconsistency between the two intake paths and a mild abuse vector.
  - **Fix:** Filter dropped files against `accept` before commit (match MIME and extension globs the same way the browser does for the picker), and optionally enforce single-file when !multiple. Surface rejected files (e.g. ignore or call an onReject).
  - **Done when:** With accept="image/*" and dropZone, dropping a .pdf results in onFilesChange not being called with the pdf (or being called with an empty/filtered list); dropping a .png still works. Add a unit test for the drop path.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — file-input.tsx:81-89 onDrop commits dataTransfer.files raw with no `accept` filtering, while the native picker honors accept={accept} (file-input.tsx:121); dropped files of any type are committed and a preview object URL is built (commit, lines 55-63).
- [x] **`INP-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-PERF` — mergeRefs is called inline in JSX across the family, recreating the ref callback every render (forwarded ref thrashes null→node)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/input.tsx:132`
  - **Evidence:** ref={mergeRefs(inputRef, ref)} // also selectable.tsx:69, otp.tsx:54, select.tsx:129, file-input.tsx:119
  - **Impact:** Because a new callback identity is produced each render, React detaches and re-attaches the ref every render: a consumer's forwarded ref is set to null then to the node on every parent re-render, which breaks focus/measurement effects that read ref.current and assume stability, and adds churn on a typing-hot input. The sibling composite masked.tsx:58 already wraps mergeRefs in useMemo, so the fix pattern exists in-repo and the atoms simply didn't adopt it.
  - **Fix:** Memoize: const setRef = useMemo(() => mergeRefs(inputRef, ref), [ref]); and pass setRef. Apply across input, selectable, otp, select, file-input.
  - **Done when:** Re-rendering the parent without changing the forwarded ref does not invoke the forwarded ref callback again (spy on a callback ref shows a single call across multiple parent renders); ref.current stays the same node.
- [ ] **`INP-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — Inconsistent onChange contract and missing state props across siblings (TKInput forwards the event; Textarea/Search/OTP/Select do not; OTP/Search/Selectable lack error/disabled-or-hint)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/textarea.tsx:11`
  - **Evidence:** onChange?: (value: string) => void; // textarea — vs input.tsx:20 onChange?: (value: string, event?: ChangeEvent<HTMLInputElement>) => void;
  - **Impact:** TKInput's onChange promises the native event (documented for caret access) but TKTextarea/TKSearch/TKOTP/TKSelect drop it, so the same family teaches two different callback shapes. Separately, TKOTP exposes no `disabled`/`error` and TKSearch no `disabled`, so an auth screen that must lock the OTP during verification, or show a 'wrong code' error inline, has to wrap the component or fork it. This prop-coverage drift is the kind of thing that forces per-component workarounds in a real form.
  - **Fix:** Standardize the onChange signature (value, event) where a DOM event exists, document the exceptions, and add disabled+error (and aria-invalid wiring) to TKOTP and disabled to TKSearch. Consider a shared TKControlBaseProps interface.
  - **Done when:** TKTextarea/TKSearch onChange receive the native event as a second arg (typed); TKOTP accepts disabled (blocks input + dims) and error (renders message + aria-invalid). Types compile and stories demonstrate the disabled/error OTP.
- [x] **`INP-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — Field errors are not announced — no role=alert / aria-live on the error region
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/form-field.tsx:36-47`
  - **Evidence:** {hint || error ? ( <div id={describedBy} style={{ ... color: error ? "var(--tk-red)" : "var(--tk-text-2)" }}> {error || hint} </div> ) : null}
  - **Impact:** The error text is only linked via aria-describedby, which is announced when focus enters the field — but an async/submit-time validation error that appears while focus is elsewhere (or after blur) is silent to screen-reader users. Mini App signup/checkout forms that set `error` after a server round-trip won't notify assistive tech, and the built-in email validator (input.tsx) only updates on blur, so the message change is unannounced.
  - **Fix:** Give the description container role="status" with aria-live="polite" (or split a dedicated live region for error vs hint, error as assertive), so error changes are announced without moving focus.
  - **Done when:** Programmatically setting the `error` prop while focus is outside the field causes a live-region announcement (the error node has aria-live/role=alert); axe and a manual VoiceOver pass confirm the message is read.
- [x] **`INP-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — Clear and reveal-password controls are ~20px hit targets — below the 44px touch minimum in a narrow WebView
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/input.tsx:169-215`
  - **Evidence:** width: 20, height: 20, ... <TKIcon name="close" size={11} strokeWidth={2.6} /> // clear button; reveal button has no min size either
  - **Impact:** The clear (20x20) and password reveal (icon-only, no min box) buttons are well under the 44x44 CSS-px target Apple/WCAG recommend. Inside a cramped Telegram WebView on a phone, users mis-tap or tap the input instead of clearing; this is exactly where the family is meant to shine. The reveal button has no padding/min-size at all.
  - **Fix:** Keep the visual glyph small but expand the hit area to >=44px via padding or a pseudo-element/overlay (e.g. position the icon in a 44px tap target), for both the clear and reveal buttons, and the Search cancel button.
  - **Done when:** The clear and reveal buttons report a >=44px hit rectangle (bounding box incl. padding/::before) in a pointer/touch context; visual layout is unchanged. A Playwright touch-target check passes.
- [ ] **`INP-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — TKMultiselect aria-activedescendant/option ids ignore the optional selectAll row, so the active id can point at the wrong/non-existent element
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/choices.tsx:113,209-246`
  - **Evidence:** aria-activedescendant={open && active >= 0 ? `${id}-opt-${active}` : undefined} // active indexes into `items` ... selectAll && <button role="option" ... (no id) ...> ... items.map((item, i) => <button id={`${id}-opt-${i}`} role="option" ...>)
  - **Impact:** When selectAll is enabled there is an extra role=option button with no id rendered before the items, but keyboard navigation (moveActive) and aria-activedescendant index into `items` only. The selectAll row is never reachable by keyboard and is announced as an unmanaged option, while the active descendant mapping assumes the first option is items[0]. Screen-reader users can't toggle 'select all' via keyboard and the active highlight semantics are inconsistent — a correctness gap for an a11y-gated kit.
  - **Fix:** Include the selectAll row in the navigable model (give it a stable id, fold it into the active-index space or give it role=checkbox outside the option list) so keyboard nav and aria-activedescendant stay consistent.
  - **Done when:** With selectAll, ArrowUp/Down can land on the select-all row, Enter toggles all, and aria-activedescendant always resolves to an element that exists in the DOM; axe finds no duplicate/missing id.
- [ ] **`INP-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-EDGE` — TKSelect re-derives firstEnabled/flatten on every render and its empty/all-disabled paths produce a dead trigger with no feedback
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/select.tsx:27-33,68-75`
  - **Evidence:** const allItems = tkFlattenOptions(options); const firstEnabled = allItems.find((item) => !item.disabled); ... const move = (dir) => { if (!items.some((item) => !item.disabled)) return; ... }
  - **Impact:** options is flattened and scanned on every render (no useMemo), so a large async-loaded option set re-allocates each keystroke of the filter; and when options is empty or every option is disabled, the trigger still opens an empty popup with no 'no results / empty' state and keyboard nav silently no-ops. A team wiring a remote-search Select sees a blank dropdown and an unselectable control with no affordance.
  - **Fix:** Wrap flatten + firstEnabled in useMemo([options]); render an empty-state row ('No options' / locale key) when items is empty or all-disabled, and reflect a loading prop if provided.
  - **Done when:** Profiling shows flatten runs once per options change, not per render; opening a Select with empty options (or all disabled) shows an explicit empty-state row instead of a blank popup.
- [ ] **`INP-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-STATE` — TKFileInput keeps internal-only file state — fully controlled usage is impossible and the preview object URL can leak across rapid swaps
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/inputs/file-input.tsx:46-63`
  - **Evidence:** const [files, setFiles] = useState<File[]>([]); ... const commit = (next: File[]) => { setFiles(next); onFilesChange?.(next); revokePreviewUrl(); ... }
  - **Impact:** There is no `files`/`value` prop — the component is uncontrolled-only, so a team that removes a file in their own UI, or resets the form after upload, cannot clear the displayed filename/preview; they're forced to remount via key. The object-URL lifecycle is handled on commit/unmount but not if the consumer needs to externally reset, so the stale preview persists. Limits real upload flows (progress is a prop but file list is not).
  - **Fix:** Accept an optional controlled `files` prop (with onFilesChange) so the parent can drive/clear the list, and revoke/rebuild the preview URL from the controlled value. Keep uncontrolled behavior as the default.
  - **Done when:** Passing files={[]} after an upload clears the displayed filename and preview without remount; passing a controlled file list renders its names; object URLs are revoked when the controlled list changes.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`INP-DX-001`** · types · effort L · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** Every component re-declares the same field props by hand, and they have drifted. TKInput.onChange is `(value, event)` (input.tsx:20) while Textarea/Search/OTP/Select are `(value) => void`; TKInput/Textarea/FileInput/FormField accept `label/hint/error/disabled`, but OTP (otp.tsx:7-20), Search (search.tsx:6-23) and Selectable (selectable.tsx:6-19) accept none of `error`/`hint`, and Multiselect lacks `name`. A developer cannot learn one mental model and reuse it; swapping TKInput for TKSelect in a form silently changes the onChange signature. The red-team logged the symptom (INP-007); the DX fix is a single shared contract.
  - **Proposal:** Introduce and export a `TKFieldProps<V>` base in inputs/base.tsx: `{ label?, hint?, error?, disabled?, required?, name?, id?, testId?, value?: V, defaultValue?: V, onChange?: (value: V, event?: ...) => void }`. Have TKInput/Textarea/Search/Select/Multiselect/OTP/Selectable extend it (`interface TKSelectProps extends TKFieldProps<string> { options; ... }`). Standardize onChange as value-first with an optional second native-event arg everywhere, so the event is always available but never required. Ship a codemod note in the migration guide. This makes 'replace one field with another' a zero-surprise edit.
- [ ] **`INP-DX-002`** · types · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKSelect/TKMultiselect throw away the caller's value type. `options` is `Array<TKOption | TKOptionGroup>` and `onChange` is hardcoded `(value: string) => void` (select.tsx:14, choices.tsx:19). A consumer with `const cities = ['lisbon','berlin'] as const` gets `onChange(value: string)` with no narrowing, so a switch over the result has no exhaustiveness checking and typos in `value="belrin"` compile clean. This is the single biggest 'types that just work' gap in the family.
  - **Proposal:** Make the option components generic over the option value: `function TKSelect<const V extends string = string>(props: TKSelectProps<V>)` where `options: Array<TKOption<V> | TKOptionGroup<V>>`, `value?: V`, `onChange?: (value: V) => void`. With `const` type params, `options={['lisbon','berlin'] as const}` infers `V = 'lisbon' | 'berlin'` and onChange/value become literal-typed for free, no extra annotation. Mirror for TKMultiselect with `V[]`. Keep the plain-string default so existing call sites are untouched.
- [ ] **`INP-DX-003`** · defaults · effort S
  - **Pain:** `required` is implemented in TKFormField (form-field.tsx:9,32 renders the asterisk) but is NOT a prop on any public atom — TKInput/Textarea/Select never forward it (input.tsx:98 passes no `required`). So the one-line common case 'mark this field required' is impossible through the public API: you get neither the visual asterisk nor `aria-required`/native `required` on the control. Developers must drop down to composing TKFormField by hand, defeating the atom.
  - **Proposal:** Add `required?: boolean` to the shared TKFieldProps (INP-DX-001) and plumb it: pass `required` into TKFormField for the asterisk, set `aria-required={required}` and the native `required` attribute on the underlying input/textarea, and on Select/Multiselect set `aria-required` on the combobox button. One prop, correct visuals + a11y + native validation, by default.
- [ ] **`INP-DX-004`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** Only TKInput exposes a `validate` hook (input.tsx:43) with touched-gated, on-blur, type=email-aware behavior. Textarea/Select/OTP/Search have no validation entry point, so any non-trivial form re-implements 'validate on blur, show on touched, announce the message' per field — and most consumers will skip the a11y announcement entirely. There is no shared way to surface a derived error message.
  - **Proposal:** Lift the touched/validate/email logic out of TKInput into a small exported `useTKFieldValidation(value, { validate, type })` hook returning `{ error, onBlur, aria }`, and accept `validate?: (value: V) => ReactNode` on TKFieldProps for every text-like field (Input/Textarea/Search/OTP). Pair it with the role=alert error region from the red-team's INP-008 so the derived message is announced automatically. Result: `validate={v => v.length < 3 ? 'Too short' : undefined}` works identically on any field and is accessible without extra wiring.
- [ ] **`INP-DX-005`** · ergonomics · effort L
  - **Pain:** TKFileInput keeps file state internal only (file-input.tsx:46 `useState<File[]>`); there is no `files`/`onFilesChange`-controlled mode and no way to render the chosen files, upload errors, or a remove-file affordance with kit styling. The red-team flagged the controllability bug (INP-012); the DX gap is that even read-back is awkward — consumers must mirror state via onFilesChange and build their own file list/remove UI from scratch, and there is no typed result object (size/type/validity).
  - **Proposal:** Ship a controlled mode (`files?: File[]`) routed through useControllable, and an exported `useTKFileInput({ accept, maxSize, multiple })` returning `{ files, getInputProps, getDropZoneProps, open, remove, errors }` so the common 'pick → validate type/size → list with remove buttons' flow is a few typed lines. Have TKFileInput consume the hook internally so its own `accept`/`maxSize` enforcement (incl. dropZone) is shared, and expose `onReject?: (rejected: {file, reason}[]) => void` for feedback. Closes the 'fully controlled is impossible' gap with an ergonomic primitive.
- [ ] **`INP-DX-006`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** There is no composition escape hatch in the family. `prefix`/`suffix` exist only on TKInput (input.tsx:34-36); Search has no slot for a trailing button, Select cannot render a custom trigger, and Selectable's leading control cannot be swapped. When a design needs a currency adornment on a Search, a custom Select trigger, or an avatar in a Selectable row, the developer must fork the component. The repo's own demo (trailhead Feed.tsx:401) wraps TKSearch in extra divs precisely because it cannot inject a trailing action.
  - **Proposal:** Add the prefix/suffix slot pattern uniformly to Search and Textarea (mirroring TKInput), and add a `renderTrigger?: (state: { selected, open, placeholder }) => ReactNode` (or `asChild`-style) escape hatch to TKSelect/TKMultiselect so consumers can supply a custom trigger while keeping the kit's listbox, keyboard nav and ARIA. For TKSelectable add a `control?: ReactNode` slot. Documents the boundary: 'style the chrome, keep the behavior'.
- [ ] **`INP-DX-007`** · defaults · effort S
  - **Pain:** Selection and code components silently drop out of native `<form>` submission and uncontrolled-form ergonomics. TKSelect/TKMultiselect/TKOTP/TKFileInput/TKSearch have no `name` prop (only Input/Textarea/Selectable do), so a plain `<form>` + FormData round-trip skips them and the developer must lift every one into React state. For a Mini App that posts a simple form this is a surprising amount of boilerplate.
  - **Proposal:** Add `name?` to TKFieldProps and render a hidden, form-associated value for the composite controls: a hidden `<input name>` mirroring the chosen value(s) in Select/Multiselect/OTP (OTP can reuse its existing visually-hidden input), and pass `name` to TKSearch's input. Then `new FormData(form).get('city')` just works, and uncontrolled forms need zero per-field onChange handlers. Pure addition.
- [ ] **`INP-DX-008`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** inputs.stories.tsx (107 lines) covers only happy-path single renders. There is no story for: a Select error/required/disabled state, Multiselect with `selectAll` (the row the red-team's INP-010 says breaks activedescendant ids), a controlled OTP with SMS autofill / onComplete, FileInput `dropZone` + reject feedback, or a full form-field matrix (hint vs error vs required vs disabled). So the components' most failure-prone and most-asked-about modes are undocumented and untested, and consumers copy the wrong (uncontrolled) pattern — which is exactly how the red-team's INP-001 (uncontrolled Select auto-select) ships into apps.
  - **Proposal:** Expand inputs.stories.tsx with copy-pasteable, controlled-by-default stories: `SelectStates` (placeholder/error/required/disabled/empty-options), `MultiselectWithSelectAll`, `ControlledOTPWithAutofill` (shows value/onChange/onComplete and autoComplete=one-time-code), `FileUploadDropZone` (accept enforcement + onReject), and `FieldMatrix` (hint/error/required/disabled grid). Add a short MDX 'Forms' page wiring several fields into one `<form>` with validate + FormData submit, as the canonical recipe.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

ASSUMPTIONS/INFERENCES: (1) ARIA/touch-target and reduced-motion findings (INP-002/004/008/009/010) are reasoned from code + tokens.css, not from a live axe/VoiceOver run — I did not execute the Playwright a11y suite; treat severities as best-estimate pending that gate. (2) INP-006 ref-thrash is a known React semantic of inline callback refs; I confirmed the in-repo memoized counter-example at src/composites/forms/masked.tsx:58 but did not measure a concrete focus-effect break in a consumer.\n\nVALIDATED-AND-DROPPED: I suspected TKSearch's expandOnFocus data-attributes had no backing CSS — false; tokens.css:287-293 implements [data-tk-search-expand] width animation (the only nit is that selector is global, not scoped under .tk). I also checked divide-by-zero on --tk-ms (motion knob): if a consumer sets --tk-ms:0 the OTP/transition calc()s break, but that's a token-misuse edge, not worth a finding.\n\nCOVERAGE GAPS in existing tests: behavioral tests exist only for TKOTP (test/otp.test.tsx) and export-wiring (test/inputs-reorg.test.tsx). There are NO unit tests for TKSelect keyboard nav / auto-select default (INP-001), TKMultiselect selectAll a11y (INP-010), TKFileInput drop-accept filtering (INP-005), controlled-value drift, or disabled/error rendering. Stories (storybook/atoms/inputs.stories.tsx) show a forced error state for TKInput but I did not confirm disabled/empty-option states are demonstrated for Select/Multiselect/FileInput. SSR-import safety: TKFileInput guards URL.createObjectURL/revokeObjectURL with typeof checks (good); TKSelect/TKMultiselect register document pointerdown listeners only inside useEffect (SSR-safe). No safe-area/viewport handling exists in this family, but these inline-positioned controls don't anchor to screen edges, so that's expected rather than a defect.

---

### Service + Icons — atoms/service.tsx, atoms/icons.tsx (icon set, VisuallyHidden/service primitives)

`SVC` · `packages/uikit/src/atoms` · 11 findings (0🔴 3🟠 4🟡 4⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility (focus, ARIA, disabled semantics, hidden content) · `D-API` API ergonomics & escape hatches · `D-TYPES` TypeScript contract quality · `D-THEME` Tokens/theming & visual correctness · `D-A11Y-MOTION` Reduced-motion & touch-target safety · `D-EDGE` Robustness of the icon registry · `D-PERF` Tree-shaking / bundle weight

#### Findings

- [x] **`SVC-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — TKTappable as="a" with disabled still navigates, focuses and fires onClick
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/service.tsx:68-77`
  - **Evidence:** `{...(Tag === "button" ? { type: "button", disabled } : { "aria-disabled": disabled || undefined })}` ... `onClick={onClick}` with no preventDefault, no tabIndex={-1}, no pointerEvents guard.
  - **Impact:** A consumer rendering `<TKTappable as="a" href="..." disabled>` (the documented link use-case) gets a link that is still keyboard-focusable, still navigates on Enter/click, and still fires onClick — aria-disabled is announced but nothing is actually disabled. Mini App teams ship a 'disabled' CTA that users can still trigger; fails axe/Playwright a11y gates.
  - **Fix:** For non-button tags when disabled: set tabIndex={-1}, gate onClick (disabled ? undefined : onClick), add pointerEvents:'none' to style, and for as="a" drop href (or preventDefault on click). Centralize so every polymorphic tag is inert when disabled.
  - **Done when:** Render `<TKTappable as="a" href="#x" disabled onClick={spy}>`; assert element is not in tab order, Enter/click does not call spy and does not change location.hash; axe reports no actionable-but-disabled violation.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — service.tsx:70 sets only aria-disabled for non-button tags (disabled attr is a no-op on <a>), and onClick is forwarded raw at line 76 with no preventDefault, tabIndex={-1}, or pointerEvents guard (grep confirms none exist). A `<TKTappable as="a" href disabled>` stays focusable, navigates, and fires onClick.
- [x] **`SVC-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-API` — TKVisuallyHidden cannot become a skip-link or receive className/style/aria — only testId passes through
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/service.tsx:12-37`
  - **Evidence:** Props are only `{ children, as, testId }`; the component hard-codes an inline style object and forwards no className, style, id, role, or other attributes: `<Tag data-testid={testId} style={{...hardcoded}}>`.
  - **Impact:** The standard sr-only/skip-link pattern (visually hidden until :focus, then revealed) is impossible because there is no class hook and inline styles can't express :focus. Consumers also can't add an id for aria-labelledby or a role for a live region, so they fork the primitive instead of using it — defeating its purpose.
  - **Fix:** Accept and forward className, style (merged over defaults), id, and rest attributes; expose an optional focusable mode (or ship a .tk-sr-only / .tk-sr-only-focusable CSS class in tokens.css) so it can reveal on focus.
  - **Done when:** `<TKVisuallyHidden className="x" id="skip" role="status">` renders those attributes; a focusable variant becomes visible on keyboard focus in a Storybook/Playwright check.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — TKVisuallyHiddenProps (service.tsx:12-16) is only {children, as, testId} with no ...rest spread; render (18-36) hard-codes an inline style and forwards no className/style/id/role, so the focus-revealed skip-link pattern and aria hooks are impossible.
- [x] **`SVC-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-THEME` — TKIcon filled mode flattens multi-element / cut-out icons into solid blobs
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/icons.tsx:100-118`
  - **Evidence:** `fill={filled ? "currentColor" : "none"}` `stroke={...}` `strokeWidth={filled ? 0 : strokeWidth}` applied to the whole svg, with no fillRule/clipRule. Icons like `verified` (badge path + inner check path) and `eye`/`user`/`bell` (outline + circle) all receive the same fill.
  - **Impact:** filled is safe only for the few single closed paths (star, heart). Passing filled to verified fills both the badge and the inner checkmark solid (check disappears), and any outline-style icon becomes an unrecognizable solid shape. A consumer building a filled active-tab state silently gets broken glyphs with no type-level warning.
  - **Fix:** Either restrict filled to a curated fill-safe subset (encode per-icon fillRule:'evenodd' and a solid flag), or store explicit filled path variants. At minimum document/type which names support filled.
  - **Done when:** Snapshot/visual test rendering every icon with filled shows recognizable solids (inner cut-outs preserved via evenodd), or filled is type-narrowed to fill-safe names.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — icons.tsx:109-111 applies fill=currentColor + strokeWidth=0 to the whole svg with no fillRule/clipRule (grep confirms absent). The `verified` icon's inner check (line 80) is an open stroke path that vanishes at strokeWidth 0, and multi-element icons like eye/user/bell fill all sub-paths solid with no even-odd cut-out.
- [x] **`SVC-004`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — TKIcon is permanently aria-hidden with no label/role escape hatch
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/icons.tsx:100-115`
  - **Evidence:** `aria-hidden="true"` is hard-coded on every TKIcon, and the prop set (name,size,strokeWidth,style,className,filled,testId) has no label/title/role/aria-* passthrough.
  - **Impact:** For decorative icons inside labeled buttons this is correct, but standalone meaningful icons (an icon-only status indicator, an inline glyph that carries meaning) can never be made accessible through this API. Consumers must wrap every such case in TKVisuallyHidden or a role=img wrapper — a recurring workaround across a Mini App.
  - **Fix:** Add optional label?: string: when present set role="img", aria-label={label}, render a <title>, and drop aria-hidden; keep aria-hidden="true" only when no label is given.
  - **Done when:** `<TKIcon name="warning" label="Warning" />` exposes an accessible name (role img / aria-label) and is not aria-hidden; without a label it remains aria-hidden.
- [x] **`SVC-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y-MOTION` — TKTappable always applies tk-press (active scale) ignoring prefers-reduced-motion
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/service.tsx:77`
  - **Evidence:** `className={["tk-press", className].filter(Boolean).join(" ")}` — tk-press is unconditional. In tokens.css `.tk-press:active { transform: scale(.96); }` sits OUTSIDE the `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` block that guards tk-pop/tk-rise/tk-shake.
  - **Impact:** Users with reduced-motion enabled still get the press scale/transition on every Tappable (and every component built on it), and there is no prop to opt out. In a narrow Telegram WebView this is exactly the motion such users disabled; fails a strict reduced-motion a11y review.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the .tk-press / .tk-press-soft :active transforms in @media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference), and/or add a pressEffect={false} prop on TKTappable to omit the class.
  - **Done when:** With prefers-reduced-motion: reduce emulated, pressing a TKTappable produces no transform/scale; a pressEffect={false} prop removes the tk-press class entirely.
- [x] **`SVC-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — TKTappable enforces no minimum 44px touch target
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/service.tsx:78-93`
  - **Evidence:** Style sets `padding: 0, margin: 0` with no minWidth/minHeight; size is entirely whatever children dictate. The kit's own internal icon usages render 11–18px icons inside this surface.
  - **Impact:** As the shared press primitive, TKTappable lets every consumer (and several internal components) ship sub-44px tap targets by default — e.g. an icon-only Tappable wrapping a 14px close icon. In a touch-first Telegram WebView this is a guaranteed WCAG 2.5.5 / mobile-usability failure unless each consumer remembers to add padding.
  - **Fix:** Add a hitSlop/minTarget default (e.g. minHeight:44, minWidth:44 or a ::before expanded hit area) that can be opted out for inline text links, so the safe default is a compliant target.
  - **Done when:** A bare `<TKTappable><TKIcon name="close" size={14}/></TKTappable>` reports >=44px interactive bounds in a layout/Playwright bounding-box assertion.
- [x] **`SVC-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — Unknown icon name renders an empty SVG silently (?? null)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/icons.tsx:116`
  - **Evidence:** `{TK_ICON_PATHS[name] ?? null}` — an out-of-set name yields an empty but still-sized <svg> with no warning.
  - **Impact:** Although TKIconName guards TS callers, real Mini Apps pass icon names from JSON/CMS/server config cast as TKIconName or via as any. A typo or a name removed in a kit upgrade renders an invisible blank box that takes layout space, with no console hint — a confusing production regression to debug.
  - **Fix:** In dev (process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production') console.warn on missing name; optionally render a visible fallback glyph (e.g. a question-mark box) so the gap is noticeable.
  - **Done when:** Rendering `<TKIcon name={'nope' as TKIconName} />` logs a dev warning (and/or shows a fallback) instead of an empty svg.
- [ ] **`SVC-008`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-EDGE` — Icon registry has a byte-identical duplicate (location === pin) and is missing chevronUp
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/icons.tsx:30,75`
  - **Evidence:** `location: <><path d="M12 21s-6.7-5.4-6.7-10.3a6.7 6.7 0 0113.4 0C18.7 15.6 12 21 12 21z" /><circle cx="12" cy="10.5" r="2.4" /></>` and `pin:` are character-for-character identical; grep -c chevronUp returns 0 while chevronDown/Left/Right exist.
  - **Impact:** pin is dead weight that misleads consumers expecting a thumbtack-style pin (they get a map marker). The missing chevronUp forces consumers building accordions/selects/sort toggles to rotate chevronDown via CSS or import their own icon — a papercut against an otherwise complete 77-icon set.
  - **Fix:** Either give pin a distinct thumbtack glyph or alias it to location intentionally with a comment; add chevronUp for symmetry with the other chevrons.
  - **Done when:** pin and location differ (or pin is documented as an alias); TK_ICON_NAMES includes chevronUp and it renders an upward chevron.
- [ ] **`SVC-009`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-PERF` — TK_ICON_PATHS eagerly instantiates all 77 icon ReactNodes; importing one icon pulls the whole set
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/icons.tsx:4-87`
  - **Evidence:** `const paths = { chevronRight: <path .../>, ... } satisfies Record<...>` (77 inlined JSX nodes) then `export const TK_ICON_PATHS: Record<TKIconName, ReactNode> = paths;` referenced by TKIcon via TK_ICON_PATHS[name].
  - **Impact:** Because TKIcon indexes the single shared object, a tree-shaker cannot drop unused icons — any consumer using even one icon bundles all 77 path nodes. For a Mini App on a metered/slow mobile connection this is avoidable KB on the critical first-paint path Telegram is strict about.
  - **Fix:** Keep raw path strings (data) rather than JSX in a const map, or split icons into individually importable modules / a getIcon(name) that allows code-splitting, so unused glyphs shake out.
  - **Done when:** A bundle/Rollup analysis of an app importing one TKIcon shows only the referenced glyph's path data, not all 77.
- [x] **`SVC-010`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-TYPES` — TKTappable impl typed as Record<string, unknown> and cast through a hand-written forwardRef signature
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/service.tsx:52-103`
  - **Evidence:** Impl param type `TKTappableOwnProps & { as?: ElementType } & Record<string, unknown>` and `ref as never`, then `export const TKTappable = forwardRef(TKTappableImpl) as <T extends ElementType = "button">(props: TKTappableProps<T> & { ref?: ForwardedRef<HTMLElement> }) => ReactElement;`.
  - **Impact:** The Record<string,unknown> widening means typos inside the impl are unchecked, and the external cast detaches the runtime from the type — if props drift, consumers get green types over wrong runtime behavior. The forced cast also fixes the consumer ref to HTMLElement regardless of as (an anchor ref isn't HTMLAnchorElement), weakening inference for the kit's flagship polymorphic primitive.
  - **Fix:** Type the impl against the polymorphic own-props without Record<string,unknown>, and make the ref generic over T (ElementRef<T>) so as="a" yields an HTMLAnchorElement ref.
  - **Done when:** `const r = useRef<HTMLAnchorElement>(null); <TKTappable as="a" ref={r} href="#" />` typechecks without cast, and an invalid attribute on the impl is flagged by tsc.
- [ ] **`SVC-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-A11Y` — TKVisuallyHidden uses deprecated clip without clip-path, risking content leak on some engines
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/atoms/service.tsx:22-32`
  - **Evidence:** Inline style relies solely on `clip: "rect(0, 0, 0, 0)"` plus a 1px box; there is no `clipPath: "inset(50%)"`. clip is deprecated and ignored in some layout contexts.
  - **Impact:** The community-standard sr-only recipe pairs clip with clip-path: inset(50%) for forward-compat; relying on deprecated clip alone risks visually-hidden text becoming partially visible/selectable in edge layout cases inside the WebView, exposing label/PII text meant only for screen readers.
  - **Fix:** Add clipPath: 'inset(50%)' alongside the existing clip, matching the canonical visually-hidden pattern.
  - **Done when:** TKVisuallyHidden style includes both clip and clipPath: inset(50%); a visual test confirms the text occupies 1px and is never painted.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`SVC-DX-001`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** atoms/service.tsx:18 TKVisuallyHidden is a one-shot label box. The single most common Mini App pattern — an icon-only tappable that is invisibly labelled for screen readers — still forces every consumer to hand-roll the markup. storybook/atoms/service.stories.tsx:33-51 spells out the boilerplate: a raw <button>, inline flex/size styles, a <TKIcon>, and a sibling <TKVisuallyHidden>. Inside the kit (atoms/buttons/icon-button.tsx) this is reimplemented privately, so app authors get none of it and routinely ship unlabelled icon buttons.
  - **Proposal:** Make the common case one line by giving TKTappable a first-class `label` (already present) the job of supplying the accessible name AND ship a tiny convenience: `<TKTappable.Icon name="bell" label="Notifications" onClick={...} />` (or a standalone `TKIconButtonBare`) that composes TKTappable + TKIcon + the hidden label, guarantees a 44px target, and emits aria-label. One import, accessible-by-default, no sibling markup. Keep the primitives for escape hatches.
- [ ] **`SVC-DX-002`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** atoms/icons.tsx:90 + every composite (atoms/buttons/button.tsx:23, controls/chips.tsx:24, inputs/input.tsx:12, overlays/action-sheet.tsx:14, navigation/tabbar.tsx, plus examples/surface-composer/src/surface/SurfaceSlots.tsx:26 and examples/trailhead/src/tabs.ts:8) types `icon?: TKIconName`. The whole API is welded to a closed 77-name union. A consumer who wants a brand glyph, an emoji, or a lucide/react-icons node literally cannot pass it to any kit component — there is no escape hatch anywhere in the surface.
  - **Proposal:** Introduce a `TKIconLike = TKIconName | ReactElement` type and a `renderIcon(icon, {size})` helper. TKIcon stays name-driven, but every `icon?` prop accepts `TKIconLike`; when given an element the kit clones it with the contextual size instead of looking up the registry. Consumers keep `icon="bell"` for built-ins and gain `icon={<BrandLogo/>}` for everything else — no fork, no wrapper components.
- [ ] **`SVC-DX-003`** · a11y · effort S
  - **Pain:** atoms/icons.tsx:114 hard-codes aria-hidden="true" with no override. This is right for decorative icons next to text, but the kit also renders meaning-only icons (e.g. the standalone star in controls/rating.tsx:63, status glyphs in overlays/toasts.tsx) where the icon IS the label. There is currently zero way to make a TKIcon expose an accessible name, so consumers either ship silent icons or abandon TKIcon and inline SVG.
  - **Proposal:** Add an optional `title?: string` prop. When present: drop aria-hidden, set role="img" and aria-label={title} (and/or render an <title> child). When absent: keep today's aria-hidden default so decorative usage stays a no-op. `<TKIcon name="warning" title="Error" />` becomes self-describing; bare `<TKIcon name="chevronRight" />` stays decorative. (Complements red-team SVC-004 — this is the ergonomic shape of the escape hatch.)
- [ ] **`SVC-DX-004`** · defaults · effort S
  - **Pain:** atoms/icons.tsx:116 `{TK_ICON_PATHS[name] ?? null}` plus icons.tsx:100 typed only as `name: TKIconName`. In a typed call site a bad name is caught, but the overwhelmingly common failure mode in real Mini Apps is a *dynamic* name from config/CMS/server (examples/trailhead/src/tabs.ts and SurfaceSlots feed icon names from data). A typo there silently paints an empty 22px box with no console hint, no fallback glyph — a debugging dead end. (red-team SVC-007 logs the correctness side; this is the DX remedy.)
  - **Proposal:** On unknown name: in DEV emit `console.warn(\`[TKIcon] unknown icon "${name}" — valid names: ...\`)` once per name, and render a visible fallback glyph (e.g. a dotted box or the `dots` icon) so the miss is obvious on screen instead of invisible. Optionally export `isTKIconName(x): x is TKIconName` so consumers can validate config-driven names at the boundary. Zero cost in prod builds.
- [x] **`SVC-DX-005`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** atoms/service.tsx:101-103 TKTappable is hand-cast through `Record<string, unknown>` and a bespoke forwardRef signature, and its own props leak no guidance. The killer DX gap surfaced by red-team SVC-001/SVC-006: as a polymorphic press surface it neither blocks navigation when `as="a" disabled` nor enforces a touch target — but from a *consumer* angle the real friction is that there is no safe, ergonomic `disabled` story for the link case and no documented minimum-hit-area knob, so authors must remember to re-add both by hand every time.
  - **Proposal:** Give TKTappable a `minTarget?: number | false` (default 44) that injects `minWidth/minHeight` + inline-flex centering so every tap surface is thumb-sized by default, and make `disabled` on a non-button render aria-disabled AND swallow onClick + strip href/tabIndex (so the same `disabled` prop behaves correctly regardless of `as`). One prop, correct-by-construction for both buttons and links.
- [ ] **`SVC-DX-006`** · perf · effort L
  - **Pain:** atoms/icons.tsx:4-81 builds all 77 icon ReactNodes eagerly into one `paths` object, then TK_ICON_PATHS/TK_ICON_NAMES re-export the whole map (icons.tsx:85-87). Because the index barrel does `export * from "./atoms/icons"` (src/index.ts:11), a consumer importing a single `<TKIcon name="bell"/>` — or even just the `TKIconName` *type* — drags the entire registry into the bundle; nothing is tree-shakeable. For a Mini App where startup payload is the whole game, this is the wrong default. (Builds on red-team SVC-009.)
  - **Proposal:** Split the type from the data: keep `TKIconName` as a pure type (zero runtime), and lazy-define paths as functions/string-`d` data behind the lookup so only referenced glyphs are retained, or document a `path-only` data module (`d` strings) that the kit and consumers import per-icon. Goal: importing one icon ships ~one path, not 77. Verify with a bundle-size story/test in storybook.
- [ ] **`SVC-DX-007`** · types · effort M
  - **Pain:** atoms/service.tsx:52-103 TKTappableImpl is typed `TKTappableOwnProps & { as?: ElementType } & Record<string, unknown>` and the public export is a manual cast `as <T extends ElementType>(...) => ReactElement`. The `Record<string,unknown>` defeats inference: with `as="a"` the editor does not strongly autocomplete/typecheck `href`, `target`, `download`, and `ref` is typed `ForwardedRef<HTMLElement>` rather than the element-specific ref. Polymorphic ergonomics — the entire point of this primitive — silently don't “just work.” (Builds on red-team SVC-010.)
  - **Proposal:** Drop the `Record<string, unknown>` and the hand-written cast; type the component via the existing TKPolymorphicProps<T> with a `PolymorphicRef<T>` so `ComponentPropsWithoutRef<T>` flows through. Result: `<TKTappable as="a" href=...>` autocompletes anchor attrs, `<TKTappable as="button">` rejects `href`, and `ref` infers the right element. No public API change, pure inference win.
- [ ] **`SVC-DX-008`** · docs · effort S
  - **Pain:** storybook/atoms/service.stories.tsx has exactly two static stories (TappableSurface, HiddenLabel) and storybook/atoms/icons.stories.tsx only renders a gallery. There is no copy-pasteable recipe for the things people actually reach for: a polymorphic link tappable (`as="a"`), a pressed/toggle tappable, a skip-link (which red-team SVC-002 shows is impossible today), a meaning-bearing labelled icon, or filled vs stroke icons. Authors learn the API by reading source.
  - **Proposal:** Add controls-driven, source-visible stories: TKIcon with `name`/`size`/`strokeWidth`/`filled`/`title` argTypes (name as a select over TK_ICON_NAMES); TKTappable stories for as="a" link, toggle (pressed), and disabled-link; and a SkipLink recipe once TKVisuallyHidden accepts className/style (red-team SVC-002). Each with a one-line copyable snippet so the common case is lift-and-drop.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

Coverage gaps / assumptions: (1) The entire Service+Icons family has ZERO tests and ZERO stories — grep for TKIcon/TKTappable/TKVisuallyHidden across *.test.tsx and *.stories.tsx returned nothing, and the package has 0 test files and 0 stories total. So every finding here is currently unguarded by CI; the Playwright a11y gate the kit advertises does not exercise these primitives directly.
