(2) TKTappable and TKVisuallyHidden have NO internal consumers in packages/uikit (other components reuse only the tk-press CSS class, not the component) — they exist purely as public API, which raises the stakes on their ergonomics (SVC-001/002/005/006/010) because external Mini App teams are the only users and have no internal reference implementation to copy. (3) TKIconName IS a load-bearing typed contract for both example apps (examples/trailhead/src/tabs.ts and examples/surface-composer SurfaceSlots/ContextChip/SurfaceContextSwitcher import it), which is why the registry's silent fallback (SVC-007) and missing chevronUp (SVC-008) matter for upgrade safety. (4) SVC-003 (filled flattening) and SVC-011 (clip) are inferred from the SVG/CSS structure, not from a rendered screenshot — I did not run a visual diff; verify by rendering verified/eye with filled. (5) SSR/CJS import safety is fine: all three are pure render-only components with no Telegram runtime/WebApp access, so D-TG SSR concerns do not apply to this family. (6) D-SEC: no dangerouslySetInnerHTML, no file/image src handling, and no PII surface here EXCEPT the visually-hidden text-leak risk noted in SVC-011.

---

### Cards — card primitives (composites/cards.tsx barrel + cards/primitives.tsx)

`CRD` · `packages/uikit/src/composites/cards` · 10 findings (0🔴 3🟠 5🟡 2⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility · `D-API` API ergonomics · `D-TG` Telegram runtime · `D-A11Y-MOTION` Reduced-motion / press feedback · `D-THEME` Tokens & contrast · `D-STATE` State model · `D-GESTURE` Touch & key gestures · `D-TYPES` TypeScript surface

#### Findings

- [x] **`CRD-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-API` — TKCard swallows all extra props — no aria-label/id/data/native attrs reach the DOM
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/cards/primitives.tsx:28-62`
  - **Evidence:** function TKCard({ children, onClick, interactive, padding = 14, inset = true, outlined, testId, className, style }, ref) { ... return (<div ref={ref} data-testid={testId} role={onClick ? "button" : undefined} ... >) } — there is no `...rest` param and no `{...rest}` spread, and TKCardProps lists no aria-* fields.
  - **Impact:** A consumer who makes a card a button (onClick) cannot give it an accessible name, id, aria-describedby, onPointerDown, or any data-* attribute. In examples/trailhead/src/features/discover/Feed.tsx:84-98 the team was forced to abandon TKCard's onClick entirely and nest a real `<button aria-label=…>` overlay plus re-apply `className="tk-press tk-press-soft"` by hand — a documented workaround proving the gap. Every clickable card becomes an unlabeled 'button' for screen readers.
  - **Fix:** Give TKCard the same treatment as TKCardCell: accept `...rest: Record<string, unknown>` (or extend ComponentPropsWithoutRef<'div'>) and spread `{...rest}` onto the div before the controlled props, and document aria-label as required when onClick is set. Add aria-label?/aria-labelledby? to TKCardProps explicitly.
  - **Done when:** Render `<TKCard onClick aria-label="Open" data-x="1" id="c" />` and assert the DOM div has aria-label="Open", data-x, id; add a test asserting role=button has an accessible name.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — primitives.tsx:14-26 TKCardProps lists no aria-*/id/data fields, and lines 29/34 have no `...rest` param or spread, so nothing reaches the div. The cited workaround is real: trailhead Feed.tsx:84-98 abandons TKCard's onClick and nests a real `<button aria-label=…>` overlay re-applying tk-press by hand (Feed.tsx:75).
- [x] **`CRD-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — TKCardChip has no accessible name pass-through and no disabled state
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/cards/primitives.tsx:157-205`
  - **Evidence:** function TKCardChip({ children, selected, tone = "accent", onClick, testId, className, style }, ref) { ... <button type="button" ref={ref} data-testid={testId} aria-pressed={selected} onClick={onClick} ...> {children} </button> } — no `...rest`, no `disabled`, no `aria-label`.
  - **Impact:** Icon-only chips (children is just an emoji/glyph) ship with no accessible name. There is also no way to disable a chip during async filtering — it stays focusable and clickable, so a Mini App showing a loading filter bar will fire onClick mid-fetch. Consumers can't pass aria-label, disabled, name, or value through.
  - **Fix:** Add `disabled?: boolean` (forward to the native button, and gate onClick), add `aria-label?` to the props, and spread `...rest` onto the button so native button attributes (disabled, name, form) pass through. Reflect disabled with reduced opacity token.
  - **Done when:** Render a disabled chip: button has the disabled attribute, onClick does not fire on click; render `<TKCardChip aria-label="Filter">★</TKCardChip>` and assert accessible name.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — primitives.tsx:157-205: TKCardChip destructures only children/selected/tone/onClick/testId/className/style with no `...rest`, no `disabled`, no `aria-label`; the `<button>` (l.182-203) only sets aria-pressed. Icon-only chips get no accessible name and the chip cannot be disabled during async filtering.
- [x] **`CRD-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — TKCardChip tap target ~24-28px tall — far below the 44px minimum
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/cards/primitives.tsx:189-200`
  - **Evidence:** style={{ ... borderRadius: "var(--tk-r-pill)", padding: "6px 10px", ... fontSize: "var(--tk-fz-caption)", fontWeight: 700, ... }} — caption font (~12-13px) + 6px vertical padding yields roughly 24-28px total height; no minHeight/minWidth.
  - **Impact:** In a narrow Telegram WebView these pills are very hard to hit reliably with a thumb and fail WCAG 2.5.5 / Apple/Telegram 44px touch-target guidance. TKCardCell sets minHeight:44/54 but the chip — the most-tapped filter control — has none.
  - **Fix:** Add `minHeight: 32` to the visual pill but expand the hit area to 44px via padding or a `::before` overlay / `min-height:44px` with negative margins, or document that chips must live in a 44px-tall row. At minimum guarantee a 44px hit slop.
  - **Done when:** Measure the rendered button bounding box (or a hit-test pseudo-element) and assert effective tappable height >= 44px in a 360px-wide viewport visual test.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — primitives.tsx:189-200 sets padding 6px vertical + fontSize var(--tk-fz-caption)=16px*.76≈12.16px (tokens.css:74,22) with no minHeight/minWidth, giving ~26px total height — below the 44px target. Contrast holds: TKCardCell sets minHeight 44/54 (l.117) but the chip sets none.
- [x] **`CRD-004`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y-MOTION` — Card press-scale animation ignores prefers-reduced-motion
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/tokens.css:184-193`
  - **Evidence:** .tk-press { transition: transform var(--tk-t2) ...; } .tk-press:active { transform: scale(.96); transition-duration: var(--tk-t1); } .tk-press-soft:active { transform: scale(.985); } — applied unconditionally; the reduced-motion block at lines 332-342 only sets --tk-t* to 1ms (collapsing duration) but the scale transform still occurs, and the pop/rise animations are gated by @media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference) while tk-press is not.
  - **Impact:** Every interactive TKCard/TKCardCell (which add tk-press/tk-press-soft) and TKCardChip still visibly shrink on tap for users who requested reduced motion. The duration collapses to 1ms so it's an abrupt jump rather than removal, which is arguably worse for motion-sensitive users in Telegram.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the :active transform rules in @media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference), or add a reduced-motion override `.tk-press:active{transform:none}` inside the existing prefers-reduced-motion: reduce block.
  - **Done when:** With prefers-reduced-motion: reduce emulated, click a TKCard and assert computed transform stays 'none' (no scale) via a Playwright/computed-style check.
- [x] **`CRD-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-THEME` — Selected chip forces --tk-on-accent ink on every tone → low contrast on light tones
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/cards/primitives.tsx:193-195`
  - **Evidence:** background: selected ? color : "var(--tk-surface-2)", color: selected ? "var(--tk-on-accent)" : ink — `color` is the tone color (e.g. tone="orange" → --tk-orange #e89623 light / #ffb224 dark) but the selected text is always --tk-on-accent (#ffffff). The per-tone `ink` variable computed just above is discarded when selected.
  - **Impact:** A selected orange chip is white text on #e89623 (contrast ~2.0:1) and white on #ffb224 in dark — both fail WCAG AA for the bold caption text. tone="gray" selected is white on --tk-text-2 (#57636f light) which is borderline. The bug is structural: the carefully-built per-tone ink map is only used for the unselected state.
  - **Fix:** Define an on-tone foreground per tone (e.g. --tk-on-orange/--tk-on-green tokens, or compute a contrast-safe ink) and use it for the selected color instead of always --tk-on-accent; or restrict the colored selected background to accent and use tinted surfaces for the others.
  - **Done when:** Snapshot/contrast-check each tone in selected state in light and dark themes; assert text/background contrast >= 4.5:1 (or >=3:1 if treated as large text).
- [x] **`CRD-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-GESTURE` — Space key on TKCard fires onClick on keydown and blocks page scroll; no double-tap guard
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/cards/primitives.tsx:40-46`
  - **Evidence:** onKeyDown={(e) => { if (!onClick) return; if (e.key === "Enter" || e.key === " ") { e.preventDefault(); onClick(); } }} — fires on keydown (so it auto-repeats while held) and preventDefault on Space; same pattern in TKCardCell lines 105-111.
  - **Impact:** Native button semantics fire click on Space *keyup*, not keydown, and only once. Here, holding Space repeatedly invokes onClick (e.g. repeated navigation or a repeated paid action), and a held Space on a focused card consumes the key so the user can't scroll the (often long) card list. Combined with no rapid-tap debounce, an interactive card that triggers a network action can double-fire.
  - **Fix:** Mirror native button: trigger Enter on keydown but Space on keyup, and ignore e.repeat. Consider documenting that consumers should debounce paid actions. Prefer rendering a real <button> for clickable cards rather than role=button on a div.
  - **Done when:** Unit test: holding Enter/Space (repeat=true) invokes onClick once; assert Space triggers on keyup; in a tall scroll container, Space on a focused card still scrolls when no onClick semantics intended.
- [ ] **`CRD-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — TKCardCell with onClick on a non-div, non-anchor `as` becomes an unlabeled, non-operable control
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/cards/primitives.tsx:102-111`
  - **Evidence:** role={onClick && Tag === "div" ? "button" : undefined} tabIndex={onClick && Tag === "div" ? 0 : undefined} onKeyDown={(e: KeyboardEvent) => { if (!onClick || Tag !== "div") return; ... }} — role/tabIndex/keyboard are gated to Tag==="div" only.
  - **Impact:** A common pattern like `<TKCardCell as="li" onClick={…}>` (list rows) or `as="label"`/`as="section"` gets a click handler with NO role, NO tabIndex, and NO keyboard handler — a mouse/touch-only control invisible to keyboard and screen-reader users, with no warning. The cursor:pointer still appears, so it looks interactive but isn't operable.
  - **Fix:** When onClick is set and Tag is neither a natively-interactive element (a/button) nor div, still apply role="button", tabIndex=0 and the keyboard handler (or dev-warn). Better: detect natively-interactive tags generically instead of hardcoding 'div'.
  - **Done when:** Render `<TKCardCell as="li" onClick={fn}/>`; assert it has role=button + tabIndex=0 and that Enter/Space invoke fn.
- [x] **`CRD-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-STATE` — No disabled/loading/readonly state on any card primitive
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/cards/primitives.tsx:14-26`
  - **Evidence:** interface TKCardProps { children?; interactive?; onClick?; padding?; inset?; outlined?; testId?; className?; style?; } — and likewise TKCardCellOwnProps / TKCardChipProps carry no disabled/loading/aria-disabled field.
  - **Impact:** Mini Apps routinely render cards/cells/chips in loading or disabled states (e.g. a row pending a network result, a chip disabled until a prerequisite is met). Consumers must hand-roll pointer-events:none + opacity + aria-disabled on every site, and risk firing onClick during loading. There's no consistent disabled affordance across the family.
  - **Fix:** Add `disabled?: boolean` to TKCardProps/TKCardCellOwnProps/TKCardChipProps that sets aria-disabled, removes tabIndex/role-button operability, blocks onClick, and applies a token-based dimmed style. Optionally a `loading?` for cells.
  - **Done when:** Render each primitive with disabled; assert onClick is not called, aria-disabled="true" is present, and the element is not in the tab order.
- [ ] **`CRD-009`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-TYPES` — onClick typed as ()=>void loses the DOM event; tone union has no escape hatch
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/cards/primitives.tsx:17,70,150,151`
  - **Evidence:** onClick?: () => void; (TKCardProps line 17, TKCardCellOwnProps line 70, TKCardChipProps line ~152) and tone?: "accent" | "green" | "red" | "orange" | "gray"; (line 150).
  - **Impact:** Consumers cannot read event.currentTarget, stopPropagation, or detect modifier keys from onClick — common needs when a card contains nested controls (the Feed.tsx overlay-button pattern exists partly because of this). The closed tone union can't be extended for a brand 'blue/purple' without forking, and there's no asChild/style override for custom tones.
  - **Fix:** Type onClick as `(e: React.MouseEvent<HTMLElement>) => void` (and likewise the cell). Either widen tone with a `(string & {})` escape or expose tone color via a prop/token override so custom tones are possible without inline style hacks.
  - **Done when:** TS: `onClick={(e)=>e.currentTarget}` type-checks; a custom tone color can be set without casting; existing call sites still compile.
- [ ] **`CRD-010`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-API` — TKCard `interactive` prop computes press styling but does nothing for a11y; `padding` accepts unsafe arbitrary string
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/cards/primitives.tsx:32,37-38,18,53`
  - **Evidence:** const isInteractive = interactive ?? !!onClick; ... role={onClick ? "button" : undefined} tabIndex={onClick ? 0 : undefined} — role/tabIndex key off onClick, NOT isInteractive; and padding?: number | string is injected raw into style padding (line 53) with no constraint.
  - **Impact:** Setting `interactive` without onClick gives a card the tk-press shrink and pointer cursor but NO role/tabIndex/keyboard — it looks tappable but isn't operable or announced, a classic affordance lie. The bare string `padding` lets `"10px;color:red"`-style values flow into the style object — low risk under React's style serialization but it is an unvalidated styling surface and easy to misuse.
  - **Fix:** Either tie role/tabIndex/keyboard to the same isInteractive signal (and require onClick when interactive) or drop `interactive` in favor of onClick-driven semantics. Document padding as CSS length only; consider number-only or a small token set.
  - **Done when:** Render `<TKCard interactive>` (no onClick) and assert there is no false button affordance (either it gets full button semantics or no pointer/press styling); add a prop-types/test note for padding.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`CRD-DX-001`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** The single most common card use case — a whole card that is one accessible tap target — is NOT one line. In examples/trailhead/src/features/discover/Feed.tsx:69-98 the author abandoned TKCard's own onClick and hand-built a 14-line absolutely-positioned <button> overlay with a manual aria-label, because TKCard's onClick gives a role='button' div with no accessible name and no way to pass one (CRD-001). So the documented happy path is unusable for the most frequent real case and consumers re-roll a worse primitive.
  - **Proposal:** Make the interactive container a first-class, labelled primitive. Add `TKCardButton` (or accept `onClick` + `label` on TKCard) that renders a real semantic control wrapping arbitrary children as one tap target: `<TKCardButton label={`${exp.title}, ${price}`} onClick={onOpen}>…</TKCardButton>`. Internally it owns the role, accessible name (aria-label/aria-labelledby), keyboard, and press feedback so Feed.tsx collapses from ~14 lines of overlay-button scaffolding to one wrapper. Keep non-interactive TKCard as the pure surface.
- [ ] **`CRD-DX-002`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** There is no escape hatch to make a card BE another element. Feed.tsx (Feed.tsx:69) needs the card itself to be the clickable element but cannot, so it wraps an overlay button; a card that should navigate (an <a>) cannot become an anchor at all (only TKCardCell is polymorphic, TKCard is hard-coded to <div> in primitives.tsx:34). The kit already ships an asChild/Slot pattern in packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/commerce.tsx, so the precedent exists.
  - **Proposal:** Give TKCard the same `as`/`asChild` escape hatch already proven in commerce.tsx. `<TKCard as="a" href="/trip/1">` renders a real link card; `<TKCard asChild><a href=…>…</a></TKCard>` merges card styling onto the child. This removes the overlay-button anti-pattern and lets a card be a link/Next-Link/router control while keeping press + a11y wiring in the primitive.
- [ ] **`CRD-DX-003`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** The `outlined` prop exists specifically so every card shares one hairline (primitives.tsx:20-22 docstring), yet 4 of 5 Trailhead call sites bypass it and inline `style={{ border: "0.5px solid var(--tk-sep)" }}` — DateSlot.tsx:111, Checkout.tsx:293, TripDetail.tsx:103, plus repeated inline flex/grid layout in GuideDirectory.tsx:162 and Feed.tsx:76. The intended canonical default loses to copy-pasted inline borders because `outlined` isn't discoverable/sufficient and there is no layout default.
  - **Proposal:** (1) Add a `variant` prop ('elevated' | 'outlined' | 'plain') so the separator choice is a named token, not a boolean + inline fallback, and document that inline `border` is disallowed. (2) Add a `layout` convenience ('stack' | 'row' | 'media') that sets the flex/grid the call sites keep re-declaring, e.g. `<TKCard layout="row">` ≡ display:flex;align-items:center;gap:12. This deletes the most-copied inline style across Trailhead.
- [ ] **`CRD-DX-004`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** Accessibility is opt-OUT-able to zero: TKCard/TKCardCell with onClick on a div produce a role='button' with NO accessible name and no prop to add one (CRD-001/CRD-007), and TKCardChip can't receive an accessible name either (CRD-002). A developer literally cannot ship an accessible interactive card with the public API today, which is why Feed.tsx escaped the primitive entirely. a11y should be impossible to omit, not impossible to add.
  - **Proposal:** Make accessible name a required, type-enforced part of the interactive contract. When `onClick`/interactive is set, require one of `label` | `aria-label` | `aria-labelledby` (or derive from `title` on TKCardCell) via a discriminated union so omitting it is a TYPE error, and spread the resolved aria-* onto the DOM node. In dev, console.warn when an interactive card has no resolvable name. Net effect: consumers can't ship an unlabeled control.
- [ ] **`CRD-DX-005`** · types · effort S · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** Two type frictions bite consumers. (1) `onClick?: () => void` (primitives.tsx:17,70) drops the DOM event, so any handler needing currentTarget/stopPropagation/key info must cast or re-wrap (CRD-009). (2) `tone` is a closed union 'accent'|'green'|'red'|'orange'|'gray' (primitives.tsx:150) with no escape hatch, so a brand/custom tone forces an inline `style` override and loses the selected-ink pairing. Inference does not 'just work' for real handlers or custom palettes.
  - **Proposal:** Type `onClick?: (e: React.MouseEvent<HTMLElement>) => void` (and pass keyboard-synthesized events through the same path). For tone, keep the union as autocomplete-friendly named tokens but widen to `tone?: TKCardTone | (string & {})` and resolve unknown tones to a `{ bg, ink }` pair via CSS vars, so `tone="var(--brand)"` or a custom token keeps correct selected ink. Both are inference-friendly and remove casts/inline overrides.
- [ ] **`CRD-DX-006`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** There is no disabled/loading/readonly state on ANY card primitive (CRD-008). Consumers building a checkout/CTA card (e.g. Checkout.tsx) must manually gate onClick, dim via inline opacity, and remember to drop tabIndex/cursor — repeated, error-prone, and easy to get half-right (visually disabled but still focusable/clickable). The 'disabled card' common case has no supported shape.
  - **Proposal:** Add `disabled?: boolean` and `loading?: boolean` to TKCard(/Button), TKCardCell, and TKCardChip. `disabled` sets aria-disabled, suppresses onClick + press class, removes from tab order, and applies the kit's standard dim token. `loading` shows a spinner/inert state with aria-busy. One prop replaces the manual gate-opacity-tabindex dance at every CTA call site.
- [ ] **`CRD-DX-007`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** There is ZERO Storybook/MDX coverage for any card primitive — grep for TKCard across *.stories.tsx/*.mdx in packages/uikit/src returns nothing. With no canonical recipes, developers reverse-engineer usage from Trailhead, which is exactly how the inaccessible overlay-button pattern in Feed.tsx propagated as de-facto guidance. There is no copy-pasteable 'do this' for interactive cards, link cards, chip groups, or cell rows.
  - **Proposal:** Ship cards.stories.tsx + a short MDX with copy-paste recipes: (a) interactive card as one labelled tap target, (b) link card via `as="a"`, (c) TKCardCell row with before/after/chevron, (d) a selectable chip group with roving tabindex, (e) disabled/loading states. Each story doubles as a visual a11y check (axe). This makes the right pattern the discoverable one and retires the overlay hack.
- [ ] **`CRD-DX-008`** · ergonomics · effort L
  - **Pain:** Chips are almost never used singly — they appear as filter/segment groups (e.g. the discover filter state machine in Feed.tsx). But TKCardChip is a lone button: each consumer must wire selection state, single-vs-multi semantics, roving tabindex, and group ARIA by hand, and CRD-002/003/005 mean every hand-rolled group inherits the unlabeled, sub-44px, low-contrast chip. The composition the domain actually needs (a chip group) doesn't exist.
  - **Proposal:** Add a `TKCardChipGroup` controlled composition: `<TKCardChipGroup value={cat} onChange={setCat} aria-label="Category">{options.map(o => <TKCardChip value={o.id}>{o.label}</TKCardChip>)}</TKCardChipGroup>`. The group owns role='radiogroup'/'listbox' semantics, roving tabindex, arrow-key nav, and single/multi mode, while chips inherit a 44px-min target and tone-correct selected ink by default. Filter UIs become declarative and accessible for free.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

COVERAGE GAPS / ASSUMPTIONS: (1) There are ZERO tests and ZERO stories for this family — `find packages/uikit/src -iname '*card*' \\( -name '*.test.*' -o -name '*.stories.*' \\)` returns nothing. The barrel is one line (`export * from \"./cards/primitives\"`). So all a11y/keyboard/contrast behaviors above are entirely unverified by CI; this is itself the single biggest risk for the family and motivates CRD-001..009. (2) Contrast figures in CRD-005 (e.g. ~2.0:1 for white on #e89623) are computed by hand from the token hex values at tokens.css:120-122/145-147; treat the exact ratios as approximate but the AA failure direction is solid. (3) SSR/import safety (D-TG): primitives.tsx imports nothing from React runtime hooks and touches no window/WebApp APIs, so it is SSR-import-safe — no finding there, which is good. (4) D-SEC: no dangerouslySetInnerHTML, no image/file src handling in this family — children are opaque ReactNode, so no injection surface; no finding. (5) D-PERF: components are simple and PURE-annotated (`/* @__PURE__ */`) for tree-shaking; the only mild concern is inline style objects recreated each render defeating memoization, but with no internal state and tiny trees this is negligible — not raised as a finding. (6) The Feed.tsx workaround (examples/trailhead/src/features/discover/Feed.tsx:69-98) is real production evidence that CRD-001's missing prop pass-through forces consumers off the intended API; worth treating CRD-001 as the highest-leverage fix. (7) `interactive` prop (CRD-010) appears in the type but I found no example consumer using it standalone, so its mis-affordance is latent rather than currently shipping.

---

### Carousel — composites/carousel.tsx

`CRS` · `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx` · 12 findings (0🔴 5🟠 5🟡 2⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-GESTURE` Touch/gesture & scroll-snap correctness · `D-STATE` State model & controlled drift · `D-A11Y` Accessibility (carousel pattern, keyboard, live region) · `D-TG` Telegram runtime (viewport resize/expand, haptics) · `D-EDGE` Robustness on edge inputs · `D-THEME` Tokens & theming · `D-API` API ergonomics

#### Findings

- [x] **`CRS-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-GESTURE` — Snap target math ignores gap and edgeInset — multi-slide carousels drift off-center
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:46-47, 62-69`
  - **Evidence:** const width = el.clientWidth || 1; const next = Math.round(el.scrollLeft / width); ... el.scrollTo({ left: clamped * el.clientWidth, behavior: ... });
  - **Impact:** Slides are flex:0 0 100% with gap between them and padding:0 edgeInset on the track. The real per-slide scroll stride is clientWidth + gap, but both syncPage (scrollLeft/clientWidth) and scrollTo (clamped*clientWidth) use clientWidth only. With the default gap=10, by slide 4 the programmatic scrollLeft is ~30px short of the native snap point and the rounding in syncPage can resolve to the wrong index. A Mini App team with 3+ wide media slides sees the dot tap land between slides and the active dot lag the visible slide.
  - **Fix:** Measure the real stride from the slide elements (e.g. second slide offsetLeft - first slide offsetLeft, or clientWidth+gap) and use that single stride for both the scrollTo left and the syncPage round; or drop scrollLeft math entirely and call slideEl.scrollIntoView({inline:'center'}). Add a test with gap>0 and 4 slides asserting scrollTo left for the last index.
  - **Done when:** With gap=10 and 5 slides, tapping the last dot scrolls to the native scroll-snap position (slide fully centered) and onScroll reports index 4; a unit test asserts scrollTo left === 4*(clientWidth+gap).
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — carousel.tsx:69 uses `clamped * el.clientWidth` and :47 uses `scrollLeft / clientWidth`, but slides are flex:0 0 100% (content-box) with `gap` between them and `padding:0 edgeInset` on the track (:103-107), so the true snap stride is (clientWidth-2*edgeInset+gap), not clientWidth. The programmatic scrollTo overshoots the native snap point and drift accumulates with slide count.
- [x] **`CRS-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — No controlled `page` prop — gallery cannot be driven from app state
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:4-21, 29`
  - **Evidence:** export interface TKGalleryProps { children?; onPageChange?; dots?; gap?; edgeInset?; height?; ... } and const [page, setPage] = useState(0);
  - **Impact:** TKGallery exposes onPageChange but no page/defaultPage input (unlike sibling TKPageDots which uses useControllable). A consumer that wants to deep-link to slide 2, sync a carousel with an external stepper, or reset to slide 0 after data reload has no API to do it and must reach into the DOM. This is an inconsistency footgun versus the rest of the kit's controlled components.
  - **Fix:** Add optional `page?: number` (controlled) and `defaultPage?: number`, route through the existing useControllable helper, and run scrollTo in an effect when the controlled value changes. Keep onPageChange as the change callback.
  - **Done when:** Rendering <TKGallery page={2}/> scrolls to and highlights slide 3 on mount and whenever the prop changes; a test asserts the controlled prop wins over internal scroll until the user swipes.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — TKGalleryProps (carousel.tsx:4-21) exposes only onPageChange — no page/defaultPage input — and state is internal `useState(0)` (:29). Sibling TKPageDots (composites/navigation/page-dots.tsx:14-16) takes page/defaultPage via useControllable, confirming the inconsistency.
- [x] **`CRS-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-TG` — No viewport-resize re-alignment — Telegram expand/keyboard leaves the carousel mid-slide
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:43-74, 86-113`
  - **Evidence:** width derived from el.clientWidth at scroll time only; there is no ResizeObserver / visualViewport / resize listener anywhere in the file.
  - **Impact:** In a Mini App the WebView width changes on expand(), on the on-screen keyboard, and on rotation. Because the snap offset is width-relative and nothing re-runs scrollTo on resize, the track ends up parked between two slides after any resize, and `page` no longer matches what is visible. Sibling components (virtual-list, segmented) use ResizeObserver; this one does not.
  - **Fix:** Add a ResizeObserver (guarded by typeof ResizeObserver) on trackRef that re-scrolls to the current page (behavior:'auto') when clientWidth changes, debounced to the next frame.
  - **Done when:** Shrinking the container width (or firing a resize) re-centers the current slide; a test that changes clientWidth and dispatches resize asserts scrollLeft is snapped back to page*stride.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — Grep for ResizeObserver/visualViewport/resize in carousel.tsx returns nothing; width is read from el.clientWidth only at scroll/scrollTo time. Siblings segmented.tsx:39-40 and lists/virtual-list.tsx:64-65 use ResizeObserver, so on a Mini App viewport change nothing re-aligns the width-relative snap.
- [x] **`CRS-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-GESTURE` — Programmatic-scroll guard can latch, permanently suppressing onPageChange
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:48-52, 64-69`
  - **Evidence:** if (programmaticRef.current) { if (next === targetRef.current) programmaticRef.current = false; return; } ... programmaticRef.current = !reduce && clamped !== page; targetRef.current = clamped;
  - **Impact:** The guard only clears when syncPage observes next === targetRef.current. If the smooth scroll never produces a frame where round(scrollLeft/clientWidth) equals the target — which is exactly the off-by-gap case in CRS-001, or if a resize/unmount interrupts the animation, or the browser cancels smooth scroll — programmaticRef stays true forever and every subsequent user swipe is silently swallowed (no setPage, no onPageChange). The pointer/touch/wheel reset only fires for those input types, not for momentum scroll continuing after the listener cleared it.
  - **Fix:** Make the guard self-clearing: store a timeout/rAF that force-clears programmaticRef after a max scroll-settle window (e.g. 400ms) regardless of whether the exact target frame was seen, and clear it on unmount. Prefer a scrollend listener where supported.
  - **Done when:** After a dot tap whose animation is interrupted (resize or never hitting the exact frame), a subsequent manual scroll still emits onPageChange; a test that sets programmaticRef via a dot tap then fires a non-matching scroll asserts the next user scroll reports the page.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — programmaticRef (carousel.tsx:67) only clears when syncPage sees round(scrollLeft/clientWidth)===targetRef (:48-52); simulation shows with large gaps / many slides the native snap rounds to a different index than target, so the guard never clears and all later scrolls are swallowed. The endProgrammatic resets (:98-100) fire only on fresh pointer/touch/wheel, not on interrupted animation or continuing momentum, and there is no timeout fallback.
- [x] **`CRS-005`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Slides and container lack ARIA carousel semantics and a live region
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:88-127`
  - **Evidence:** <div ref={trackRef} role="group" aria-roledescription="carousel" tabIndex={0} ...> ... slides.map(... <div key={index} data-tk-gallery-slide style={...}>{slide}</div>)
  - **Impact:** The track is announced as 'carousel' but has no accessible name (no aria-label), and each slide div has no role="group"/aria-roledescription="slide"/aria-label="N of M". There is no aria-live region announcing the current slide on change. Screen-reader users in Telegram hear an unnamed carousel and get no feedback when paging, failing the kit's a11y gate intent and the WAI-ARIA carousel pattern.
  - **Fix:** Add aria-label (or accept an ariaLabel prop) on the track; wrap each slide with role="group", aria-roledescription="slide", aria-label=`${index+1} of ${count}` (localized via the existing i18n locale); add a visually-hidden aria-live="polite" node updated to 'Slide N of M' on page change.
  - **Done when:** axe/Storybook a11y run reports the carousel has a name and each slide is a labelled group; changing slides updates a polite live region (verifiable via getByRole('group',{name:/2 of 3/})).
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — Track (carousel.tsx:88-92) has role=group + aria-roledescription=carousel but no aria-label/accessible name; each slide div (:114-126) carries only data-tk-gallery-slide with no role/aria-roledescription=slide/aria-label='N of M', and there is no aria-live region announcing the active slide on change.
- [x] **`CRS-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — Zero haptic feedback on swipe/dot-tap, inconsistent with sibling composites
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:43-74`
  - **Evidence:** No haptic call anywhere in the file; by contrast segmented.tsx, tabbar.tsx, swipe-cell.tsx, pin.tsx and pull-to-refresh.tsx all import and fire Telegram haptics.
  - **Impact:** In a native Telegram Mini App, paging a gallery (the canonical product-media use case in trailhead/surface-composer) feels dead compared to the rest of the kit which fires selectionChanged on discrete index changes. Inconsistent tactile language across the design system.
  - **Fix:** Fire the kit's selection-haptic when page settles to a new index (in the syncPage commit and in scrollTo), guarded by the runtime-absent fallback the other composites already use; consider a `haptics?: boolean` opt-out.
  - **Done when:** Paging the gallery on a device/WebApp stub calls the haptic selectionChanged exactly once per landed slide; a test spying the haptic util asserts one call per page change and zero when the runtime is absent.
- [x] **`CRS-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — Empty/zero-slide carousel produces negative clamp and NaN-prone scroll math
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:28, 62-69, 128`
  - **Evidence:** const slides = Children.toArray(children); ... const clamped = Math.min(Math.max(index, 0), slides.length - 1); ... el.scrollTo({ left: clamped * el.clientWidth, ... });
  - **Impact:** With no children, slides.length-1 = -1, so clamped becomes -1 and scrollTo is called with left: -clientWidth on any arrow keypress; with exactly one slide the dots are hidden (length>1 gate) but Arrow keys still call scrollTo with a 0/0 no-op that needlessly toggles the guard. A data-driven Mini App that renders an empty gallery during loading hits negative-offset scrolls instead of a clean empty state.
  - **Fix:** Early-return a stable empty/placeholder render when slides.length === 0, and guard scrollTo/onKeyDown to no-op when slides.length <= 1 (clamp lower bound to 0 when the list is empty).
  - **Done when:** Rendering <TKGallery> with no children shows no track errors and arrow keys are inert; a test with zero and one child asserts scrollTo is never called with a negative left.
- [x] **`CRS-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — Slides keyed by array index — reorder/removal remounts slide content
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:114-116`
  - **Evidence:** {slides.map((slide, index) => ( <div key={index} data-tk-gallery-slide ...>
  - **Impact:** Keying the wrapper by index means inserting/removing/reordering gallery items (e.g. paginated media, removing a sold-out product slide) remounts every slide after the change point, losing per-slide DOM state (video playback position, image decode, focus) and can desync the page index against content. Standard React list footgun in a component explicitly sold for 'product media' galleries.
  - **Fix:** Derive a stable key from the child element's own key when present (Children.toArray already prefixes child keys) and fall back to index only for keyless children; document that consumers should pass keyed children.
  - **Done when:** Removing the first of three slides keeps slides 2 and 3 mounted (no remount); a test tracking mount/unmount counts asserts only the removed slide unmounts.
- [ ] **`CRS-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — Keyboard support limited to Arrow keys — no Home/End, no escape from the scroll trap
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:76-84`
  - **Evidence:** if (e.key === "ArrowLeft") {...} else if (e.key === "ArrowRight") {...}
  - **Impact:** The carousel pattern expects Home/End to jump to first/last slide; their absence means keyboard users tab-stop on a focusable scroll region with only single-step paging. Combined with the missing container label (CRS-005), keyboard users get a degraded experience versus the dots (which do implement roving Home/End-style navigation via tkRovingNext).
  - **Fix:** Handle Home/End (and optionally PageUp/PageDown) in onKeyDown to scrollTo(0)/scrollTo(last); reuse the same roving helper semantics the dots already use for consistency.
  - **Done when:** Focusing the track and pressing End scrolls to the last slide and Home to the first; a test fires keydown Home/End and asserts the resulting page.
- [ ] **`CRS-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-THEME` — Hardcoded raw-px spacing instead of semantic tokens
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:27, 87, 103-109`
  - **Evidence:** gap = 10, edgeInset = 16 defaults; outer wrapper style={{ ... gap: 10, ...style }}; track padding: `0 ${edgeInset}px`, scrollPaddingInline: edgeInset
  - **Impact:** The outer dots-to-track gap is a literal 10 (not even the gap prop), and the default gap/edgeInset are raw pixels rather than the kit's --tk spacing scale used everywhere else. Density/spacing theming and RTL logical-property consistency don't reach the carousel, so it visually drifts from token-driven siblings and ignores any global spacing scale a consumer sets.
  - **Fix:** Reference the spacing/radius tokens (e.g. var(--tk-gap-*) / var(--tk-space-*)) for the wrapper gap and the default edge inset, and use logical padding (padding-inline) consistently; keep numeric props as overrides.
  - **Done when:** With a custom token spacing scale applied, the carousel's inter-element gaps scale accordingly; visual snapshot shows token-driven spacing, and no literal 10/16 remains as the sole source of spacing.
- [x] **`CRS-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-API` — No ref forwarding or imperative paging handle
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:27, 86-87`
  - **Evidence:** export function TKGallery({...}) { ... return ( <div data-testid={testId} style={...}> — no forwardRef, no useImperativeHandle.
  - **Impact:** Consumers cannot get a ref to the carousel root for measurement/scroll-into-view, nor an imperative next()/goTo() handle. Combined with the missing controlled prop (CRS-002), an app that wants 'auto-advance every 5s' or 'scroll to slide on external event' has no supported path and must query the DOM by data-testid.
  - **Fix:** forwardRef the root and optionally expose a small imperative handle ({ goTo(index), next(), prev() }) via useImperativeHandle, or rely on the controlled prop from CRS-002.
  - **Done when:** A ref passed to TKGallery resolves to the root element and (if implemented) ref.current.goTo(2) pages to slide 3; covered by a test.
- [ ] **`CRS-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-GESTURE` — edgeInset comment claims Telegram edge-swipe protection but center-snap defeats it
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx:12-16, 107-120`
  - **Evidence:** /** Keeps a side inset so horizontal swipes start away from the screen edges (iOS Telegram reserves edge swipes for its own navigation) */ ... padding: `0 ${edgeInset}px` ... scrollSnapAlign: "center"
  - **Impact:** Slides are flex:0 0 100% and snap-aligned center, so each slide fills the full track width minus padding; the 16px edgeInset is the only buffer and it is also where the previous/next slide peeks. On a narrow iOS Telegram WebView a swipe that begins within ~16px of the screen edge can still be claimed by Telegram's own back/minimize gesture, so the documented protection is thin and unconfigurable per-platform. This is an assumption worth validating on-device.
  - **Fix:** Document the residual risk, consider a larger default edgeInset on iOS, and ensure the swipe start region is inside the inset; optionally expose a guard that disables Telegram vertical-swipe-to-minimize while a horizontal drag is in progress (as pull-to-refresh does).
  - **Done when:** On an iOS Telegram device, horizontal swipes started near the edge page the carousel rather than triggering Telegram navigation; documented as a manual on-device check.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`CRS-DX-001`** · api · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** carousel.tsx:7,27 — TKGallery only exposes `onPageChange` and an internal `useState(0)`; there is no `page`/`defaultPage` prop. Every sibling (tabbar.tsx:16-25, segmented.tsx, page-dots.tsx:8-16) standardizes on `value`/`defaultValue`/`onChange` via `useControllable`. A developer who already knows the pattern from TKTabbar guesses `value`/`onChange`, gets nothing, and cannot drive the gallery from app state (deep-link to slide 3, reset to 0 on filter change).
  - **Proposal:** Adopt the kit-wide controllable contract instead of inventing a one-off. Rename to `page?: number` (controlled), add `defaultPage = 0` (uncontrolled initial), keep `onChange?: (page: number) => void`, and back it with the existing `useControllable(page, defaultPage, onChange)` helper — deleting the bespoke `useState`+programmatic-guard duplication. `onPageChange` becomes a deprecated alias forwarded to `onChange` for one minor. Result: `<TKGallery page={i} onChange={setI}>` works identically to every other composite, and the red-team's CRS-002 'controlled page' gap closes as a side-effect of API consistency rather than a custom prop.
- [ ] **`CRS-DX-002`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** carousel.tsx:88-126 — the track is `role="group" aria-roledescription="carousel"` with no `aria-label`, and each slide is a bare `<div data-tk-gallery-slide>` with no `role`, no `aria-roledescription="slide"`, no `aria-label="3 of 5"`, and no live region. A consumer literally cannot make this accessible from the outside — there is no prop to pass a label, so they ship an unlabeled carousel by default. TKPageDots (page-dots.tsx) is already fully a11y-correct, which makes the silent track regression worse by contrast.
  - **Proposal:** Make a11y the default, not an opt-in the consumer can't reach. Add `label?: string` (applied as `aria-label` on the track, defaulting to a localized 'Gallery' via the existing `useTKLocale`). Inside, wrap each slide with `role="group" aria-roledescription="slide" aria-label={tkFormat(locale.slideXofY, {x:i+1, y:n})}`, mark off-screen slides `aria-hidden`/`inert`, and add a visually-hidden `aria-live="polite"` region that announces the current slide on settle. This bakes in CRS-005's semantics so consumers physically cannot ship the inaccessible variant, reusing the same i18n+format primitives TKPageDots already imports.
- [ ] **`CRS-DX-003`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** carousel.tsx:27,114-122 — slides are hardcoded `flex: "0 0 100%"`, so the only achievable layout is one full-width slide. The single most-requested mini-app gallery pattern — a peeking next card to signal 'swipeable' — is impossible without forking the component. The `edgeInset` prop even hints at peeking intent, but the 100% basis defeats it (cf. CRS-012). A developer building a horizontally-scrolling card rail today copy-pastes their own scroll-snap div instead of using TKGallery.
  - **Proposal:** Add `slideWidth?: number | string` (default `"100%"`, accepts e.g. `"85%"` or px) driving the slide `flex-basis`, plus optional `peek?: number` sugar that computes basis as `calc(100% - {peek}px)` so `<TKGallery peek={32}>` yields the canonical edge-peek in one line. Snap math (the CRS-001 fix) must read the real per-slide stride rather than `clientWidth`, so this and the snap fix land together. Keeps the zero-config full-bleed default while unlocking the rail/peek case that currently forces a fork.
- [x] **`CRS-DX-004`** · ergonomics · effort S
  - **Pain:** carousel.tsx:1,27 — there is no haptic feedback anywhere, while every tactile sibling fires it: tabbar.tsx:26/37 (`haptics.selection()`), segmented.tsx, pin.tsx, pull-to-refresh.tsx all call `useOptionalHaptics()`. A swipe between slides or a dot tap feels dead compared to the rest of the kit, and a developer has no hook to add it — they'd have to reimplement settle detection. This is the inconsistency CRS-006 flags, but the DX angle is that it should be automatic, not a prop.
  - **Proposal:** Import `useOptionalHaptics` from `../../foundation/telegram` and fire `haptics.selection()` exactly on committed page change (in the `useControllable` setter from CRS-DX-001), covering swipe-settle, dot tap, and keyboard nav through one code path — never on intermediate frames. Expose `haptics?: boolean` (default `true`) for the rare opt-out. This matches the SSR-safe optional pattern siblings already use, so it no-ops outside Telegram with zero consumer wiring.
- [ ] **`CRS-DX-005`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** carousel.tsx:27,86 — TKGallery is a plain function component with no `ref` forwarding and no imperative handle. TKCell (cell.tsx:192) and TKVirtualList (virtual-list.tsx:131) both `forwardRef`, so the kit's own convention is broken here. A developer who wants 'jump to next slide from an external Next button' or 'scroll to the slide containing an error' has no escape hatch — they must lift the entire scroll-snap implementation out.
  - **Proposal:** `forwardRef` the component (matching TKCell/TKVirtualList) and expose a typed imperative handle via `useImperativeHandle`: `{ next(): void; prev(): void; scrollTo(page: number): void; getPage(): number }`. This is the kit-idiomatic complement to the controlled `page` prop (CRS-DX-001) for fire-and-forget paging from sibling UI, and resolves CRS-011 using the existing forwardRef pattern rather than a new convention.
- [x] **`CRS-DX-006`** · types · effort L
  - **Pain:** carousel.tsx:5,28,114 — slides come only from `children` via `Children.toArray`, keyed by array index (CRS-008's remount bug). For data-driven galleries (the Trailhead case, ExperienceDetail.tsx:86-101, maps `e.gallery` to JSX inline) the developer must hand-write the `.map`, the `key`, and the wrapper, and there is no place to supply a stable key. There is precedent for a render-prop+items API in the kit: TKVirtualList (virtual-list.tsx:21,39) takes `items` + `renderItem` and infers the item type.
  - **Proposal:** Add an optional generic data API alongside children: `items?: T[]`, `renderItem?: (item: T, index: number) => ReactNode`, `getKey?: (item: T, index: number) => React.Key`. With `forwardRef` generics (as TKVirtualList does) `T` is inferred from `items`, so `renderItem` is fully typed with zero annotations. When `getKey` is supplied, slides key by stable id instead of index — fixing CRS-008's reorder remount for the data-driven path — while plain `children` stays supported for static galleries. Lets the Trailhead gallery collapse from a manual map to `<TKGallery items={e.gallery} renderItem={...} getKey={g=>g.id} />`.
- [ ] **`CRS-DX-007`** · defaults · effort S
  - **Pain:** carousel.tsx:27,87,103,107 — spacing is raw px scattered and even duplicated: prop defaults `gap = 10`, `edgeInset = 16`, AND a second hardcoded `gap: 10` on the outer column at line 87. A developer aligning the gallery to the surrounding 8pt grid has to discover three different numbers, and the column gap isn't even overridable. This is CRS-010's token gap, but the DX cost is the magic-number sprawl and the un-themeable inner gap.
  - **Proposal:** Replace literals with the kit's semantic CSS variables (the same `var(--tk-r-lg)`, `var(--tk-t2)` family already used in page-dots.tsx:50 and consumed in ExperienceDetail.tsx:92) — e.g. `gap` defaults to `var(--tk-gap)` (add the token if missing), `edgeInset` to a `--tk-gutter`-style token, and the column-to-dots spacing to `var(--tk-gap-2)` instead of the hardcoded `gap: 10`. Props still accept overrides but the defaults inherit the theme, so a re-themed app gets a consistent gallery for free. Pure default/token swap, no signature change.
- [ ] **`CRS-DX-008`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** No file under packages/uikit/src references TKGallery except carousel.tsx itself — grep for TKGallery across the package finds zero stories, zero MDX, zero index re-export check. The only working example lives in the Trailhead app (ExperienceDetail.tsx:86), so a developer has no copy-pasteable reference for the controlled, peek, items, or a11y-label usages, and no visual harness to catch the CRS-003 resize / CRS-007 empty-state regressions.
  - **Proposal:** Add a `carousel` Storybook/MDX entry colocated with the component covering the load-bearing recipes as copy-paste snippets: (1) zero-config `<TKGallery>{slides}</TKGallery>`, (2) controlled `page`/`onChange` driven from app state, (3) `peek`/`slideWidth` rail, (4) data-driven `items`/`renderItem`/`getKey`, (5) labeled + live-region a11y, and explicit empty (0/1-slide) and resize stories so the CRS-003/CRS-007 fixes are visible and regression-guarded. Document `edgeInset`'s real Telegram edge-swipe rationale (clearing up CRS-012) right in the story.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

Scope: read packages/uikit/src/composites/carousel.tsx (the only file in the family; there is no carousel/ subfolder, no carousel.css, no dedicated *.stories beyond storybook/composites/carousel.stories.tsx and one test test/carousel-reorg.test.tsx). Also read the hard dependency TKPageDots (composites/navigation/page-dots.tsx) and confirmed consumer usage in examples/trailhead ExperienceDetail.tsx (testId 'detail-gallery', uncontrolled) and the surface-composer story. COVERAGE GAPS in existing tests: carousel-reorg.test.tsx only checks (a) barrel re-export identity and (b) a single happy-path scroll + dot click with gap effectively ignored (it stubs clientWidth=120, scrollLeft=120). There are NO tests for: gap>0 stride math (CRS-001), controlled paging (CRS-002, prop does not exist), resize (CRS-003), the latching guard (CRS-004), ARIA/live-region (CRS-005), haptics (CRS-006), 0/1-slide edges (CRS-007), key stability (CRS-008), Home/End keys (CRS-009). ASSUMPTIONS: CRS-001/CRS-004 interaction is reasoned from the code (clientWidth-only stride vs native gap-aware snap) — exact pixel drift depends on the browser's snap rounding and was not measured on-device; CRS-012 (iOS edge-swipe claim) is a documentation-vs-behavior concern that requires a real iOS Telegram client to confirm. TKPageDots itself is solid (uses useControllable, roving tabindex, i18n labels, aria-current) — its only relevance here is that TKGallery does NOT reuse that controllability pattern for its own page state (CRS-002). The dot button hit target is 8px tall (page-dots.tsx line 43-45) which is well under the 44px touch-target guideline, but that belongs to the navigation/page-dots family audit, not carousel, so it is noted here rather than filed as a carousel finding.

### Feedback — AsyncBoundary, Bars, EmptyState, Progress, Skeletons, Timeline

`FBK` · `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback` · 12 findings (0🔴 3🟠 7🟡 2⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility · `D-STATE` State & edge values · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView / i18n · `D-API` API ergonomics · `D-PERF` Performance / robustness of inputs · `D-THEME` Tokens & theming · `D-TG` Telegram runtime / reduced-motion

#### Findings

- [x] **`FBK-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — AsyncBoundary loading state is silent to screen readers — no role/status/aria-busy
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback/async-boundary.tsx:49`
  - **Evidence:** if (status === "loading") return <>{loader ?? <TKSkeletonList rows={4} testId={testId} />}</>;
  - **Impact:** Trailhead/Surface-Composer wrap whole screens (GuideDirectory, GuideThread, SessionDetail) in AsyncBoundary. While loading, the rendered TKSkeletonList is a plain styled <div> with no role, aria-busy or live region, so a screen-reader user hears nothing — the screen appears empty/finished. When data arrives the swap is also unannounced. This is the single most common loading wrapper in the kit and it fails the most basic a11y expectation for async UI.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the loading branch in a container with role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-busy="true" and a visually-hidden 'Loading…' label (localized via useTKLocale). Set aria-busy back to false / announce completion when children render. Keep skeletons themselves aria-hidden so only the status text is announced.
  - **Done when:** Render <AsyncBoundary loading> and assert getByRole("status") exists with aria-busy="true"; toggle loading=false and assert the busy region is gone and children are exposed. Add a Playwright a11y snapshot showing the polite announcement.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — async-boundary.tsx:49 renders the loading branch as a bare fragment wrapping TKSkeletonList, and skeletons.tsx:42-75 (TKSkeletonList) is a plain styled <div> with no role/aria-busy/live region; confirmed usage wrapping whole screens in GuideThread/GuideDirectory/SessionDetail, so screen readers get no loading announcement.
- [x] **`FBK-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — TKProgress reports unclamped aria-valuenow while clamping the visual
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback/progress.tsx:27-35`
  - **Evidence:** aria-valuenow={value} ... width: `${Math.min(100, Math.max(0, value))}%`
  - **Impact:** The bar width is clamped to 0–100 but aria-valuenow passes the raw value. A consumer passing value={140} (e.g. upload bytes/total miscomputed) shows a full bar but a screen reader announces '140 percent'; value={-10} or NaN announces '-10'/'NaN' against aria-valuemax=100, which is an invalid ARIA state. TKRing on the same screen clamps correctly (percent), so the two progress components disagree, surprising consumers.
  - **Fix:** Clamp once: const v = Number.isFinite(value) ? Math.min(100, Math.max(0, value)) : 0; use v for both width and aria-valuenow. Optionally add aria-valuetext for a localized '72%'.
  - **Done when:** Render <TKProgress value={140}/> and assert aria-valuenow="100"; <TKProgress value={-10}/> → "0"; <TKProgress value={NaN}/> → "0" and width 0%. Mirror TKRing's clamp behavior in a shared test.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — progress.tsx:27 passes raw aria-valuenow={value} while line 35 clamps only the visual width via Math.min(100,Math.max(0,value)); TKRing (line 71) clamps aria-valuenow to percent, so the two components disagree as claimed.
- [x] **`FBK-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Non-interactive TKBars are aria-hidden — chart data is invisible to assistive tech
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback/bars.tsx:58-76`
  - **Evidence:** <div aria-hidden="true" onMouseEnter={...} style={barStyle} /> ... {labels?.[i] != null ? <span ...>{labels[i]}</span> : null}
  - **Impact:** When onBarClick is omitted (the common read-only chart case, e.g. Train weekly stats), every bar is aria-hidden="true" and the value is conveyed only by pixel height — there is no text, title, aria-label or table semantics for the magnitude. A screen-reader user gets, at best, a bare day label ('Mon') with no number. The chart is effectively content-free for non-sighted users.
  - **Fix:** Give the chart container role="img" with an aria-label summarizing the series (or role="list" with each bar exposing an accessible name like `${label}: ${value}`). Stop relying on aria-hidden for non-interactive bars; render an SR-only value per bar.
  - **Done when:** Render <TKBars data={[3,7]} labels={['Mon','Tue']}/> (no onBarClick) and assert an accessible name exposing both values via getByRole("img"/"list")/byText; axe/Playwright a11y scan reports the chart values are reachable.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — bars.tsx:59-64 marks the non-interactive bar aria-hidden="true", and the label span at lines 66-76 only emits the text label (e.g. 'Mon'), never the numeric value b; no title/aria-label/table semantics convey magnitude, so read-only charts are content-free for assistive tech.
- [x] **`FBK-004`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — Interactive bars hardcode aria-pressed={false} — toggle-button semantics that never update
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback/bars.tsx:45`
  - **Evidence:** aria-pressed={false}
  - **Impact:** Every clickable bar is announced by screen readers as a toggle button in the 'not pressed' state, permanently. onBarClick is a one-shot select callback, not a toggle, and the component never knows which bar is selected — so the state is both wrong and frozen. A consumer building a 'select a day' bar picker gets misleading 'not pressed' announcements with no way to mark the active bar.
  - **Fix:** Remove aria-pressed entirely (it's a plain action button), or add an optional `selectedIndex` prop and set aria-pressed={i === selectedIndex} so the semantics are real and controllable.
  - **Done when:** Without a selection model, assert the bar button has no aria-pressed attribute. If a selectedIndex prop is added, assert aria-pressed flips to "true" only for the selected bar.
- [x] **`FBK-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — Async copy defaults are hardcoded English while Progress/Ring localize via useTKLocale
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback/async-boundary.tsx:39-43`
  - **Evidence:** errorTitle = "Something went wrong", errorText = "Please try again.", retryLabel = "Retry", emptyIcon = "search", emptyTitle = "Nothing here yet"
  - **Impact:** Inconsistent localization story across the family: TKProgress/TKRing read aria-label from useTKLocale().progress, but AsyncBoundary/TKAsyncState bake English strings. A Mini App shipping in Russian/Arabic that forgets to pass every *Title/*Text/*Label prop silently renders English error/empty UI — and there are five separate props to remember per call site, a prop-explosion footgun. The kit already proves locale strings exist (locale.progress), so the inconsistency is gratuitous.
  - **Fix:** Pull default error/empty/retry copy from useTKLocale (add asyncErrorTitle/asyncRetry/asyncEmpty keys to the locale, EN+RU already present pattern) so untranslated call sites inherit the app locale; keep prop overrides for custom copy.
  - **Done when:** Render <AsyncBoundary error/> under a Russian TKLocaleProvider with no errorTitle prop and assert the localized Russian default appears, not 'Something went wrong'.
- [x] **`FBK-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-PERF` — TKBars uses Math.max(...data) — stack overflow / O(n) recompute on large series
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback/bars.tsx:15`
  - **Evidence:** const max = Math.max(...data, 1);
  - **Impact:** Spreading the array as function arguments throws RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded once data length crosses the engine arg limit (~100k+ on some engines, far lower under memory pressure in a low-end Android Telegram WebView). Even below that, every parent re-render recomputes max and the whole bar list re-renders, and each hover (setHover) re-renders all bars. A consumer charting a long history series gets a hard crash or jank.
  - **Fix:** Compute max with a reduce loop (data.reduce((m,v)=>v>m?v:m,1)) guarding non-finite values; memoize max with useMemo over data; consider a CSS-variable hover or per-bar memo so hovering one bar doesn't re-render all.
  - **Done when:** Render <TKBars data={Array(200000).fill(1)}/> without throwing; profile shows max computed once per data change and hovering a single bar does not re-render siblings.
- [ ] **`FBK-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-RESP` — Bars width:100% + flex:1 with no min-width or truncation overflows in narrow WebView
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback/bars.tsx:17-20`
  - **Evidence:** style={{ display: "flex", alignItems: "flex-end", gap: 8, height }} ... const label = labels?.[i] ?? `Bar ${i + 1}`
  - **Impact:** With many bars (e.g. 14-day series) at gap:8 and no horizontal scroll/min-width, bars are squeezed to sub-pixel widths in a 360px Telegram viewport. The caption labels under each bar have no maxWidth, no white-space:nowrap+ellipsis and no overflow handling, so a translated label ('Понедельник' vs 'Mon') wraps or pushes siblings, breaking the baseline alignment. No RTL handling for the label row either.
  - **Fix:** Add a maxBars/scroll affordance or min bar width; constrain labels with overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis; white-space:nowrap and a sensible min-width; verify RTL by using logical properties.
  - **Done when:** Visual/Playwright snapshot at 360px with 14 long-text labels shows no overflow past the container and labels truncate; add an RTL story that renders cleanly.
- [x] **`FBK-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-THEME` — Timeline node hardcodes color:'#fff' — invisible check / poor contrast in light themes
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback/timeline.tsx:41`
  - **Evidence:** color: "#fff",
  - **Impact:** The step node forces white foreground regardless of theme/accent. The 'done' node uses var(--tk-green) and 'active' uses var(--tk-accent); a Mini App whose Telegram theme yields a light green/accent (some custom themes do) gets a white check on a light circle — low contrast or invisible. It also bypasses the token system the rest of the kit relies on for dark/light correctness, so it can't be re-themed by consumers.
  - **Fix:** Replace #fff with an on-accent/on-success token (e.g. var(--tk-on-accent), var(--tk-on-green)) or var(--tk-surface) where appropriate, so contrast tracks the theme.
  - **Done when:** Render TKTimeline under a light-accent theme and assert (visual snapshot / contrast check) the check icon meets >=3:1 against the node fill; the color resolves from a token, not a literal.
- [x] **`FBK-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — EmptyState/AsyncState CTA is size='sm' (34px tall) — below 44px Telegram touch target
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback/empty-state.tsx:57`
  - **Evidence:** <TKButton variant={tone === "red" ? "destructive" : "tonal"} pill size="sm" onClick={onCta}>
  - **Impact:** BTN_SIZES.sm.h = 34 (packages/uikit/src/atoms/buttons/shared.ts:8). The empty/error CTA is the primary recovery action — 'Retry' on a failed load, 'Reset' on no results — and AsyncBoundary funnels every screen-level error through it. A 34px-tall pill in a thumb-driven Telegram WebView is under the 44px minimum and easy to miss-tap, exactly when the user is already frustrated by an error.
  - **Fix:** Use size="md" (h=44) for the empty/error CTA, or expose a ctaSize prop defaulting to md; ensure error retries in particular meet 44px.
  - **Done when:** Render TKEmptyState with a cta and assert the button's computed height >= 44px (or md size); update visual snapshots.
- [x] **`FBK-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — EmptyState title is a styled div, not a heading — no landmark/heading for error & empty screens
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback/empty-state.tsx:51`
  - **Evidence:** <div style={{ fontSize: "var(--tk-fz-title3)", fontWeight: 700 }}>{title}</div>
  - **Impact:** Empty/error states are whole-screen messages ('No orders', 'Something went wrong'), but the title renders as a bare div with no role="heading"/aria-level and no aria-labelledby on the container. Screen-reader users navigating by headings (a primary nav mode) skip right past the only thing on screen, and the error has no programmatic name. Same applies to every error/empty branch of AsyncBoundary since it composes TKEmptyState.
  - **Fix:** Render the title as a real heading (configurable element/level, e.g. an `as`/`headingLevel` prop defaulting to role="heading" aria-level={2}) and/or label the container via aria-labelledby pointing at the title id.
  - **Done when:** Render TKEmptyState title="No orders" and assert getByRole("heading", { name: "No orders" }) resolves; AsyncBoundary error/empty branches expose a heading.
- [x] **`FBK-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-API` — Feedback components drop className/style/ref — no escape hatch for layout integration
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/feedback/empty-state.tsx:19-33`
  - **Evidence:** export function TKEmptyState({ icon = "cart", media, title, text, cta, onCta, tone = "accent", testId }) { ... <div data-testid={testId} style={{ ...fixed... }}>
  - **Impact:** TKEmptyState, TKBars, TKTimeline, TKSkeletonList accept neither className, style, nor a forwarded ref (only TKProgress/TKSkeleton/Table/Text take style). Consumers cannot add a margin, grid placement, scroll container, or attach an IntersectionObserver/measure ref to the root without an extra wrapper div — a recurring papercut across the whole family and inconsistent with the components that do accept style.
  - **Fix:** Standardize: forwardRef + accept className and style (merged after internal styles) on every public feedback root. Document the precedence (consumer style wins).
  - **Done when:** Render each component with className="x" style={{margin:8}} ref={r} and assert the root carries the class, merged style, and r.current is the root element.
- [ ] **`FBK-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-TG` — Skeleton shimmer / timeline pulse only stop under '.tk'-scoped reduced-motion, not in isolation
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/tokens.css:331-342`
  - **Evidence:** @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { .tk { ... } .tk .tk-skel::after { animation: none; } .tk .tk-pulse::after { animation: none; opacity: 0; } }
  - **Impact:** The shimmer (.tk-skel::after) and active-step pulse (.tk-pulse) animate via infinite keyframes. The reduced-motion override is scoped to descendants of an element with class `tk`. A Mini App that mounts a TKSkeletonList/TKTimeline outside a `.tk` root (a common partial adoption pattern, or in a portal/teleport that loses the class) keeps full motion even when the user has 'reduce motion' enabled — a vestibular-accessibility and battery concern in a long-lived WebView. The component JS itself never reads prefers-reduced-motion.
  - **Fix:** Either guarantee/document that consumers must wrap in the .tk root, or make the reduced-motion rules unscoped (target .tk-skel::after/.tk-pulse::after directly), or gate the animations in JS via a matchMedia('prefers-reduced-motion') check so behavior doesn't depend on ancestor class.
  - **Done when:** With prefers-reduced-motion forced on, mount a TKSkeleton/TKTimeline NOT wrapped in .tk and assert no running shimmer/pulse animation (computed animation-name none or JS guard).

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`FBK-DX-01`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** AsyncBoundary's three states are decoupled in a way that forces every screen to re-derive readiness twice. In examples/trailhead/src/features/guide/GuideDirectory.tsx the consumer passes loading/error/onRetry to <AsyncBoundary>, then STILL hand-writes `{people ? (<>...</>) : null}` inside children to guard against the data being null. async-boundary.tsx exposes `empty?: boolean` but no data channel, so the null-check leaks into every call site and the empty branch is rarely wired. The common 'fetch → render or skeleton/error/empty' case is ~8 lines + a manual ternary instead of one.
  - **Proposal:** Add a data-driven overload that infers readiness and narrows the child type. New signature: `AsyncBoundary<T>({ data, loading, error, isEmpty?, children }: { data: T | undefined | null; loading?: boolean; error?: unknown; isEmpty?: (data: T) => boolean; children: (data: NonNullable<T>) => ReactNode } & AsyncCopy)`. Internally: error→error state, loading||data==null→loading state, isEmpty?.(data)→empty state, else `children(data)`. Call site collapses to `<AsyncBoundary data={people} loading={loading} error={error} onRetry={reload} isEmpty={(p)=>p.length===0}>{(people)=> <List items={people}/>}</AsyncBoundary>` — no null-guard, T inferred as the array element type inside the render prop. Keep the existing ReactNode-children form as a non-breaking overload.
- [ ] **`FBK-DX-02`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** AsyncBoundary makes every consumer hand-thread error/empty copy on EVERY screen. GuideDirectory.tsx passes errorTitle/errorText/retryLabel, ExperienceDetail/Feed/TripsList each re-pass the same t('discover.error.*') keys. There's no app-level place to set 'this is what our error/empty states say' — the localized strings (FBK-005 hardcodes EN) and the per-screen copy both have to be repeated. This is the single biggest source of boilerplate in the family.
  - **Proposal:** Introduce `<TKAsyncDefaultsProvider value={{ errorTitle, errorText, retryLabel, loader, emptyIcon, ... }}>` (React context) that AsyncBoundary/TKAsyncState read and merge under explicit props (prop > provider > localized default). Wire the localized defaults from FBK-005's fix here too. Then the common screen is just `<AsyncBoundary data={x} loading error onRetry={reload}>{...}</AsyncBoundary>` and the app sets copy once at the root. Pairs with extending TKLocale with `error`/`retry`/`empty` keys so even provider-less apps localize.
- [ ] **`FBK-DX-03`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKBars takes parallel arrays (`data: number[]` + `labels?: string[]`) that must be kept index-aligned by hand (bars.tsx:5-11). There's no way to attach a click payload, an aria description, or a per-bar color/state — onBarClick gives back only an index, so consumers maintain a side lookup `data[i]`/`labels[i]`. With FBK-003/004/006/007 piling a11y, perf and overflow issues onto this shape, the array-pair API is the root that makes all of them awkward to fix cleanly.
  - **Proposal:** Accept a single typed series: `data: ReadonlyArray<number | { value: number; label?: string; valueLabel?: string; color?: string; active?: boolean }>` and an `onBarClick?: (bar: Bar, index: number) => void` that hands back the full datum, not just the index. Normalize numbers→objects internally. This makes the toggle semantics (FBK-004) expressible via `active`, gives each bar a proper accessible value string for FBK-003, and lets a `max?: number` prop bypass the Math.max recompute (FBK-006). Keep `number[]` accepted for back-compat; only the callback's extra arg is additive.
- [ ] **`FBK-DX-04`** · composition · effort L
  - **Pain:** Feedback components drop className/style/ref (FBK-011) AND offer no slot to swap the rendered element. TKEmptyState always renders the title as a styled <div> (empty-state.tsx:51, also FBK-010) and the CTA always as a size='sm' TKButton (empty-state.tsx:57, also FBK-009) — consumers who need a heading, a link-styled CTA, or a TKMainButton have to abandon the component and rebuild it. The kit already standardizes ref-forwarding (forwardRef is used across atoms/buttons, controls, inputs), so feedback is an inconsistent outlier.
  - **Proposal:** Two-part escape hatch consistent with the rest of the kit: (1) wrap each public component in forwardRef and spread `{ className, style, ...rest }` onto the root so layout integration works; (2) add an `asChild`/slot pattern (establish the kit's first Slot util) for the structural pieces: `<TKEmptyState title={<TKTitle level={2}>No orders</TKTitle>} cta={<TKMainButton>Reset</TKMainButton>} />` should render the passed node as-is rather than re-wrapping. Make `titleAs?: keyof JSX.IntrinsicElements` the lightweight version so the default fixes FBK-010 (renders an <h*>) without forcing a Slot.
- [ ] **`FBK-DX-05`** · types · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKTimeline forces consumers to build a `TKTimelineStep[]` with a status string literal per node, and there's no notion of 'which step is current' beyond manually setting exactly one to 'active'. In examples/trailhead/src/features/discover/ExperienceDetail.tsx the consumer maps domain data into this shape and is responsible for the done/active/pending invariant by hand — easy to set two 'active' or zero. The color/contrast bug (FBK-008) and the hardcoded check icon also live here because the node has no typed customization point.
  - **Proposal:** Add a derived-status convenience: `<TKTimeline steps={['Ordered','Shipped','Delivered']} currentStep={1} />` where steps can be plain strings/nodes and `currentStep` auto-derives done(<current)/active(=current)/pending(>current). Keep the explicit `{label,status}[]` form for non-linear flows. Also add `icon?: TKIconName` and `tone?` per step so the done-checkmark and node color (FBK-008) are themeable instead of hardcoded `#fff`. The string+currentStep path removes the most common hand-rolled mapping and makes the single-active invariant impossible to violate.
- [ ] **`FBK-DX-06`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** Skeletons require consumers to hand-replicate the shape of their real content. examples/trailhead/src/features/discover/Feed.tsx builds a bespoke skeleton out of raw <TKSkeleton> primitives (lines 195-203) with magic widths/heights to mirror a feed row, and GuideProfile/DateSlot/RescheduleSlot each pick a different ad-hoc `rows` count for TKSkeletonList. There's no `count` multiplier and no way to derive a skeleton from the same layout, so loading states visually drift from the loaded state and every screen re-tunes pixels.
  - **Proposal:** (1) Add `count?: number` to TKSkeletonCard (and a `TKSkeletonGroup count={n}>` wrapper) so `<TKSkeletonCard count={6} />` replaces the Array.from boilerplate seen in Feed.tsx. (2) Ship a `<TKSkeleton.Of>`-style matcher or a documented recipe: pass the same item renderer used for real rows with a `loading` flag that swaps text for shimmer, keeping skeleton and content in lockstep. Minimum viable: export the magic line-width ramp as a `lines`/`avatar` preset on TKSkeletonList so the common 'avatar + 2 lines' row never needs raw primitives.
- [ ] **`FBK-DX-07`** · api · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** TKProgress takes 0-100 while TKRing takes 0-1 (progress.tsx:7 vs :49). A developer using both on the same screen — exactly what examples/trailhead/src/features/train/Train.tsx does (`<TKRing value={streak.dayOfWeek/7}>` next to `<TKProgress value={goalPct}>`) — has to remember two different scales and manually divide/multiply. This mismatch is a silent footgun: passing a 0-1 fraction to TKProgress renders an invisible 1%-wide bar with no warning, and it's the kind of inconsistency that erodes trust in the whole family.
  - **Proposal:** Unify on a single scale across both components. Default both to 0-1 fractions (the more composable choice) and add an opt-in `max?: number` so `<TKProgress value={72} max={100} />` and percentage inputs still work. Alternatively accept `value` + optional `max` on BOTH so `value/max` is the fraction everywhere. In DEV, warn when `value` is outside `[0, max]` (this also surfaces FBK-002's unclamped aria bug at author time). One mental model, no per-component arithmetic.
- [ ] **`FBK-DX-08`** · docs · effort S
  - **Pain:** The Storybook (packages/uikit/storybook/composites/feedback.stories.tsx) shows each component in its happy state but omits the states developers actually struggle to wire: the AsyncBoundary 'ready data' path (only loading/error/empty are shown), interactive/clickable TKBars, the reduced-motion behavior of skeletons/timeline (FBK-012), the dark/light contrast of the timeline node (FBK-008), and the empty-state with a custom heading/CTA. There's no single copy-pasteable 'fetch-to-render screen' recipe, which is why every trailhead screen reinvents the AsyncBoundary wiring.
  - **Proposal:** Add stories + a short MDX recipe page covering: (a) one canonical 'data-loading screen' using FBK-DX-01's render-prop form, copy-pasteable; (b) interactive TKBars with toggle state; (c) a reduced-motion story (prefers-reduced-motion emulation) proving shimmer/pulse stop; (d) timeline in both themes; (e) RTL/long-copy empty state. Each story doubles as a visual-regression anchor for the red-team a11y fixes so they can't silently regress.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

Coverage gaps / assumptions: (1) Existing tests (test/async-boundary.test.tsx, test/feedback-reorg.test.tsx) cover wiring/precedence and that a progressbar role exists, but assert nothing about aria-valuenow correctness, live-region announcements, heading semantics, touch-target size, or large-data behavior — every finding above is uncovered. (2) FBK-001/010 assume screen-reader behavior from ARIA structure (verified by reading code, not by running a real AT); the missing role/heading is fact, the UX impact is the standard consequence. (3) FBK-006 stack-overflow threshold is engine-dependent (assumption); the O(n)-per-render recompute and full hover re-render are certain from the code. (4) FBK-008/009 contrast/touch claims are grounded in literals (#fff, sm.h=34 from shared.ts:8) but final contrast depends on the consumer's Telegram theme tokens. (5) Not flagged but worth noting: timeline/async use array-index keys (timeline.tsx:22, skeletons map by i) — acceptable for static placeholder lists but would cause reconciliation bugs if a consumer animates step insert/removal. (6) No D-SEC issues found — no dangerouslySetInnerHTML, file inputs, or raw URL handling in this family; `media`/`children`/`icon` are typed ReactNode/TKIconName. (7) TKSkeleton* components have no role/aria-hidden, which is fine only if FBK-001's wrapping status region is added; standalone skeletons used directly (Feed.tsx) are silent placeholders — borderline acceptable, folded into FBK-001.

---

### Forms — Calendar, ChipsDate, Masked, NativeInput, Pin (large, validation-heavy)

`FRM` · `packages/uikit/src/composites/forms` · 10 findings (0🔴 4🟠 6🟡 0⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-TG` Telegram runtime & SSR safety · `D-A11Y` Accessibility (keyboard, ARIA name/state, live regions, touch target) · `D-API` API ergonomics (controlled/uncontrolled, prop forwarding, defaults) · `D-STATE` State model & controlled-value drift · `D-EDGE` Robustness on edge data (all-disabled months, overflow, unmount) · `D-THEME` Tokens / density / touch-target sizing · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView / i18n length / RTL

#### Findings

- [x] **`FRM-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-TG` — TKMaskedInput silently drops its `inputMode` prop — numeric masks raise the full alphanumeric keyboard
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/forms/masked.tsx:143-176`
