  - **Evidence:** `function TKMaskedInput({ mask, value, defaultValue = "", onChange, inputMode = "numeric", ...rest }, ref)` then renders `return (<TKInput {...rest} ref={inputRef} type="text" value={formatted} onChange={...} />)`. `inputMode` is destructured out of `rest` but never passed to `<TKInput>`, even though TKInput accepts `inputMode` (input.tsx:71,141).
  - **Impact:** Any consumer using TKMaskedInput for a card number, postal code, or any `#`-mask (e.g. `(###) ###-##-##`) gets the wrong soft keyboard on the device — the default text QWERTY instead of the numeric pad — and the documented `inputMode="numeric"|"tel"` prop is a no-op. This is the single most common masked use case in a Mini App checkout.
  - **Fix:** Forward it explicitly: `return <TKInput {...rest} ref={inputRef} type="text" inputMode={inputMode} value={formatted} onChange={...} />;`
  - **Done when:** Render `<TKMaskedInput mask="#### #### #### ####" inputMode="numeric" />`, assert the inner `<input>` has `inputmode="numeric"`; on iOS Telegram the numeric keypad appears.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — masked.tsx:144 destructures `inputMode` into its own variable, then the render at lines 164-175 spreads `{...rest}` (which no longer contains inputMode) and never re-passes it, so TKInput receives inputMode=undefined and with type="text" resolves to undefined (input.tsx:93,141) — numeric masks raise the alphanumeric keyboard.
- [x] **`FRM-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Country-picker phone field's national input has no accessible name when `label` is omitted
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/forms/masked.tsx:369-382`
  - **Evidence:** The national `<input ref={inputRef} id={fieldId} type="text" inputMode="tel" ... aria-invalid={!!error} ...>` has no `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby`; it relies solely on `TKFormField label={label} htmlFor={fieldId}`. When a consumer renders `<TKPhoneInput countrySelect />` without `label`, no `<label>` is associated.
  - **Impact:** Screen-reader users in a Mini App hear an unlabeled edit field for the phone number (the only label present is on the country `<select>`, which is `locale.countryCode`). Fails the kit's own a11y/Playwright gate and WCAG 4.1.2 for a high-frequency signup field.
  - **Fix:** Add `aria-label={locale.phoneNumber}` (new locale key) to the national `<input>` as a fallback, or render a visually-hidden label always; mirror the same on `TKPhoneSimpleField`.
  - **Done when:** `getByRole('textbox', { name: /phone/i })` resolves for `<TKPhoneInput countrySelect />` with no `label` prop; axe reports no "form element has no accessible name".
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — The countrySelect national <input> (masked.tsx:369-382) has only id/aria-invalid and no aria-label/aria-labelledby; TKFormField renders a <label htmlFor> only when `label` is truthy (form-field.tsx:19), so `<TKPhoneInput countrySelect />` without a label leaves the phone field with no accessible name (the only aria-label is on the <select>).
- [x] **`FRM-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — PIN entry has no live-region feedback — progress and error are silent to assistive tech
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/forms/pin.tsx:119-140`
  - **Evidence:** Dots are rendered as `<span data-dot ...>` inside a non-announced div; the only ARIA is `aria-label={locale.oneTimeCode}` on the keypad `role="group"` (line 141) and on the visually-hidden `<input inputMode="none">`. There is no `aria-live` region for digit count or for the `error` state (which only triggers `haptics.notification("error")` + CSS shake at lines 48-54).
  - **Impact:** A VoiceOver/TalkBack user entering a PIN on the on-screen pad gets no feedback that a digit registered, how many remain, or that the code was rejected — they only feel a haptic (absent outside Telegram). The custom pad is effectively unusable non-visually, and there's no `role="alert"` for the wrong-PIN case.
  - **Fix:** Add a visually-hidden `aria-live="polite"` region announcing e.g. `"{n} of {length} digits entered"` on change, and an `role="alert"`/`aria-live="assertive"` message when `error` fires. Give each pad key a number-name where the glyph alone is ambiguous.
  - **Done when:** Entering a digit updates a polite live region with the count; setting `error` surfaces an assertive announcement. Verified with the kit's axe/Playwright a11y snapshot.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — pin.tsx has no aria-live/role=alert/role=status (grep returns none); dots are decorative <span data-dot> in a plain div, the error path (lines 48-54) only fires haptics+CSS shake, and the hidden input is intentionally not focused during pad entry (lines 124-126), so SR users get no digit/remaining/rejection feedback.
- [x] **`FRM-010`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-API` — Zero tests and zero stories for the entire forms family — no regression net for its heavy state/caret/locale logic
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/forms.tsx:1-4`
  - **Evidence:** Searching `packages/uikit` for any `*.test.*`/`*.stories.*` referencing `TKCalendar|TKPinInput|TKMaskedInput|TKPhoneInput|TKChipsInput|TKDateInput|TKTimeInput|TKNativeField` returns nothing (51 test files + 28 story files exist, none cover this family). The OTP atom has `test/otp.test.tsx`; these composites have none.
  - **Impact:** The most logic-dense components in the kit — caret-preserving mask math (useCaretMask), locale date-order parsing, range pending-start reconciliation, time clamping, overlapping dial-code resolution — ship with no unit or visual coverage, so regressions like FRM-001 reach consumers undetected and the Playwright visual/a11y gate never exercises them.
  - **Fix:** Add unit tests for `tkApplyMask`, `clampTimeDigits`, `parseDateInput` (locale order), `tkResolveCountry`, and the range/pending-start flow; add Storybook stories so the visual + axe gates cover Calendar/PIN/Masked/Phone/Chips. Backfill a story that would have caught the missing `inputMode`.
  - **Done when:** CI runs forms-family unit tests and at least one story per component through the visual/a11y gate; coverage report shows the mask/parse/resolve helpers exercised.
  - **Verify:** ❌ refuted — Tests exist in packages/uikit/test/ (not src/, which is where the finding's grep looked): m4-forms.test.tsx exercises TKCalendar, TKMaskedInput, TKPhoneInput, TKTimeInput, TKPinInput, TKChipsInput, TKDateInput, and forms-extras.test.tsx covers countrySelect + native date/time — every named composite has unit coverage, so the 'zero tests' premise is false.
- [ ] **`FRM-003`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — Calendar with a fully-disabled visible month leaves a disabled cell as the only tab stop (grid unreachable / no roving anchor)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/forms/calendar.tsx:238-242 and 342-348`
  - **Evidence:** `const tabbableDate = (...) ?? weeks.flat().find((d) => d.getMonth() === visibleMonth.getMonth() && !isDisabled(d)) ?? weeks[0][0];` — when every in-month day is disabled the final fallback is `weeks[0][0]`, and the cell renders `disabled={disabled}` with `tabIndex={sameDay(d, tabbableDate) ? 0 : -1}`.
  - **Impact:** For a date picker constrained so the current month has no selectable day (e.g. `min`/`max` straddling, or `disabledDates` excluding the whole month — common for booking calendars), the only `tabIndex=0` button is `disabled`, so keyboard users cannot tab into or arrow within the grid until they mouse to another month. Roving-tabindex contract is broken.
  - **Fix:** Prefer the first NON-disabled cell across the whole 6-week grid (drop the in-month restriction) before falling back, and if all are disabled make the grid container `tabIndex={0}` with arrow-key entry, or expose a focusable but enabled outside-month day.
  - **Done when:** Mount a TKCalendar whose visible month is entirely disabled; Tab reaches a focusable element inside `[role=grid]` and ArrowRight moves focus to a selectable day in an adjacent month.
- [x] **`FRM-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-STATE` — PIN does not reconcile entered digits when `length`/`maxLength` shrink, and never auto-completes after a length change
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/forms/pin.tsx:46-73`
  - **Evidence:** `const maxDigits = maxLength || length;` is recomputed each render but `pin` is only re-sliced inside `setDigits` on the next edit: `const next = raw.replace(/\D/g, "").slice(0, maxDigits)`. There is no effect reconciling `pin` to a reduced `maxDigits`, and `if (!maxLength && next.length === length) complete(next)` only fires from a user edit.
  - **Impact:** A consumer that switches `length` (e.g. a settings screen toggling 4↔6-digit PIN, or Trailhead's Checkout which already uses `length={4} maxLength={8}`) can leave a longer-than-allowed `pin` in state until the next keypress, and a programmatic length reduction to the current digit count won't fire `onComplete`. Edge but real for dynamic PIN length.
  - **Fix:** Add `useEffect(() => setPin((p) => p.slice(0, maxDigits)), [maxDigits])` and re-evaluate auto-complete when `length` changes for the fixed-length path.
  - **Done when:** Type 6 digits with `maxLength={6}`, then re-render with `length={4}` and no `maxLength`; `pin` truncates to 4 and `onComplete` fires once with the 4-digit code.
- [ ] **`FRM-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — Standalone TKCalendar (and its scrollable month/year part-lists) has no Telegram vertical-swipe-to-minimize guard
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/forms/calendar.tsx:493-507`
  - **Evidence:** The year/month picker list uses `<div role="listbox" style={{ maxHeight: 286, overflowY: "auto", overscrollBehavior: "contain", ... }}>`. `overscrollBehavior: contain` limits scroll chaining but does NOT stop Telegram's swipe-down-to-minimize; only TKSheet wires `disableVerticalSwipes` (overlays/sheet.tsx:86). TKCalendar is used standalone in consumers (trailhead RescheduleSlot.tsx:121, DateSlot.tsx:141).
  - **Impact:** When a TKCalendar is placed inline (not inside the sheet), a downward drag that overscrolls the year list — or a flick on the grid near the top of the viewport — can minimize the whole Mini App instead of scrolling the list, the classic Telegram gesture conflict. The sheet path is safe; the documented standalone path is not.
  - **Fix:** Either call the platform `disableVerticalSwipes` while a scrollable picker is open, or document that standalone TKCalendar must be wrapped by the consumer; add `touch-action` handling on the scroll container.
  - **Done when:** In a real/emulated Telegram WebView, overscrolling the year list inside a standalone TKCalendar scrolls the list and does not trigger app minimize.
- [x] **`FRM-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-THEME` — Calendar day cells are a hardcoded 38×38px — below the 44px touch-target minimum and not density/scale aware
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/forms/calendar.tsx:352-356`
  - **Evidence:** Each day `<button>` uses literal `width: 38, height: 38` with `fontSize: "var(--tk-fz-sub)"`. Sizes are raw px (only radius/colors are tokenized). There is no density/touch-target knob across the family (PIN keys are `minWidth: 44, height: 56`; fields are `height: 48`).
  - **Impact:** On a narrow Telegram WebView the 38px day targets are under Apple/Google/WCAG 2.5.8 minimum (44/48px), causing mis-taps next to range edges; and because the size is hardcoded, a consumer can't scale the calendar for large dynamic-type or a compact density without overriding internals. Inconsistent sizing across the family also breaks visual rhythm.
  - **Fix:** Drive cell size from a token (e.g. `--tk-tap-min`/`--tk-control-h`) with a minimum of 44px, or expose a `density`/`size` prop; let the grid grow the cells to fill the 7-column track instead of fixing 38px.
  - **Done when:** Day buttons measure >=44px in the default density; a density/size prop (or token override) changes cell size without editing the component.
- [ ] **`FRM-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-RESP` — Country `<option>` rows and the phone display cell can't truncate — long localized names overflow / clip the dial code
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/forms/masked.tsx:346-367`
  - **Evidence:** The display cell is `whiteSpace: "nowrap"` with flag + `+{country.dial}` + chevron, and `<option>{tkCountryFlag(c.iso)} {c.name} +{c.dial}</option>` rows are full country names from `Intl.DisplayNames`. The transparent `<select>` overlays a fixed-width cell; there is no truncation/ellipsis budget for long names (e.g. "United States Minor Outlying Islands", localized RU/DE names) in a ~360px viewport.
  - **Impact:** In narrow WebViews and verbose locales the native select list and the inline `+dial` chrome can overflow or push the number input, and RTL locales aren't accounted for (no `dir` handling on the flag/dial run). Mini App teams targeting non-Latin locales hit layout breakage on the most-used field.
  - **Fix:** Constrain the display run with `min-width:0; overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis`, cap the cell, and verify the native option list under long localized names; add an RTL test for the dial-prefix ordering.
  - **Done when:** With `lang="ru"`/`lang="de"` and a 360px container, the phone field renders without horizontal overflow and the dial code stays visible; RTL locale keeps the `+dial` adjacent to the flag correctly.
- [x] **`FRM-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — TKNativeField is used by the family but never exported — `native` date/time consumers can't type the field or its props
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/forms.tsx:1-4`
  - **Evidence:** The forms barrel re-exports only `./forms/calendar`, `./forms/masked`, `./forms/pin`, `./forms/chips-date`. `native-input.tsx` (which defines `TKNativeField`/`TKNativeFieldProps` and is imported by masked.tsx:21 and chips-date.tsx:9) is not re-exported, and `TKDateInput`/`TKTimeInput`'s `native` mode is therefore only configurable through the wrapper props.
  - **Impact:** A consumer who wants the OS-native date/time field directly (a common Mini App choice, since it gives the system picker + keyboard) has no public component and no exported `TKNativeFieldProps` type to reference; they must reimplement the wrapper. Minor surface-completeness gap, but it forces a workaround.
  - **Fix:** Either intentionally keep it internal and document that `native` is only reachable via TKDateInput/TKTimeInput, or add `export * from "./forms/native-input"` to forms.tsx and ship `TKNativeFieldProps`.
  - **Done when:** Decide and document: either `import { TKNativeField } from "tg-mini-app-uikit"` resolves, or the README/types make clear native mode is wrapper-only.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`FRM-DX-001`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** The whole family is divorced from native HTML forms. `TKPinInput` (pin.tsx:8), `TKChipsInput` (chips-date.tsx:124), `TKDateInput` (chips-date.tsx:308), `TKCalendar` (calendar.tsx:11) define flat prop bags with NO `name`, `required`, or `id` — so none of them participate in a `<form>` submit or FormData. Worse, the `countrySelect` phone variant (masked.tsx:252-253) destructures only a fixed subset of props and silently swallows `name`, `id`, `autoComplete`, `inputMode`, `validate`, `prefix/suffix` that the simple phone variant and `TKMaskedInput` (which spread `...rest` of TKInputProps) DO forward — so the same `<TKPhoneInput name=… />` works in one mode and is a no-op in the other.
  - **Proposal:** Make every family member form-aware and consistent. (1) Add `name?: string` to `TKPinInput`/`TKChipsInput`/`TKDateInput`/`TKTimeInput`/`TKCalendar` and render a hidden `<input type="hidden" name value>` carrying the canonical value (PIN string, ISO date, `HH:MM`, joined chips) so `new FormData(form)` just works. (2) In `TKPhoneCountryField` replace the hand-picked destructure with `{ ...rest }` passthrough to the national `<input>` (name/id/autoComplete/inputMode/aria-*), matching `TKPhoneSimpleField`. (3) Add `required?: boolean` that flows into the existing `TKFormField.required` (form-field.tsx:9) so the asterisk/aria are consistent across the family.
- [ ] **`FRM-DX-002`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** `TKPinInput.error` (pin.tsx:11) is a transient boolean the consumer must imperatively flip back off. Every real consumer reimplements the same dance: PinGate.tsx:46-50 does `setError(true); …; setTimeout(() => setError(false), 600)`, and Checkout.tsx never clears it. The component already owns the shake/haptic/clear timing internally (pin.tsx:48-65) but exposes none of it — so the common 'verify a code, show error, let user retry' flow is ~15 lines of boilerplate at every call site, and getting the reset delay wrong desyncs the shake from the clear.
  - **Proposal:** Add a declarative verification API so the one-shot error owns its own lifecycle. Accept `onComplete?: (pin: string) => boolean | void | Promise<boolean | void>` — returning/resolving `false` triggers the built-in shake+haptic+clear with no consumer timer; `true`/`void` keeps current behavior. Keep `error` as an escape hatch for externally-driven errors. PinGate collapses to `onComplete={(p) => p === pin}`. Optionally add `status?: "idle" | "verifying" | "success"` to drive a spinner/checkmark on the dots so async verification has built-in feedback instead of a parent-managed `phase` state machine (Checkout.tsx:308-325).
- [ ] **`FRM-DX-003`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** `TKMaskedInput` exposes a raw `mask` string (masked.tsx:122) and a positional `onChange(formatted, raw)` (masked.tsx:127) — the two most common masks (bank card, expiry, SSN/ITN-style) have to be hand-written by every consumer, the `#`-only grammar is undocumented at the type level, and the two-string callback is trivially swappable (no compiler help if you read `raw` as `formatted`). The story file hand-rolls `mask="##-##"` (forms.stories.tsx:60) precisely because there is no preset.
  - **Proposal:** Ship named presets so the common case is one prop. Export `TK_MASKS = { card: "#### #### #### ####", expiry: "##/##", cvc: "###", date: "##/##/####" }` and accept `mask: string | keyof typeof TK_MASKS`, auto-selecting `inputMode="numeric"` + sensible `autoComplete` (`cc-number`, `cc-exp`, `cc-csc`) per preset so card fields are accessible-and-autofillable in one line: `<TKMaskedInput mask="card" />`. Keep the positional callback but add an object overload `onChange?: (e: { formatted: string; raw: string }) => void` (detected by arity/shape) so new code can destructure by name and not transpose the two strings.
- [ ] **`FRM-DX-004`** · composition · effort L
  - **Pain:** `CalendarPartTrigger`/`CalendarPartList` in calendar.tsx (lines 405-536) and `DatePartTrigger`/`DatePartList` in chips-date.tsx (lines 556-687) are ~250 lines of near-identical month/year picker chrome, copy-pasted with only pixel tweaks (40px vs 34px row height, 256 vs 286 max-height). Any a11y or token fix (e.g. the roving-tabindex work behind FRM-003) has to be made twice and kept in sync by hand, and there is no shared, tested unit. There is also no `renderDay` / `dayContent` hook, so a consumer who wants price badges or availability dots on calendar cells (a core booking-app need — see RescheduleSlot.tsx / DateSlot.tsx) has to fork `TKCalendar` wholesale.
  - **Proposal:** Extract one internal `TKPartPicker` (the trigger + listbox pair) into `forms/part-picker.tsx`, parameterized by `rowHeight`/`maxHeight` tokens, and consume it from both calendar.tsx and chips-date.tsx so the keyboard/roving/scroll logic lives in one tested place. Add a public `renderDay?: (date: Date, ctx: { selected: boolean; inRange: boolean; disabled: boolean; today: boolean }) => ReactNode` slot to `TKCalendarProps` (calendar.tsx:11) so booking apps can decorate cells without forking. The default render stays exactly as today.
- [ ] **`FRM-DX-005`** · ergonomics · effort S
  - **Pain:** `TKDateInput` (chips-date.tsx:308) flattens 8 calendar-shaping props (min/max/disabledDates/weekStartsOn/lang/sheetTitle/invalidText/native) onto its own surface and re-passes them by hand into the embedded `TKCalendar` (chips-date.tsx:532-549). Any new `TKCalendar` prop (e.g. the `renderDay` from FRM-DX-004) must be threaded through `TKDateInput` manually or it's unreachable from the field — the field can never stay at feature-parity with the calendar it wraps, and consumers can't reach calendar behavior the field author didn't anticipate.
  - **Proposal:** Add a single `calendarProps?: Partial<TKCalendarProps>` escape hatch on `TKDateInputProps` that is spread into the inner `<TKCalendar {...calendarProps} … />`, with the field's own min/max/lang taking precedence. This future-proofs the wrapper (new calendar features are reachable for free), shrinks the field's prop surface, and gives a clear 'field for the common case, `calendarProps` for the long tail' story.
- [ ] **`FRM-DX-006`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** Touch-target and sizing constants are scattered magic numbers with no token or scale knob: calendar day cells are hardcoded `width:38,height:38` (calendar.tsx:355-356), pin keys `height:56` with `minWidth:44` (pin.tsx:81-82), every field cell is a literal `height:48` (masked.tsx:335, native-input.tsx:64, chips-date.tsx:199). A consumer building a denser desktop-in-Telegram layout, or a larger-tap accessibility mode, cannot scale any of it without forking, and the 38px calendar cell is the root of FRM-007's sub-44px touch target — there is nowhere to fix it once.
  - **Proposal:** Introduce a `size?: "md" | "lg"` (or a `density` token) shared across the family, mapping to CSS custom props (`--tk-field-h`, `--tk-cell`, `--tk-key-h`) defined once in foundation. Default `md` keeps current visuals except the calendar cell snaps to the 44px min; `lg` scales for accessibility. This gives one lever for FRM-007 and density instead of editing four files of literals, and lets the demo's surface-composer expose a real density control.
- [ ] **`FRM-DX-007`** · types · effort S
  - **Pain:** `TKNativeField` is the shared engine for `native` date/time (used at chips-date.tsx:339 and masked.tsx:517) but is not in the barrel — forms.tsx exports only calendar/masked/pin/chips-date, never native-input.tsx — so `TKNativeFieldProps` is untyped/unreachable for consumers (this is FRM-009's symptom). Independently, the family's `onChange` contracts are inconsistent and easy to misuse: masked is `(formatted, raw)` positional, time is `(time: string | null)`, date is `(date: Date | null)`, phone is `(formatted, raw)`. There is no exported union/result type a generic form layer can switch on, and no `TKMaskedInputProps`-style export is re-exported for `native` consumers to type their own wrappers.
  - **Proposal:** (1) Add `export * from "./forms/native-input"` to forms.tsx so `TKNativeField`/`TKNativeFieldProps` are public and typeable (resolves the export half of FRM-009). (2) Export a small, documented value-type vocabulary — `TKDateValue = Date | null`, `TKTimeValue = string | null`, `TKMaskedChange = { formatted: string; raw: string }` — and reference them in each props interface so a generic `<Field>` wrapper can be written with full inference instead of `any`. No runtime change.
- [ ] **`FRM-DX-008`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** There is no copy-paste 'forms cookbook'. The single story (storybook/composites/forms.stories.tsx) shows each component in isolation but never the real flows consumers actually build: a card+expiry+cvc payment row, a phone field wired to a country picker with validation, a PIN gate that verifies and resets (the exact thing PinGate.tsx/Checkout.tsx had to invent), or a date range in a sheet. The load-bearing gotchas — that PIN `error` is one-shot, that masked `onChange` is `(formatted, raw)` and you almost always want `raw`, that `countrySelect` changes which props are honored (see FRM-DX-001) — are buried in source comments, so consumers rediscover them by trial and error.
  - **Proposal:** Add a `Forms/Cookbook` MDX (or grouped stories) with 4-5 runnable recipes: (a) payment row using the FRM-DX-003 presets, (b) phone with `countrySelect` + `validate`, (c) PIN verify/retry using the FRM-DX-002 boolean-return `onComplete`, (d) date-range sheet via `TKDateInput` + `mode="range"`, (e) a native `<form>` round-trip showing FormData (FRM-DX-001). Each recipe doubles as a regression surface and documents the one-line 'happy path' for the common case.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

Coverage gaps / assumptions: (1) No *.test.* or *.stories.* exist for this family anywhere in packages/uikit (verified by grep across 51 test + 28 story files), so all dynamic behavior was assessed by reading source only — FRM-001/003/005 are code-evident but not runtime-confirmed. (2) The Telegram swipe-minimize claim (FRM-006) is grounded in overlays/sheet.tsx:86 wiring `disableVerticalSwipes` for the sheet path while TKCalendar's own scroll containers do not; not reproduced on a device. (3) SSR: `documentLang()`/`phoneDocumentLang()` guard `document` correctly and Intl/`new Date()` are SSR-safe, so I found no hard SSR import crash in this family — not raised as a finding. (4) Adjacent/atom-level issue worth noting but outside the forms family: input.tsx:115 computes the focus ring with the raw `error` prop while the border uses `shownError`, so a built-in `validate` failure (email/time-incomplete) shows a red border AND a blue focus ring simultaneously — applies to all TKInput-based fields, including TKMaskedTimeField's incomplete state. (5) PIN's hidden `<input value={pin}>` exposes the live code as plain text for one-time-code autofill by design; flagged only as context, not a finding, since it is the intended SMS-autofill target and uses `inputMode=\"none\"`. (6) Calendar single-mode `onChange` is publicly typed `(date: Date) => void` but internally cast to accept null (calendar.tsx:101); harmless today because `pick` always emits a Date, but the cast is a latent type-safety footgun if a clear-action is added later.

---

### Gestures — LongPress, PullToRefresh, SwipeCell (touch + Telegram minimize guard)

`GES` · `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures` · 12 findings (0🔴 5🟠 6🟡 1⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-GESTURE` Touch/gesture correctness · `D-TG` Telegram runtime · `D-A11Y` Accessibility · `D-API` API ergonomics · `D-STATE` State model & async · `D-THEME` Tokens/theming · `D-EDGE` Robustness/races

#### Findings

- [x] **`GES-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-API` — useLongPress fires but never suppresses the synthetic click — every consumer must hand-roll a suppress ref
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/long-press.tsx:55-76`
  - **Evidence:** `return { onPointerDown(e){...timer=setTimeout(()=>{origin.current=null; fnRef.current();},duration)}, onPointerUp: cancel, ... }` — after the long-press callback fires, the browser still dispatches the normal click/tap that follows pointerup, and the hook exposes no fired-state or click guard.
  - **Impact:** On touch, a hold that triggers the context action ALSO triggers the row's onClick (navigation/open), so the long-press and the tap both run. The real consumer documented and worked around this: examples/trailhead/.../GuideDirectory.tsx:52 — "The kit's long-press doesn't suppress the synthetic click that follows a touch hold, so guard the row's click" with a manual `suppress` ref wired through a wrapped onPointerDown. Every team copies that boilerplate or ships a double-action bug.
  - **Fix:** Return an `onClickCapture` (or expose a `pressedRef`/`isLongPressing`) from the hook that calls e.stopPropagation()/preventDefault() for the click immediately following a fired press, and reset it on the next pointerdown. Document the pattern so consumers spread `{...longPress}` without a wrapper.
  - **Done when:** A jsdom/Playwright test: pointerdown, advance 500ms (press fires), pointerup, then the synthetic click is swallowed — onClick handler is NOT called once within the same gesture; a short tap still fires onClick exactly once.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — long-press.tsx:55-76 returns only pointer/contextmenu handlers with no fired-state or click guard; onPointerUp just clears the timer. The consumer GuideDirectory.tsx:52-58,64-67,75-78 documents the missing suppression and hand-rolls a `suppress` ref to skip the synthetic click.
- [x] **`GES-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-GESTURE` — Long-press has no pointer-type / button guard — mouse right-click and a second finger both start a press
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/long-press.tsx:56-63`
  - **Evidence:** `onPointerDown(e) { origin.current = { x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY }; window.clearTimeout(timer.current); timer.current = window.setTimeout(...) }` — no check of `e.button`, `e.isPrimary`, or `e.pointerType`.
  - **Impact:** A right-mouse-button press, a stylus barrel press, or a SECOND finger landing during an existing touch all (re)start the hold timer. On a list row this means a two-finger pan or an accidental second touch fires the destructive/context long-press action. Multi-touch in a Telegram WebView (e.g. user steadying the phone) is common.
  - **Fix:** In onPointerDown, bail unless `e.isPrimary` and (`e.pointerType !== 'mouse' || e.button === 0`). Ignore non-primary pointers entirely.
  - **Done when:** Tests: a non-primary pointerdown (isPrimary:false) does not start the timer; a mouse pointerdown with button:2 does not start it; a primary touch/left-click does.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — long-press.tsx:56-63 onPointerDown reads only e.clientX/clientY and (re)starts the timer with no check of e.button, e.isPrimary, or e.pointerType, so a right-click or a second finger both start/restart the hold.
- [x] **`GES-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Long-press is touch/mouse-only — no keyboard equivalent, and the hook never advertises that
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/long-press.tsx:25-28`
  - **Evidence:** `export function useLongPress(onLongPress, {duration, moveTolerance}={}): TKLongPressHandlers` returns only pointer/contextmenu handlers; there is no keyboard handler or documented alternative trigger.
  - **Impact:** A keyboard or switch-control user can never reach the long-press action (rename, delete, context menu). In the consumer this is the only way to invoke onLongPress, and GuideDirectory hand-adds role/tabIndex/onKeyDown only for plain open — the long-press action stays keyboard-inaccessible, an a11y/WCAG 2.1.1 failure that ships in the package's own demo.
  - **Fix:** Document that consumers MUST provide a keyboard-reachable equivalent (e.g. a menu button / Shift+F10 / context-menu key), or extend the hook to return an `onKeyDown` that invokes onLongPress on the ContextMenu key / a documented shortcut.
  - **Done when:** Docs/JSDoc state the keyboard requirement; if implemented, pressing the ContextMenu key (or chosen shortcut) on the focused element invokes onLongPress in a test, and Storybook a11y/Playwright keyboard run reaches the action.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — useLongPress (long-press.tsx:25-28,55-76) exposes no keyboard handler. In GuideDirectory.tsx:61-73 the role/tabIndex/onKeyDown only invoke onOpen; the onLongPress action has no keyboard-reachable path, a WCAG 2.1.1 gap.
- [x] **`GES-005`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — PullToRefresh refresh state is entirely silent to assistive tech (no role/aria-live)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/pull-to-refresh.tsx:167-185`
  - **Evidence:** The indicator is `<div style={{...}} ref={indicatorRef}><span className="tk-ptr"><TKSpinner size={20} /></span></div>` — no role, aria-live, aria-busy, or text. TKSpinner is decorative.
  - **Impact:** A screen-reader user who triggers (or whose content reloads via) refresh hears nothing: no "Refreshing…", no "Updated", and the list silently swaps under them. For a data-refresh surface this is a real comprehension gap and an a11y-gate risk for teams with WCAG targets.
  - **Fix:** Add a visually-hidden `aria-live="polite"` region (or aria-busy on the scroll region) that announces refresh start/finish, with overridable label props (i18n). Set aria-busy=true on the content while `refreshing`.
  - **Done when:** While refreshing, an aria-live region contains a localized "refreshing" string and clears/announces completion when the promise settles; verified via testing-library `findByText`/`toHaveAttribute('aria-busy')` and the Playwright a11y snapshot.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — pull-to-refresh.tsx:167-185 indicator div has no role/aria-live/aria-busy/text — only a decorative TKSpinner; grep found zero aria attributes in the gestures dir, so refresh start/finish is silent to assistive tech.
- [x] **`GES-007`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-API` — SwipeCell defaults fullSwipe=true — an over-swipe auto-fires the FIRST action with no confirmation
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/swipe-cell.tsx:49,99-104`
  - **Evidence:** `fullSwipe = true` default; in onEnd: `if (fullSwipe && side.length && Math.abs(raw) > width * 0.6) { haptics.impact("medium"); setOffset(0); side[0].onAction(); return; }`.
  - **Impact:** By default, dragging a row ~60% fires the first action immediately — and the first action is frequently destructive (delete). The kit's own demo had to opt out: TripsList.tsx:111 sets `fullSwipe={false}` with the comment "the destructive Cancel must be a deliberate tap … never auto-fired by an over-swipe." Teams that don't notice ship an accidental-delete footgun; haptics is the only safeguard, and outside Telegram there isn't even that.
  - **Fix:** Either flip the default to `fullSwipe=false`, or add a per-action `destructive`/`confirm` flag so full-swipe never auto-fires a destructive action without a second confirm step; document the risk in JSDoc.
  - **Done when:** With default props and a destructive first action, an over-swipe does NOT immediately call onAction (it opens or requires confirm); a non-destructive action still full-swipe-fires when explicitly enabled. Covered by a test asserting onAction call counts.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — swipe-cell.tsx:49 defaults fullSwipe=true; lines 99-104 auto-fire side[0].onAction() on >60% over-swipe guarded only by haptics. Consumer TripsList.tsx:109-111 opts out with the documented 'never auto-fired by an over-swipe' comment for the destructive Cancel.
- [x] **`GES-003`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-GESTURE` — onContextMenu unconditionally preventDefaults — kills legitimate right-click/text-selection on the wrapped element
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/long-press.tsx:71-74`
  - **Evidence:** `onContextMenu(e) { // Stop the native long-press menu/selection from racing our timer. e.preventDefault(); }` — fires for every context-menu event, including a desktop right-click when no long-press is pending.
  - **Impact:** Any element using useLongPress loses the native context menu and the iOS/Android text-selection callout entirely, even on desktop or when the user never initiated a hold. Consumers wrapping selectable text or wanting normal right-click behavior get a surprising dead zone with no opt-out.
  - **Fix:** Only preventDefault while a press timer/origin is active (e.g. `if (origin.current || timer.current) e.preventDefault()`), or expose a `suppressContextMenu` option defaulting to true so consumers can keep native selection.
  - **Done when:** A contextmenu event dispatched with no prior pointerdown is NOT preventDefaulted; one dispatched mid-hold is. Toggling the option flips the behavior.
- [x] **`GES-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — SwipeCell actions are focusable while fully invisible (opacity:0) with no ARIA grouping
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/swipe-cell.tsx:115-159`
  - **Evidence:** Action container: `opacity: offset === 0 && !focused ? 0 : 1` with `<button ... onFocus={()=>setFocused(true)} onBlur={()=>setFocused(false)}>`. The buttons stay in the DOM/tab order at opacity 0 and there is no role/aria-label naming the actionable region or its relationship to the row.
  - **Impact:** Tabbing into a closed row lands focus on an invisible control before opacity flips (a brief invisible-focus state), and screen readers encounter loose buttons ("Cancel", "Reschedule") with no announced association to the row they act on. Sighted-keyboard users may also see focus jump to off-screen edges. It works, but it's fragile and unlabelled.
  - **Fix:** Wrap each rail in a `role="group"` with an `aria-label` referencing the row, ensure the reveal (opacity→1) is driven before focus is visually committed, and consider `visibility:hidden`+inert when closed unless `focused`, toggling to visible on focus so there is no invisible-but-focusable state.
  - **Done when:** With the row closed, tabbing makes the focused action visible in the same frame (no invisible focus target in a Playwright screenshot), and an a11y tree query shows each rail grouped/labelled relative to its row.
- [x] **`GES-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-THEME` — SwipeCell hardcodes #fff label color and 76px action width — invisible to tokens/density/contrast
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/swipe-cell.tsx:41,146-149`
  - **Evidence:** `const ACTION_W = 76;` and the button style `background: SWIPE_TONES[action.tone ?? "accent"], color: "#fff", ... fontSize: "var(--tk-fz-caption)"`. Tones map to vars but the text color is a literal `#fff`.
  - **Impact:** The gray tone is `var(--tk-text-2)` (a muted gray); white text on it can fail contrast, and any light/high-contrast theme or accent that isn't dark-enough produces low-contrast labels — with no token to fix it. The fixed 76px width ignores the kit's density/type-scale knobs, so on large-text or compact density the label clips or looks oversized. There's no min-44px height guarantee either; on a short row the touch target shrinks below 44px.
  - **Fix:** Use an on-tone foreground token (e.g. `var(--tk-on-accent)` per tone) instead of `#fff`, derive ACTION_W from a token/clamp, and enforce `min-height: 44px` (or min touch size) on the buttons.
  - **Done when:** Switching to a light/high-contrast theme keeps label contrast ≥ 4.5:1 (token-driven); a density change resizes the rail; buttons report ≥44px hit area in a layout test.
- [x] **`GES-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — Long-press never fires haptics — inconsistent with the family's own Telegram feedback contract
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/long-press.tsx:59-62`
  - **Evidence:** The fire path `timer.current = window.setTimeout(() => { origin.current = null; fnRef.current(); }, duration)` calls the callback with no haptic; the file does not import `useOptionalHaptics` at all, whereas pull-to-refresh.tsx:125 (`haptics.impact("light")`) and swipe-cell.tsx:102 (`haptics.impact("medium")`) both buzz on commit.
  - **Impact:** On Telegram, a long-press that mutates state (delete/menu) gives no tactile confirmation, so the gesture feels unresponsive and users over-hold or double-fire. It's an inconsistency across the gesture family and a missed Telegram-native affordance.
  - **Fix:** Call `useOptionalHaptics().impact("medium")` (or selection feedback) at the moment the press fires, behind the optional-haptics guard so it's a no-op outside Telegram. Optionally expose a `haptic?: boolean` option.
  - **Done when:** In a Telegram-runtime test/mock, firing the long-press triggers one haptic impact; outside the runtime the call is a safe no-op (no throw).
- [x] **`GES-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-GESTURE` — Long-press lacks pointer capture, so a finger that drifts off-element can fire a stale press
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/long-press.tsx:56-70`
  - **Evidence:** onPointerDown starts the timer but never calls `setPointerCapture`; cancel relies on `onPointerMove`/`onPointerUp` firing on the same element. If the pointer leaves the element, pointermove/up may dispatch to the new target, so the move-tolerance cancel and the release-cancel never run on this handler — only the 500ms timer does.
  - **Impact:** Hold, then slide the finger off the row (onto a sibling/scroll area) without exceeding tolerance before leaving: pointermove stops reaching the handler, the timer still fires, and the long-press triggers on a row the finger is no longer over. Combined with GES-001 (no click suppression) this is a surprising mis-fire.
  - **Fix:** Call `e.currentTarget.setPointerCapture(e.pointerId)` on pointerdown (release on up/cancel), as useDragGesture already does at line 129, so move/up/cancel always reach this element; keep the scroll/blur/visibilitychange global cancels as backups.
  - **Done when:** A test where pointerdown is followed by the pointer leaving the element (pointermove dispatched elsewhere) results in the press being canceled (or capture keeping events on-element), not a spurious fire after the timer.
- [x] **`GES-011`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — PullToRefresh can setState after unmount when onRefresh resolves late
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/pull-to-refresh.tsx:140-156`
  - **Evidence:** After committing: `setRefreshing(true); ... Promise.resolve(result).catch(()=>{}).finally(() => { applyPull(0); setRefreshing(false); setGuardingActive(false); });` — the finally runs unconditionally with no mounted/aborted guard.
  - **Impact:** If the user navigates away (the surface unmounts) while a slow refresh is in flight, the settle calls `setRefreshing`/`setGuardingActive` on an unmounted component (React 18 warns; logic state churns). On a tab/route swap in a Mini App this is a routine path. The DOM writes via `indicatorRef`/`scrollRef` are null-guarded, but the React state setters are not.
  - **Fix:** Track a mounted ref (set false in a cleanup effect) or an AbortController/ignore flag captured at commit time, and skip the setState calls in finally when unmounted.
  - **Done when:** A test mounts PTR, triggers a refresh with a deferred promise, unmounts, then resolves the promise — no act()/state-update-on-unmounted warning is emitted.
- [ ] **`GES-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-TG` — PullToRefresh forces its own overflow:auto scroller, risking a nested double-scroll with TKPage
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/gestures/pull-to-refresh.tsx:48-53,186-195`
  - **Evidence:** `resolveScrollTarget` prefers a child `[data-tk-page-scroll]` for scrollTop reads, but the rendered `scrollRef` div is hardcoded `overflowY: "auto"` and is also what receives the translateY pull. If a TKPage (which itself owns `data-tk-page-scroll`) is rendered as the child, there are two nested scroll containers.
  - **Impact:** Wrapping a TKPage in TKPullToRefresh yields nested scrollers: momentum/scroll-chaining and the at-top detection (used to gate preventDefault) can disagree, and on iOS WebViews nested overflow scrolling is janky. Consumers must know to feed raw content, not a full TKPage, but nothing enforces or documents it.
  - **Fix:** Either make the wrapper non-scrolling (overflow:visible) and always translate the resolved scroll target, or detect a child page-scroll and avoid adding a second overflow:auto; document the expected child contract in JSDoc/quickstart.
  - **Done when:** Rendering TKPullToRefresh around a TKPage produces a single scroll container (asserted by counting elements with non-visible overflowY in the subtree), and pull-at-top still gates correctly.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`GES-DX-001`** · ergonomics · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** useLongPress returns only raw pointer handlers, so every consumer re-implements the same plumbing. In examples/trailhead/src/features/guide/GuideDirectory.tsx:54-78 the row hand-rolls a `suppress` ref, manually re-wraps `onPointerDown` to reset it, and guards `onClick`. The Storybook story (packages/uikit/storybook/composites/gestures.stories.tsx:106-127) repeats the same dance plus a hand-added `onPointerLeave` cancel. The common case ('hold to do X, tap to do Y, don't double-fire') takes ~20 lines and is easy to get wrong.
  - **Proposal:** Ship a one-line binding API: `useLongPress({ onLongPress, onClick?, duration?, moveTolerance? })` returns a single spreadable `bind` object (or keep returning the handlers object but add `onClick` + internal click suppression). Internally own the suppress-click ref (closes GES-001), pointer capture (GES-010), and pointer-type/button guard (GES-002) so consumers write `<div {...useLongPress({ onLongPress, onClick: onOpen })} />` and get correct behavior with zero refs. Keep the low-level handler shape available as `useLongPress.handlers` for advanced cases.
- [ ] **`GES-DX-002`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** useLongPress is touch/mouse-only and silently inaccessible (GES-004): the Trailhead GuideRow (GuideDirectory.tsx:59-74) has to add its own role/tabIndex/onKeyDown for the *primary* tap, but the long-press action has no keyboard path at all and the hook never advertises this. SwipeCell actions are focusable-but-invisible (swipe-cell.tsx:124-126, opacity toggled on focus) with no grouping, and PullToRefresh's refreshing state (pull-to-refresh.tsx:160-185) is silent to AT — none of the three is accessible by default.
  - **Proposal:** Make a11y the default, not the consumer's job. (a) useLongPress: accept an optional `keyboardKey`/`longPressKeys` (default Space-hold or a configurable shortcut) and return `role`/`aria` hints, or expose a `TKLongPressable` wrapper that injects role=button + keyboard activation. (b) TKPullToRefresh: add `role="status"` + `aria-live="polite"` on the indicator with a localizable `refreshingLabel` prop, default 'Refreshing'. (c) TKSwipeCell: wrap each action rail in `role="group"` with `aria-label` (leading/trailing), and gate the rail with `inert`/`tabIndex=-1` when closed instead of just `opacity:0` so invisible buttons aren't tab targets. Consumers get accessible widgets with no extra code.
- [ ] **`GES-DX-003`** · defaults · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** TKSwipeCell defaults `fullSwipe=true` (swipe-cell.tsx:49,99-104), so an over-swipe auto-fires `side[0].onAction()` with no confirmation — dangerous when the first action is destructive. Trailhead's TripsList already has to opt OUT with `fullSwipe={false}` and a comment explaining the destructive Cancel must never auto-fire (TripsList.tsx:109-111). The safe choice is the one every careful consumer reverses.
  - **Proposal:** Flip the default to `fullSwipe=false`, and make full-swipe destructive-safe when enabled: add a per-action `confirm?: boolean` (or `destructive?: boolean`) so a full-swipe on a destructive action snaps to a confirm-armed state instead of firing immediately, while non-destructive actions fire as today. Tone `red` could imply `destructive` by default. This makes the common case safe-by-default and removes the boilerplate opt-out.
- [ ] **`GES-DX-004`** · composition · effort L
  - **Pain:** All three exports force their own DOM wrapper. TKPullToRefresh hardcodes an `overflow:auto` scroller div (pull-to-refresh.tsx:186-195) which double-scrolls when wrapping a TKPage that already scrolls (GES-012), and TKSwipeCell wraps children in its own `position:relative` surface div (swipe-cell.tsx:178-188). There is no `asChild`/render-prop escape hatch, so consumers can't merge the gesture behavior onto their own element — they must accept an extra layout node and its scroll/clip semantics.
  - **Proposal:** Add an `asChild` prop (Slot-style merge of refs + handlers + style onto the single child) to TKPullToRefresh and TKSwipeCell, mirroring the asChild pattern used elsewhere in the kit. For PullToRefresh, also support `scrollElement`/`scrollRef` (or `asChild`) so it can attach to an existing scroller (e.g. TKPage's `[data-tk-page-scroll]`, which it already probes at pull-to-refresh.tsx:50) instead of creating a competing one. Eliminates the nested-scroll footgun and gives full composition control.
- [ ] **`GES-DX-005`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKSwipeAction takes a flat `onAction` and a string `label`, but the row offers no per-cell or per-action knobs that the common iOS pattern needs: action width is a module constant (ACTION_W=76, swipe-cell.tsx:41), label color is hardcoded `#fff` (swipe-cell.tsx:147), and there's no way to disable an action, mark it destructive, or give it an accessible name distinct from the visible label. Consumers can't adapt to density/contrast/RTL tokens (GES-008) or express intent.
  - **Proposal:** Widen TKSwipeAction to a token-aware, intent-carrying shape: `{ label; icon?; tone?; destructive?; disabled?; ariaLabel?; onAction }` and add a cell-level `actionWidth?: number | string` (default a token like `var(--tk-swipe-action-w)`). Derive label/icon color from the tone token's on-color instead of literal `#fff`. This lets one prop adapt the rail to density/contrast and gives screen readers a real name without changing the happy-path call site.
- [ ] **`GES-DX-006`** · docs · effort S
  - **Pain:** Storybook coverage exists but is thin and actually showcases the boilerplate rather than the intended ergonomics: the LongPress story (gestures.stories.tsx:95-127) wires onPointerDown/Up/Cancel/Leave by hand and reimplements suppression, the SwipeCell story uses `fullSwipe` default with a destructive 'Delete' as the auto-fire action (gestures.stories.tsx:83), and there is no keyboard-only, RTL, leading+trailing, async-error, or 'tap vs hold' story. No copy-paste recipe shows the correct one-liner. A developer reading the docs would learn the wrong pattern.
  - **Proposal:** Add focused stories + a short MDX recipe page: (1) LongPress 'tap or hold' using the new one-line API (GES-DX-001) with a visible note that keyboard works; (2) SwipeCell with both leading+trailing, a destructive-confirm full-swipe, and an RTL variant; (3) PullToRefresh with an onRefresh that rejects, showing the spinner clears and AT announces. Include a copy-pasteable 'common case' snippet for each at the top of the page so the first thing a dev sees is the correct minimal usage.
- [ ] **`GES-DX-007`** · ergonomics · effort S
  - **Pain:** The family advertises a Telegram-feedback contract (PullToRefresh fires haptics.impact('light') when armed, pull-to-refresh.tsx:125; SwipeCell fires haptics.impact('medium') on full-swipe, swipe-cell.tsx:101), but useLongPress fires no haptics at all (GES-009) and TKSwipeAction can't request feedback when its button is tapped. Feedback is inconsistent across the three siblings, and a consumer who wants a buzz on long-press or on a swipe action has no built-in hook and must reach into the raw Telegram SDK.
  - **Proposal:** Make haptics a first-class, on-by-default-but-overridable option across the family. useLongPress: fire `haptics.impact('medium')` on trigger by default, with `haptics?: false | 'light'|'medium'|'heavy'` to override/disable. TKSwipeAction: add optional `haptic?: 'light'|'medium'|'heavy'|'success'|'warning'` fired on action commit (default by tone, e.g. red→warning). Centralize via the existing useOptionalHaptics so it stays a no-op outside Telegram. Gives a consistent, predictable feedback contract with no extra wiring.
- [ ] **`GES-DX-008`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** useLongPress also breaks legitimate interactions because onContextMenu unconditionally preventDefaults (long-press.tsx:71-74, GES-003), killing right-click and text selection on the wrapped element; and the hook has no way to know whether a press came from touch vs mouse-right-click (GES-002), so a 'long-press' can fire from a held right mouse button. There is no prop to scope or opt out of this suppression, so a consumer wrapping selectable text or a context-menu host is stuck.
  - **Proposal:** Add explicit, well-named options so misuse is impossible by construction: `pointerTypes?: ('touch'|'pen'|'mouse')[]` (default ['touch','pen']) to scope which inputs arm a press, `button?: number` (default primary), and `suppressContextMenu?: boolean | 'touch'` (default 'touch' — only suppress the native callout on touch, leaving mouse right-click and text selection alone). Surface a dev-time console.warn when a press is ignored due to a non-matching pointer type so the misconfiguration is discoverable instead of silent.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

ASSUMPTIONS / COVERAGE GAPS: (1) Zero unit tests and zero stories exist for this family — `find` for *.test.tsx/*.stories.tsx matching gesture/long-press/pull/swipe/drag returned nothing; the only behavioral evidence is the examples/trailhead consumers (GuideDirectory, TripsList, Feed, Train) and the in-source comments. So none of the touch paths are regression-guarded in the kit; the source comment at pull-to-refresh.tsx:73-76 explicitly admits \"the gesture is dead on touch while desktop mouse works (which is why the unit and e2e tests miss it)\" — a self-documented coverage hole. (2) GES-001/003/010 click-after-hold, contextmenu, and pointer-capture behaviors were reasoned from the code and the consumer workaround (GuideDirectory.tsx:52-58, copied into this audit); I did not run a device/Playwright touch session to reproduce, so treat the exact platform timing as an inference. (3) Reduced-motion is partially handled OUTSIDE these files: tokens.css:332 collapses --tk-t2/--tk-ease to 1ms, and both PTR (lines 63/193) and SwipeCell (line 183) use those vars for snap-back, so settle animation honors reduced-motion — BUT only when the subtree is inside a `.tk` scope; nothing in these components scopes themselves, so a consumer who forgets the `.tk` class gets un-reduced transitions. Not filed as a separate finding (it's a kit-wide scoping assumption) but worth a docs note. (4) SSR import safety looks OK: long-press guards `typeof window` (line 43), useVerticalSwipeGuard/useWebApp are SSR-safe, and DOM refs are touched in effects/handlers, not render — no finding there. (5) D-TYPES reviewed: prop/handler types are clean (TKLongPressOptions/Handlers, TKDragState, discriminated-ish action tones) with no stray `any`; the only ergonomic gap is the hook not surfacing a fired/cancel handle (folded into GES-001), so I did not file a standalone types finding. (6) useVerticalSwipeGuard ref-counting (D-TG) was inspected and appears correct under stacked/concurrent consumers and rapid active toggles, so it is intentionally NOT flagged.

---

### Layout — BottomBar, Page, SafeArea (safe areas, viewport)

`LAY` · `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout` · 12 findings (0🔴 3🟠 6🟡 3⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-TG` Telegram runtime & safe-area correctness · `D-PERF` Re-render / scroll performance · `D-A11Y` Accessibility (landmarks, focus, labels) · `D-THEME` Tokens & theming · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView, landscape & RTL · `D-API` API ergonomics & escape hatches · `D-STATE` Edge values & keyboard math

#### Findings

- [x] **`LAY-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-PERF` — TKPage re-renders the entire page subtree on every scroll frame, even when nothing reads scrollTop
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/page.tsx:49,76`
  - **Evidence:** const [scrollTop, setScrollTop] = useState(0); ... onScroll={(e) => setScrollTop(e.currentTarget.scrollTop)}
  - **Impact:** Every scroll event calls setState on TKPage, re-rendering header + the (often large/virtualized) content list + footer on each frame. The value is only consumed by a collapsing large TKHeader (header.tsx usePageScrollTop), so for the overwhelmingly common case (plain header, no header, or non-collapsing header) consumers pay a full-tree re-render per scroll tick in a narrow low-end WebView — visible jank on long feeds like trailhead Feed.tsx.
  - **Fix:** Only track scroll when a collapsing header needs it: gate the onScroll handler behind a flag (e.g. derive from header presence or an explicit `trackScroll`/`collapsingHeader` prop), and push scrollTop through a ref + cheap subscription (or rAF-throttle the setState) so the content/footer subtrees don't re-render. Keep the context value stable when unused.
  - **Done when:** Render a TKPage with a non-collapsing header and a render-counting child; scroll the content node and assert the child's render count does not increase. With a collapsing large header, the header still collapses. Add a Playwright perf assertion that scrolling a long list does not re-render list rows.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — page.tsx:49,76 set scrollTop state on every onScroll frame; the value is only consumed by a collapsing large TKHeader (header.tsx:23,36 acts only when collapsible), so for plain/no/non-collapsing headers TKPage re-renders its whole render output per scroll tick for nothing. Content/footer are passed-in props so they may reconcile-bail, but TKPage itself re-renders every frame as claimed.
- [x] **`LAY-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-THEME` — Layout family re-derives safe-area math instead of consuming --tk-safe-*, so consumer/provider token overrides are silently ignored
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/safe-area.tsx:20-24`
  - **Evidence:** return extraPx > 0 ? `calc(max(${EDGE_ENV[edge]}, ${devicePx}px) + ${extraPx}px)` : `max(${EDGE_ENV[edge]}, ${devicePx}px)`;
  - **Impact:** theme.tsx already publishes `--tk-safe-{edge} = max(env(safe-area-inset-{edge}), jsPx)` (theme.tsx:140), and sibling components (TKHeader header.tsx:51, TKTabbar tabbar.tsx:55) read `var(--tk-safe-*)`. tkSafePad/SafeArea/Page/BottomBar instead recompute `max(env, jsPx)` from the hook. Result: (a) a Mini App team that overrides `--tk-safe-bottom` (e.g. to add a brand gutter or zero it in an embedded preview) sees TKHeader/TKTabbar honor it but TKPage/TKBottomBar/TKSafeArea ignore it; (b) two sources of truth drift if the provider's jsPx and the component's hook read at different times. Inconsistent insets across the same screen.
  - **Fix:** Make tkSafePad emit `max(var(--tk-safe-{edge}, env(safe-area-inset-{edge}, 0px)), {devicePx}px)` (or simply `var(--tk-safe-{edge})` + extra), so the layout family reads the same token the provider and header/tabbar already use. One safe-area source of truth.
  - **Done when:** In a story/test set `--tk-safe-bottom: 40px` on the .tk root and assert TKBottomBar/TKPage bottom padding reflects 40px (matching TKTabbar). Grep shows tkSafePad references `--tk-safe-*`.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — theme.tsx:140 publishes --tk-safe-{edge}=max(env,jsPx) and header.tsx:51/tabbar.tsx:55 read var(--tk-safe-*), but tkSafePad (safe-area.tsx:20-24) recomputes max(env,devicePx) from the JS hook and is used by page.tsx:64,93 and bottom-bar.tsx:46. A consumer override of --tk-safe-bottom is honored by header/tabbar but ignored by Page/BottomBar/SafeArea — two sources of truth, as claimed.
- [x] **`LAY-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-RESP` — TKBottomBar and TKPage ignore left/right safe-area insets — content collides with the notch in landscape
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/bottom-bar.tsx:45`
  - **Evidence:** padding: `${paddingTop}px ${paddingX}px`,
  - **Impact:** Horizontal padding is a single symmetric `paddingX` with no `env(safe-area-inset-left/right)` / `--tk-safe-left/right`. In landscape (or on devices with a side cutout / rounded corners), bottom-bar actions and TKPage content (page.tsx:92 `padding`) sit under the notch or rounded corner and get clipped/untappable. Telegram Mini Apps can run fullscreen landscape, so this is a real clipping bug for action buttons.
  - **Fix:** Add the left/right insets to horizontal padding: `paddingLeft: max(var(--tk-safe-left), 0px) + paddingX`, same for right (and the same in TKPage content). Or expose `safeX`/per-edge padding props. Reuse tkSafePad('left'/'right', ...).
  - **Done when:** In a frame that simulates safe-area-inset-left/right (the layout.stories device frame), assert the bottom-bar's first action's left edge is >= the inset, and content does not overlap the simulated cutout. Visual snapshot in landscape.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — bottom-bar.tsx:45 uses symmetric `${paddingTop}px ${paddingX}px` with no env(safe-area-inset-left/right) or --tk-safe-left/right, and page.tsx:92 content padding is likewise symmetric. Only top/bottom insets are handled (tkSafePad), so left/right cutouts in landscape are ignored as described.
- [x] **`LAY-004`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — TKPage scroll region is tabIndex=0 with no accessible name and no scrollability check
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/page.tsx:73-85`
  - **Evidence:** <div tabIndex={0} data-tk-page-scroll onScroll={...} style={{ flex: 1, minHeight: 0, overflowY: "auto", ... }}>
  - **Impact:** The scroll container is unconditionally focusable but has no role or aria-label, so VoiceOver/TalkBack announce an unnamed focusable group and add a keyboard tab stop even when content isn't scrollable. Screen-reader users hit a silent, purposeless stop on every page; there's no `aria-label`/`region` to describe it. (Per WAI, a scrollable region should be a labelled region only when it actually scrolls.)
  - **Fix:** Drop the static tabIndex=0 (or apply it only when the content actually overflows, via a ResizeObserver) and add `role="region"` + an `aria-label` prop (e.g. `scrollLabel`) when focusable. At minimum expose a prop to set the label.
  - **Done when:** axe/Storybook a11y check on TKPage passes with no 'focusable element without accessible name' or redundant-tabstop violation; a non-overflowing page has no extra tab stop.
- [x] **`LAY-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — TKBottomBar emits a bare div — no landmark/role, so pinned actions aren't a discoverable region
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/bottom-bar.tsx:36-49`
  - **Evidence:** <div className={className} data-testid={testId} style={{ background: blur ? "var(--tk-glass)" : "var(--tk-bg)", ... }}>{children}</div>
  - **Impact:** The bottom action bar (typically the primary CTA / totals) renders as an anonymous div with no `role` (e.g. `contentinfo`/`toolbar`/`region`) and no way to pass aria attributes through. Assistive-tech users get no landmark to jump to the primary action, and there's no prop to add one — they'd have to wrap it.
  - **Fix:** Default to a meaningful element/role (e.g. render a `<footer>` or accept `role`/`aria-label`) and spread arbitrary aria-* via a `...rest` or explicit `role`/`aria-label` props. Same treatment for TKSafeArea's wrapper div.
  - **Done when:** TKBottomBar exposes role/aria-label props (or a sensible default landmark); a test asserts the rendered node has the landmark role and the label is announced.
- [x] **`LAY-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — No ref forwarding on any layout component — consumers can't reach the scroll node or measure the bar
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/page.tsx:33-44`
  - **Evidence:** export function TKPage({ header, footer, children, padding = 16, ... }: TKPageProps) { ... } // plain function component, no ref param
  - **Impact:** None of TKPage/TKBottomBar/TKSafeArea forward a ref. A Mini App team that needs to programmatically scroll the page (scroll-to-top on tab re-tap, restore scroll position, scroll a newly-added item into view) or measure the bottom bar height (to offset a floating element) cannot get the DOM node — they must query `[data-tk-page-scroll]` by hand or wrap in another div. The scroll node is the most-needed handle and it's unreachable.
  - **Fix:** Wrap each component in forwardRef. For TKPage, forward a ref to the scroll container (or expose an imperative handle with `scrollTo`/`scrollTop`) since that's the useful target; for BottomBar/SafeArea forward to the root div.
  - **Done when:** A test mounts TKPage with a ref and asserts `ref.current` is the scrollable element (or imperative handle exposes scrollTo); calling it changes scrollTop.
- [x] **`LAY-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-THEME` — backdrop-filter blur has no @supports fallback — text on a translucent --tk-glass with no blur is unreadable
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/bottom-bar.tsx:41-43`
  - **Evidence:** background: blur ? "var(--tk-glass)" : "var(--tk-bg)", backdropFilter: blur ? "blur(14px)" : undefined, WebkitBackdropFilter: blur ? "blur(14px)" : undefined,
  - **Impact:** When blur=true, the background is the translucent `--tk-glass` (rgba(255,255,255,.72)) and relies on backdrop-filter to separate the bar from scrolling content. In WebViews where backdrop-filter is unsupported or disabled (older Android WebView, or users with prefers-reduced-transparency / GPU-blocked), the bar is a flat 72%-opacity panel with content bleeding through behind the CTA — a contrast/legibility failure for the primary action. There is no solid fallback and no honoring of reduced-transparency.
  - **Fix:** Provide an opaque fallback when blur isn't applied: either gate `--tk-glass` behind `@supports (backdrop-filter: blur(1px))` with a solid `--tk-bg` fallback, or render a solid bg under prefers-reduced-transparency. Add a CSS class instead of inline so `@supports`/media queries can override.
  - **Done when:** Under a simulated no-backdrop-filter environment (or prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce) the bottom bar background computes to an opaque color; contrast of CTA text over the bar passes AA.
- [x] **`LAY-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-RESP` — TKSafeArea uses physical left/right env() that don't flip for RTL locales
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/safe-area.tsx:6-18`
  - **Evidence:** const EDGE_ENV = { ... left: "env(safe-area-inset-left, 0px)", right: "env(safe-area-inset-right, 0px)" }; const EDGE_PADDING = { ... left: "paddingLeft", right: "paddingRight" };
  - **Impact:** `left`/`right` map to physical paddingLeft/paddingRight. The kit ships i18n (foundation/i18n.tsx) and targets RTL locales, but there's no `dir`/logical-property handling. While device insets are physically symmetric in most cases, a consumer passing `edges={['left']}` to clear a notch reasons in logical terms and gets the wrong side in RTL; there's also no inline-start/inline-end vocabulary. Minor but a latent i18n footgun for a published kit.
  - **Fix:** Either document that edges are physical, or add logical-edge support (paddingInlineStart/End) and map env() with `dir` awareness; at minimum expose start/end aliases.
  - **Done when:** A test rendering TKSafeArea edges=['left'] inside a `dir="rtl"` tree pads the visually-correct (start) side, or docs explicitly state edges are physical and a start/end variant exists.
- [ ] **`LAY-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — TKPage shrinks height by absolute keyboard px against a percentage-height box, only when a footer is present
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/page.tsx:59`
  - **Evidence:** height: footer && keyboard.visible ? `calc(100% - ${keyboard.height}px)` : "100%",
  - **Impact:** useKeyboard reports `keyboard.height = window.innerHeight - visualViewport.height` (device.ts:34), an absolute px relative to the full window. TKPage is `height:100%` of its parent. If the parent is not exactly innerHeight (TKProvider sets position:relative but no height; an app shell may letterbox or pad it), subtracting an absolute keyboard height from a percentage box over/under-shrinks, leaving the footer (write bar) either floating above the keyboard or hidden behind it. Also, scroll-only pages (no footer) get NO keyboard handling at all (the ternary requires footer), so a focused input in a footer-less TKPage can sit under the keyboard. Assumption: parent height == innerHeight in the happy path, which examples satisfy but external apps may not.
  - **Fix:** Drive keyboard avoidance off the same viewport basis the height is in (e.g. use 100dvh / visualViewport-derived height, or apply the keyboard offset as paddingBottom on the scroll content rather than shrinking a percentage box), and handle the footer-less case so focused inputs stay visible. Document the parent-must-be-full-height assumption if kept.
  - **Done when:** With a non-full-height parent and keyboard open, the footer stays flush above the keyboard; a footer-less TKPage scrolls a focused input above the keyboard. Playwright keyboard-open snapshot matches in both cases.
- [x] **`LAY-009`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-STATE` — tkSafePad does not clamp devicePx/extraPx — negative or NaN props produce invalid CSS
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/safe-area.tsx:20-24`
  - **Evidence:** export function tkSafePad(edge, devicePx, extraPx = 0): string { return extraPx > 0 ? `calc(max(${EDGE_ENV[edge]}, ${devicePx}px) + ${extraPx}px)` : `max(${EDGE_ENV[edge]}, ${devicePx}px)`; }
  - **Impact:** BottomBar passes consumer `paddingBottom`/`paddingTop`/`paddingX` and TKPage passes `padding` straight through as devicePx/extraPx with no validation. A negative `paddingBottom` (or `padding={NaN}` from an unguarded calc) yields `calc(max(env(...), -10px) + ...)` / `max(env(...), NaNpx)`, which silently breaks the inset clamp (negative loses the max guard; NaN voids the whole declaration), removing safe-area protection with no warning. Assumption: consumers occasionally compute these dynamically.
  - **Fix:** Clamp devicePx/extraPx to `Math.max(0, value)` (and guard NaN) inside tkSafePad, or clamp the props at the component boundary. Optionally dev-warn on negative input.
  - **Done when:** Unit test: tkSafePad('bottom', -5, -3) returns a string with no negative px and preserves the env() floor; TKBottomBar paddingBottom={-10} still clears the home indicator.
- [ ] **`LAY-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-TG` — TKPage relies solely on overscroll-behavior:contain to guard the swipe-to-minimize gesture; no disableVerticalSwipes hook
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/page.tsx:82-84`
  - **Evidence:** // Keep an overscroll at the top/bottom edge from chaining to the body, which Telegram reads as the swipe-down-to-minimize gesture. overscrollBehavior: "contain",
  - **Impact:** `overscroll-behavior: contain` stops scroll *chaining*, but in several Telegram clients the swipe-down-to-minimize gesture fires on a top-edge drag regardless of overscroll containment unless the app calls `WebApp.disableVerticalSwipes()`. The kit exposes `useVerticalSwipes()` (device.ts:413) but TKPage never wires it, so a user pulling down at the top of a TKPage feed can still accidentally minimize/close the Mini App. Assumption: contain alone is insufficient on iOS Telegram per known platform behavior.
  - **Fix:** Offer an opt-in (e.g. `guardVerticalSwipe`/`lockSwipeToClose` prop) that calls disableVerticalSwipes while the page is mounted/scrolled, restoring on unmount — composing the existing useVerticalSwipes hook. Document the overscroll-only limitation.
  - **Done when:** With the guard enabled, a top-edge pull-down inside TKPage does not trigger Telegram minimize (verified against mock webApp's enable/disableVerticalSwipes call), and the original swipe state is restored on unmount.
- [ ] **`LAY-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-API` — TKPage double-counts bottom safe area is avoided only by an implicit footer flag; safeBottom is silently ignored whenever any footer is passed
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/layout/page.tsx:93`
  - **Evidence:** paddingBottom: !footer && safeBottom ? tkSafePad("bottom", bottom, padding) : padding,
  - **Impact:** Bottom safe-area padding on the content is applied only when there is no footer; the assumption is that any footer clears the home indicator itself (true for TKBottomBar/TKTabbar/TKWriteBar). But a consumer passing a *custom* footer that does NOT pad the safe area gets content/footer flush against the home indicator with no way to re-enable safeBottom (the prop is dead while a footer exists). The coupling is implicit and undocumented in the prop's JSDoc beyond 'when there is no footer'.
  - **Fix:** Decouple: let `safeBottom` still apply the inset to the footer wrapper (or content) when the footer opts out, or expose `footerSafeArea`/allow safeBottom to win. At minimum, document that custom footers must handle their own safe area.
  - **Done when:** A TKPage with a custom non-safe footer and safeBottom renders the footer clearing the home indicator (or docs/types make the contract explicit); existing TKBottomBar footer does not double-pad.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`LAY-DX-001`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** The single most common Mini App page — scroll + one pinned primary action that clears the home indicator — takes a 3-component dance every time. In examples/trailhead the funnel ships a hand-rolled `PrimaryAction` wrapper (examples/trailhead/src/components/PrimaryAction.tsx) that itself wraps `TKBottomBar` + `TKMainButton`, and every checkout/detail screen (Checkout.tsx:248, DateSlot.tsx:94, ExperienceDetail.tsx:74) re-passes a JSX `footer={<PrimaryAction .../>}`. `TKBottomBar` is literally never used directly in either example except through that wrapper. The common case is not one line.
  - **Proposal:** Add a first-class `primaryAction` convenience to TKPage that renders a correctly-insetted bottom bar around a single CTA so the 90% case is declarative: ```tsx <TKPage primaryAction={{ label: t('checkout.pay'), onClick: pay, disabled: !ready }}> … </TKPage> ``` It expands internally to `footer={<TKBottomBar><TKMainButton {...primaryAction}/></TKBottomBar>}`, keeps `footer` as the escape hatch for custom bars, and (when `useHasNativeChrome()` is true) defers to the native MainButton automatically — folding the trailhead wrapper's branching logic into the kit. `footer` and `primaryAction` are mutually exclusive (typed union).
- [ ] **`LAY-DX-002`** · composition · effort S
  - **Pain:** Consumers who need the live scroll position (collapsing header, scroll-to-top FAB, ScrollSaver) have no public hook. The kit ships `usePageScrollContext`/`usePageScrollTop` only as an *internal* (packages/uikit/src/internal/pageScroll.ts) and the value is only provided to the header slot subtree (page.tsx:69), not to `children`. So a consumer rendering a 'back to top' button inside the page body literally cannot read scrollTop without re-attaching their own onScroll to a node they also can't reach (no ref — LAY-006). The escape hatch is missing entirely.
  - **Proposal:** Promote `usePageScrollTop()` to a public export and wrap the *entire* TKPage tree (header + scroll body + footer) in `TKPageScrollContext.Provider`, not just the header. Then `const top = usePageScrollTop()` works anywhere under a `<TKPage>`. Pair it with an `onScrollChange?: (top: number) => void` prop on TKPage for consumers who want the value in their own state without a context read. Document both in the layout story.
- [ ] **`LAY-DX-003`** · ergonomics · effort S
  - **Pain:** `tkSafePad` — the kit's own helper for `max(env(safe-area-inset-X), jsPx)` math — is NOT publicly exported (absent from composites/layout.tsx barrel and src index). The result: consumers who need to inset a custom element fall back to hand-writing `paddingBottom: 'calc(var(--tk-safe-bottom, 0px) + 8px)'` literally inline (examples/trailhead/src/features/discover/Checkout.tsx:283, and 3 more `var(--tk-safe-*)` sites across examples). Each is a chance to forget the `max()`, the fallback `0px`, or the right edge. `TKSafeArea` exists for exactly this but is used zero times in either example — too heavy (it forces an extra wrapper div) for 'just pad this one edge'.
  - **Proposal:** Export a tiny, copy-pasteable utility set as public API: ```tsx export const tkSafe = (edge, extra = 0) => `calc(var(--tk-safe-${edge}, 0px) + ${extra}px)`; // usage: style={{ paddingBottom: tkSafe('bottom', 8) }} ``` It reads the same `--tk-safe-*` vars the theme already emits (foundation/theme.tsx:138) so provider overrides are respected (ties into LAY-002), needs no wrapper div, and replaces every raw `var(--tk-safe-*)` literal. Keep `TKSafeArea` for the wrapper case; this is the inline-style ergonomic path.
- [ ] **`LAY-DX-004`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** A consumer cannot ship an accessible layout even if they try. `TKBottomBar` emits a bare `<div>` with no landmark (bottom-bar.tsx:36) so a screen-reader user gets no 'pinned actions' region; the TKPage scroll node is `tabIndex={0}` with no accessible name (page.tsx:74). Nothing in the prop API lets the consumer fix this — there's no `aria-label`, no `role` passthrough, no way to opt the bar into a `contentinfo`/`region` landmark. a11y is impossible-by-default, not just off-by-default.
  - **Proposal:** Bake correct semantics in and expose the labels as props: `TKBottomBar` renders `<footer role="contentinfo">` (or `role="region"` + required `aria-label` when nested), and TKPage's scroll region gets `role="region"` with an `aria-label` defaulting to a sensible value, overridable via a new `scrollLabel?: string` prop. Add `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby` passthrough to both. Consumers get accessible output with zero extra props, and a clear knob when they need to name the region.
- [ ] **`LAY-DX-005`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** The keyboard/swipe behavior that makes a Mini App page feel native is entirely implicit and unconfigurable. TKPage hard-codes `overscrollBehavior: 'contain'` (page.tsx:84) as its only swipe-to-minimize guard, never calls the kit's own `useVerticalSwipeGuard` (which already exists at internal/useVerticalSwipeGuard.ts), and the keyboard-shrink only fires when a `footer` is present (page.tsx:59). A consumer building a focused search/feed page has no prop to say 'this page owns the vertical axis, suppress Telegram's swipe-to-close' — they'd have to import an internal hook and wire it by hand.
  - **Proposal:** Add two intent-level props to TKPage: ```tsx disableVerticalSwipes?: boolean; // wires useVerticalSwipeGuard(true) — native gesture suppressed while mounted keyboard?: 'resize' | 'overlay' | 'none'; // explicit keyboard strategy, default 'resize' ``` This turns the brittle implicit `footer && keyboard.visible` heuristic (and the footer-coupled `safeBottom` swallowing — LAY-012) into a declared contract, and gives consumers the swipe guard as one boolean instead of an internal-hook import.
- [ ] **`LAY-DX-006`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** No layout component forwards a ref or supports an `asChild`/polymorphic escape hatch, while the rest of the kit forwards refs in 55 places (atoms/buttons, controls, inputs all do). So `TKBottomBar` can never *be* a `<form>` (checkout bars routinely want this), and consumers can't measure the bar's height to offset a FAB or reach the scroll node to call `scrollTo`. The layout family is the one place in the kit where the ref/composition convention silently breaks, which is surprising and forces wrapper divs.
  - **Proposal:** Adopt the kit's existing convention: `forwardRef` on all three components (TKPage's ref points at the scroll node so `ref.current.scrollTo(...)` works; TKBottomBar/TKSafeArea forward to the root). Add a minimal `as?: ElementType` (or `asChild`) to TKBottomBar so `<TKBottomBar as="form" onSubmit={...}>` is possible without a nested element. Matches buttons/inputs so it's the obvious, learnable pattern.
- [ ] **`LAY-DX-007`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** Storybook covers only the two happy paths — `PageShell` and `SafeArea` (packages/uikit/storybook/foundation/layout.stories.tsx:62,86) — both inside a faux device frame. There's no story for the patterns developers actually hit and get wrong: a keyboard-open page, a landscape/notch (left-right inset) page, RTL, a scroll-only page with no footer, or a translucent bar with the blur fallback. A dev copy-pasting from Storybook has no canonical 'page with a sticky CTA that clears the keyboard' to crib from, so they reinvent it (and the trailhead PrimaryAction wrapper is the proof).
  - **Proposal:** Add a `Layout/Recipes` story group with copy-pasteable, captioned cases: (1) Scroll + sticky CTA over keyboard; (2) Landscape with left/right notch insets; (3) RTL safe-area; (4) Scroll-only page (safeBottom honored); (5) Translucent bar in a `@supports`-no-blur sandbox so the unreadable-text failure is visible.
