Each story's source doubles as the recipe a consumer pastes. Cross-link from README's layout section.
- [ ] **`LAY-DX-008`** · types · effort L
  - **Pain:** TKPage's slot/inset props are independent booleans/nodes with subtle, undocumented coupling that only the JSDoc warns about: `safeTop` is silently ignored when `header` is set (page.tsx:64), `safeBottom` is silently ignored when any `footer` is passed (page.tsx:93), and keyboard-shrink only happens with a footer (page.tsx:59). The type system permits `<TKPage header={h} safeTop footer={f} safeBottom keyboard-shrink>` even though half those props are dead in that combination. Misuse is invisible at the type and runtime level — nothing tells the developer their `safeBottom` did nothing.
  - **Proposal:** Model the inset ownership explicitly instead of via implicit flag interactions. Introduce a discriminated `insets?: { top?: 'auto' | 'off'; bottom?: 'auto' | 'off' }` where `'auto'` means 'pad unless an adjacent pinned slot already owns it' — making the header/footer-owns-the-inset rule a documented value, not a hidden branch. In dev builds, `console.warn` when a passed inset prop is overridden by a slot (e.g. `safeBottom` + `footer`) so the no-op is discoverable. Keep the current boolean props as deprecated aliases for one minor.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

Coverage/assumptions: (1) No unit/story coverage exists for the layout family's behavior beyond layout-reorg.test.tsx (export wiring + a smoke render) and a visual layout.stories.tsx; there are zero tests for keyboard avoidance, footer/safeBottom interaction, RTL, landscape insets, or scroll-driven re-render — most findings above are untested behaviors. (2) useSafeArea/useKeyboard live in the peer package @tg-mini-app/telegram (packages/telegram/src/layout.ts:74, device.ts:27); I read their source. (3) LAY-010 and LAY-011 rest on documented Telegram client behavior (percentage-vs-absolute height mismatch; swipe-to-minimize firing despite overscroll:contain) that I could not exercise in a real client here — flagged as assumptions. (4) SSR import safety is fine for this family: components only read hooks (no top-level window access); useKeyboard/useSafeArea guard for undefined window/visualViewport. (5) Runtime-absent fallback is correct: useSafeArea returns ZERO_INSET and tkSafePad's env() floor still applies, so a plain browser degrades gracefully. (6) No D-SEC issues found — no dangerouslySetInnerHTML, file inputs, or PII surfaces in this family. (7) D-TYPES is clean: props are well-typed, no stray any, JSDoc present; the only type gap is the missing ref types (LAY-006). (8) The TKPageScrollContext provider only wraps the header (page.tsx:69-71), so content/footer do not subscribe — but the setState in LAY-001 still re-renders the whole TKPage component, which is the actual perf cost.

---

### Lists — Accordion, Cell, InfiniteList, ListGroup, VirtualList

`LST` · `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists` · 12 findings (0🔴 5🟠 6🟡 1⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility · `D-TG` Telegram runtime fit · `D-STATE` State & edge values · `D-API` API ergonomics & footguns · `D-TYPES` TypeScript surface · `D-PERF` Performance / virtualization · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView / i18n · `D-THEME` Tokens & theming

#### Findings

- [x] **`LST-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-TG` — VirtualList requires a fixed pixel viewport height, so the real Telegram page-scroll pattern forces consumers to disable windowing
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/virtual-list.tsx:24-31, 103-114`
  - **Evidence:** `/** Viewport height, px. */ height: number;` … `style={{ height, overflowY: "auto", ... }}` — the only height source is the `height` prop and the scroll container owns its own `overflowY:auto`.
  - **Impact:** A Mini App's natural layout is a single page-level scroller under Telegram's safe-area/viewport. To keep VirtualList inside that scroll, consumers must pass the FULL content height, which defeats windowing entirely. The repo's own trailhead demo does exactly this: `height={people.length * 98}` (examples/trailhead/.../GuideDirectory.tsx:183), rendering every row. The component's headline benefit ('windowed list for weak WebViews') is unusable without a nested fixed-height scroll area that fights Telegram's vertical-swipe-to-minimize.
  - **Fix:** Offer a `window`/scroll-parent mode: accept an optional `scrollParentRef` (or default to `window`) and compute the visible window from the parent's scroll offset and the element's offsetTop, instead of owning a fixed-height inner scroller. Keep the fixed-height mode as an opt-in.
  - **Done when:** A story/e2e places VirtualList in a page-level scroller with `height` unset; scrolling the page renders only ~viewport+overscan rows (assert rendered row count << items.length), and trailhead's GuideDirectory no longer needs `height={people.length*98}`.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — virtual-list.tsx:25 makes the `height` prop the only viewport source and :114 gives the container its own `overflowY:auto`; `viewport` seeds from `height` and `visible=ceil(viewport/itemHeight)+...`, so the trailhead demo's `height={people.length*98}` (GuideDirectory.tsx:183) renders every row — windowing is defeated exactly as claimed.
- [x] **`LST-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-TYPES` — TKCell implementation is typed as Record<string, unknown> and double-cast through `never`, erasing all prop type-checking
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/cell.tsx:82-83, 100, 192-194`
  - **Evidence:** `}: TKCellOwnProps & { as?: ElementType; className?: string } & Record<string, unknown>,` … `ref={ref as never}` … `forwardRef(TKCellImpl as never) as unknown as <T extends ElementType="div">(...)`.
  - **Impact:** Because the impl params include `Record<string, unknown>`, the public polymorphic cast is the ONLY thing typing the component. Any internal regression to the prop spread or rest handling will not be caught by tsc, and inside the impl `rest` is `unknown`-valued so spreading it onto a DOM node is unchecked. Consumers get correct types only by luck of the outer cast; maintainers editing the impl get no safety net — a classic place for a silently-wrong `aria-*`/`href` to slip through.
  - **Fix:** Type the impl with a concrete `TKCellOwnProps & { as?: ElementType; className?: string } & ComponentPropsWithoutRef<ElementType>` (or a small internal generic) and drop `Record<string, unknown>`. Replace `as never` casts with a single well-typed `forwardRef<HTMLElement, TKCellOwnProps & {as?:ElementType}>` wrapper.
  - **Done when:** `Record<string, unknown>` and the `as never`/`as unknown as` chain are gone from cell.tsx; `tsc --noEmit` passes; a deliberately wrong prop inside the impl (e.g. `aria-foo={123}`) now fails typecheck.
  - **Verify:** ❌ refuted — The cited casts (`Record<string,unknown>`, `ref as never`, `as never`/`as unknown as` at cell.tsx:82,100,192) exist, but consumers ARE fully typed by the public `TKCellProps<T>` polymorphic signature (polymorphic.ts:7) — this is the standard react polymorphic-forwardRef pattern, not 'type-checking erased'; the claimed consumer-facing impact is false.
- [x] **`LST-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — TKCell drops falsy `value` and `badge` (value={0}, value="" render nothing)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/cell.tsx:162-165`
  - **Evidence:** `{badge ? <TKBadge tone="red">{badge}</TKBadge> : null}` and `{value ? (<span ...>{value}</span>) : null}`.
  - **Impact:** A settings/stats row that legitimately shows `value={0}` (count, balance, '0 unread'), `value=""`, or `badge={0}` renders blank instead of the value. For a list library whose primary job is key/value rows this is a real data-loss footgun — the consumer sees the row but the trailing number vanishes only when it happens to be zero/empty, the hardest case to notice in QA.
  - **Fix:** Use `value != null` / `badge != null` nullish checks instead of truthiness so 0 and empty string render. Keep `null`/`undefined` as the 'absent' sentinel.
  - **Done when:** `render(<TKCell title="x" value={0} badge={0} />)` shows '0' for both; a test asserts the value span and badge are present when value/badge are `0` and `""`.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — cell.tsx:162 `{badge ? ...}` and :163 `{value ? ...}` use truthiness, so `value={0}`, `value=""`, and `badge={0}` render null instead of the value — a real falsy-data footgun for key/value rows.
- [x] **`LST-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — InfiniteList never announces loading/new-content to screen readers (no live region, no aria-busy)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/infinite-list.tsx:119-127`
  - **Evidence:** `return (<div data-testid={testId} style={style}>{children}{hasMore ? (<div ref={sentinelRef} data-tk-sentinel ...>{loader ?? <span className="tk-skel" .../>}</div>) : null}</div>);` — the loader is a bare visual skeleton; no `aria-live`, `role="status"`, or `aria-busy`.
  - **Impact:** For a VoiceOver/TalkBack user in a Telegram feed, infinite-appended items arrive with zero announcement and the 'loading more' state is invisible to AT — the list appears frozen. This is a core a11y gap for the one component whose whole purpose is async content growth, and it will fail any Playwright a11y gate that checks for live status on async loaders.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the sentinel/loader in `role="status" aria-live="polite"` and set `aria-busy={loading}` on the list root (or render a visually-hidden 'Loading more…' string, ideally via an i18n-able `loadingLabel` prop). Announce a count delta when children grow if feasible.
  - **Done when:** axe/Playwright a11y check passes; toggling `loading` exposes an `aria-busy`/live-region status; a unit test asserts `role="status"`/`aria-live` is present on the loader while `hasMore`.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — infinite-list.tsx:119-126 wraps children plus a bare visual skeleton sentinel; grep for aria-live/aria-busy/role="status" across the lists dir returns nothing, so loading/new-content is never announced to AT.
- [x] **`LST-005`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-PERF` — VirtualList keys rows by absolute index, remounting every visible row on each window shift
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/virtual-list.tsx:117-124`
  - **Evidence:** `{slice.map((item, i) => (<div key={first + i} ...>{renderItem(item, first + i)}</div>))}`
  - **Impact:** `first` changes as you scroll, so the React keys of the visible rows change every time the window shifts — React unmounts/remounts row subtrees instead of repositioning them. Any row with internal state (an expanded swipe-cell, a focused input, an image mid-decode, a CSS press animation) is destroyed mid-scroll, and the 'weak WebView' target pays the full mount cost each frame — the opposite of virtualization's intent.
  - **Fix:** Key by stable item identity, not position: accept an optional `getKey?: (item, index) => string` (default to a stable id field), and only fall back to index when no key is derivable. Render rows keyed by item identity so they reposition rather than remount.
  - **Done when:** With a `getKey`, scrolling does not remount a row that stays in-window (assert a row component's mount-count stays 1 across a scroll that keeps it visible); focus/animation in a row survives a scroll.
  - **Verify:** ❌ refuted — Key `first+i` (virtual-list.tsx:119) equals the item's ABSOLUTE index since `slice[i]===items[first+i]`; a given item keeps the same key across window shifts, so React repositions rather than remounts. The claim that visible-row keys change every shift and rows remount each frame is false.
- [x] **`LST-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-RESP` — Cell and Accordion hard-clamp every title/subtitle to one nowrap-ellipsis line with no wrap/clamp opt-out
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/cell.tsx:136-160`
  - **Evidence:** Cell title/subtitle both use `whiteSpace: "nowrap", overflow: "hidden", textOverflow: "ellipsis"` inline; Accordion repeats the identical clamp for `item.title`/`item.subtitle` (accordion.tsx:132-149). No prop gates it.
  - **Impact:** In a narrow Telegram WebView, longer-language strings (German/Russian/Finnish), a two-line setting label, or RTL text are silently truncated to one line with no way to opt into wrapping or a 2-line clamp. Because the styles are inline, a consumer cannot even override via className/CSS without `!important`. This is a hard i18n/responsive ceiling baked into the two most-used rows.
  - **Fix:** Add a `wrap`/`lines` prop (or expose `titleProps`/CSS hooks) that switches title/subtitle to multi-line or a `-webkit-line-clamp` clamp; default to current single-line behavior. Move the clamp to a CSS class so consumer CSS can override it.
  - **Done when:** `<TKCell title={longString} wrap />` (or lines={2}) renders multiple lines without ellipsis; a test asserts the wrapping style is applied and the default stays single-line.
- [x] **`LST-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — Cell conflates clickable affordance with interactivity: chevron-only and link cells get press feedback but no button role/keyboard
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/cell.tsx:88-106`
  - **Evidence:** `const actionable = Boolean(onClick) && Tag !== "a" && !hasToggle;` … `const tappable = Boolean(onClick) || Tag === "a" || Boolean(chevron);` … `role={actionable ? "button" : undefined} tabIndex={actionable ? 0 : undefined}`.
  - **Impact:** A `<TKCell chevron />` with no onClick gets the `tk-cell-tap` :active/hover background (it LOOKS tappable) but has no role, no tabIndex, and no key handler — a misleading affordance that does nothing for pointer or keyboard users. Conversely a row with both `onClick` and a `toggle` becomes non-`actionable` (correctly, to avoid nested interactive), so its `onClick` silently loses keyboard activation. The matrix of onClick × as × chevron × toggle is a guess-and-check footgun.
  - **Fix:** Decouple visual press feedback from interactivity: only apply `tk-cell-tap` when the row is actually actionable (onClick/link), not for a decorative chevron. Document/validate the toggle+onClick combination (warn in dev that the row click is non-keyboard-activatable, or route activation explicitly).
  - **Done when:** `<TKCell chevron />` (no onClick) has no `tk-cell-tap` class and no hover/active background; a test asserts press feedback only appears for actionable/link cells.
- [x] **`LST-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — Accordion grid-rows expand/collapse and chevron rotation ignore prefers-reduced-motion
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/accordion.tsx:159-175`
  - **Evidence:** Chevron: `transform: isOpen ? "rotate(180deg)" : "none", transition: "transform var(--tk-t2) var(--tk-spring)"`; panel: `gridTemplateRows: isOpen ? "1fr" : "0fr", transition: "grid-template-rows var(--tk-t3) var(--tk-ease)"`. No reduced-motion guard.
  - **Impact:** Users with prefers-reduced-motion (vestibular sensitivity) still get the animated grid-row reveal and chevron spin. Since transitions are inline styles, the consumer cannot disable them via CSS media query either. Fails the family's own motion-accessibility bar and any reduced-motion audit.
  - **Fix:** Drive the transitions from a CSS class that wraps the durations in `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce){ transition: none }` (the kit already injects a shared style element pattern in cell.tsx), instead of inline `transition`.
  - **Done when:** Under emulated prefers-reduced-motion, the accordion panel and chevron change state instantly (no transition); verified via a reduced-motion story/e2e or computed-style assertion.
- [x] **`LST-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-PERF` — ListGroup re-wraps and re-keys all children via Children.toArray on every render, breaking child identity and re-cloning
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/list-group.tsx:43-50`
  - **Evidence:** `{Children.toArray(children).map((child, i) => (<div key={isValidElement(child) && child.key != null ? child.key : i}>{i>0 ? <div .../> : null}{child}</div>))}`
  - **Impact:** `Children.toArray` clones each child and PREFIXES its key (e.g. `.$id`), and the wrapper div falls back to positional index `i` whenever a child lacks an explicit key. Reordering/removing rows that rely on index keys causes wrapper remounts (lost state/focus in the wrapped row), and every parent render re-runs the toArray clone over the whole group. For a large grouped list inside a virtualized parent this is avoidable churn and a subtle key-stability bug.
  - **Fix:** Prefer `Children.map` (no key-prefixing clone) and require/encourage stable child keys; only synthesize the separator without re-wrapping in an index-keyed div, or pass the original child key through unprefixed.
  - **Done when:** Reordering keyed children in a ListGroup preserves their DOM/state (assert a wrapped input keeps focus/value across a reorder); no positional-index key remount when children have stable keys.
- [x] **`LST-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-STATE` — Accordion clamps controlled single-mode value in an effect, causing an extra commit and fighting the controller
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/accordion.tsx:62-65`
  - **Evidence:** `const normalizedOpen = !multiple && open.length > 1 ? open.slice(0,1) : open;` … `useEffect(() => { if (!multiple && open.length > 1) setOpen(open.slice(0,1)); }, [multiple, open, setOpen]);`
  - **Impact:** When a consumer drives `value` as controlled (`value={['a','b']}` in single mode), `setOpen` calls `onChange(['a'])` from an effect — the component mutates the consumer's controlled state on its own. If the consumer's reducer doesn't echo that back identically, `open` stays length>1 and the effect re-fires on every render (controlled drift / potential update loop). Even in the benign case it ships an extra render. Clamping should be render-only (already done via `normalizedOpen`), not an effect that calls back into `onChange`.
  - **Fix:** Drop the effect; rely solely on `normalizedOpen` for rendering and clamp inside `toggle`/`onChange` payloads. Never call `setOpen`/`onChange` purely to 'fix' a controlled value the consumer owns.
  - **Done when:** A controlled single-mode Accordion fed `value={['a','b']}` renders only 'a' open, does NOT call onChange on mount, and produces no extra commit/loop (assert onChange call count and render count).
- [ ] **`LST-011`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — VirtualList exposes no list semantics and `inert` on collapsed accordion panels is unsupported in React 18 / older WebViews
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/virtual-list.tsx:108-126`
  - **Evidence:** VirtualList root is a bare scrollable `<div tabIndex={0}>` with absolutely-positioned row `<div>`s and no `role="list"`/`listitem`; Accordion relies on `{...(!isOpen && mounted ? { inert: true } : null)}` (accordion.tsx:181) for focus containment.
  - **Impact:** (1) A virtualized list announces nothing structural to AT — no 'list, N items', no item boundaries — and since only a window is in the DOM, the total count is also invisible (no `aria-setsize`/`aria-rowcount`). (2) `inert` is not in React 18's JSX types and is unsupported in WebViews older than Chromium ~102; on those (still present in some Android Telegram builds) collapsed-but-mounted lazy panels remain focusable/readable, defeating the documented focus guard. Assumption: exact min WebView version not pinned in repo, so treat as a graceful-degradation risk.
  - **Fix:** Add opt-in `role="list"` + per-row `role="listitem"` (and `aria-setsize`/`aria-posinset` from item index/total) to VirtualList. For Accordion, pair `inert` with a fallback (`tabIndex=-1` on focusables or `visibility:hidden` on fully-collapsed panels) and feature-detect/ponyfill `inert` for React 18 type + old-WebView safety.
  - **Done when:** VirtualList rows expose list/listitem with setsize=items.length; a keyboard-tab test on a collapsed lazy accordion panel cannot focus inside it even when `inert` is unsupported (simulate by deleting the attribute).
- [x] **`LST-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-THEME` — Icon chips hardcode color:#fff and the family bakes layout/type into inline styles, bypassing tokens and density knobs
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/lists/cell.tsx:118-133`
  - **Evidence:** Cell icon chip: `background: iconBg, color: "#fff"` with `width:30,height:30,borderRadius:"var(--tk-r-xs)"`; Accordion repeats `color: "#fff"` on its chip (accordion.tsx:119) and `iconBg`/`iconBg` default to `var(--tk-accent)`.
  - **Impact:** The chip glyph is locked to pure white regardless of theme: a light/pale `iconBg` (or a light-on-light Telegram theme) yields white-on-light, an invisible/low-contrast icon, with no token to fix it (it's a literal, not `var(--tk-on-accent)`). More broadly, all spacing/typography is inline, so a consumer cannot retune density/radius/contrast via CSS-var overrides for these rows — they must fork the component.
  - **Fix:** Replace `color:"#fff"` with a semantic `var(--tk-on-accent)` (and let `iconBg` pair with it), and expose the chip's fixed px (size/radius) as tokens. Consider moving structural styles to a CSS class so token/density overrides reach them.
  - **Done when:** Setting a light `iconBg` keeps the glyph legible via the on-accent token; a contrast check on the chip passes in both light and dark themes, and overriding `--tk-on-accent` recolors all chips.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`LST-DX-001`** · types · effort M
  - **Pain:** cell.tsx:61-83,192-194 — TKCell's public signature is TKPolymorphicProps<T,...>, but the impl param is typed `TKCellOwnProps & { as?: ElementType } & Record<string, unknown>` and forwardRef is double-cast through `never`. The Record<string, unknown> swallows everything, so the most common Telegram row (`<TKCell as="a" href={url}>`) gives NO autocomplete and NO error when you mistype `herf`, and `onClick: () => void` rejects the standard `(e) => void` handler. Neither example app uses `as="a"` even though link rows are the canonical pattern — the broken inference made it not worth the friction.
  - **Proposal:** Replace the Record<string,unknown> impl type with the real polymorphic generic and drop the `never` casts so inference flows end-to-end. Sketch: `function TKCellImpl<T extends ElementType = 'div'>(props: TKCellProps<T>, ref: PolymorphicRef<T>)` returning a `forwardRef` typed as `<T extends ElementType='div'>(p: TKCellProps<T> & { ref?: PolymorphicRef<T> }) => ReactElement`. Widen `onClick` to `ComponentPropsWithoutRef<T>['onClick']` so it accepts the event. Result: `as="a" href` typechecks, `as="button" type="submit"` typechecks, typos error. One-line common case stays one line; the escape hatch finally has types.
- [ ] **`LST-DX-002`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** infinite-list.tsx:5-28 + Feed.tsx:359-393 — TKInfiniteList only knows hasMore/loading/loader. The real-world error and end states are not modeled, so the Feed example must thread feed.phase==='page-error' through `hasMore={feed.hasMore && feed.phase !== 'page-error'}` and then render a whole separate `<div data-testid="feed-page-error">` retry block OUTSIDE the list. Every consumer reinvents this, and the subtle 'set hasMore false to stop the retry loop' contract is a footgun (it conflates 'no more data' with 'this page errored').
  - **Proposal:** Add first-class terminal-state props so the common cases are declarative: `error?: ReactNode`, `onRetry?: () => void`, `endMessage?: ReactNode`. When `error` is set the component renders the error row with a built-in Retry button (calls onRetry) IN PLACE of the loader and pauses the observer — no more overloading hasMore. When `hasMore` is false and children exist, render `endMessage`. Sketch: `<TKInfiniteList onLoadMore loading hasMore error={feed.error} onRetry={feed.retry} endMessage={t('feed.end')} />`. Collapses Feed's ~20 lines of hand-rolled error UI into two props.
- [x] **`LST-DX-003`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** virtual-list.tsx:20-31,119 — TKVirtualList forces `itemHeight`, `height`, AND `renderItem` as required props and keys rows by `first + i` (absolute index), with no way to pass a stable key. There is no `getKey`, so consumers cannot fix the remount-on-shift problem even if they want to, and `height` being a required pixel number is exactly the friction red-team logged in LST-001. The minimal call is 4 mandatory props for what should be a near-drop-in for `items.map`.
  - **Proposal:** Add `getKey?: (item: T, index: number) => string | number` and key the absolute row by it (fixes identity; falls back to index only when omitted). Make `height` optional: when absent, default to `height: '100%'` so the list fills its flex parent (the Telegram page-scroll case) and document `height={n}` as the explicit-viewport override. Sketch: `<TKVirtualList items={rows} itemHeight={56} getKey={r => r.id} renderItem={r => <TKCell ... />} />`. Common case drops to 3 props with correct keying by default.
- [ ] **`LST-DX-004`** · composition · effort L
  - **Pain:** accordion.tsx:8-43 — TKAccordion is config-object-only (`items: TKAccordionItem[]` with fixed title/subtitle/icon/content fields). The moment a consumer needs a custom trigger (a badge next to the title, a trailing count, a TKCell-style row, two-line wrapped content) they have no escape hatch and must abandon the component entirely. There is no way to compose, only to fill the slots the array allows.
  - **Proposal:** Keep the `items` fast path but add a compound API for rich cases: `<TKAccordion><TKAccordion.Item value="shipping" trigger={<TKCell title="Delivery" badge={3} />}>{content}</TKAccordion.Item></TKAccordion>`. Item registers with the same controllable open-set context the array path uses, so single/multiple/lazy/value all keep working. The array form lowers to Items internally. Gives an asChild-style escape hatch without a second component.
- [ ] **`LST-DX-005`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** cell.tsx:107-186 + accordion.tsx:95-164 + list-group.tsx:18 — Every layout dimension (padding 11px/14px, icon chip 30x30, gap 12, separatorInset 54) is hardcoded inline. There is no single density knob, so a consumer who wants a compact settings list or comfortable feed rows must override per-cell or fork. ListGroup can't tell its children to match its own compact mode; the inline `color:#fff` icon chip (LST-012) also can't be themed.
  - **Proposal:** Add a `density?: 'comfortable' | 'compact'` prop on TKListGroup/TKAccordion that sets CSS custom props (`--tk-cell-py`, `--tk-cell-chip`, `--tk-cell-gap`) on the group, and have TKCell read those vars with the current values as fallbacks. One prop on the group cascades to every row: `<TKListGroup density="compact">`. Replace `color:#fff` chip text with `var(--tk-on-accent, #fff)` so dark/light icon chips theme correctly. No call-site change for existing default-density users.
- [ ] **`LST-DX-006`** · ergonomics · effort S
  - **Pain:** cell.tsx:46-54,162-164 — A settings row that shows BOTH a numeric value and a toggle, or `value={0}` ("0 unread"), is a daily case yet `value`/`badge` silently vanish on falsy values (the LST-003 bug) and there is no `description`/wrap affordance for the two-line-detail case (LST-006). Consumers reach for `after={...}` and rebuild the trailing layout by hand (Profile.tsx:130-138 wires a raw TKSwitch into `after` instead of using the built-in `toggle`, because the built-in trailing slot ordering isn't ergonomic).
  - **Proposal:** Two small fixes that remove the most common reasons to drop to `after`: (1) render `value`/`badge` on `!= null` instead of truthiness so `value={0}` and `value=""` show; (2) add `wrap?: boolean` (or `lines?: 1 | 2`) on TKCell/Accordion title+subtitle to opt out of the nowrap clamp. Sketch: `<TKCell title="Bio" subtitle={longText} wrap />` and `<TKCell title="Unread" value={0} />`. Makes the obvious code do the obvious thing.
- [ ] **`LST-DX-007`** · a11y · effort S
  - **Pain:** cell.tsx:88-106 — Interactivity is decided by an opaque rule: `actionable = onClick && Tag !== 'a' && !hasToggle`. A chevron-only or `onClick`-on-an-`<a>` row gets the press flash (`tappable`) but no button role/keyboard (LST-007), and there's no way for a consumer to force-correct it. The single most common Telegram navigation row — chevron that pushes a screen — looks tappable but isn't keyboard-operable, and nothing warns the author.
  - **Proposal:** Make the interactive contract explicit and safe by default: when `chevron` and `onClick` are both set on a non-link, treat it as actionable (role=button + tabIndex + Enter/Space) — the visual affordance and the a11y semantics can no longer diverge. Add a dev-only `console.warn` when a row has `onClick` but resolves to non-interactive (e.g. via a bad `as`), telling the author to use `as="button"`/`as="a"`. Consumers literally cannot ship the inaccessible-but-tappable nav row anymore.
- [ ] **`LST-DX-008`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** storybook/composites/lists.stories.tsx:27-79 — The three stories cover only the happy path: a static settings group, a 2-item accordion, and a stub InfiniteList whose `onLoadMore` is a no-op (`() => undefined`). There is NO story for a link row (`as="a"`), the page-error/retry flow, a working paginated InfiniteList, `value={0}`, controlled Accordion, or VirtualList with `getKey` — i.e. exactly the cases consumers get stuck on. There is no docs page enumerating the trailing-slot order (badge → value → toggle → after → chevron) so authors guess.
  - **Proposal:** Add copy-pasteable stories that mirror real friction: 'LinkRows' (`<TKCell as="a" href>`), 'PaginatedFeed' (a real stateful onLoadMore with loading + error + retry), 'ControlledAccordion', and 'VirtualListWithKeys'. Add an MDX/autodocs section documenting the trailing-slot render order and the InfiniteList progress contract as a labeled diagram. Each story should be lift-and-drop usable. Turns the components' tricky parts into starting templates.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

Coverage already strong (do NOT re-report): InfiniteList double-fetch guard, stall/no-progress non-loop, and re-arm-on-children-growth are covered in packages/uikit/test/audit-fixes.test.tsx (#12, M0) and m5-display.test.tsx; Accordion lazy-cache reset on id change is covered in m1-fixes.test.tsx (#8); InfiniteList observer disconnect-on-unmount is covered in m5-display.test.tsx. Cell keyboard activation + switch stopPropagation is covered in coverage-components.test.tsx.\n\nVerified vs assumed: LST-001 grounded in examples/trailhead/.../GuideDirectory.tsx:183 (`height={people.length*98}`) — real consumer defeats windowing. LST-003/006/007/009/010/012 are read directly from source. LST-005 remount claim verified by reading the `key={first+i}` expression (key changes with scroll). For LST-011, the `inert`-unsupported risk is an ASSUMPTION about old Android Telegram WebView versions (repo pins no min Chromium version); the React-18 JSX-type gap for `inert` is factual (it's a React 19 typing). VirtualList `itemHeight={0}` is a soft footgun confirmed by hand (visible=Infinity → renders ALL rows, no crash) — folded into the windowing/perf concerns rather than filed separately to respect the finding cap.\n\nNot deeply audited (out of time/scope): the Storybook stories rendering correctness, the shared `tk-skel` skeleton animation tokens, and ListGroup `separatorInset` interaction with RTL (separator uses `marginLeft` which would not flip under RTL — a likely additional D-RESP finding worth a follow-up).

---

### Navigation — CategoryTabs, Header, PageDots, Segmented, Steps, TabView, Tabbar

`NAV` · `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation` · 12 findings (0🔴 4🟠 5🟡 3⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility & ARIA semantics · `D-STATE` State model & edge values · `D-API` API ergonomics & consistency · `D-TG` Telegram runtime & haptics consistency · `D-EDGE` Robustness: keys, duplicates, races · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView, i18n length & truncation · `D-PERF` Performance & layout thrash

#### Findings

- [x] **`NAV-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — TKTabbar/TKTabView expose tabs as role="navigation" with no tab/tablist semantics, so SRs never announce "tab 2 of 4 selected"
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/tabbar.tsx:45-67`
  - **Evidence:** role="navigation" on the grid, children are plain <button aria-current={on ? "page" : undefined}>. No role="tablist"/role="tab", no aria-selected, and TKTabView panels are <div aria-hidden> with no role="tabpanel"/aria-labelledby.
  - **Impact:** A screen-reader user in a Mini App hears unlabeled buttons inside an unnamed 'navigation' landmark with no position/selection/relationship info; the keep-mounted panels are not tied to their tabs. This is the app's primary chrome, so the whole bottom-tab UX is effectively unlabeled for AT.
  - **Fix:** Either commit to the link-nav pattern (give the <nav> an aria-label like locale.tabs and keep aria-current="page"), or adopt the tabs pattern: role="tablist" on the container, role="tab" + aria-selected + aria-controls on buttons, and role="tabpanel" + aria-labelledby + tabIndex={0} on each TKTabView panel. Pick one and apply it consistently across Tabbar+TabView.
  - **Done when:** An a11y test asserts the tabbar container has an accessible name and that the selected tab exposes aria-selected (or aria-current) and is linked to its panel via aria-controls/labelledby; axe reports no 'tabpanel without tab' or 'landmark without name' violation.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — tabbar.tsx:45 uses role="navigation" with plain buttons (aria-current="page" only, no role=tab/tablist/aria-selected); tab-view.tsx:49-54 panels are <div aria-hidden> with no role="tabpanel"/aria-labelledby tying them to tabs. Note: buttons are NOT 'unlabeled' — each has <span>{tab.label}</span> (tabbar.tsx:109) as accessible name, and bottom-nav-as-landmark is a defensible pattern, but the structural missing tab/panel relationship claim holds.
- [x] **`NAV-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — TKSegmented is a single-select control but renders multiple aria-pressed toggle buttons with no group role or name
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/segmented.tsx:46-82`
  - **Evidence:** Container <div> has no role/aria-label; each option is <button aria-pressed={item.value === val}> with roving tabindex. A single-select segmented control is semantically a radiogroup/tablist, not N independent toggle buttons.
  - **Impact:** SR users hear N 'toggle button, pressed/not pressed' with no 'group', no group name, and no 'X of N' — they cannot tell it is a single-choice control. The roving-tabindex arrow keys (only one tab stop) also conflict with the toggle-button model, where every button should normally be tabbable.
  - **Fix:** Give the container role="radiogroup" (or role="tablist") with an aria-label/aria-labelledby, change each option to role="radio" aria-checked (or role="tab" aria-selected), and drop aria-pressed. The roving tabindex is correct for radiogroup/tablist.
  - **Done when:** Test asserts getByRole('radiogroup') (or 'tablist') exists with a name and exactly one option has aria-checked=true; axe passes; arrow-key roving still moves selection.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — segmented.tsx:47 container <div> has no role/aria-label; each option is <button aria-pressed={item.value===val}> (line 82) with roving tabindex (line 80). A single-select control presented as N independent toggle buttons with no group role/name and one tab stop matches the claim exactly.
- [x] **`NAV-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — TKSteps does not clamp current; out-of-range or negative values break the rail, aria and roving focus
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/steps.tsx:15-29,103`
  - **Evidence:** current is used raw: const done = index < current; const active = index === current; tkTabbableIndex(current, steps.length); and connector width index <= current ? '100%' : '0%'. No Math.min/max guard. examples/trailhead/src/features/train/SessionDetail.tsx:43 passes current={session.done ? session.steps.length : 1}.
  - **Impact:** Driving steps from async data with current === steps.length on completion (as trailhead does) yields active===-1, so NO circle gets aria-current="step" and SR users lose the 'current step' anchor; a negative current (e.g. -1 for 'not started') or current beyond length similarly leaves no active circle while filling connectors. Edge inputs silently corrupt the indicator and focus model.
  - **Fix:** Clamp internally: const cur = Math.max(0, Math.min(current, steps.length - 1)) and define explicit semantics for the 'fully complete' case (current === steps.length → all done, none active). Document that current is 0-based.
  - **Done when:** Tests with current=-1, current=steps.length and current=99 render without an active===-1 gap, keep exactly one tab stop, and connectors fill monotonically; aria-current="step" is present in the in-range cases.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — steps.tsx uses current unclamped: active=index===current (line 30), tkTabbableIndex(current,...) (line 19), connector index<=current (line 103). Trailhead SessionDetail.tsx:43 passes current=steps.length on completion, so no index === current → no circle gets aria-current="step" while all connectors fill. Reproduced as described.
- [x] **`NAV-005`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-API` — TKTabView is fully controlled with required value/onChange, breaking the uncontrolled convention every sibling honors
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/tab-view.tsx:8-22,60`
  - **Evidence:** value: number; onChange: (index: number) => void; are non-optional and passed straight to TKTabbar. Every sibling (Tabbar, Segmented, CategoryTabs, PageDots) uses useControllable with value?/defaultValue?/onChange?.
  - **Impact:** A Mini App team that just wants a self-managing bottom-tab shell is forced to lift state and wire onChange, unlike the bare TKTabbar. Inconsistent with the documented controlled/uncontrolled pattern and there is no defaultValue escape hatch — avoidable boilerplate for the most common shell.
  - **Fix:** Make value optional with a defaultValue and an optional onChange (delegate to TKTabbar's useControllable) so TKTabView works uncontrolled like the rest of the family while still supporting controlled use.
  - **Done when:** A test renders <TKTabView tabs panels defaultValue={1}/> with no value/onChange, taps a tab, and sees the panel switch; TS no longer requires value/onChange.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — tab-view.tsx:9-10 declare value:number and onChange non-optional and forward them raw to TKTabbar (line 60), with no defaultValue. Siblings Tabbar (24), Segmented (16,19), CategoryTabs (19) all use useControllable with optional value?/defaultValue?/onChange?, confirming the inconsistency; severity is partly a convention argument but the code claim is accurate.
- [x] **`NAV-003`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — TKCategoryTabs and TKPageDots have no group role/name and no positional total
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/category-tabs.tsx:36-46`
  - **Evidence:** CategoryTabs container is a bare <div style={{display:'flex',overflowX:'auto'}}> with buttons using aria-current; PageDots container (page-dots.tsx:28-29) is <div style={{display:'flex'}}> with dot buttons. Neither has role="tablist"/aria-label nor positional info; PageDots label is just 'Page {page}'.
  - **Impact:** Category filters and the carousel pager are announced as a loose pile of buttons with no group boundary or name; a Mini App with several CategoryTabs rows gives SR users no way to tell which row they are in. PageDots buttons say 'Page 2' but never 'of 5'.
  - **Fix:** Add role="tablist" + aria-label (new locale key or a passed-in label prop) to both containers, and include the total in the dot label template (e.g. locale.page = 'Page {page} of {total}').
  - **Done when:** Test asserts both containers expose an accessible group name and PageDots aria-label includes the total count; axe reports no ungrouped/unnamed control warning.
- [ ] **`NAV-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — Selection haptics fire on TKSegmented/TKTabbar but are missing on TKCategoryTabs, TKPageDots and TKSteps
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/category-tabs.tsx:53-62`
  - **Evidence:** CategoryTabs onClick={() => setActive(index)} and onKeyDown setActive(next) call no haptics; PageDots (page-dots.tsx:24,40) and Steps (steps.tsx:117) never import useOptionalHaptics. Compare Segmented (segmented.tsx:84 haptics.selection()) and Tabbar (tabbar.tsx:37,71).
  - **Impact:** Inconsistent native feel inside Telegram: tapping the bottom tabs or a segmented control buzzes, but switching category tabs, swiping pager dots or advancing a step is silent. Teams chasing a native feel must wrap these to add haptics, defeating the point of the kit owning the runtime wiring.
  - **Fix:** Add useOptionalHaptics().selection() on tap and arrow-key activation in CategoryTabs, PageDots and Steps (Steps only when onStepClick is provided), matching Segmented/Tabbar. Optionally expose a haptics?:boolean opt-out for parity.
  - **Done when:** Tests with a mock haptics provider assert selectionChanged fires on click and arrow activation for CategoryTabs, PageDots and Steps; runtime-absent (useOptionalHaptics no-op) still renders.
- [x] **`NAV-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — React keys derive from user content (tab.label / option.value), causing collisions and state-bleed on duplicates
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/tabbar.tsx:63`
  - **Evidence:** TKTabbar key={tab.label}; TKSegmented (segmented.tsx:76) key={item.value}; TKCategoryTabs (category-tabs.tsx:47) key={item.value}. Labels/values are arbitrary consumer strings.
  - **Impact:** Two tabs labelled 'More' or two options with the same value (easy with i18n or generated data) collide on key — React drops/merges nodes, the roving-tabindex ref array and the measured Segmented indicator desync, and clicks can land on the wrong panel. This surfaces only in production data, not the demos, so it ships silently.
  - **Fix:** Key by index (Tabbar) or by `${index}-${item.value}` (Segmented/CategoryTabs) so keys are stable and unique regardless of duplicate labels/values; or dev-warn on duplicate values.
  - **Done when:** Test renders a tabbar with two identical labels and a segmented with two equal values; both render the correct number of buttons, arrow-roving visits each, and selecting the second updates the indicator to the second slot.
- [ ] **`NAV-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-RESP` — TKTabbar fixed 46px icon pill + non-truncating caption overflow long i18n labels in a 320px WebView
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/tabbar.tsx:46-49,109`
  - **Evidence:** gridTemplateColumns: repeat(${tabs.length}, 1fr) with per-tab fixed pill width:46 and label <span style={{fontSize:'var(--tk-fz-caption2)'}}> with no truncation. CategoryTabs/Segmented use whiteSpace:'nowrap' (category-tabs.tsx:78, segmented.tsx:107).
  - **Impact:** With 5 tabs and a localized label like 'Einstellungen'/'Уведомления' on a 320px Telegram WebView each column is ~64px; the caption neither truncates nor wraps predictably and collides with neighbors, and nowrap segmented options force horizontal overflow without a scroll affordance. Real i18n apps get a broken bottom bar.
  - **Fix:** Add text-overflow:ellipsis + overflow:hidden + max-width to the tabbar label, and either rely on CategoryTabs' scroll but cap Segmented option width with ellipsis (or document a max sensible option count); consider a 1-line clamp on tab labels.
  - **Done when:** Visual/Playwright test at 320px with long localized labels shows truncated-with-ellipsis tab captions and no horizontal page overflow from Segmented.
- [ ] **`NAV-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-PERF` — TKTabView keeps every panel permanently mounted with no lazy/idle option, paying full render+effect cost for unseen tabs
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/tab-view.tsx:48-57`
  - **Evidence:** panels.map((panel,index) => <div ... style={{display: index===value ? 'block':'none'}}>{panel}</div>) — all panels render eagerly; inactive ones are only display:none. Comment: 'kept mounted ... so their scroll + state survive'.
  - **Impact:** A 4-tab shell mounts all four trees (and runs their data fetches/effects/observers) on first paint, hurting Mini App cold-start TTI inside a constrained WebView. There is no opt-in to defer a heavy tab until first visit while still preserving it afterward.
  - **Fix:** Add a `lazy` (mount-on-first-activate, then keep mounted) and/or `keepMounted?: boolean|number` prop. Default can stay keep-all for back-compat, but give teams the knob.
  - **Done when:** Test asserts that with lazy enabled an unvisited panel's content is not in the DOM until its tab is first selected, and remains mounted after switching away.
- [ ] **`NAV-008`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-EDGE` — Per-index ref arrays are never trimmed when item count shrinks, retaining stale element refs
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/page-dots.tsx:18,33-36`
  - **Evidence:** btnRefs = useRef<(HTMLButtonElement|null)[]>([]); refs assigned by index (ref={el => refs.current[index] = el}) in PageDots, Steps, Segmented, CategoryTabs, Tabbar. Nothing resets refs.current.length when count/items shrink.
  - **Impact:** When a Mini App reduces count (e.g. a carousel that drops a slide) the refs array keeps stale entries for removed indices; arrow-key focus math stays correct (modulo current count), but holding detached DOM nodes is a minor leak and a latent bug if any code iterates refs.current by length.
  - **Fix:** Truncate the array each render (refs.current.length = count) or store refs in a Map keyed by stable id and clean up in the ref callback's cleanup return.
  - **Done when:** Test shrinks count from 5 to 2 and asserts refs.current.length === 2 (no detached nodes retained) and arrow roving still wraps within the 2 remaining items.
- [ ] **`NAV-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-STATE` — TKHeader collapsing silently no-ops without large, and a collapsed large header drops its subtitle
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/header.tsx:33,80-94`
  - **Evidence:** const collapsible = !!collapsing && large === true; — collapsing without large does nothing. In the compact branch the subtitle only renders when !large (line 91: subtitle && !large), so a large collapsing header that collapses shows the title but never the subtitle.
  - **Impact:** A Mini App that sets collapsing on a non-large header sees no collapse and gets no warning; a large header with a subtitle loses the subtitle the moment it collapses into the compact bar, so context (e.g. 'Today') disappears on scroll. Surprising, undocumented prop coupling.
  - **Fix:** Dev-warn (or document loudly) that collapsing requires large; and render the subtitle in the collapsed compact bar (drop the !large guard for the isCollapsed branch) so collapsing large headers keep their subtitle.
  - **Done when:** Test: <TKHeader large collapsing subtitle> after scrolling past the threshold shows both title and subtitle in the collapsed bar; a console warning fires for collapsing without large (or it is documented in the prop JSDoc).
- [ ] **`NAV-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-PERF` — TKCategoryTabs calls scrollIntoView on initial mount, causing an unwanted scroll/jump for non-zero defaultValue
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/navigation/category-tabs.tsx:28-34`
  - **Evidence:** useEffect(() => { refs.current[active]?.scrollIntoView?.({inline:'center', block:'nearest', behavior:...}); }, [active]); runs on mount with the default/controlled active.
  - **Impact:** On first render the effect scrolls the tab row to center the initially-active tab even when it is already visible; with block:'nearest' inside a scrollable page it can also nudge the page. For a non-zero defaultValue this yields a visible jump on load in the Mini App.
  - **Fix:** Skip the first run (mount) with a ref flag, or only scroll when the active button is actually outside the scroller viewport (compare offsetLeft/clientWidth before calling scrollIntoView).
  - **Done when:** Test/visual: mounting CategoryTabs with defaultValue at an already-visible index does not trigger scrollIntoView; selecting an off-screen tab still centers it.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`NAV-DX-001`** · types · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKSegmented.onChange is typed (value: string) => void (segmented.tsx:11), so the literal union of the option values is erased. Every real consumer pays for it with a cast: examples/trailhead/src/features/profile/PlatformLab.tsx casts onChange args three times — `m as TKMotion` (line 142), `s as "light" | "dark"` (line 155), `l as Lang` (line 168). The cast defeats the type checker: a typo in an option value or a stale union member compiles clean and ships a runtime bug.
  - **Proposal:** Make the component generic over the option value so the union flows from `options` into `onChange`/`value`/`defaultValue`: ```ts export function TKSegmented<T extends string = string>(props: { options: ReadonlyArray<T | (TKOptionItem & { value: T })>; value?: NoInfer<T>; defaultValue?: NoInfer<T>; onChange?: (value: T) => void; full?: boolean; testId?: string; }): JSX.Element ``` Now `onChange={(m) => set({ motion: m })}` infers `m: "springy" | "smooth"` with zero casts, and `value={...}` is checked against the same union. Apply the identical generic to TKCategoryTabs (onChange index is fine, but expose `valueOf`/typed items) and keep TKPageDots/TKSteps numeric. Backward compatible: defaulting `T = string` means existing untyped call sites keep working.
- [ ] **`NAV-DX-002`** · ergonomics · effort L
  - **Pain:** TKTabView takes two positionally-correlated arrays — `tabs: TKTabItem[]` and `panels: ReactNode[]` (tab-view.tsx:6-8). The consumer must keep them index-aligned by hand: examples/trailhead/src/App.tsx maps TABS once for `tabItems` (line 45-48) and again for `panels` (line 78), and threads `panelTestId={(index) => TABS[index].id}` (line 79). Insert/reorder one list and the other silently desyncs — a whole tab renders the wrong screen with no type error. There is also no stable per-tab id, which is what NAV-007's key-collision bug ultimately needs.
  - **Proposal:** Accept a single co-located item list so a tab and its panel can never drift apart, and give each item a stable id used for React keys: ```ts interface TKTabViewItem extends TKTabItem { id: string; panel: ReactNode; testId?: string; } <TKTabView items={items} /* value/defaultValue by id */ value={activeId} onChange={setActiveId} /> ``` Keep the current `tabs`+`panels` form as a deprecated overload for one minor. Keying panels and tabbar buttons off `item.id` (not array index / label) also closes the structural side of NAV-007/NAV-008. App.tsx collapses from two parallel `.map`s + a `panelTestId` callback to one `items` array.
- [ ] **`NAV-DX-003`** · composition · effort L
  - **Pain:** Tabs/segments/tabbar/steps always render a hard-coded <button> (e.g. tabbar.tsx:61, segmented.tsx:74, category-tabs.tsx:44, steps.tsx:110). A Mini App that wants a tab to be a real router link (`<a href>` for deep-linkable, right-click-able, middle-click-able navigation) or to wrap an item in an analytics/tooltip component has no escape hatch — they must fork the component. This is the single biggest reason teams stop using a nav kit and hand-roll.
  - **Proposal:** Add a Radix-style `asChild`/`renderItem` escape hatch that lets the consumer supply the interactive element while the kit keeps owning a11y + roving-tabindex wiring: ```tsx <TKTabbar tabs={tabs} renderItem={(child, { index, selected }) => <Link to={routes[index]} aria-current={selected ? 'page' : undefined}>{child}</Link>} /> ``` The kit injects `tabIndex`, `aria-current`, key handlers and ref onto whichever element comes back (mergeProps). Default stays the internal <button>, so nothing breaks. Unlocks real URL-backed tabs without forking.
- [ ] **`NAV-DX-004`** · defaults · effort S
  - **Pain:** TKHeader.collapsing only takes effect when `large === true` (header.tsx:33 `const collapsible = !!collapsing && large === true`). A developer who writes `<TKHeader collapsing title=... />` gets a silent no-op — the most natural reading of the API does nothing, with no warning and no type-level hint. (NAV-011 logs the silent no-op as a bug; the DX fix is to make the common 'I want a big collapsing iOS-style title' case correct by default.)
  - **Proposal:** Make `collapsing` imply `large` so the one-line intent works: derive `const isLarge = large ?? collapsing` and compute `collapsible = !!collapsing`. Then `<TKHeader collapsing title="Trips" />` renders a large collapsing header with no extra prop. Optionally model the three real shapes as one `variant?: 'compact' | 'large' | 'collapsing'` prop (default 'compact') so the impossible combos (collapsing-but-not-large) are unrepresentable in the type. Low effort for the implication; medium if you add the variant union.
- [ ] **`NAV-DX-005`** · perf · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKTabView mounts every panel up front and only hides inactive ones with display:none (tab-view.tsx:48-57). For Trailhead, that means all five tab stacks (Discover/Trips/Train/Guide/Profile in App.tsx:56-62) run their data fetches, effects and renders on first paint, even though the user sees one. The doc comment frames keep-mounted as the only behavior, so a consumer who wants lazy first-paint has to rebuild the shell. (NAV-010 logs the missing lazy option as a perf bug; the DX gap is that the fast path isn't even reachable through the API.)
  - **Proposal:** Add a `mount?: 'eager' | 'lazy' | 'lazy-keep'` prop (default 'eager' to preserve today's behavior): 'lazy' mounts a panel on first visit and unmounts on leave; 'lazy-keep' mounts on first visit and then keeps it mounted (best of both — defers cold tabs' fetch+effect cost but preserves scroll/state once seen). Pairs naturally with NAV-DX-002's `items` (track a Set of visited ids). Lets Trailhead pay first-paint cost for one tab instead of five with a single prop.
- [ ] **`NAV-DX-006`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKSteps only accepts `steps: string[]` plus a scalar `current` (steps.tsx:5-11). There is no way to express a per-step status — an errored step, an optional/skippable step, or a custom description — even though those are the states a multi-step Mini App checkout actually needs. examples/trailhead/src/features/train/SessionDetail.tsx already works around the thin API by computing `current={session.done ? session.steps.length : 1}` (line 43) to fake a 'done' state, because there is no first-class completed/error vocabulary.
  - **Proposal:** Allow a richer item shape alongside the string shorthand, and a derived status callback: ```ts type TKStepItem = string | { label: string; description?: string; status?: 'pending'|'current'|'done'|'error' }; <TKSteps steps={items} current={i} /> // string[] still works ``` When `status` is supplied it overrides the index-vs-current heuristic (so an error step renders red with role/aria-invalid even though it's < current). Internally clamp `current` into [0, n-1] as part of this work, closing NAV-004's out-of-range break. Strings keep the current behavior, so it's additive.
- [ ] **`NAV-DX-007`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** Accessibility is opt-out-by-omission across the family: TKTabbar/TKTabView render role="navigation" with no tab/tablist semantics (NAV-001), TKSegmented emits N aria-pressed toggles with no rad/group name (NAV-002), TKCategoryTabs/TKPageDots have no group role or accessible name (NAV-003). The common thread is that a developer who just passes `tabs`/`options` cannot tell they shipped something a screen reader can't announce — there's no name input and no dev-time signal. This is a DX problem, not only an a11y bug: the correct output should be unavoidable, and misuse should be loud.
  - **Proposal:** Bake the roles in and surface a single `label`/`aria-label` (or `aria-labelledby`) prop on every grouped control so naming the group is one prop, with a dev-only console.warn when it's missing on an interactive (onChange-bearing) instance: ```tsx <TKSegmented label="Theme" .../> // -> role=radiogroup + aria-label, children role=radio <TKCategoryTabs label="Filter" .../> // -> role=tablist + aria-label ``` Ship the role plumbing (tablist/tab + aria-selected for tabs, radiogroup/radio for segmented, group + aria-label + 'n of m' for dots/category) as the default render so consumers cannot ship the inaccessible variant. The new `label` prop is the only surface change and is additive.
- [ ] **`NAV-DX-008`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** Storybook lumps the whole family into 4 combined stories (storybook/composites/navigation.stories.tsx: HeaderAndTabbar, SegmentedAndTabs, StepsAndDots, TabView) and there is a second overlapping 'Composites/Nav' file (nav.stories.tsx) — so a developer browsing the catalog can't find a per-component, copy-pasteable example. None of the stories demonstrate the states that bite in production: controlled vs uncontrolled, keyboard/roving focus, disabled options, long i18n labels in a 320px WebView (NAV-009), error/clamped steps (NAV-004), or the collapsing header (NAV-011). There's no usage doc pairing TKHeader with TKPage's `header` slot even though page.tsx:7-8 expects exactly that.
  - **Proposal:** Split into one CSF story file per component with named, intent-revealing stories (e.g. Segmented: Default/Controlled/Full/Disabled/LongLabels; Tabbar: ThreeTabs/WithBadges/FiveTabsOverflow; Steps: InProgress/Complete/Error; Header: Compact/Large/Collapsing+WithBackAndActions). Add a short MDX 'Navigation patterns' page showing the canonical TKPage `header={<TKHeader .../>}` + TKTabView shell with a copy button, and delete or merge the duplicate nav.stories.tsx so there's one source of truth. Each story doubles as a visual-regression anchor for the states the red-team flagged.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

Coverage gaps / assumptions: (1) There are NO co-located unit or story tests for this family in packages/uikit/src; coverage lives in packages/uikit/test/{navigation-reorg,m6-nav,a11y-semantics}.test.tsx and storybook/composites/navigation.stories.tsx. navigation-reorg only smoke-tests render + one click per component; a11y-semantics checks Tabbar role=navigation+aria-current and Steps aria-current but asserts nothing about Segmented/CategoryTabs/PageDots grouping — so NAV-001/002/003 are real coverage gaps, not just style preferences. (2) Reduced-motion is handled globally in packages/uikit/src/tokens/tokens.css (lines 331-340 zero --tk-t1/2/3 and linearize easing under prefers-reduced-motion:reduce), and the nav inline `transition` declarations reference those vars, so motion IS neutralized PROVIDED the component renders under a `.tk` root; I found no per-component fallback if mounted outside TKProvider — treat that as an assumption worth a test, not a confirmed bug. (3) SSR/import safety looks fine: prefersReducedMotion guards window, scrollIntoView is optional-chained, Segmented guards ResizeObserver===undefined, and safe-area/keyboard/haptics come from @tg-mini-app/telegram which zeroes/no-ops outside Telegram. (4) NAV-004 severity assumes consumers pass current === steps.length on completion — confirmed in examples/trailhead/src/features/train/SessionDetail.tsx:43, which produces active===-1 today. (5) I did not run the vitest suite, Storybook, or Playwright; every finding is from source reading with cited line evidence. (6) TKHeader back=\"auto\" nav-stack integration (useOptionalNav) was read but not deep-audited; it appears correct.

---

### Nav shell — composites/nav.tsx (app navigation shell)

`NAV2` · `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx` · 12 findings (0🔴 4🟠 6🟡 2⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility & focus management · `D-TG` Telegram runtime & token/theme scoping · `D-GESTURE` Swipe-back gesture correctness · `D-TYPES` TypeScript ergonomics of params API · `D-STATE` State model & controlled-value drift · `D-PERF` Re-render & layout-thrash during drag · `D-EDGE` Robustness on rapid taps / unmount / unknown targets

#### Findings

- [x] **`NAV2-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Push/pop never move focus — keyboard & screen-reader users are stranded on the hidden panel
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:190-228`
  - **Evidence:** The mapped panel div has no focus handling at all: `<div key={entry.key} data-tk-nav-panel={entry.panel} aria-hidden={top ? undefined : true} style={{ … visibility: top || … ? "visible" : "hidden" …}}>` — `grep focus|tabIndex|role=|aria-live` over nav.tsx returns nothing.
  - **Impact:** When a Mini App calls `nav.push("detail")`, DOM focus stays on the trigger button in the now-`visibility:hidden` lower panel. Keyboard focus is lost (tabbing restarts at document top) and VoiceOver/TalkBack keep reading the old screen — the new screen is silently entered. On `pop` focus is never restored to the originating control. This fails WCAG 2.4.3 Focus Order and makes the shell unusable with assistive tech.
  - **Fix:** On stack change, move focus into the new top panel: give each top panel container `tabIndex={-1}` (or focus its first focusable/heading) in a layout effect keyed on `entry.key`, and stash/restore the previously-focused element on pop. Add `role="region"`/`aria-label` per panel so the region has a name.
  - **Done when:** A test that pushes a panel asserts `document.activeElement` is inside the new `[data-tk-nav-panel]`; popping restores focus to the triggering control. Manual VoiceOver: pushing announces the new screen's heading.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — nav.tsx has zero focus handling: no tabIndex/role on the panel divs and no .focus() call anywhere in the file (grep returns nothing). On push/pop focus is never moved to the new panel nor restored to the trigger; panels only get aria-hidden (line 197), so keyboard/SR focus is stranded — WCAG 2.4.3 issue is real.
- [x] **`NAV2-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-TG` — Stack root uses design tokens but never carries the `.tk` class that defines them
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:184-219`
  - **Evidence:** `<div ref={rootRef} … style={{ position: "relative", height: "100%", overflow: "hidden", ...style }}>` and child panels set `background: "var(--tk-bg)"`, `transition: … var(--tk-t2) var(--tk-ease)`, `animation: "tk-nav-in var(--tk-t2) var(--tk-ease) both"`. Tokens are scoped to `.tk` only (tokens.css:13 `.tk { … --tk-t2: …; --tk-ease: … }`), applied solely by the theme provider (theme.tsx:147 `className={["tk", …]}`). TKNavStack adds no `tk` class.
  - **Impact:** If a Mini App mounts `TKNavStack` outside the kit's theme provider (a very natural thing for an app shell that owns its own root), `--tk-bg` resolves to nothing → panels render with a transparent background (old screen bleeds through during transitions), and `--tk-t2`/`--tk-ease` are invalid → `animation`/`transition` durations fall back to `0s`, killing the slide. The dependency on an ancestor `.tk` is undocumented in the props.
  - **Fix:** Self-apply the token scope on the stack root (e.g. add `className="tk"` or accept/merge a `className` prop), or document `TKNavStack` as requiring a `.tk`/theme-provider ancestor and assert it in dev. Prefer making the root self-sufficient so the component works standalone.
  - **Done when:** Rendering `<TKNavStack>` with no `.tk` ancestor still shows an opaque `--tk-bg` background and a non-zero transition duration on push (computed style check).
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — Panels use var(--tk-bg)/var(--tk-t2)/var(--tk-ease) (nav.tsx:201,212,217) but those tokens are scoped to .tk selectors (tokens.css:13,109,134), applied solely by the theme provider (theme.tsx:147). TKNavStack adds no tk class and relies on an undocumented ancestor .tk; mounted outside the provider the background/animation tokens resolve to nothing.
- [x] **`NAV2-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-GESTURE` — Swipe-back drag has no `touch-action` guard — it fights vertical scroll and Telegram swipe-to-minimize
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:168-189`
  - **Evidence:** Pointer handlers are spread onto the root with no CSS hint: `{...handlers}` on a div whose style is `{ position: "relative", height: "100%", overflow: "hidden", ...style }}`; `grep touchAction|touch-action|overscroll` over nav.tsx returns nothing. The default `swipeBack="edge"` only narrows the *start* x (line 174) but the move tracking afterwards has no axis lock in CSS.
  - **Impact:** In a narrow Telegram WebView, a horizontal-ish drag begun near the left edge competes with the browser's own scrolling/gesture handling; without `touch-action: pan-y` the browser may claim the gesture or the WebView's vertical-swipe-to-minimize can trigger mid-drag. Consumers can't opt the root out of native gestures here — they have to wrap it themselves (App.tsx already disables vertical swipes app-wide as a workaround).
  - **Fix:** Set `touchAction: "pan-y"` (and `overscrollBehavior: "contain"`) on the stack root when `swipeBack` is enabled so the browser yields horizontal pans to the gesture while preserving vertical scroll; optionally guard Telegram vertical-swipe during an active drag.
  - **Done when:** Root computed `touch-action` is `pan-y` when `swipeBack !== false`; a Playwright drag test confirms a left-edge horizontal drag pops the panel without scrolling the page.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — Root div spreads {...handlers} with style {position:relative,height:100%,overflow:hidden} (nav.tsx:187-188) and no touch-action/overscroll anywhere in the file. The drag hook does JS cross-axis cancel, but the specific CSS gesture guard the finding cites is genuinely absent, so horizontal drags can still contend with native WebView scroll/swipe-to-minimize.
- [x] **`NAV2-007`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — Fully uncontrolled stack: no controlled `stack`/`onChange`, `initial` ignored after mount — blocks deep-linking & history sync
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:82-145`
  - **Evidence:** `const [stack, setStack] = useState<NavEntry[]>([{ panel: initial, key: 0 }]);` — `initial` is consumed only in the lazy initializer; the API exposes only an imperative `useNav()` and an `onStackChange` notifier, with no `stack`/`value` + `onChange` controlled mode.
  - **Impact:** A Mini App that wants the nav stack to reflect a deep-link param, persist across reloads, or sync with `start_param`/router state cannot drive the stack from props — changing `initial` after mount does nothing, and there's no way to restore a multi-entry stack. Teams must reach into refs or remount with a `key` to reset, a real workaround (App.tsx keeps stacks mounted precisely because there's no other reset story).
  - **Fix:** Add an optional controlled mode (`stack?: NavEntry[]` + `onChange`) or at least an imperative `reset(entries)` / honor `initial` changes, so deep-link restoration and history integration are possible.
  - **Done when:** Driving `stack` from props renders the matching panels and round-trips through `onChange`; restoring a 3-entry stack on mount shows the correct top panel with its params.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — stack is local useState seeded from `initial` only in the lazy initializer (nav.tsx:92); props expose just `initial`/`onStackChange` and the API is imperative useNav() with no controlled stack/value+onChange. Changing initial after mount is ignored and a multi-entry stack can't be restored, blocking deep-link/history sync. (Minor: the App.tsx 'kept mounted' comment is about preserving scroll on tab-switch, not a reset workaround.)
- [x] **`NAV2-002`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — No live-region announcement on navigation; the panel container exposes no landmark/name
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:193-220`
  - **Evidence:** Panels are bare `<div data-tk-nav-panel … aria-hidden={top ? undefined : true}>` with no `role`, `aria-label`, or sibling `aria-live` region; nav.tsx contains no `aria-live`.
  - **Impact:** Even apart from focus, a screen-reader user gets no signal that the route changed — there is no polite announcement of the destination and no navigation landmark to jump to. Consumers (Trailhead's Discover/Guide funnels) ship full screen transitions that are invisible to AT.
  - **Fix:** Render a visually-hidden `aria-live="polite"` region inside the stack root and write the new `activePanel` (or a consumer-supplied title) on each commit; give the top panel container `role="region"` with an accessible name.
  - **Done when:** After `push`, an `aria-live` node's text content updates to the destination panel; axe/Playwright a11y snapshot shows a named region for the active panel.
- [ ] **`NAV2-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-GESTURE` — Swipe-back is the only way back on the top panel for keyboard/mouse users — no in-component back affordance
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:155-181`
  - **Evidence:** Back navigation is exposed only via the gesture (`useDragGesture` → `api.pop`) and the native Telegram BackButton (`useBackIntercept(stack.length > 1, api.pop, backButton)`, line 145). There is no rendered back control and no keyboard handler in the stack.
  - **Impact:** Outside Telegram (Storybook, plain-browser preview, desktop a11y testing) there is no native BackButton, so a keyboard or mouse user on a pushed panel has no component-provided way to go back — they depend entirely on each consumer screen wiring its own header back button (Trailhead does this manually via `useMockBackHeader`). The shell ships no fallback, which surprises teams expecting the stack to be self-navigable.
  - **Fix:** Provide an optional rendered back affordance or at least a documented `Esc`/Backspace key handler on the root that calls `api.pop()` when `depth > 1`, gated to not interfere with inputs.
  - **Done when:** With no Telegram runtime, pressing the documented key (or clicking the provided control) on a depth>1 stack pops a panel; covered by a test that renders without `TKTelegramProvider`.
- [x] **`NAV2-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TYPES` — `params` is `unknown` everywhere — every consumer hand-casts, no generic inference
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:17-27`
  - **Evidence:** `push: (panel: string, params?: unknown) => void; … params: unknown;` in `TKNavApi`. Consumers cast on every read: `const id = (nav.params as { id?: string } | undefined)?.id ?? ""` (ExperienceDetail.tsx:36, GuideThread.tsx:18, GuideProfile.tsx:12, SessionDetail.tsx:11).
  - **Impact:** There is zero type safety between `push("detail", { id })` and the receiver — a typo'd or missing param key compiles fine and surfaces as a runtime `undefined`. Mini App teams write boilerplate casts in every panel and get no autocomplete for params, which is a recurring footgun across the family.
  - **Fix:** Make the panel-id/params relationship typed: e.g. `TKNavStack<Routes extends Record<string, unknown>>` so `push<K extends keyof Routes>(panel: K, params: Routes[K])` and `useNav<Routes>()` returns a discriminated `{ activePanel: K; params: Routes[K] }`. At minimum expose a `useNav<TParams>()` generic so consumers stop casting.
  - **Done when:** `push("detail", { id })` type-errors if `id` is omitted/mistyped given a route map; `nav.params` is inferred without a cast in a typed consumer.
- [x] **`NAV2-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — Unknown / missing panel id renders a blank screen with no warning
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:147-153,224`
  - **Evidence:** `Children.forEach(children, (child) => { if (isValidElement<TKNavPanelProps>(child) && child.props.id) map.set(child.props.id, child); })` then `{panels.get(entry.panel) ?? null}`. A push to an id with no matching `<TKNavPanel>` (or to `initial` when no panel declares it) yields `null`.
  - **Impact:** `nav.push("detial")` (typo) or pushing before the matching `<TKNavPanel>` is mounted silently shows a blank, transparent, swipe-back-able screen with no console warning — a confusing dead end for the user and a hard-to-spot bug for the dev. Same for an `initial` that matches no panel.
  - **Fix:** In dev, `console.warn` when `push`/`replace`/`popTo`/`initial` reference an id absent from the panels map (or when duplicate ids are registered); optionally render a visible dev-only placeholder.
  - **Done when:** Pushing an unknown id logs a dev warning naming the id and the available panel ids; production build stays silent.
- [x] **`NAV2-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — Rapid double-push / push during transition is unguarded — stacks duplicate entries and desync depth
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:112-140`
  - **Evidence:** `push: (panel, params) => { dirRef.current = "push"; commit([...stackRef.current, { panel, params, key: keyRef.current++ }]); }` — no in-flight guard; `commit` reads `stackRef.current` which is updated synchronously each render but two taps in the same frame both see the pre-push ref.
  - **Impact:** A user double-tapping a list row (common on a laggy WebView) pushes the same panel twice; the stack now has two identical entries, depth is off by one, and the user must press back twice to escape. Consumers like Feed (`nav.push("detail", { id })`) and GuideDirectory have no debounce, so this surfaces in real funnels.
  - **Fix:** Debounce/guard navigation while a transition is animating (ignore a `push` to the same `panel`+`params` within the transition window), or expose an `onStackChange`-driven `isTransitioning` flag consumers can gate on.
  - **Done when:** Two synchronous `push("detail")` calls result in a single new entry (depth 2, not 3); test fires two clicks in one act() and asserts stack length.
- [ ] **`NAV2-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-PERF` — Each swipe frame rebuilds a per-under-panel context object, re-rendering all lower-panel `useNav` consumers
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:159,210,221-223`
  - **Evidence:** `onMove: (state) => setDragX(Math.max(0, state.delta))` runs every rAF frame; the render then reads `rootRef.current?.clientWidth` in the transform (line 210, layout read in render) and supplies under-panels a brand-new context value each render: `value={top ? api : { ...api, activePanel: entry.panel, params: entry.params, depth: index + 1 }}`.
  - **Impact:** During a back-swipe the whole stack re-renders ~60×/sec, and because the under-panel `TKNavContext` value is a fresh object every frame, every `useNav()` consumer inside lower panels re-renders too — plus a synchronous `clientWidth` layout read per frame causes layout thrash. On a mid-tier phone in a narrow WebView this makes the swipe-back janky, the opposite of the intended native feel.
  - **Fix:** Memoize the per-entry context object by `entry.key` so its identity is stable across drag frames; drive the under-panel transform via a CSS variable / direct ref style write instead of re-rendering, and cache `clientWidth` at drag start instead of reading it in render.
  - **Done when:** During a simulated drag, lower-panel `useNav` consumers do not re-render (render-count probe stays flat); no `clientWidth` read occurs inside the render path.
- [ ] **`NAV2-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-A11Y` — During a drag the revealed under-panel is visible but `aria-hidden`, exposing focusable content to sighted users yet hiding it from AT
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:197,204`
  - **Evidence:** `aria-hidden={top ? undefined : true}` is fixed by stack position, while visibility flips on during drag: `visibility: top || (under && (dragging || dragX > 0)) ? "visible" : "hidden"`. So the under panel can be on-screen (peeking from the left) while still `aria-hidden="true"` and containing tabbable controls.
  - **Impact:** While a swipe-back is in progress (or partially committed), the peeking previous screen is painted and its buttons are focusable via Tab, but screen readers treat it as hidden — an inconsistency between the visual and accessibility trees that confuses AT users and can let keyboard focus land on an `aria-hidden` subtree (an axe violation).
  - **Fix:** Add `inert` to non-top panels (covers both focusability and AT visibility) instead of relying on `aria-hidden` + `visibility`, or only mark `aria-hidden` once the panel is fully hidden after the drag settles.
  - **Done when:** A partially-dragged stack shows no focusable elements inside an `aria-hidden` subtree (axe passes); Tab cannot reach the under-panel during a drag.
- [ ] **`NAV2-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-TG` — `backButton` prop hands native-button visibility to the provider unconditionally — multiple mounted stacks can over-count
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:145`
  - **Evidence:** `useBackIntercept(stack.length > 1, api.pop, backButton);` and in the provider `if (showNativeButton) { backButtonWant += 1; … }` (provider.tsx:60-69) using a module-global counter `backButtonWant`.
  - **Impact:** Trailhead's App.tsx keeps one mounted `TKNavStack` per tab simultaneously (each tab's stack stays mounted, only hidden). A hidden tab whose stack is deep (`stack.length > 1`) still increments the global `backButtonWant`, so the native Back button can remain visible while the user is on a *different*, root-level tab — the back press then pops a stack the user can't see. The component has no notion of being inactive/hidden.
  - **Fix:** Gate the native-button request on an `active`/`visible` prop (or pause interception when the stack is hidden), so only the foreground stack drives BackButton visibility; document that mounted-but-hidden stacks must pass `backButton={false}` or an inactive flag.
  - **Done when:** With two mounted stacks where the background one is depth>1 and foreground is depth 1, the native Back button is hidden; pressing back does not pop the background stack.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`NAV2-DX-001`** · types · effort L
  - **Pain:** `params` is typed `unknown` (nav.tsx:26), so every consumer hand-casts it. The cast `(nav.params as { id?: string } | undefined)?.id ?? ""` is copy-pasted verbatim in GuideProfile.tsx:12, GuideThread.tsx:18, ExperienceDetail.tsx:36, SessionDetail.tsx:11, RescheduleSlot.tsx:35 — and even the kit's own Storybook (nav.stories.tsx:36 `nav.params as { order?: number }`). The cast is unsafe (no runtime check), verbose, and easy to get wrong (forgetting `| undefined`).
  - **Proposal:** Add a route-map generic so params infer per panel. Define `type TKNavRoutes = Record<string, unknown>` and parametrize the API: `useNav<R extends TKNavRoutes>()` returns `push<K extends keyof R>(panel: K, params: R[K])` and a discriminated `current: { [K in keyof R]: { panel: K; params: R[K] } }[keyof R]`. Consumers declare once `interface GuideRoutes { directory: void; profile: { id: string }; thread: { id: string } }` and `const nav = useNav<GuideRoutes>()`; then `nav.push("profile", { id })` is checked and `nav.current.params.id` is typed. Ship a typed `useNavParams<R, K>()` convenience: `const { id } = useNavParams<GuideRoutes, "profile">()` to kill the cast entirely. Keep the untyped overload for back-compat.
- [ ] **`NAV2-DX-002`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** Every stack re-implements the same depth/active boilerplate to drive the shell. GuideStack.tsx:18-23, DiscoverStack.tsx, TrainStack.tsx, ProfileStack.tsx all do `useState(panels)` + a `handleStackChange` that calls `setPanels` AND `onDepthChange?.(nextPanels.length)`, only to derive `active`/`depth` that the stack already knows. App.tsx:22-28 then keeps a parallel `depthByTab` record. The single useful fact (current depth) requires ~8 lines of bridging state per stack.
  - **Proposal:** Expose depth without forcing the consumer to mirror the whole stack.
