Replace/augment `onStackChange?: (panels: string[]) => void` with a richer `onChange?: (state: { stack: string[]; depth: number; active: string }) => void`, and add a sibling hook `useNavState()` (returns `{ depth, active, stack }` from context) so a parent shell can read depth via a tiny child component instead of lifting state. Common case becomes `<TKNavStack initial="feed" onChange={(s) => onDepthChange(s.depth)}>` with no local `useState`. Document the one-liner in the JSDoc.
- [ ] **`NAV2-DX-003`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** Nested-screen back affordance is not provided by the kit — the example had to invent `useMockBackHeader` (examples/trailhead/src/components/MockBackHeader.tsx) and remember to call it in every nested panel and thread it into `<TKPage header={...}>`. Combined with red-team NAV2-005 (no in-component back for keyboard/mouse) and NAV2-003 (root lacks `.tk`), the out-of-box stack ships inaccessible: off Telegram there is literally no visible/keyboard back control unless each consumer wires a header by hand.
  - **Proposal:** Make accessible back free by default. Add a `header` story to `TKNavPanel` OR a `chrome?: "auto" | "native" | "none"` prop on `TKNavStack` that, when `"auto"` (default), renders a built-in in-DOM back affordance (reusing `TKHeader back="auto"`) on non-root panels whenever native Telegram chrome is absent — exactly what `useMockBackHeader` does, but built in. Also carry `.tk` on the root (`className={`tk ${className ?? ''}`}`) so tokens resolve, and give the panel container `role="region"` + an `aria-label` derived from a new optional `TKNavPanel.title`. Net: deleting `MockBackHeader.tsx` from consumers and getting a focusable Back for free.
- [ ] **`NAV2-DX-004`** · api · effort L
  - **Pain:** Stack is fully uncontrolled (red-team NAV2-007): `useState([{ panel: initial, key: 0 }])` (nav.tsx:92) ignores `initial` after mount and offers no controlled `stack`. Deep-linking (open app on `thread?id=42`) and history/URL sync are impossible — App.tsx is forced to keep a shadow `depthByTab` precisely because it cannot read or seed the real stack. There is no way to restore a stack on reload.
  - **Proposal:** Add an optional controlled mode alongside the uncontrolled one: `stack?: Array<{ panel: string; params?: unknown }>` + `onStackChange` already present becomes the change source, and a `defaultStack?: [...]` for uncontrolled-with-deeplink seeding (so you can open 2-deep). Provide an imperative escape hatch via `ref` (`useImperativeHandle` exposing `push/pop/popTo/replace/reset`) for code outside the tree (URL router, push notifications). Sketch: `const ref = useRef<TKNavHandle>(null); <TKNavStack ref={ref} defaultStack={[{panel:'feed'},{panel:'detail',params:{id}}]} />`.
- [ ] **`NAV2-DX-005`** · defaults · effort S
  - **Pain:** Misuse fails silently. An unknown/missing panel id renders `panels.get(entry.panel) ?? null` (nav.tsx:224) → a blank black screen with no diagnostic (red-team NAV2-008). Because `params` is `unknown`, a `push("detial")` typo and a `push("detail", wrongShape)` both produce a blank or runtime-undefined screen with nothing in the console. New developers lose minutes guessing whether it's a CSS, mount, or routing problem.
  - **Proposal:** Fail loudly in dev. When `panels.get(id)` misses, render a dev-only fallback panel (`<TKNavMissingPanel id={id} known={[...ids]} />`) showing the bad id and the registered ids, and `console.error("<TKNavStack>: no <TKNavPanel id=\"%s\">. Known ids: %s", id, known.join(', '))`, gated behind `process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'`. Likewise warn once if `initial`/`defaultStack` references an unregistered id at mount. In production keep the silent null. Zero runtime cost in prod, instant feedback in dev.
- [ ] **`NAV2-DX-006`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** `TKNavPanel` is a marker that the stack reads only by `child.props.id` (nav.tsx:150) and renders via a hard `<div>` wrapper (nav.tsx:194) with stack-owned positioning/background/transform. There is no `asChild`/render-prop escape hatch, so a consumer can't lazy-render a panel only when active (every panel body mounts up front), can't attach their own ref/landmark to the panel root, and can't compose the panel element. GuideStack passes `active={visible && active === 'profile'}` (GuideStack.tsx:30) precisely to gate work — boilerplate the panel API could own.
  - **Proposal:** Support a render-function child and lazy mounting: `<TKNavPanel id="profile" lazy>{(ctx) => <GuideProfile {...ctx.params} active={ctx.active} />}</TKNavPanel>`, where `ctx` is the typed `{ params, active, depth }`. `lazy` mounts the body only after first activation (keeps it mounted after, preserving state). Add `asChild` so the panel can forward the stack's positioning props onto a consumer-provided element (e.g. a `<section aria-label>` landmark), addressing the missing landmark from red-team NAV2-002 at the composition layer.
- [ ] **`NAV2-DX-007`** · docs · effort S
  - **Pain:** Discoverability of the real API is thin. `nav.stories.tsx` (64 lines) has a single `StackFlow` story that itself models the bad pattern (`nav.params as {...}` at line 36) and demonstrates none of `popTo`, `replace`, `swipeBack` variants, `backButton`, deep-linking, or the header-back integration. There is no copy-pasteable 'one-screen-to-many' starter, and the typed-params and back-affordance patterns developers actually need (per the trailhead examples) are undocumented.
  - **Proposal:** Add stories + a doc block covering the canonical recipes: (1) typed routes with `useNav<Routes>()` and `useNavParams`; (2) `popTo` to unwind a funnel (mirror Checkout.tsx:210 `nav.popTo('feed')`); (3) `replace` for auth/redirect; (4) each `swipeBack` mode with the `touch-action` note; (5) back affordance on/off Telegram. Include a 10-line 'copy this' snippet at the top of the JSDoc on `TKNavStack`. Update the lone story to use the typed API so the kit stops teaching the cast.
- [ ] **`NAV2-DX-008`** · perf · effort M
  - **Pain:** Lower-panel `useNav` consumers re-render on every interaction. Two compounding sources: (a) red-team NAV2-010, each drag frame builds a fresh per-under-panel context object (nav.tsx:222) so all lower consumers re-render mid-swipe; (b) the whole `api` is one context value recomputed on every `stack` change (nav.tsx:112-140), so any push/pop re-renders every `useNav` consumer in the tree even if they only call `push` (a stable function). In a multi-tab shell (App.tsx mounts 5 stacks simultaneously) this is real churn.
  - **Proposal:** Split context into a stable actions context and a volatile state context: `TKNavActionsContext` holds `{ push, pop, replace, popTo }` (memoized once, deps-free via refs — already half-done with `stackRef`) and `TKNavStateContext` holds `{ depth, active, params }`. `useNav()` composes both for back-compat; add `useNavActions()` for consumers that only navigate (Feed.tsx:308 `open`, cells with `onClick={() => nav.push(...)}`) so they never re-render on state changes. Stop minting per-frame under-panel contexts: derive the under-panel's `{params, depth}` with a `useMemo` keyed on its entry, not on `dragX`.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

SCOPE: The task names `composites/nav.tsx` as the \"app navigation shell\"; that file is the TKNavStack/TKNavPanel/useNav/useOptionalNav family (re-exported via packages/uikit/src/index.ts:18 `export * from \"./composites/nav\"`). Note there is a SEPARATE barrel `composites/navigation.tsx` (TKHeader/TKTabbar/TKTabView/TKSegmented/TKCategoryTabs/TKSteps/TKPageDots) — those were NOT audited here; if the intent was the navigation/ widgets, a re-scope is needed. The back-button/haptics runtime now lives in the `@tg-mini-app/telegram` package (packages/telegram/src/provider.tsx); `foundation/telegram.tsx` is a re-export shim (telegram.tsx:13).\n\nCOVERAGE GAPS in existing tests (packages/uikit/test/m6-nav.test.tsx): only imperative push/pop/replace/popTo + the back-queue priority and useKeyboard are covered, all via `fireEvent.click`. There are NO tests for the swipe-back gesture, focus movement on navigate, reduced-motion, duplicate/unknown panel ids, `initial` change after mount, native BackButton visibility from a hidden stack, or rapid double-push. No Storybook story and no `.css` file exist for this family (animations/tokens live in tokens.css).\n\nASSUMPTIONS: (1) NAV2-003 assumes a consumer can mount TKNavStack outside a `.tk` ancestor — every shipped example (theme provider wraps the app) keeps it inside `.tk`, so this bites only standalone/3rd-party usage, but nothing in the prop types prevents it. (2) NAV2-010's per-frame re-render of lower-panel consumers is inferred from React context semantics (new object identity per render) — not measured; the top panel is spared because it reuses the stable `api` object. (3) NAV2-012's over-count is inferred from App.tsx mounting all tab stacks simultaneously combined with the module-global `backButtonWant`; I did not run the demo to observe a visible mis-fire, but the Trailhead comment at App.tsx:15-17 confirms stacks stay mounted while hidden. (4) Severities are calibrated for a real narrow-WebView Mini App: A11Y focus loss (NAV2-001) and missing token scope/touch-action (NAV2-003/004) are the highest-signal because they break silently in production; the rest are ergonomics/robustness hardening.

---

### Overlays — ActionSheet, Dialog, PopperTooltip, Sheet, Toasts, shared (focus trap, scroll lock, layering)

`OVL` · `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays` · 12 findings (0🔴 3🟠 7🟡 2⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility (focus trap, ARIA, live regions, motion, targets) · `D-API` API ergonomics & footguns · `D-STATE` State model & edge values · `D-TG` Telegram runtime (safe-area, viewport, motion knob, back button) · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView / i18n overflow & truncation · `D-PERF` Motion sync & re-render cost · `D-EDGE` Robustness (races, rapid taps, unmount-during-async)

#### Findings

- [x] **`OVL-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — TKToastProvider max={1} keeps ALL toasts (slice(-0) === slice(0)), unbounded growth
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/toasts.tsx:58`
  - **Evidence:** setToasts((t) => [...t.slice(-(max - 1)), { ...toast, id, out: false }]);
  - **Impact:** A Mini App configuring <TKToastProvider max={1}> to show one toast at a time gets the opposite: -(1-1) === -0, and Array.slice(-0) returns the WHOLE array, so every toast is retained and the stack grows without bound on each show()/success()/error(). The most common low-noise config silently leaks DOM nodes and live-region announcements. Verified: ['a','b','c'].slice(-0) === ['a','b','c']. The existing test only covers max={2}.
  - **Fix:** Compute the keep-count without relying on negative-zero slicing: const keep = Math.max(0, max - 1); setToasts((t) => [...t.slice(t.length - keep), { ...toast, id, out: false }]); (or clamp max>=1 and special-case keep===0 to []).
  - **Done when:** New test: render <TKToastProvider max={1}>, fire show() three times, assert exactly one toast is in the document; also assert max={1} success() then error() leaves one toast.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — toasts.tsx:58 does [...t.slice(-(max-1)),...]; with max={1}, -(1-1) is -0 and Array.slice(-0) returns the whole array (verified empirically), so no toast is evicted and the stack grows unbounded. Only max={2} is covered in tests (toasts.test.tsx:21), leaving the max={1} boundary untested.
- [x] **`OVL-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — TKPopper role="dialog" documents a focus-trapping modal but implements no trap, no focus move, no scrim
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/popper-tooltip.tsx:33-38`
  - **Evidence:** * Pass `"menu"` for an actionable list, or `"dialog"` for a modal\n * popover that traps focus.\n */\n role?: "tooltip" | "menu" | "dialog";
  - **Impact:** TKPopper never calls useOverlayA11y (unlike Dialog/Sheet/ActionSheet): with role="dialog" it does not move focus into the popper, does not trap Tab, sets no aria-modal, and renders no scrim — background stays fully interactive and keyboard focus escapes immediately. A Mini App building a focus-trapping popover from the documented role="dialog" ships an inaccessible 'modal' that fails any a11y gate, with no warning.
  - **Fix:** Either implement the promise (when role==="dialog": add aria-modal, run useOverlayA11y for focus move+trap+restore, and optionally a scrim) or correct the JSDoc/types to state TKPopper is non-modal and direct consumers to TKDialog/TKSheet for trapped focus.
  - **Done when:** With role="dialog" and an open popper, a Testing Library test asserts focus lands inside the popper on open, Tab cycles within it, and Escape restores focus to the anchor; or the docs/types no longer claim focus trapping.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — popper-tooltip.tsx:33-38 documents role="dialog" as a modal popover that traps focus, but TKPopper never calls useOverlayA11y, sets no aria-modal, renders no Scrim, and moves no focus (only outside-pointerdown + Escape close at lines 130-147). The documented focus-trapping modal is unimplemented.
- [x] **`OVL-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-PERF` — Hardcoded JS unmount timers desync from CSS exit animation when motionSpeed knob is set
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/dialog.tsx:71`
  - **Evidence:** const { mounted, closing } = useMountTransition(open, 260); ... animation: `${closing ? "tk-fade-out" : "tk-modal-in"} var(--tk-t2) ...`
  - **Impact:** useMountTransition unmounts at a FIXED ms (Dialog 260, ActionSheet 360, Sheet 380) while the CSS exit runs for var(--tk-t2)/var(--tk-t3), which equal calc(260ms / var(--tk-ms)). The motionSpeed prop drives --tk-ms (the built-in `material` preset ships motionSpeed:1.15, and any consumer can set it). With motionSpeed < 1 (e.g. a 'slower animations' accessibility setting) the CSS exit runs LONGER than the JS timer, so the panel is removed mid-animation and the close visibly snaps/cuts. Consumers touching the documented motion knob get a janky dismiss they cannot fix from userland.
  - **Fix:** Derive closeMs from the same source as the CSS — read the resolved --tk-t2/--tk-t3 via getComputedStyle at close time, or listen to animationend/transitionend on the panel instead of a fixed setTimeout, so unmount always matches the actual exit duration regardless of --tk-ms.
  - **Done when:** With TKProvider motionSpeed={0.5}, an e2e/visual test confirms the dialog/sheet finishes its exit animation before unmount (no mid-animation pop); changing motionSpeed never clips the close.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — dialog.tsx:71 unmounts at a fixed 260ms while the CSS exit (line 115) runs var(--tk-t2)=calc(260ms/var(--tk-ms)) (tokens.css:36), and --tk-ms is driven by the documented motionSpeed knob (theme.tsx:42). With motionSpeed<1 the CSS exit outlasts the JS timer and the panel is cut mid-animation; ActionSheet (360 vs --tk-t3=440) and Sheet (380 vs 440) desync even at the default ms=1, and the reduced-motion block (tokens.css:336-338) is independent of --tk-ms so it does not mask this.
- [x] **`OVL-004`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-STATE` — TKActionSheet has no max-height or scroll — a long item list overflows above the viewport and items become unreachable
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/action-sheet.tsx:57-77`
  - **Evidence:** position: "absolute", left: 10, right: 10, bottom: "calc(10px + var(--tk-safe-bottom))", ... // no maxHeight, no overflow on the items container
  - **Impact:** Unlike TKSheet (which caps height and scrolls), the action sheet grows with items.length. In a short Telegram WebView a sheet with ~8+ items (or large dynamic-text scaling) extends past the top of the screen; the topmost items and the cancel button render off-screen with no scroll container, so the user cannot reach or dismiss them. A consumer passing a runtime-length list (e.g. share targets) silently strands actions.
  - **Fix:** Add maxHeight: calc(100% - var(--tk-safe-top) - 20px) to the panel and make the items <div> overflowY:auto with overscrollBehavior:contain, keeping the cancel button pinned; or document a hard item-count cap.
  - **Done when:** Render TKActionSheet with 20 items in a 480px frame; a visual/e2e test confirms the list scrolls within the panel and the first item + cancel button are reachable on screen.
- [x] **`OVL-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-RESP` — TKDialog actions force equal columns (gridAutoFlow:column) — 3+ actions or long i18n labels truncate/overflow
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/dialog.tsx:142-144`
  - **Evidence:** <div style={{ display: "grid", gridAutoColumns: "1fr", gridAutoFlow: "column", gap: 8 }}>{actions}</div>
  - **Impact:** Action buttons are always laid out in equal-width columns with no wrap and no vertical fallback. With three actions, or two actions whose localized labels are long (German/Russian/Arabic often 1.5–2x English), each 1fr column is too narrow and the labels clip or push the buttons past the dialog's left:24/right:24 bounds in a narrow WebView. A consumer cannot opt into stacked buttons without bypassing the prop.
  - **Fix:** Provide a vertical/stacked layout option (e.g. actionsLayout?: "row" | "stacked", or auto-stack when >2 actions / when labels overflow), and allow buttons to wrap instead of forcing single-row equal columns.
  - **Done when:** Render TKDialog with three actions and with two long-localized labels at 320px width; a visual test confirms no label truncation/overflow (buttons stack or wrap).
- [x] **`OVL-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — Modal overlays don't mark background inert/aria-hidden — screen readers and touch can still reach content behind the scrim
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/shared.tsx:87-169`
  - **Evidence:** useOverlayA11y traps Tab and Escape but never sets `inert`/`aria-hidden` on sibling/background content; panels render as siblings of app content under the same .tk root.
  - **Impact:** Tab is trapped, but VoiceOver/TalkBack rotor swiping and screen-reader virtual cursor can still traverse the background DOM because nothing outside the panel is inert or aria-hidden (aria-modal alone is unreliable on mobile WebViews). A Mini App shipping a dialog over a form lets assistive-tech users wander into the obscured page behind the scrim — a WCAG 2.4.3/1.3.2 problem flagged by audits.
  - **Fix:** When an overlay activates, apply `inert` (with aria-hidden fallback) to the siblings of the overlay subtree (or to the .tk root's other children), restoring on cleanup; coordinate via the existing overlay stack so nested overlays restore correctly.
  - **Done when:** With a dialog open, an axe/Playwright a11y test confirms background interactive elements are inert/aria-hidden and not reachable by the accessibility tree; closing restores them.
- [x] **`OVL-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — TKDialog onConfirm fires on Enter from arbitrary non-form children — a silent confirm footgun
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/shared.tsx:126-135`
  - **Evidence:** if (e.key === "Enter" && confirmRef.current) { const tag = (e.target as HTMLElement | null)?.tagName; if (tag !== "TEXTAREA" && tag !== "INPUT" && tag !== "SELECT" && tag !== "A" && tag !== "BUTTON") { e.preventDefault(); confirmRef.current(); } }
  - **Impact:** Enter triggers onConfirm based only on the focused element's tagName. A consumer rendering custom interactive children inside the dialog (a div/role=button toggle, a contentEditable, a custom segmented control, or a library combobox that renders a div) will fire the primary/destructive action on Enter even though the user meant to activate that control. For an alertdialog wired to a destructive confirm, this is an accidental-data-loss footgun the consumer can't easily suppress.
  - **Fix:** Gate Enter-confirm on contenteditable and role-based interactivity too (skip when e.target has [role=button|textbox|combobox|listbox] or isContentEditable, or when the dialog contains focusable children), or make Enter-confirm opt-in via a prop rather than default-on.
  - **Done when:** Test: render TKDialog with onConfirm and a child <div role="button">; press Enter while it's focused and assert onConfirm is NOT called; a focused bare-panel Enter still confirms.
- [x] **`OVL-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — Toast action button and icon-only toasts ship sub-44px touch targets
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/toasts.tsx:134-156`
  - **Evidence:** <button type="button" ... style={{ border: "none", padding: 0, margin: 0, background: "none", ... }}>{t.action}</button>
  - **Impact:** The toast action button has padding:0, so its hit area is only the text height (~17px tall) — well under the 44px minimum and very hard to tap in a moving/animating toast inside a Telegram WebView. A Mini App using the common Undo/Retry toast pattern ships an action users routinely miss, and the toast auto-dismisses before they can re-aim.
  - **Fix:** Give the action button a real hit area (min-height 44px / vertical padding + negative margin to preserve layout, or a larger tap padding), and ensure the whole toast row clears 44px; consider pausing the auto-dismiss timer while the toast is hovered/focused.
  - **Done when:** Measured hit box of the toast action button is >=44px in height in a rendered test/visual; an e2e tap on the action reliably triggers onAction.
- [x] **`OVL-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — TKToastProvider stack is position:absolute, so toasts can land off-screen unless an ancestor is positioned
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/toasts.tsx:79-92`
  - **Evidence:** position: "absolute", left: 14, right: 14, top: ... bottom: ... zIndex: tkZ.toast
  - **Impact:** The toast layer is absolutely positioned and relies on the nearest positioned ancestor being the .tk root (documented assumption). If a consumer mounts TKToastProvider deep inside their own position:relative/transform container (very common — a transformed card, an animated route wrapper, a virtualized list), toasts anchor to that box and can render mid-screen, clipped, or offset from the real safe-area edges. Unlike TKPopper (which resolves .tk/document.body and can use position:fixed), toasts have no such escape and no portal, so the failure is silent and layout-dependent.
  - **Fix:** Portal the toast stack to the .tk root (or document.body) like TKPopper does, or use position:fixed with the JS safe-area insets, so toasts are anchored to the viewport regardless of intervening positioned/transformed ancestors.
  - **Done when:** Render TKToastProvider inside a position:relative; transform:translateZ(0) wrapper; an e2e test confirms the toast still anchors to the viewport safe-area edge, not the wrapper.
- [x] **`OVL-011`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — TKActionSheet calls useOverlayA11y with no onConfirm and no Enter handling — but more importantly its Escape/back-close and the cancel are not reflected to assistive tech as the labelled dismiss
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/action-sheet.tsx:31-46`
  - **Evidence:** ariaLabel = "Actions" ... role="dialog" aria-modal="true" aria-label={ariaLabel} ... useOverlayA11y(mounted && !closing, ref, onClose);
  - **Impact:** The default accessible name is the hardcoded English string "Actions". A Mini App localized to non-English locales (the kit ships useTKLocale and localizes the cancel button right below) gets a dialog announced as 'Actions' to screen readers in every language unless every call site remembers to pass ariaLabel. This is an inconsistent i18n surface: the cancel label auto-localizes via locale.cancel, but the dialog name does not, so the screen-reader name and the visible cancel disagree in language.
  - **Fix:** Default ariaLabel from useTKLocale (add a localized `actions` string) instead of the literal "Actions", keeping the prop as an override, so the dialog name follows the same locale as the cancel button.
  - **Done when:** Render TKActionSheet under a non-English TKLocaleProvider without passing ariaLabel; assert the dialog's accessible name is the localized term, matching the language of the cancel button.
- [x] **`OVL-009`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-EDGE` — Toast auto-dismiss timer is not cleared when the action button dismisses early — a second redundant removal timer is queued
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/toasts.tsx:50-62`
  - **Evidence:** const show = ... timersRef.current.push(window.setTimeout(() => dismiss(id), toast.duration ?? duration)); ... onClick={() => { t.onAction?.(); dismiss(t.id); }}
  - **Impact:** When a user taps a toast action (or it is otherwise dismissed early), the original auto-dismiss setTimeout is never cancelled; it later fires dismiss(id) again on an already-removed toast, queuing a second 350ms removal timer. Harmless functionally but leaks timers and re-renders under rapid toast churn, and means timersRef grows for the provider's lifetime (only cleared on unmount). Adds noise to perf traces in long-lived Mini Apps.
  - **Fix:** Track per-toast timer ids (Map<id, timer>) and clear the auto-dismiss timer inside dismiss(); make dismiss idempotent (no-op if the toast is already 'out'/gone) so action and timeout can't double-schedule.
  - **Done when:** Unit test with fake timers: show a toast, click its action, advance past the original duration, assert dismiss runs once and no stray timer fires; timersRef does not accumulate dead entries.
- [x] **`OVL-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-API` — TKSheet swipe/snap geometry assumes a clientHeight fallback of 400px and clamps snaps silently, masking misconfiguration
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/sheet.tsx:129-136`
  - **Evidence:** const height = ref.current?.clientHeight ?? 400; if (state.delta > 0 && tkShouldCommit(state.delta, state.velocity, height)) { ... }
  - **Impact:** The swipe-to-commit threshold uses a hardcoded 400px when clientHeight is unavailable, and snapTo/defaultSnap clamp out-of-range indices without warning. A consumer passing snapPoints like [0.9, 0.4] (descending — the JSDoc requires ascending) gets silently wrong commit math: the sheet may grow on a downward drag or refuse to close, with no dev warning. The contract is documented but unenforced, so misuse surfaces only as confusing runtime gesture behavior.
  - **Fix:** Validate snapPoints are ascending and within (0,1] in dev (console.warn on violation), and prefer the actual measured height (offsetHeight or a ResizeObserver value) over the 400 fallback; document that defaultSnap/snapTo clamp.
  - **Done when:** In dev, passing descending or out-of-range snapPoints logs a warning; an e2e test with valid ascending snaps confirms downward drag steps down/closes and upward drag grows, using measured height.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`OVL-DX-001`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** Every confirm flow re-hand-builds the action row. dialog.tsx exposes only `actions?: ReactNode`, so consumers wire two <TKButton>s plus onClose/onConfirm state by hand — see examples/trailhead/src/features/profile/Profile.tsx:170-178 (cancel + destructive disconnect) and the same shape repeats in RescheduleSlot/Checkout. The common case (one cancel, one confirm) is ~8 lines of JSX every time, and it is easy to forget to wire onConfirm or close-on-cancel.
  - **Proposal:** Add a declarative confirm shorthand to TKDialog: `confirmLabel?: ReactNode`, `cancelLabel?: ReactNode`, `confirmTone?: 'accent'|'red'` (defaults from `tone`), `onConfirm?`, `loading?: boolean`. When `confirmLabel` is set, TKDialog renders the button row itself (cancel = plain, confirm = filled/destructive), auto-calls onClose after cancel, and shows a spinner on the confirm button while `loading`. `actions` stays as the escape hatch for custom rows. Common case collapses to `<TKDialog open onClose icon='warning' tone='red' title=… text=… confirmLabel='Disconnect' onConfirm={disconnect} />`.
- [x] **`OVL-DX-002`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** The toast API is anemic and forces verbose `show({...})` for anything beyond success/error. toasts.tsx:19-23 TKToastApi only has `show/success/error`; there is no `warning`/`info`, no way to dismiss a specific toast programmatically, and no promise integration. A loading→resolved/rejected flow (the canonical async toast) must be choreographed by the caller with manual show() calls and timing.
  - **Proposal:** Expand TKToastApi to `{ show(opts) => id; dismiss(id); success/info/warning/error(text, opts?); promise(p, {loading, success, error}) }`. `show` returns the toast id so callers can dismiss early; `info`/`warning` reuse the existing icon/color/assertive plumbing (`info`→accent/polite, `warning`→orange/polite). `promise` shows a non-auto-dismiss loading toast, then swaps to success/error on settle — covering the most common Mini App pattern (submit → spinner → result) in one line.
- [ ] **`OVL-DX-003`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** The kit's a11y primitives are private, so a consumer building any bespoke overlay cannot inherit correct behavior. shared.tsx exports useOverlayA11y, useMountTransition and Scrim, but the barrel (composites/overlays.tsx) re-exports only TKFrame from shared — useOverlayA11y/useMountTransition/Scrim/useOverlayLayer/useScrollLock/useVerticalSwipeGuard are all absent from the public surface. Anyone needing a custom popover/menu must re-implement focus trapping, the Escape LIFO stack, scroll lock and the Telegram swipe guard from scratch, and will get it wrong.
  - **Proposal:** Publicly export the overlay building blocks under a documented `useOverlay()` composition entry: re-export `useOverlayA11y`, `useMountTransition`, `Scrim`, plus the internal `useScrollLock`/`useOverlayLayer`/`useVerticalSwipeGuard`/`useBackIntercept` as a cohesive `@/overlays/primitives` group. Optionally bundle them into a single `useOverlay({ open, onClose, onConfirm, ref, dismissible })` hook returning `{ mounted, closing, scrimProps, panelProps }` so a custom overlay is a11y-correct by default. This is the asChild/escape-hatch for the whole family.
- [ ] **`OVL-DX-004`** · docs · effort L
  - **Pain:** There is zero Storybook/docs coverage for the entire overlays family — `find ... -name '*.stories.*'` and the grep for stories both return nothing across packages/uikit/src. New consumers can only learn the API by reading source or copying from examples/trailhead, so non-obvious contracts (TKSheet snapPoints semantics, TKPopper role variants, toast position needing a positioned ancestor, dialog onConfirm-on-Enter) are undiscoverable and easy to misuse.
  - **Proposal:** Add a `composites/overlays/*.stories.tsx` set wrapped in TKFrame: Dialog (confirm/destructive/with-input/keyboard-center), ActionSheet (short + long-list), Sheet (content-mode, snapPoints, non-dismissible, imperative snapTo), Toasts (success/error/warning/promise/with-action), Popper/Tooltip (each placement + autoFlip + role variants). Each story carries a copy-pasteable snippet and an a11y note. This is the single highest-leverage discoverability fix and doubles as visual regression coverage.
- [ ] **`OVL-DX-005`** · a11y · effort S · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** TKPopper's `role='dialog'` type promises a focus-trapping modal popover (documented at popper-tooltip.tsx:36-38 'a modal popover that traps focus') but the component implements no trap, no focus move, no scrim and no aria-modal — it is a non-modal anchored div regardless of role. A consumer who follows the type to build an actionable menu/popover ships something inaccessible, and there is no menu keyboard navigation for `role='menu'` either. (Builds on red-team OVL-002 by making the type honest rather than just flagging the gap.)
  - **Proposal:** Narrow TKPopper's public type to what it actually is — non-modal anchored content (`role?: 'tooltip'|'menu'|'listbox'|'group'`) — and remove the misleading `'dialog'` option and its JSDoc. For genuinely modal anchored content, point consumers to TKSheet/TKDialog (or the OVL-DX-003 primitives). If a modal popover is in scope, gate it behind an explicit `modal` prop that opts into useOverlayA11y + Scrim + aria-modal so the behavior matches the role. The type can no longer over-promise.
- [ ] **`OVL-DX-006`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** Toasts silently no-op unless the consumer happens to have a positioned ancestor and renders the provider in the right place. toasts.tsx:81 uses `position:'absolute'` against the nearest positioned ancestor (the TKProvider root) — but there is no runtime guidance, no portal, and `position` (top/bottom) is the only knob. A toast can land off-screen or behind sticky chrome with no error (related to red-team OVL-010 but framed as a default/DX problem: make the safe path the default).
  - **Proposal:** Make toasts robust by default: portal the toast stack to the TKProvider root (the `.tk` element) via createPortal — matching how TKPopper already resolves `anchor.closest('.tk') ?? document.body` — so placement no longer depends on the provider's position in the tree. In dev, if no `.tk`/positioned ancestor is found, console.warn once with a one-line fix. Keep `position` but add `'top'|'bottom'` safe-area-aware defaults so the common case is `<TKToastProvider>` with zero layout knowledge required.
- [ ] **`OVL-DX-007`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKActionSheet can't express the destructive-confirm pattern it is most used for, forcing a second dialog. action-sheet.tsx TKActionItem has `danger` (color only) but no per-item `disabled`, no `description`/subtitle, and no section grouping — and the destructive item still fires onSelect immediately with no confirmation affordance. examples/trailhead GuideDirectory builds message/share/mute items by hand; a 'Delete' item there would need a follow-up TKDialog wired separately.
  - **Proposal:** Extend TKActionItem with `disabled?: boolean`, `description?: ReactNode` (secondary line), and optional `confirm?: { title; confirmLabel }` so a destructive item presents an inline confirm step before firing onSelect — TKActionSheet owns the chained confirm internally. Also accept `items: (TKActionItem | { divider: true })[]` (or `sections`) for visual grouping. This turns the sheet into a complete action menu instead of a styled button list, removing the most common reason to pair it with a Dialog.
- [ ] **`OVL-DX-008`** · types · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKSheet's imperative snap API isn't type-safe and silently degrades. sheet.tsx:107-113 snapTo(index) is a no-op when snapPoints is undefined and clamps out-of-range indices silently; snapIndex is a plain number with no relation to the configured points. A consumer calling sheetRef.current.snapTo(2) on a 2-point sheet gets no type error and no runtime feedback — the misconfiguration is invisible (compounds red-team OVL-012's silent clamping).
  - **Proposal:** Tighten the imperative contract: type snapTo as `snapTo(index: number): void` documented to clamp, but add `snapTo` returning the resolved index, expose `snapCount: number` on the handle, and in dev console.warn when snapTo is called with snapPoints undefined or index out of range. Better: make snapPoints a const-friendly tuple and infer `snapIndex` as a literal union (`0 | 1 | …`) via a generic on TKSheetHandle so out-of-range indices are a compile error. Misuse becomes visible at the type or dev-warning layer instead of vanishing.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

ASSUMPTIONS & COVERAGE GAPS: (1) Reduced motion: the overlays themselves are correctly neutralized because the global `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` rule in tokens/tokens.css (line 332) collapses --tk-t1/t2/t3 to 1ms under the .tk scope, and the inline overlays render inside the .tk root — so I did NOT raise a reduced-motion finding for Dialog/Sheet/ActionSheet/Toast. The ONE residual gap (not raised as its own finding to stay within the cap): TKPopper's `useFixed` branch portals into document.body (popper-tooltip.tsx:78,231) which is OUTSIDE the .tk scope, so its `tk-modal-in` animation is NOT collapsed under reduced-motion when no .tk ancestor exists — worth a follow-up. (2) I did not run the suite; the toast slice bug (OVL-001) is confirmed by direct Node evaluation of Array.prototype.slice(-0), and the existing packages/uikit/test/toasts.test.tsx only exercises max={2}, so max={1} is genuinely untested. (3) TEST COVERAGE: there are ZERO *.test.tsx and ZERO *.stories.tsx co-located in src/composites/overlays; the only overlay test is packages/uikit/test/toasts.test.tsx (toasts only). Dialog, Sheet, ActionSheet, Popper and Tooltip have no dedicated unit tests in the searched paths — focus trap/restore, Escape-stack LIFO (shared.tsx overlayEscapeStack), useOverlayLayer z-ordering, and useScrollLock reference counting are exercised only indirectly (if at all) via the broader audit-fixes/a11y-semantics/m6-nav suites. (4) The popper first-paint uses a stale default popperSize {220,80} (popper-tooltip.tsx:65) for one frame before useLayoutEffect measures, which can momentarily misposition/mis-clamp a large tooltip; low severity, folded into the general popper assessment rather than raised separately. (5) useBackIntercept / useSafeArea / useWebApp behavior was read from packages/telegram/src/provider.tsx and the re-export shim foundation/telegram.tsx (export * from \"@tg-mini-app/telegram\"); the overlays' back-button and swipe-guard wiring (scroll-lock, vertical-swipe-guard, layering) all reference-count correctly and looked sound — no finding raised there.

---

### Foundation — theme, i18n, telegram runtime hooks, chrome, options (providers + runtime adapters)

`FND` · `packages/uikit/src/foundation` · 11 findings (0🔴 3🟠 6🟡 2⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-TG` Telegram runtime correctness · `D-STATE` Async state & promise lifecycle · `D-API` API ergonomics & footguns · `D-THEME` Tokens/theming knobs · `D-EDGE` Robustness / races / cleanup · `D-TYPES` TypeScript surface quality · `D-A11Y` Accessibility / reduced-motion · `D-SEC` Link/URL safety

#### Findings

- [x] **`FND-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — useShare.shareToStory promise hangs forever when no story-result event fires
  - **Where:** `packages/telegram/src/capabilities.ts:254-272`
  - **Evidence:** if (canShareToStory && wa?.shareToStory) { try { wa.shareToStory(...) } ... // Leave status pending — never report a synthetic success. ... return new Promise<boolean>((resolve) => { pendingStory.current = resolve; }); }
  - **Impact:** shareToStory is fire-and-forget in Telegram and emits no shareMessageSent/shareMessageFailed event on most clients. `const ok = await share.shareToStory(url)` then never resolves: the await suspends indefinitely, leaving the calling component stuck in a 'sharing' spinner with no timeout and no way out. There is also no unmount cleanup, so the captured `resolve` (and any closure it holds) leaks.
  - **Fix:** Resolve optimistically (return true after a successful synchronous call) OR add a timeout that settles to a neutral result, and reject/resolve pendingStory.current in a useEffect cleanup on unmount so an in-flight await never dangles.
  - **Done when:** Add a test: call shareToStory with a mock that fires no event; assert the returned promise settles (success/timeout) within a bounded time, and that unmounting the hook settles any pending promise instead of leaking it.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — capabilities.ts:269-271 returns a Promise stored in pendingStory.current that resolves only via the shareMessageSent/shareMessageFailed events; the comment (266-268) itself admits status "stays pending" if neither fires. No timeout and no unmount cleanup of pendingStory (grep shows it's only nulled inside settleStory), so a fire-and-forget shareToStory with no story event hangs the await forever.
- [x] **`FND-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-THEME` — motionSpeed={0} poisons every motion token via calc(.../0)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/foundation/theme.tsx:42`
  - **Evidence:** if (knobs.motionSpeed != null) vars["--tk-ms"] = knobs.motionSpeed; // consumed as: --tk-t1: calc(140ms / var(--tk-ms)) in tokens.css:35-37
  - **Impact:** `motionSpeed` is typed `number` with no clamp. A consumer passing 0 (a natural 'no motion' attempt) makes every duration `calc(140ms / 0)`, which is an invalid computed value — transitions/animations across the entire subtree silently break or jump. Negative values produce negative durations. There is no validation or documented safe range enforced.
  - **Fix:** Clamp in tkThemeVars (e.g. `Math.max(0.05, motionSpeed)`), or restructure tokens to multiply rather than divide so 0 means 'instant'. For a true reduced-motion intent, route motionSpeed=0 to a 0ms path instead of a divide.
  - **Done when:** Render TKProvider motionSpeed={0}; assert getComputedStyle of a token-driven element yields a finite, non-broken transition-duration (e.g. 0ms), not an invalid calc.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — theme.tsx:42 assigns motionSpeed raw to --tk-ms with no clamp, and tokens.css:35-37 consume it as calc(140ms / var(--tk-ms)); motionSpeed=0 produces calc(140ms / 0) (invalid) and negatives produce negative durations across the whole subtree. No validation exists anywhere.
- [x] **`FND-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-EDGE` — telegram theme mirroring writes global <html> vars and never cleans up; multiple providers fight
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/foundation/theme.tsx:56-64,121-124`
  - **Evidence:** root.style.setProperty(`--tg-theme-${key.replace(/_/g, "-")}`, value); ... useEffect(() => { if (telegram) applyTelegramThemeVars(wa?.themeParams); }, [telegram, wa]);
  - **Impact:** applyTelegramThemeVars mutates document.documentElement (global), with no cleanup and no removeProperty on unmount or when `telegram` flips to false. Two TKProviders (e.g. a portalled overlay subtree, or a demo embedding two themed roots) clobber each other's --tg-theme-* on <html>; a provider that unmounts leaves stale Telegram colors painted on the page root. Toggling `telegram` off does not revert.
  - **Fix:** Scope the mirrored vars to the provider's own rootRef element instead of documentElement, or track applied keys and removeProperty them in the effect cleanup; re-apply on every relevant change.
  - **Done when:** Mount provider with telegram + themeParams, unmount it, assert the --tg-theme-* properties are removed from the host element; mount two providers with different params and assert each subtree resolves its own values.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — applyTelegramThemeVars (theme.tsx:56-64) calls setProperty on document.documentElement with no matching removeProperty anywhere, and the effects at lines 121-124 return no cleanup. Stale --tg-theme-* vars persist on unmount and toggling telegram off never reverts; multiple providers clobber the shared <html> root.
- [x] **`FND-004`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — Back-button want-count and queue are module-level mutable globals — duplicate package instance silently breaks native Back
  - **Where:** `packages/telegram/src/provider.tsx:21-27,53-73`
  - **Evidence:** const backQueue: BackHandler[] = []; let backButtonWant = 0; const backButtonListeners = new Set<() => void>();
  - **Impact:** The whole back-intercept mechanism relies on a single physical module instance (the package even documents itself as a peer for this reason). If a consumer's bundler duplicates @tg-mini-app/telegram (version mismatch, pnpm hoist miss, mixing the deprecated uikit re-export with a direct dep), useBackIntercept bumps one module's counter while the provider reads another's — the native Back button never shows, and a Back press/edge-swipe closes the entire Mini App instead of popping an open sheet. The failure is invisible until a real device.
  - **Fix:** Anchor the queue/want-count on a window-scoped singleton (e.g. a Symbol on globalThis) so duplicate instances share state, and/or warn in dev when two instances initialize. Document the single-instance requirement in the package peer setup.
  - **Done when:** Simulate two module instances sharing the same globalThis singleton; assert useBackIntercept in instance A makes the provider in instance B show BackButton and route the press.
- [ ] **`FND-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TYPES` — tkFormat silently swallows missing/typo'd placeholders to empty string
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/foundation/i18n.tsx:209-211`
  - **Evidence:** return template.replace(/\{(\w+)\}/g, (_match, key: string) => String(vars[key] ?? ""));
  - **Impact:** Placeholder names are untyped strings unconnected to the vars object. A locale override like `ratingValue: "{value} sur {max}"` mistyped as `{valeur}` renders as an empty gap with zero warning; a caller forgetting a var (`tkFormat(t.page, {})`) yields 'Page ' — a broken aria-label/visible string shipped silently. Nothing surfaces the mismatch in dev or tests.
  - **Fix:** In dev, leave the literal `{key}` (or console.warn) when a var is missing instead of collapsing to ''; optionally type tkFormat with a template-literal generic so required keys are inferred from the template.
  - **Done when:** tkFormat('Page {page}', {}) returns a visibly-broken or warned result in dev (not 'Page '); add a test asserting missing-var behavior is detectable.
- [x] **`FND-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — useViewport reports isExpanded:true and useActivity isActive:true outside Telegram — fallback masks real 'unknown'
  - **Where:** `packages/telegram/src/layout.ts:47-49`
  - **Evidence:** isExpanded: wa?.isExpanded ?? true, isFullscreen: wa?.isFullscreen ?? false,
  - **Impact:** Outside Telegram (plain browser, SSR, mock) isExpanded defaults to true and height/stableHeight are undefined. A consumer gating an 'Expand' affordance on `!isExpanded` hides it in the browser preview, and any layout math reading height gets undefined with no isSupported signal on the height fields. The boolean fallback is indistinguishable from a genuinely-expanded client, so dev/preview behavior diverges from device.
  - **Fix:** Expose an isSupported/inTelegram flag consumers can branch on (useViewport already returns isSupported via wa?.expand — document that height is undefined when false), and consider leaving isExpanded undefined rather than defaulting true so 'unknown' is representable.
  - **Done when:** Render useViewport with no WebApp; assert isSupported is false and that consumers can distinguish 'no client' from 'expanded' (height undefined + isSupported false documented/tested).
- [x] **`FND-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-SEC` — useTelegramLinks.openLink / openInvoice forward consumer URLs without scheme validation
  - **Where:** `packages/telegram/src/capabilities.ts:96-104,196-203`
  - **Evidence:** openLink: (url, options) => { if (wa?.openLink) { wa.openLink(url, ...); return true; } ... window.open(url, "_blank", "noopener,noreferrer"); ...
  - **Impact:** openLink/openTelegramLink/openInvoice pass any string straight to window.open / location.href / native openers. If a Mini App feeds a server- or user-derived URL (deep link, invoice slip) without sanitizing, a `javascript:` or unexpected-scheme URL reaches window.location.href (openTelegramLink) — a DOM-XSS/redirect footgun the kit invites by presenting these as safe one-call helpers. window.open at least gets noopener, but openTelegramLink uses raw location.href.
  - **Fix:** Validate scheme (allow http/https/tg/mailto/tel) before opening; reject or no-op javascript:/data: and return false. Document that callers must pass trusted absolute URLs.
  - **Done when:** openLink('javascript:alert(1)') and openTelegramLink with a non-tg scheme return false / do not navigate; add a test asserting disallowed schemes are blocked.
- [x] **`FND-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — Native CloudStorage path skips key/value validation that the localStorage fallback enforces
  - **Where:** `packages/telegram/src/storage.ts:94-103,154-156`
  - **Evidence:** set: (key, value) => new Promise((resolve, reject) => { try { assertValidEntry(key, value); } catch (err) { reject(err); return; } storageApi.setItem!(key, value, ...) }) // but getMany/getItems and removeMany on the native path never call assertValidEntry
  - **Impact:** Only set() validates. getMany/removeMany pass arbitrary keys to Telegram's getItems/removeItems; an invalid key (length>128 or illegal chars) is silently sent and the native call may reject with an opaque error or return partial results, while the same input on the localStorage fallback behaves differently — so behavior diverges between in-Telegram and browser-preview, exactly where bugs hide.
  - **Fix:** Run KEY_PATTERN validation on every key in get/getMany/remove/removeMany on both paths so a bad key fails fast and identically in both environments.
  - **Done when:** Call getMany(['ok','bad key!']) on the native path; assert it rejects (or filters) consistently with the localStorage fallback for the same input.
- [x] **`FND-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — useKeyboard toggles a process-wide .tk-kb-open class on ALL roots via querySelectorAll
  - **Where:** `packages/telegram/src/device.ts:85-88`
  - **Evidence:** function tkSetKeyboardOpenClass(open: boolean) { ... document.querySelectorAll(".tk").forEach((el) => el.classList.toggle("tk-kb-open", open)); }
  - **Impact:** With multiple .tk roots (a page plus a portalled themed overlay, or two embedded apps), one keyboard event flips the class on every root, so an unrelated subtree gets lifted by a keyboard it doesn't contain. The ref-count guards only the clear-on-last-unmount, not which roots get the class — the open state is global, not per-root. layout thrash also: every focusin schedules an extra setTimeout(sync).
  - **Fix:** Scope the class to the root(s) actually containing the focused editable (walk up from document.activeElement to nearest .tk), or accept a target ref, instead of a blanket querySelectorAll.
  - **Done when:** Mount two .tk roots, focus an input in one; assert only that root receives .tk-kb-open.
- [ ] **`FND-010`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-API` — TKLocale is a fully-required flat interface — every new string is a breaking change for full-dictionary consumers
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/foundation/i18n.tsx:9-91`
  - **Evidence:** export interface TKLocale { done: string; back: string; ... openCalendar: string; } // all required; ruLocale must restate all 40+ keys
  - **Impact:** A team that builds a complete TKLocale (not Partial) — e.g. to share one typed dictionary across apps — breaks on every minor that adds a key (a TS error on their object literal), making string additions a semver hazard. ruLocale itself must be kept exhaustively in sync by hand; a missed key is a compile error rather than an English fallback. The provider only falls back per-key for Partial inputs.
  - **Fix:** Document that consumers should type their dictionaries as Partial<TKLocale> and rely on the provider's per-key English fallback; consider splitting TKLocale into namespaced sub-objects so component additions don't widen one flat required type.
  - **Done when:** Adding a new key to TKLocale does not produce a type error for a consumer passing Partial<TKLocale>; ruLocale completeness is enforced by a test, not just the type.
- [ ] **`FND-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-A11Y` — TKProvider theme is set only via data-theme/color-scheme on the .tk div, not synced to native reduced-motion or a documented motion-off path
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/foundation/theme.tsx:143-151`
  - **Evidence:** <div ... data-theme={theme} ... style={{ position: "relative", ...vars, ...style }}> // motionSpeed/motion knobs feed CSS only; no reduced-motion bridge
  - **Impact:** prefers-reduced-motion is honored only by tokens.css media queries; the provider's motion knobs (motion/motionSpeed) give no first-class 'off' switch and, per FND-002, 0 breaks rather than disables. A consumer wanting to respect an in-app accessibility toggle (separate from the OS setting) has no supported knob — they must hand-write CSS, the exact workaround the kit aims to remove.
  - **Fix:** Add a documented reducedMotion?: boolean prop (or motion:'none') that sets durations to 0ms safely, independent of the OS media query, and have it interact correctly with the divide-based motion tokens.
  - **Done when:** TKProvider with the reduced-motion prop yields 0ms/instant transitions on token-driven elements without invalid calc; documented in the provider's JSDoc.

#### DX proposals

- [x] **`FND-DX-001`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** Every app re-implements the same 3-layer provider ladder by hand. `examples/trailhead/src/main.tsx:99` + `AppFrame.tsx:35` and `examples/surface-composer/src/main.tsx:45` + `TelegramSurfaceFrame.tsx:23` both wire `TKTelegramProvider` → (bridge `useTelegramTheme()`/`useSafeArea` themselves) → `TKProvider theme=… telegram` → `TKLocaleProvider`/`TKToastProvider`. The theme bridge in `useTelegramThemeBridge.ts` exists ONLY because the kit makes you read the Telegram scheme and feed it back into `TKProvider` manually. Nothing ties the runtime provider to the visual provider, so the common case is ~30 lines of glue per app and easy to get wrong (forget `telegram`, forget to pass the live theme).
  - **Proposal:** Ship a single batteries-included root: `<TKApp webApp={…} haptics locale={ruLocale} preset='ios'>…</TKApp>` that composes `TKTelegramProvider` + `TKProvider` + `TKLocaleProvider` (+ optional `TKToastProvider`) and, by default, auto-syncs `theme` from `useTelegramTheme()` and `telegram` from `useHasNativeChrome()` so the in-DOM-vs-native decision and live light/dark mirroring are free. Allow `theme='auto' | TKTheme` so apps that own theme (the mock Platform Lab) can still override. Keep the individual providers exported for advanced nesting. Reduces the happy path from ~30 lines across two files to one element.
- [x] **`FND-DX-002`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** `TKProvider` (`packages/uikit/src/foundation/theme.tsx`) never connects the kit's motion system to the user's OS reduced-motion preference except via the static CSS `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block in `tokens.css:332`. There is no JS-observable `prefersReducedMotion` and no documented motion-off knob (red-team FND-011), and `motionSpeed` (theme.tsx:42) is a raw multiplier with no clamp, which is exactly what lets `motionSpeed={0}` poison `calc(140ms / var(--tk-ms))` (FND-002). A consumer building an accessible app has no first-class lever — they must reach into CSS.
  - **Proposal:** Add a `reduceMotion?: boolean | 'auto'` prop to `TKProvider`/`TKApp` (default `'auto'`). `'auto'` subscribes to `matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')` and, when reduced, sets a documented `data-tk-motion='off'` (or `--tk-ms` to a safe floor) so motion-heavy components quiet down without the consumer touching CSS. Expose a `useReducedMotion(): boolean` hook for components/consumers that animate in JS. This makes a11y-by-default the path of least resistance and gives the motion-off path the red-team flagged as missing.
- [ ] **`FND-DX-003`** · types · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** `TKLocale` (`packages/uikit/src/foundation/i18n.tsx:9-91`) is a flat, fully-required interface of ~45 keys (red-team FND-010): a consumer who types out a complete dictionary breaks at compile time on every kit release that adds a string, and `TKLocaleProvider` already accepts `Partial<TKLocale>` so the required-ness buys nothing at the provider boundary. Both example apps work around this by defining `KIT_LOCALES` as `Partial` records (`examples/*/src/i18n/index.tsx`). There is no namespacing, so it is impossible to scan which strings a given component needs.
  - **Proposal:** Make the public-facing dictionary type `Partial<TKLocale>` everywhere a consumer supplies one (keep `TKLocale` as the internal resolved shape used by `enLocale`/`ruLocale`). Provide `defineLocale(partial)` that returns a fully-typed dictionary with autocomplete but no required-key error, and optionally group keys by component (`TKLocale['otp']['resend']`) so adding strings to a new component is non-breaking for existing full dictionaries. Net: new kit strings stop being breaking changes for dictionary authors.
- [ ] **`FND-DX-004`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** `tkFormat` (`packages/uikit/src/foundation/i18n.tsx:209`) replaces missing/typo'd `{placeholder}` slots with empty string (red-team FND-005), so `tkFormat(locale.ratingValue, { vlaue: 3, max: 5 })` silently renders ' of 5' with no warning at author time or runtime. It also has no number/plural/date formatting, so apps bolt on a separate `@tg-mini-app/intl` (`packages/intl`) for anything beyond literal interpolation — two formatting systems for one app.
  - **Proposal:** In DEV, have `tkFormat` `console.warn` (once per template+key) when a `{slot}` has no matching var, and leave the literal `{slot}` visible instead of collapsing to '' so the gap is obvious in the UI. Add a typed overload `tkFormat<T extends string>(template: T, vars: ExtractSlots<T>)` (template-literal slot extraction) so a missing/misspelled key is a compile error. Optionally accept an `Intl.NumberFormat`-aware value (`{count, number}`) to bridge to the intl package for the common 'N items' case. Misuse becomes loud instead of silent.
- [x] **`FND-DX-005`** · defaults · effort S
  - **Pain:** Outside Telegram `useViewport` returns `isExpanded:true` (`packages/telegram/src/layout.ts:48`) and `useActivity` returns `isActive:true` (`packages/telegram/src/capabilities.ts:29`) — confident lies that mask the real 'unknown' (red-team FND-006). A consumer gating UI on `isExpanded` gets the in-Telegram answer in a browser preview, so the fallback path never renders in dev. The `isSupported` flag is the only escape, but it sits on the side and is easy to ignore; there is no single 'am I really inside Telegram' primitive co-located with these hooks (only `useHasNativeChrome` lives over in `foundation/chrome.ts`).
  - **Proposal:** Promote a shared `useTelegramEnvironment(): { inside: boolean; reason: 'webapp' | 'mock' | 'browser' }` (built on `useWebApp()`/`getTelegramWebApp()`) and re-export it from the foundation barrel so the native-vs-fallback decision has one obvious home. Document (and lint via JSDoc) that `isExpanded`/`isActive` are only meaningful when `isSupported`. Optionally let `useViewport`/`useActivity` expose the value as `boolean | undefined` behind an opt-in so honest consumers can branch on 'unknown' without reverse-engineering it from `isSupported`.
- [ ] **`FND-DX-006`** · perf · effort M
  - **Pain:** `tkSetKeyboardOpenClass` (`packages/telegram/src/device.ts:85-88`) toggles `.tk-kb-open` on EVERY `.tk` root in the document via `querySelectorAll('.tk')` on every visualViewport resize/scroll (red-team FND-009). In an app with several `TKProvider` subtrees this both fights between roots and walks the whole DOM on a high-frequency event — a perf-by-default footgun. The hook also has no way to scope the lift to the root that owns the focused input.
  - **Proposal:** Scope keyboard state to the nearest provider: have `TKProvider` register its root node in a context, and `useKeyboard` toggle `.tk-kb-open` only on the root that contains `document.activeElement` (or accept a `ref`), instead of a process-wide `querySelectorAll`. Throttle the sync to a rAF. Default behavior stays the same for single-root apps but stops the multi-root fight and the per-event full-tree scan.
- [ ] **`FND-DX-007`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** The foundation surface is large (theme knobs, ~40 hooks across `packages/telegram/src`, locale, options) but the only worked examples live inside the demo apps. There is no Storybook/MDX page showing the canonical provider setup, the `telegram`/`theme`/`preset` knob matrix, or a copy-pasteable 'first 5 lines' for a new Mini App. New consumers reverse-engineer the wiring from `trailhead/main.tsx` (which is tangled with TonConnect, mock-vs-real bootstrap, and deep-link knobs that are irrelevant to the kit).
  - **Proposal:** Add a Storybook 'Getting started' + 'Theming' MDX pair under the kit: (1) the minimal `TKApp`/provider snippet that runs in a plain browser, (2) a live knob playground for `accent`/`roundness`/`motion`/`motionSpeed`/`fontSize`/`preset`/`theme`/`telegram` rendering representative components, (3) a 'inside vs outside Telegram' toggle backed by `createMockTelegram`. Gives a one-screen, copy-pasteable mental model and a place to surface the safe ranges (e.g. roundness 0.4–1.6, motionSpeed > 0) the JSDoc mentions but nothing enforces.
- [ ] **`FND-DX-008`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** `applyTelegramThemeVars` (`packages/uikit/src/foundation/theme.tsx:56-64`) writes `--tg-theme-*` onto the global `<html>` element whenever `telegram` is set and never cleans up, so two `TKProvider`s (e.g. a real subtree + a Storybook/preview frame, or app + embedded widget) clobber each other's globals (red-team FND-003). The `telegram` prop is a single boolean with no scoping story, so the 'mirror into THIS root, scoped' case — which is what a multi-surface app like surface-composer actually wants — is impossible.
  - **Proposal:** Make Telegram theme mirroring scope-aware and reversible: write `--tg-theme-*` onto the provider's own `rootRef` node (already present at theme.tsx:132) instead of `document.documentElement`, and on unmount/`telegram` flip remove the keys it set (track applied keys in a ref). Add `telegram?: boolean | 'global' | 'scoped'` (default `'scoped'`) so the rare app that genuinely needs the html-level vars opts in explicitly. Eliminates the cross-provider fight and the leak while keeping the one-prop ergonomics.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

Scope: foundation/telegram.tsx and foundation/chrome.ts are thin re-export shims over @tg-mini-app/telegram, so the substantive runtime logic was audited in packages/telegram/src (provider, buttons, layout, storage, capabilities, identity, device); these are the files foundation re-exports and what consumers actually run. theme.tsx and i18n.tsx hold real logic; options.ts is a small pure normalizer and looked clean (tkOptionItem/tkFlattenOptions handle string|item|group correctly; one minor: duplicate option `value`s are not de-duped, but that is a consumer concern, not flagged).\n\nWell-covered already (not flagged): runtime-absent fallbacks and isSupported gating are thorough and well-tested (telegram-capabilities.test.tsx, telegram-runtime-policy.test.tsx); version gating via tkSupports is consistently applied; CloudStorage value cap correctly measures code points not bytes (storage.ts:43-53); MainButton setParams merge/reset of color+icon is handled carefully (buttons.ts:128-142); initData trust boundary is well-documented as display-only (storage.ts:222-260).\n\nAssumptions / could not fully verify at runtime: FND-002 (calc/0) is inferred from tokens.css:35-37 + theme.tsx:42 — not reproduced in a browser. FND-004 duplicate-instance hazard is architectural inference from the module-global pattern + the package's own peer-dependency note; not reproduced. FND-001 is confirmed by code (no unmount cleanup of pendingStory, comment explicitly leaves status pending) but real-client event behavior (whether any client emits shareMessageSent for stories) was not tested on-device. Coverage gaps worth a test: no test asserts shareToStory settles when no event fires (FND-001), no test for motionSpeed=0 (FND-002), no test for telegram-theme var cleanup on unmount (FND-003), no test for native CloudStorage key validation on getMany/removeMany (FND-008), no multi-root useKeyboard test (FND-009).

---

### Template cards — ProductCard, PromotionalCard

`TCRD` · `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards` · 12 findings (0🔴 4🟠 5🟡 3⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility & keyboard operability · `D-STATE` State & numeric edge values · `D-THEME` Tokens / theming correctness · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView overflow & i18n length · `D-API` API ergonomics & consistency · `D-PERF` Re-render & memoization · `D-EDGE` Robustness of clickable composition

#### Findings

- [x] **`TCRD-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — TKStatTile: empty bars[] yields Math.max() = -Infinity → invalid 'height: -Infinity%' on every bar
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/promotional.tsx:202, 242-254`
  - **Evidence:** const max = Math.max(...bars); … height: `${(b / max) * 100}%`
  - **Impact:** A Mini App that passes an empty series (e.g. a metric with no data yet — a normal empty state) gets `const max = Math.max(...[])` === -Infinity. Each bar renders `height: -Infinity%`, which browsers reject, so the chart silently collapses to 0 and the component is unusable for the empty case it should handle gracefully. Verified: node `Math.max(...[])` → -Infinity.
  - **Fix:** Guard the domain: `const max = bars.length ? Math.max(...bars) : 0;` and clamp the height, e.g. `height: max > 0 ? `${(b / max) * 100}%` : 0`. Render a flat baseline (or skip the bar row) when `bars.length === 0`.
  - **Done when:** Render `<TKStatTile bars={[]} />`; assert no bar has a computed height containing 'Infinity' or 'NaN' and the component does not throw or collapse; add a unit test asserting `getComputedStyle(bar).height` is a finite px/percent.
  - **Verify:** ❌ refuted — promotional.tsx:202 does compute max=Math.max(...[])=-Infinity for an empty series, but the render uses bars.map() (line 243) which yields zero elements for [], so no bar is ever rendered with 'height: -Infinity%'. The claimed 'every bar renders -Infinity%' cannot occur; an empty array produces an empty (valid) bar container.
- [x] **`TCRD-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — TKStatTile: all-zero bars (e.g. [0,0,0]) divide by zero → 'height: NaN%'
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/promotional.tsx:242-254`
  - **Evidence:** height: `${(b / max) * 100}%` // with bars=[0,0,…] → max=0 → 0/0 = NaN
  - **Impact:** A legitimately-zero week of activity (0 sessions, 0 km — trailhead literally feeds session counts) makes `max` 0, so every bar is `height: NaN%`. Browsers drop the property and the bars jump to their min height instead of reading as 'empty', misrepresenting the data. Verified: node `(0/Math.max(...[0]))*100` → NaN.
  - **Fix:** Compute `const max = Math.max(1, ...bars);` (or guard `max <= 0`) so the divisor is never 0; treat all-zero as a flat empty chart.
  - **Done when:** Render `<TKStatTile bars={[0,0,0]} />`; assert each bar's height resolves to a finite value (0%) and no inline style string contains 'NaN'.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — With bars=[0,0,0], max=Math.max(0,0,0)=0 (line 202), and the three mapped elements each get height:`${(0/0)*100}%` = 'height: NaN%' (line 249). Verified in node: [0,0,0].map -> [NaN,NaN,NaN].
- [x] **`TCRD-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — TKBookingCard action is a clickable <span> with no role/tabIndex/keyboard — and it carries real handlers (check-in / reschedule)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/promotional.tsx:157-172`
  - **Evidence:** <span onClick={onAction} style={{ … cursor: "pointer" }}>{actionLabel}</span>
  - **Impact:** In trailhead this exact `onAction` is wired to onCheckIn/onReschedule/onCancel (examples/trailhead/src/features/trips/TripsList.tsx:117,123,140). The action is unreachable by keyboard, invisible to screen readers as a control (no role/name beyond text), and not focusable — it fails Playwright a11y gates and WCAG 2.1.1. A consumer shipping booking flows has a non-operable primary action for keyboard/AT users.
  - **Fix:** Render the action as a real `<button type="button">` (or `TKButton variant="text"`); keep the inline-flex/icon styling but drop the bare span+cursor. If text-only styling is desired, add `role="button" tabIndex={0}` plus Enter/Space key handlers.
  - **Done when:** Tab to the booking action and activate with Enter and Space; assert onAction fires and the element is exposed with role=button and an accessible name in the a11y tree (axe/Playwright a11y snapshot passes).
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — promotional.tsx:157-171 renders the action as a bare <span onClick={onAction} style={cursor:'pointer'}> with no role/tabIndex/onKeyDown; trailhead wires this onAction to onReschedule/onCheckIn/onCancel (TripsList.tsx:117,123,140), so it is a real keyboard-inaccessible, AT-invisible control.
- [x] **`TCRD-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — TKProductCardA: whole card is a clickable <div> with no keyboard semantics
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/product.tsx:25-38`
  - **Evidence:** <div data-testid={testId} className="tk-press tk-press-soft" onClick={onClick} style={{ … cursor: onClick ? "pointer" : "default" }}>
  - **Impact:** surface-composer/trailhead use product tiles in grids; when `onClick` opens a detail view, the card is mouse/touch-only — no tabIndex, no role=button, no Enter/Space. Keyboard and switch-control users cannot open a product. Nested <img>/buttons further confuse AT because the outer div has no role. This blocks any consumer needing keyboard-accessible catalog navigation.
  - **Fix:** When `onClick` is provided, expose the card as a button: add `role="button" tabIndex={0}` and an onKeyDown for Enter/Space, or wrap with an accessible pressable primitive. Ensure the inner add button still stops propagation.
  - **Done when:** With `onClick` set, Tab focuses the card, Enter/Space fires onClick, and axe reports no 'interactive control is not keyboard accessible' violation.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — product.tsx:25-38 makes the whole card a <div onClick={onClick}> with cursor:pointer but no role/tabIndex/onKeyDown; the tk-press class (tokens.css:184) is visual-only (scale animation), so when a consumer passes onClick the tile is mouse/touch-only. Defect is intrinsic to the reusable component even though no example app currently passes onClick.
- [ ] **`TCRD-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — Favorite toggle is a 32×32 hit target (<44px) and never exposes pressed state
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/product.tsx:148-173`
  - **Evidence:** width: 32, height: 32, … aria-label={locale.toggleFavorite} … onClick={() => setFav(!isFav)} — no aria-pressed
  - **Impact:** 32px is below the 44px Telegram/WCAG touch-target minimum, easy to mis-tap on a phone; and the button never sets `aria-pressed={isFav}`, so AT users cannot tell whether the item is favorited (only the heart fill, which is color-only). A favorites feature is effectively unusable for AT users and fiddly on touch.
  - **Fix:** Give the button a ≥44px touch area (e.g. 36–40px visual with padded hit area, or `min-width/height:44px` via an invisible inset), and add `aria-pressed={isFav}` so state is announced.
  - **Done when:** Pointer/CSS shows the tappable area ≥44×44px; toggling favorite flips `aria-pressed` in the a11y tree; touch-target audit passes.
- [x] **`TCRD-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-THEME` — TKBannerCard hardcodes color:#fff and rgba white overlays — not theme/accent-aware, risks contrast failure on light accents
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/promotional.tsx:22-78`
  - **Evidence:** color: "#fff", … background: "rgba(255,255,255,.12)", … background: "rgba(255,255,255,.92)", color: "var(--tk-accent-ink)"
  - **Impact:** The gradient is derived from `--tk-accent`, but the foreground is always white. A consumer whose Telegram theme sets a light/pastel accent (common with `theme_params.button_color`) gets white-on-light text and decorations with insufficient contrast, and dark-mode correctness is left to chance. This is the family's only card that ignores semantic text tokens.
  - **Fix:** Use a semantic on-accent token for text (e.g. `var(--tk-on-accent)`) and token-driven overlays, or compute readable foreground via color-mix against `--tk-accent` instead of literal #fff/rgba(255,255,255,…).
  - **Done when:** With a light `--tk-accent`, automated contrast check on banner title/CTA text passes ≥4.5:1; visually verify in both Telegram light and dark themes.
- [ ] **`TCRD-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-RESP` — BookingCard name/subtitle and BannerCard title/text have no truncation — long i18n strings clip or wrap unbounded in narrow WebView
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/promotional.tsx:56-57, 128-131`
  - **Evidence:** <div style={{ fontSize: "var(--tk-fz-body)", fontWeight: 600 }}>{name}</div> // no whiteSpace/overflow/ellipsis (cf. ProductCardA title which has ellipsis)
  - **Impact:** ProductCardA truncates its title (product.tsx:55-65) but BookingCard's name/subtitle and BannerCard's title don't. A long localized place name or German/Russian string either wraps to several lines (breaking the fixed avatar-row rhythm and pushing status off-screen) or is clipped by the container `overflow:hidden` with no ellipsis affordance. Inconsistent behavior across the same family surprises consumers laying out lists.
  - **Fix:** Apply the same single-line ellipsis (or `-webkit-line-clamp`) treatment used in ProductCardA to BookingCard name/subtitle and BannerCard title/text, keeping the row layout stable.
  - **Done when:** Render each card at 320px width with a 60-char name/title; assert the text is clamped with an ellipsis (or N-line clamp) and the status/CTA stays inside the card bounds.
- [ ] **`TCRD-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — Confusing prop naming: `img` is the placeholder label while `src` is the photo; easy to mis-wire
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/product.tsx:13-16, 22, 40-44`
  - **Evidence:** /** Placeholder label when there is no photo. */ img?: string; /** Photo URL … */ src?: string; … img = "product photo" … <TKImg label={img} … />
  - **Impact:** A consumer reasonably reads `img` as 'the image URL' and passes a URL there while leaving `src` empty, getting a wireframe placeholder whose label is the full URL string and no actual photo. The naming directly contradicts the conventional HTML meaning of `img`/`src`, producing a silent footgun across product tiles.
  - **Fix:** Rename `img` to `placeholderLabel` (align with TKImage's `fallbackLabel`) and keep `src` for the photo; deprecate `img` with a JSDoc note for one release.
  - **Done when:** Type/JSDoc clearly distinguishes `src` (photo URL) from `placeholderLabel`; a story/test confirms passing only the photo URL via `src` renders the image and the placeholder label never shows a URL.
- [ ] **`TCRD-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — Add-to-cart / favorite actions have no disabled, loading, or rapid-tap guard
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/product.tsx:68-79, 202-204`
  - **Evidence:** onClick={(e) => { e.stopPropagation(); onAdd(); }} … <TKButton variant="tonal" size="sm" full icon="cart" onClick={onAdd}>
  - **Impact:** `onAdd` typically triggers an async cart mutation. There is no `loading`/`disabled` pass-through on either ProductCard variant, so a user can double/triple-tap and fire duplicate add-to-cart calls (a real concern in a laggy Telegram WebView), and the button cannot reflect in-flight or out-of-stock state. Consumers must wrap the card to debounce, defeating the template's purpose.
  - **Fix:** Add `addLoading?: boolean` / `addDisabled?: boolean` (and reuse TKButton's built-in `loading` which already blocks clicks and sets aria-busy). For ProductCardA's icon button, accept `disabled`.
  - **Done when:** Set the loading/disabled prop; assert repeated taps fire onAdd at most once and the control shows a busy/disabled state (aria-busy / disabled in the DOM).
- [x] **`TCRD-010`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-API` — No ref forwarding on any card; testId is the only DOM hook
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/product.tsx:22, 106`
  - **Evidence:** export function TKProductCardA({ … testId }) { … <div data-testid={testId} …> // plain function components, no forwardRef, no className/style passthrough on the root
  - **Impact:** Consumers cannot attach a ref (for IntersectionObserver lazy-mount, scroll-into-view, measuring, or animation libraries) or pass `className`/`style` to the card root, unlike sibling atoms (TKIconButton/TKButton use forwardRef + TKDomProps). In a virtualized product grid this forces a wrapper div, adding layout nodes. Inconsistent with the kit's own DOM-props convention.
  - **Fix:** Wrap each card in `forwardRef` and spread a `TKDomProps`/`className`/`style` passthrough onto the root, matching the atoms' pattern.
  - **Done when:** Pass a ref and className to each card; assert the ref resolves to the root element and the className lands on it without breaking existing styles.
- [ ] **`TCRD-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-PERF` — List/grid cards are not memoized and rebuild inline style objects every render
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/promotional.tsx:192-258`
  - **Evidence:** export function TKStatTile({ … bars = [5,8,6,10,9,13,12] … }) { const max = Math.max(...bars); … bars.map(...) } // no React.memo; default array literal is a fresh reference each render
  - **Impact:** surface-composer and trailhead render rows of StatTile/ProductCard. None are wrapped in React.memo, so a parent state change re-renders every card; the default `bars` literal is a new array identity each call, and Math.max + the bars.map run unconditionally. Minor in small lists, but for catalog grids it adds avoidable work and defeats memo at the call site.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the card components in `React.memo`, hoist default arrays to module constants, and (optionally) `useMemo` the max/heights. Document that consumers should pass stable handler/array references.
  - **Done when:** With a memo wrapper and stable props, re-rendering the parent does not re-render unchanged cards (verify via React Profiler or a render-count spy in a test).
- [ ] **`TCRD-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-A11Y` — ProductCardB price/oldPrice strikethrough conveys 'was/now' by visual styling only — no accessible savings semantics
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/cards/product.tsx:192-201`
  - **Evidence:** <span style={{ fontSize: "var(--tk-fz-title3)", fontWeight: 700 }}>{price}</span> … <span style={{ … textDecoration: "line-through" }}>{oldPrice}</span>
  - **Impact:** The relationship 'old price (struck through) vs current price' is communicated purely through line-through styling and position. A screen-reader user hears two bare numbers with no indication which is the discounted price, and the `discount` badge is positionally separate. Pricing clarity is a conversion-critical detail for commerce Mini Apps.
  - **Fix:** Wrap oldPrice in `<s>` with a visually-hidden 'was' / current-price label (or aria-label like 'Was {oldPrice}, now {price}'), so the savings are announced.
  - **Done when:** With oldPrice set, the a11y tree exposes a label distinguishing original vs current price (e.g. 'was X, now Y'); verify with a screen-reader snapshot or accessible-name assertion.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`TCRD-DX-001`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** The `image` concept is split across two props with non-obvious names: `img` is the placeholder *label* string and `src` is the *photo URL* (product.tsx:13-16, 93-96). At every real call site the example authors pass `img="camera"`/`img="tripod"` as a label (storybook/templates/cards.stories.tsx:25,33) while production photos go to `src` — exactly the mis-wire RT flagged in TCRD-008. Worse, the alt text is derived from `title` (product.tsx:41,139), so a developer has no per-card way to give an image its own alt. Two overlapping string props for one visual slot is the single biggest cause of confusion in this family.
  - **Proposal:** Collapse to a single `image` prop that accepts a shape the eye can't mis-wire: `image?: string | { src?: string; alt?: string; placeholder?: string; ratio?: string }`. A bare string is treated as `src`.
