Internally: `const img = typeof image === 'string' ? { src: image } : image ?? {}`, then render `<TKImage src={img.src} alt={img.alt ?? altFromTitle} fallbackLabel={img.placeholder} />` or `<TKImg label={img.placeholder} />` when no src. Keep `img`/`src` as deprecated aliases for one minor (JSDoc `@deprecated use \`image\``) so the change is additive. Result: the common case is `image="https://…"` (one line), and placeholder/alt are namespaced where they can't collide.
- [ ] **`TCRD-DX-002`** · defaults · effort S
  - **Pain:** `TKStatTile` computes `const max = Math.max(...bars)` and divides every bar by it (promotional.tsx:202,249) with zero guarding — empty `bars=[]` gives -Infinity heights (RT TCRD-001) and all-zero gives NaN (RT TCRD-002). The red team scoped this as a *bug*; the DX root cause is that the component asks the consumer to hand it pre-shaped sparkline data and silently produces invalid CSS instead of a safe, self-normalizing default. Every Trailhead call site (Train.tsx:117-120) passes hand-tuned tiny arrays just to avoid an ugly result.
  - **Proposal:** Make the spark self-normalizing and unbreakable by construction so consumers can throw any numbers at it. Signature: `bars?: number[]` stays, but internally `const safe = bars.filter(Number.isFinite); const max = safe.length ? Math.max(...safe, 0) : 0; const h = (b) => max > 0 ? Math.max(2, (Math.max(b,0)/max)*100) : 0;` — empty/all-zero collapses every bar to a flat baseline (height 0 → render a 2px stub or hide the spark row entirely) instead of NaN/-Infinity. Add `bars?: number[] | 'auto'` where omitting `bars` hides the chart row rather than injecting the fake demo array `[5,8,6,10,9,13,12]` (promotional.tsx:197) that ships as real-looking data when a dev forgets to wire it. A min-bar floor also fixes the invisible-bar problem for legitimately tiny values.
- [ ] **`TCRD-DX-003`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** Interactivity a11y is opt-out-by-omission across the family and consumers can't fix it from the outside: the whole `TKProductCardA` is a click `<div>` with no role/tabIndex/key handler (product.tsx:25-39, RT TCRD-004); `TKBookingCard`'s action is a click `<span>` carrying real check-in/reschedule handlers (promotional.tsx:158-171, RT TCRD-003); `TKBannerCard` forces `color:#fff` on whatever accent the theme supplies (promotional.tsx:26-27, RT TCRD-006). Because these are templates, the consumer has no escape hatch — they cannot pass a className or wrap the inner node to retrofit keyboard support. A Telegram Mini App built from these ships inaccessible by default.
  - **Proposal:** Make accessible interactivity the *only* path the API offers. (1) When `onClick`/`onAction` is present, render the interactive node as a real `<button type=button>` (BookingCard action) or give the card `role=button tabIndex={0}` + Enter/Space handler (CardA) — never a bare clickable div/span. Extract a tiny internal `useActivation(onAction)` returning `{ role, tabIndex, onKeyDown, onClick }` and spread it, so every card in the family is keyboard-correct from one helper. (2) For BannerCard, derive ink from the accent via the existing token (`color: var(--tk-accent-ink)` instead of `#fff`, and replace the two `rgba(255,255,255,.1x)` blobs with `color-mix(in srgb, var(--tk-accent-ink) …)`), so contrast holds on light accents. Net: consumers literally cannot construct an inaccessible or low-contrast card.
- [ ] **`TCRD-DX-004`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** These templates are closed boxes: there is no `className`, `style` (except StatTile), ref, or slot escape hatch on any card. A consumer who needs to add an analytics attribute, a wrapper link, a drag handle, or even a margin must either fork the component or wrap it in an extra div that breaks the layout. Trailhead already wraps `TKBookingCard` in `TKSwipeCell` and has to manage the relationship externally (TripsList.tsx:127). RT TCRD-010 noted the missing ref; the deeper DX gap is the absence of any composition seam at all.
  - **Proposal:** Add a minimal, consistent escape-hatch contract to every card: `className?`, `style?`, and `ref` forwarding via `forwardRef<HTMLDivElement>` (wires straight into the existing root `<div>`, merging with internal style). For the card-as-link / card-as-router-Link case, add an `asChild?: boolean` (Radix-style Slot) or at minimum an `as?: ElementType` on `TKProductCardA`/`TKBookingCard` so the clickable root can become an `<a>`/`<Link>` without nesting interactive elements. This unblocks the most common real-app needs (deep-linking a product, attaching `data-*` for analytics, custom spacing) without forking, and gives test code a real ref hook instead of relying on `testId` alone.
- [ ] **`TCRD-DX-005`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** Action props give no way to express pending/disabled state, so the common e-commerce flows are unsafe by default: `onAdd` and `onFavChange` fire on every tap with no `disabled`/`loading`/rapid-tap guard (product.tsx:68-79, 148-173; RT TCRD-009). In a Mini App, add-to-cart and check-in hit the network — a double-tap double-orders. There is also no way to reflect the optimistic 'adding…' state the UI needs, forcing consumers to build a wrapper around an action the card already owns.
  - **Proposal:** Promote actions from bare callbacks to a small optional state contract the card renders for you. Add `addState?: 'idle' | 'loading' | 'done' | 'disabled'` (and same for the booking action) plus `disabled?: boolean`. The card maps `loading`→spinner + `aria-busy` + suppressed re-fire, `done`→check icon for ~1.2s, `disabled`→dimmed + `aria-disabled` + no-op. Provide `onAdd?: () => void | Promise<void>`: if a promise is returned, the card auto-manages loading→done→idle internally so the one-line case `onAdd={() => api.addToCart(id)}` is rapid-tap-safe and shows feedback with zero extra wiring. This is the single biggest 'common case in one line' win for a shopping Mini App.
- [ ] **`TCRD-DX-006`** · types · effort S
  - **Pain:** `TKProductCardB` exposes the controlled/uncontrolled favorite pair (`fav`, `defaultFav`, `onFavChange`, product.tsx:98-100) with no type-level relationship, so nothing stops a consumer passing both `fav` and `defaultFav` (the classic controlled-and-uncontrolled mistake `useControllable` silently tolerates). And `TKProductCardA` accepts `onClick` *and* `onAdd` with no signal that the whole-card click and the add-button click are different intents — a dev can't tell from the types which one to wire. There is no dev-time warning for any of these misuses, and no JSDoc steering toward the right one.
  - **Proposal:** Tighten the contract two ways. (1) Type the favorite state as a discriminated pair so the impossible combo is unrepresentable: overload props as `({ fav: boolean; onFavChange: (v:boolean)=>void } | { defaultFav?: boolean; onFavChange?: (v:boolean)=>void })`, or keep runtime tolerance but add a `process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'` `console.warn('TKProductCardB: pass either `fav` (controlled) or `defaultFav` (uncontrolled), not both')`. (2) Add JSDoc on `onClick` vs `onAdd` clarifying 'card-level open' vs 'add-to-cart', and on the favorite props. Clear, named misuse warnings beat silent wrong behavior and cut the debugging loop.
- [ ] **`TCRD-DX-007`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** The Storybook coverage (storybook/templates/cards.stories.tsx) shows only the happy path: every story passes `img` as a label and never a real `src`, never an empty/zero `bars`, never the favorite/loading/disabled states, never long-i18n truncation (RT TCRD-007), and never the booking action keyboard path. So the exact failure modes the red team found are invisible in the one place a developer copy-pastes from — and the 'add a real photo' path (the most common production need) has no example at all.
  - **Proposal:** Add a 'States & edge cases' section to the cards story that doubles as the copy-paste source of truth: a `TKProductCardB` with a real `src` photo + loading + error fallback; a favorite in loading/disabled state; `TKStatTile` with `bars={[]}` and `bars={[0,0,0]}` rendering safely (the DX-002 fix made visible); a `TKBookingCard` whose action is reached by keyboard (focus ring shown); and a 'long i18n string' row demonstrating the new truncation. Pair it with a short MDX/JSDoc 'Recipes' block: 'product grid in one line', 'card as a link' (DX-004), 'controlled vs uncontrolled favorite' (DX-006). This is the cheapest way to make the right pattern the obvious one.
- [ ] **`TCRD-DX-008`** · perf · effort S
  - **Pain:** Every card rebuilds large inline style objects on each render and none are memoized (product.tsx and promotional.tsx throughout; RT TCRD-011). In the real consumers these render in lists/grids — `TripsList` maps `TKBookingCard` per booking and `SurfaceSlots`/`Train` render multiple `TKStatTile`s — so a parent state change (e.g. a swipe, a tab switch) re-creates dozens of style objects and re-renders untouched cards. There is also no `content-visibility` hint for off-screen cards in a long feed, which matters on low-end Telegram Android WebViews.
  - **Proposal:** Make these perf-safe by default since they are list primitives: wrap each card export in `React.memo` (props are mostly primitives/ReactNodes, so referential equality holds for the common case), and hoist the static style objects to module-level `const` literals, merging only the few dynamic bits (`cursor`, accent, `height%`) via spread. Optionally add `style={{ contentVisibility: 'auto', containIntrinsicSize: '…' }}` on the card root behind a `lazy?: boolean` prop for long feeds. Zero API change, and it removes the per-render allocation churn that bites exactly where these are used most.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

COVERAGE GAPS: There are NO test (*.test.tsx) or story (*.stories.tsx) files anywhere under packages/uikit/src/templates for this family — confirmed via find/grep — so every finding here is currently unguarded by CI/visual/a11y gates. Adding the missing Storybook stories + Playwright a11y snapshots is itself a prerequisite to verifying most acceptance checks (especially TCRD-003/004/005/012).\n\nSCOPE / IMPORT SAFETY: The two source files contain no window/document/navigator/WebApp access (grep clean), so SSR import safety (D-TG) is fine for this family and was intentionally dropped from the dimension set. No dangerouslySetInnerHTML and no file-input handling exist here, so D-SEC has no concrete finding — the only image path goes through TKImage (atoms/display/image.tsx), which renders a plain <img src>; if consumers feed untrusted/`javascript:`-style src that's an atom-level concern, not this family. No haptics/MainButton/BackButton are wired by these cards (they delegate to TKButton/TKIconButton), so D-TG runtime wiring is out of scope for the family.\n\nASSUMPTIONS: (1) Telegram's documented 44px touch-target guidance underlies TCRD-005; the 32px value is read directly from product.tsx:160-161. (2) The contrast risk in TCRD-006 assumes a light `--tk-accent` is reachable via Telegram theme_params — plausible but not exercised in the repo's current themes; severity set to medium accordingly. (3) TCRD-009 assumes onAdd is commonly async (cart mutation) — true for any real commerce consumer though the examples stub it. The numeric bugs TCRD-001/002 were reproduced directly with node (Math.max(...[])===-Infinity, (0/0)*100===NaN) and are not assumptions.\n\nSEVERITY RATIONALE: TCRD-001/002 are 'high' not 'critical' because they degrade rather than crash (browser drops the invalid height); TCRD-003/004 are 'high' because they make real, wired-up primary actions non-operable for keyboard/AT users in shipping example apps.

---

### Chat templates — MessageBubble, Messages, WriteBar

`CHT` · `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat` · 11 findings (0🔴 4🟠 5🟡 2⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView / i18n / RTL · `D-API` API ergonomics · `D-STATE` State model · `D-PERF` Performance · `D-GESTURE` Touch / input correctness · `D-TG` Telegram runtime · `D-THEME` Tokens / theming

#### Findings

- [x] **`CHT-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — WriteBar textarea has no accessible name when placeholder is omitted
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/write-bar.tsx:57-59`
  - **Evidence:** placeholder={placeholder}\n disabled={disabled}\n aria-label={placeholder}
  - **Impact:** placeholder is optional (TKWriteBarProps has no default), so a consumer who renders <TKWriteBar onSend={...} /> ships a message input with no visible placeholder AND aria-label={undefined} — the textarea is an unlabeled control, failing axe/Playwright a11y gates and leaving screen-reader users with a nameless edit field. The kit already has locale.send for the button but nothing for the field.
  - **Fix:** Add a locale key (e.g. locale.writePlaceholder / locale.composeLabel) and use aria-label={placeholder ?? locale.composeMessage}; never let aria-label be undefined. Optionally default placeholder to the locale string so the field is always labelled.
  - **Done when:** Render TKWriteBar with no placeholder; getByRole('textbox') resolves with a non-empty accessible name and axe reports no 'aria-input-field-name'/'label' violation.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — write-bar.tsx:8 makes placeholder optional with no default, and line 59 sets aria-label={placeholder}, so omitting it yields aria-label={undefined} on a textarea with no other label source (the locale.send at line 92 labels only the button). Unlabeled control.
- [x] **`CHT-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-API` — WriteBar is uncontrolled-only: no value/onChange/ref, cannot clear, prefill, or restore drafts
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/write-bar.tsx:6-23`
  - **Evidence:** export interface TKWriteBarProps {\n onSend: (text: string) => void;\n ...\n}\n...\nconst [text, setText] = useState(""); // text is private internal state; no value/onChange/defaultValue/inputRef props exported
  - **Impact:** A Mini App team cannot prefill a reply quote, restore a CloudStorage draft, programmatically clear after a failed send, or attach a ref for focus management — all common chat needs. There is no escape hatch, forcing a fork of the component. Also footgun: send() clears unconditionally even if onSend throws.
  - **Fix:** Add controlled props (value?/onChange?/defaultValue?) and inputRef?: Ref<HTMLTextAreaElement>; when controlled, derive text from props. Optionally make onSend support a returned boolean/promise to decide whether to clear.
  - **Done when:** With value/onChange passed, typing updates parent state and the bar reflects external value changes; passing inputRef gives focus()/clear access; clearing externally resets the textarea height.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — TKWriteBarProps (write-bar.tsx:6-18) exposes only onSend; text is private useState (line 23) and areaRef is internal (line 24) with no value/onChange/defaultValue/inputRef escape hatch, so prefill/clear/draft-restore/focus management are impossible without forking. send() also clears state right after onSend (lines 31-32).
- [x] **`CHT-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-RESP` — Long unbroken text/URLs overflow the bubble; float meta can collide with last line
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/message-bubble.tsx:31-46`
  - **Evidence:** maxWidth: "78%",\n padding: "8px 12px",\n borderRadius: "var(--tk-r-lg)",\n ... (no wordBreak / overflowWrap / whiteSpace)
  - **Impact:** In a narrow Telegram WebView a single long token (URL, hash, mention, CJK string) blows past the 78% cap and forces horizontal scroll of the whole feed. The meta row uses float:right + translateY(3px) (lines 50-61) with no clearing, so short last-line text overlaps the time/ticks. Real chat content (links, addresses) routinely triggers this.
  - **Fix:** Add overflowWrap:'anywhere' (or wordBreak:'break-word') and minWidth:0 on the bubble; render text in a block and put the meta in a flex row that wraps below long text instead of floating, or reserve trailing space with a non-breaking spacer.
  - **Done when:** A message with a 200-char no-space URL stays within the bubble (no horizontal scroll of the feed); a short outgoing message with time+read ticks shows no visual overlap at 320px width.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — message-bubble.tsx:31-46 sets maxWidth 78% but no wordBreak/overflowWrap/whiteSpace, and no global CSS supplies it (grep of tokens.css and src found none), so a long unbroken token overflows. Meta span uses float:right + translateY(3px) (lines 60-61) with no clearing, so a short last line collides with the time/ticks.
- [x] **`CHT-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-GESTURE` — Enter-to-send fires during IME composition — breaks CJK/voice input
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/write-bar.tsx:66-71`
  - **Evidence:** onKeyDown={(event) => {\n if (event.key === "Enter" && !event.shiftKey) {\n event.preventDefault();\n send();\n }\n }}
  - **Impact:** When a user composes Japanese/Chinese/Korean (or uses an IME/voice candidate list), pressing Enter to CONFIRM a candidate is intercepted as 'send', sending a half-composed message or losing the candidate. This is a hard blocker for non-Latin Mini App audiences and a known React textarea footgun.
  - **Fix:** Guard with the composing flag: check event.nativeEvent.isComposing (and/or onCompositionStart/End state) and return early while composing before calling send().
  - **Done when:** With an IME active, Enter selecting a composition candidate does not trigger onSend; Enter after composition ends sends normally.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — write-bar.tsx:66-71 fires send() on Enter when !shiftKey with no IME guard (no isComposing / nativeEvent.isComposing / keyCode 229 check), and no composition handlers exist anywhere in the chat templates, so Enter intercepts IME candidate confirmation.
- [x] **`CHT-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-PERF` — TKMessages re-renders every bubble on each parent update; no memo, virtualization, or scroll container
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/messages.tsx:11-25`
  - **Evidence:** export function TKMessages({ messages, testId, style }: TKMessagesProps) {\n return (\n <div ... style={{ display: "flex", flexDirection: "column", gap: 3, ...style }}>\n {messages.map((message, index) => {
  - **Impact:** The component is documented as a 'Chat feed' but has no own scroll/overflow, no auto-scroll-to-bottom, and TKMessageBubble is not memoized. In the trailhead consumer the parent re-renders on every WriteBar keystroke-derived state change, re-rendering the entire bubble list; a long thread (hundreds of messages) re-runs all inline-style objects each time, causing jank on low-end Telegram devices.
  - **Fix:** Wrap TKMessageBubble in React.memo; key/group computation is fine. Document/optionally provide a scroll container with stick-to-bottom behaviour, and offer a render-window/virtualization hook for long feeds.
  - **Done when:** Profiler shows unchanged bubbles do not re-render when an unrelated sibling state updates; a 500-message feed scrolls without dropped frames in a throttled WebView profile.
- [ ] **`CHT-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-STATE` — Auto-grow textarea height only resets in send()/onChange — stale tall box on external clear or disable
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/write-bar.tsx:28-34`
  - **Evidence:** const send = () => {\n const value = text.trim();\n if (!value) return;\n onSend(value);\n setText("");\n if (areaRef.current) areaRef.current.style.height = "auto";\n };
  - **Impact:** Height is driven by imperative el.style.height in onChange (lines 62-64) and reset only inside send(). There is no effect tying height to text, so any height reset that doesn't go through these paths (e.g. a controlled clear once CHT-002 is addressed, or a disable mid-compose) leaves the box frozen at the previous multi-line height. State and rendered size can diverge.
  - **Fix:** Derive height in a useLayoutEffect keyed on the value (reset to 'auto' then set to min(scrollHeight,120)) instead of scattering imperative writes, so any value change — internal or external — reconciles the height.
  - **Done when:** Type 5 lines, then clear the value through any path; the textarea collapses back to one row without requiring another keystroke.
- [ ] **`CHT-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-THEME` — Read vs delivered status conveyed by tick opacity alone — no semantic read color, low contrast
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/message-bubble.tsx:65-72`
  - **Evidence:** <TKIcon name="check" size={12} strokeWidth={2.6} />\n {status !== "sent" ? (\n <TKIcon name="check" size={12} ... style={{ marginLeft: -7, opacity: status === "read" ? 1 : 0.55 }} />\n ) : null}
  - **Impact:** Telegram users expect 'read' to be visually distinct (blue double-tick). Here read vs delivered differ only by the second tick's opacity (1 vs .55) over an already 0.75-opacity meta row, both inheriting on-accent color — the difference is nearly invisible and inaccessible to low-vision users. There is no semantic --tk-status-read token to recolor.
  - **Fix:** Introduce a read-state color (e.g. var(--tk-status-read)) applied to both ticks when status==='read', and/or distinguish delivered vs read by more than opacity; ensure the meta row meets contrast against both surface and accent backgrounds.
  - **Done when:** delivered and read render with a perceptibly different, tokenized appearance (color, not just opacity) and pass contrast checks on both incoming and outgoing bubbles in light and dark themes.
- [ ] **`CHT-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-RESP` — Bubble layout is hardcoded LTR — no RTL/logical-property support
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/message-bubble.tsx:29-61`
  - **Evidence:** justifyContent: out ? "flex-end" : "flex-start" ... borderBottomRightRadius/borderBottomLeftRadius keyed on out ... marginLeft: 8 ... float: "right"
  - **Impact:** For RTL locales (Arabic, Hebrew, Persian — real Mini App markets) the outgoing/incoming sides, the tail corner, the meta marginLeft and float:right are all physically LTR, so the conversation mirrors incorrectly: outgoing stays on the right and meta floats the wrong way relative to text direction.
  - **Fix:** Use logical properties (marginInlineStart, borderEnd*Radius, inset-inline) and let side be derived from out within the writing direction, or expose a dir-aware path; replace float:right with a flex meta that follows text direction.
  - **Done when:** Under dir='rtl', incoming bubbles align to the right and outgoing to the left with correct tail corner and meta alignment in a visual snapshot.
- [ ] **`CHT-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — Feed and bubbles expose no list/log semantics or in/out distinction to assistive tech
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/messages.tsx:12-24`
  - **Evidence:** <div data-testid={testId} style={{ display: "flex", flexDirection: "column", gap: 3, ...style }}>\n {messages.map(...)=> <div key={message.id} ...><TKMessageBubble {...message} tail={tail} /></div>)}
  - **Impact:** The feed is a plain stack of divs: no role='log'/list, no per-message role/group, and TKMessageBubble (message-bubble.tsx) carries no aria to mark incoming vs outgoing or to label the sender/time. A screen-reader user cannot tell who sent a message or that new messages arrived (no live region), so an a11y-conscious Mini App must rebuild semantics.
  - **Fix:** Add role='log' (or list/listitem) with an aria-live='polite' option for incoming messages, and give each bubble an accessible label (e.g. 'You' / sender, time, status) so it reads coherently.
  - **Done when:** Screen-reader/axe walk announces each message with sender+time and announces newly appended incoming messages via the live region; structural roles are present.
- [ ] **`CHT-010`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-TG` — WriteBar can double-pad the home indicator when page already applies safe-area
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/write-bar.tsx:25-44`
  - **Evidence:** const { inset, contentInset } = useSafeArea();\n const bottom = inset.bottom + contentInset.bottom;\n ...\n paddingBottom: safeArea ? `max(env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px), ${bottom}px, 8px)` : 8,
  - **Impact:** The bar both reads Telegram insets (inset.bottom + contentInset.bottom) AND env(safe-area-inset-bottom) inside max(). If a host page/footer slot already applies bottom safe-area (common with TKPage footers), the composer gets extra padding and floats above the home indicator. max() prevents additive doubling within the bar, but not against an outer safe-area wrapper.
  - **Fix:** Document that safeArea should be false when the parent footer already pads, or detect/opt out of env() when Telegram insets are present; clarify in JSDoc which layer owns safe-area.
  - **Done when:** In a TKPage footer that already applies safe-area, TKWriteBar with default safeArea does not add a second gap below the input (verified visually at the home-indicator edge).
- [ ] **`CHT-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-API` — No maxLength / length control on the composer textarea
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/write-bar.tsx:53-65`
  - **Evidence:** <textarea\n ref={areaRef}\n rows={1}\n value={text}\n placeholder={placeholder}\n disabled={disabled}\n aria-label={placeholder}\n onChange={...}
  - **Impact:** There is no maxLength prop or pass-through, so consumers cannot cap input length (Telegram messages have practical limits) without wrapping; combined with the uncontrolled model (CHT-002) there is no clean way to enforce or display a character budget.
  - **Fix:** Accept maxLength?: number (and ideally a rest textareaProps spread) and forward it to the textarea.
  - **Done when:** Passing maxLength={100} prevents typing beyond 100 chars and is reflected on the rendered textarea element.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`CHT-DX-001`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKMessages (packages/uikit/src/templates/chat/messages.tsx) renders only a `display:flex column` div — it is NOT a scroll container and never sticks to the latest message. Every consumer re-implements the same boilerplate: storybook/templates/chat.stories.tsx wraps it in `{flex:1, minHeight:0, overflowY:'auto', justifyContent:'flex-end'}`, and trailhead leans on TKPage to provide scroll. New messages don't auto-scroll into view, so a developer wiring a live thread sees their own sent message stay off-screen and has to add a ref + scrollIntoView effect themselves. This is the single biggest 'why doesn't the chat just work' friction.
  - **Proposal:** Add an opt-in scroll-and-pin behavior so the common case is one component. New prop `scrollable?: boolean` (or a sibling `<TKMessageFeed>`): when true, TKMessages becomes a flex column with `flex:1; min-height:0; overflow-y:auto`, `justify-content:flex-end`, and an internal `useEffect` that scrolls to bottom whenever `messages.length` (or last id) changes — guarded by a 'was the user already near the bottom' check so it doesn't yank a user reading history. Expose `autoScroll?: boolean` (default true when scrollable) and a `pinThreshold?: number` (px). Result: `<TKMessages scrollable messages={messages} />` is a complete, self-pinning feed; the manual overflow wrappers in the story and trailhead disappear.
- [ ] **`CHT-DX-002`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** Turning raw thread data into TKMessage[] is hand-rolled per app. In examples/trailhead/src/features/guide/GuideThread.tsx the dev writes a `clockOf()` time formatter, a manual sent→delivered→read status-progression with setTimeout/timers, and a `.map()` that projects `{id, text, out: authorId==='me', time, status}`. TKMessage forces `time` and `status` to be pre-computed ReactNodes, so the template gives no help with the two most universal chat chores: formatting timestamps and deriving in/out from the current user.
  - **Proposal:** Move the common projection into the template. (a) Accept `time?: ReactNode | Date | number` on TKMessage and add a `formatTime?: (d: Date) => string` prop on TKMessages with a sensible HH:MM default keyed off useTKLocale — so consumers pass a Date and drop their clockOf helper. (b) Add an optional `currentUserId` + `senderId` pair (or keep `out` as today) so `out` can be derived: `out = msg.senderId === currentUserId`. Keep the explicit `out`/ReactNode `time` paths fully backward-compatible (union types), so existing call sites compile unchanged while new code gets one-prop ergonomics.
- [ ] **`CHT-DX-003`** · api · effort S
  - **Pain:** TKWriteBar (write-bar.tsx) only exposes `onSend(text)`. There is no imperative handle, so a developer cannot focus the composer after navigation, blur it on submit, or programmatically insert a quick-reply / quoted text. Combined with the uncontrolled-only state (already logged as CHT-002), the bar is a black box: in GuideThread there's literally no way to focus the input when the thread opens, which is table-stakes for a messaging UI.
  - **Proposal:** Forward a typed imperative ref via `forwardRef<TKWriteBarHandle, TKWriteBarProps>` exposing `{ focus(): void; blur(): void; clear(): void; insert(text: string): void; getValue(): string }`. Implement with `useImperativeHandle` over the existing `areaRef`/`setText`. This complements the controlled value/onChange from CHT-002 for imperative cases (focus-on-mount, quick replies) where lifting all state is overkill, and gives the auto-grow reset (CHT-006) a clean home inside `clear()`.
- [ ] **`CHT-DX-004`** · a11y · effort S
  - **Pain:** TKWriteBar derives its textarea accessible name solely from `placeholder` (`aria-label={placeholder}`, write-bar.tsx:59). The locale (foundation/i18n.tsx) ships a `send` label for the button but NO message-input label, so when a consumer omits `placeholder` the input is nameless (the root of red-team CHT-001) and there is no localized fallback to lean on. The default is 'inaccessible unless you remember a prop'.
  - **Proposal:** Add a localized `composerLabel` (en: 'Message', ru: 'Сообщение') to TKLocale and resolve the textarea name as `aria-label = props['aria-label'] ?? placeholder ?? locale.composerLabel`. Also add an explicit optional `label?: string` prop so the accessible name is decoupled from the visible placeholder (placeholder can be empty for design reasons without losing the name). Now the textarea is always named, in the right language, with zero required props — a11y-by-default.
- [ ] **`CHT-DX-005`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKMessageBubble hardcodes the entire bubble shell (max-width, padding, radius, accent/surface background, meta row). There is no escape hatch to wrap or replace a bubble — e.g. to add an avatar gutter, a reply-quote header, a reaction footer, a long-press menu, or a sender name for group chats. A developer who needs any of these must fork the component, because `children` only injects *inside* the existing bubble and TKMessages controls the wrapper entirely.
  - **Proposal:** Add a `renderBubble?: (props: TKMessageBubbleProps & { defaultBubble: ReactNode }) => ReactNode` (render-prop) on TKMessages, and an `asChild`-style `wrapper?` / `before?`/`after?` slot on TKMessageBubble for avatar and reaction rows. Also surface optional `name?: ReactNode` and `avatar?: ReactNode` fields on TKMessage so the most common group-chat needs are first-class. Keeps the zero-config path identical while making custom bubbles a one-function override instead of a fork.
- [ ] **`CHT-DX-006`** · perf · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKMessages (messages.tsx) maps over the full array and renders an un-memoized TKMessageBubble for every item on every parent render (already noted as CHT-005). From a DX angle the pain is that the component gives the developer no knob to make a long thread fast — they cannot supply a stable key strategy, cannot lazily window, and a 500-message support thread will jank with no in-library remedy, pushing them to react-window glue by hand.
  - **Proposal:** Make perf the default, not a project. (a) Wrap TKMessageBubble in React.memo with a shallow comparator (id/text/out/time/status/tail) so unaffected bubbles skip re-render for free. (b) Hoist tail/groupStart computation so it's O(n) once (already is) and pass primitive props only. (c) Offer optional `virtualize?: boolean` on the scrollable feed from CHT-DX-001 that windows off-screen bubbles. Even just the memo step is an S-effort win that every consumer gets without opting in.
- [ ] **`CHT-DX-007`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** storybook/templates/chat.stories.tsx covers only a static SupportThread and three BubbleStates. There is no live/interactive story where typing in TKWriteBar appends to TKMessages, no story showing the status-tick progression, no RTL story, no long-text/URL overflow story, and no copy-pasteable 'full screen with TKPage footer' recipe matching how trailhead actually wires it. A developer evaluating the family can't see it behave, so they discover the rough edges (no auto-scroll, controlled-input gaps) only after wiring their own app.
  - **Proposal:** Add interactive stories: (1) `LiveThread` — local useState thread where TKWriteBar.onSend appends a message and auto-scroll kicks in (doubles as the smoke test for CHT-DX-001/002). (2) `StatusTicks` — animated sent→delivered→read. (3) `LongContent` — pasted URL + long word to lock the overflow fix. (4) `RTL` story under a `dir='rtl'` wrapper. (5) A docs MDX 'Recipe' block with the exact TKPage `footer={<TKWriteBar/>}` snippet from GuideThread so the happy path is copy-paste.
- [ ] **`CHT-DX-008`** · types · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** TKMessageBubbleProps = `Omit<TKMessage,'id'>` + `{tail,testId}` (message-bubble.tsx:17). Because `out` and `status` are independent optionals, the type permits nonsensical states the runtime silently ignores: an incoming bubble (`out` falsy) with a `status:'read'` renders no ticks (status only shows when `out`), and there is no compile-time signal that read/delivered/sent ticks are an outgoing-only concept. A developer mapping API data can ship `{out:false, status:'read'}` and get a silently statusless bubble with no warning.
  - **Proposal:** Tighten the model with a discriminated union: `type TKMessage = TKIncomingMessage | TKOutgoingMessage` where `TKOutgoingMessage = { out: true; status?: TKMessageStatus; ... }` and `TKIncomingMessage = { out?: false; status?: never; ... }`. Type inference then rejects `status` on incoming messages at the call site. Pair with a dev-only `console.warn` (stripped in prod) when an invalid combo or empty bubble (no text/children) reaches runtime, so misuse fails loud instead of rendering nothing.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

Coverage gaps / assumptions: (1) There are NO tests or stories for this family — find under packages/uikit/src/templates/chat returned only message-bubble.tsx, messages.tsx, write-bar.tsx and the barrel templates/chat.tsx; no *.test.tsx / *.stories.tsx exist, and no per-family CSS (data-tk-bubble has zero matching CSS rule anywhere in packages/uikit, so the entire visual is inline-styled and untested by the Playwright/axe gates). Every finding above is currently un-gated. (2) useSafeArea is re-exported from @tg-mini-app/telegram (packages/telegram/src/layout.ts:74) and returns ZERO_INSET outside Telegram, so SSR/runtime-absent import safety for WriteBar is fine — no finding there. (3) The 'check' icon used for ticks is registered (atoms/icons.tsx:8), so ticks render; the issue is purely the read/delivered distinction (CHT-007). (4) TKMessages keys on message.id (messages.tsx:19) — duplicate ids would collide and drop bubbles, but that's a documented id contract; only noted, not filed separately. (5) Reduced-motion: the send-button inline transition references var(--tk-t2), which the tokens.css reduce block (lines 332-339) collapses to 1ms ONLY under the .tk scope — assumed the kit root carries .tk; if a consumer mounts WriteBar outside a .tk root the color transition won't honor prefers-reduced-motion (low risk, not filed). (6) Security/D-SEC: text and children are ReactNode rendered directly (no dangerouslySetInnerHTML, no img src handling), so no injection surface in the bubble — nothing to file. (7) The trailhead consumer (examples/trailhead/src/features/guide/GuideThread.tsx) confirms real usage: optimistic send + status ticks + AsyncBoundary wrapper, and it manages its own scrolling/empty state, corroborating CHT-005 (TKMessages owns no scroll/empty behavior). (8) Did not run the build/test suite; all findings are from static reading of source with cited file:line.

---

### Patterns — Commerce, Gamification, Wallet templates

`PTN` · `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns` · 11 findings (0🔴 3🟠 5🟡 3⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility & selection semantics · `D-THEME` Tokens & Telegram theming correctness · `D-STATE` Selection state & controlled-value drift · `D-API` API ergonomics & footguns · `D-TG` Telegram runtime integration · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView / i18n robustness · `D-EDGE` Robustness with malformed/empty data · `D-TYPES` TypeScript contract quality

#### Findings

- [x] **`PTN-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-THEME` — TKXPHeader hardcodes #fff/rgba(255,255,255) on var(--tk-accent) instead of --tk-on-accent — invisible text on light-accent Telegram themes
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/gamification.tsx:26-58`
  - **Evidence:** `background: "var(--tk-accent)"` with `color: "#fff"` (l.26-27); level badge `background: "rgba(255,255,255,.22)"` (l.45); progress track/fill `background: "rgba(255,255,255,.25)"` … `background: "#fff"` (l.52,58). The kit defines `--tk-on-accent: var(--tg-theme-button-text-color, #ffffff)` in tokens.css l.163.
  - **Impact:** On any Telegram theme (or custom accent) where the button text color is dark because the accent is light — a common real Telegram theme — the entire header renders white-on-light: name, level badge and the XP bar all wash out. Every other accent surface in the kit (buttons, slot picker, wallet connect) honors --tk-on-accent, so this one template visibly breaks theming the rest of the package gets right.
  - **Fix:** Replace `color: "#fff"` with `color: "var(--tk-on-accent)"`, and derive the translucent overlays from on-accent (e.g. `color-mix(in srgb, var(--tk-on-accent) 22%, transparent)`) instead of literal rgba(255,255,255,…). Same fix for the wallet icon chip at wallet.tsx:59.
  - **Done when:** In Storybook, set a light accent (e.g. `--tk-accent: #ffd60a`) and verify TKXPHeader name/level/bar remain legible; a token-lint test asserts no `#fff`/`rgba(255,255,255` literals remain in patterns/*.tsx.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — gamification.tsx TKXPHeader hardcodes color:"#fff" on background:var(--tk-accent) (l.26-27) plus rgba(255,255,255,.22/.25) and #fff for badge/track/fill (l.34,45,52,58), ignoring the kit's --tk-on-accent token which resolves to var(--tg-theme-button-text-color,#fff) (tokens.css:163). The sibling SlotPicker, buttons, chips, and wallet all honor --tk-on-accent, so this header washes out on light-accent Telegram themes with dark button text.
- [x] **`PTN-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — SlotPicker day & slot buttons expose no selected state to assistive tech (no aria-pressed/role=radio)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/commerce.tsx:45-112`
  - **Evidence:** Day button: `<button type="button" … onClick={() => setDayIdx(index)}>` with selection conveyed only via `background: on ? "var(--tk-accent)" …` (l.45-65). Slot button identically lacks any aria state (l.88-110). Compare the kit's own selectables: segmented.tsx:82 `aria-pressed`, radio-group.tsx:32 `role="radio"`, chips.tsx:94 `aria-pressed`.
  - **Impact:** A screen-reader user picking a delivery date/time (the core commerce flow consumed by trailhead DateSlot.tsx) hears six identical unlabeled buttons with no indication of which day or slot is currently chosen, and the only differentiator is color — which also fails the WCAG 'not by color alone' rule. This is the kit's primary booking surface shipping below its own a11y bar.
  - **Fix:** Add `aria-pressed={on}` to both day and slot buttons (or wrap as `role="radiogroup"` + `role="radio" aria-checked`), and surface busy state as `aria-disabled` plus an accessible suffix (e.g. visually-hidden 'unavailable') rather than only line-through.
  - **Done when:** Testing-Library test: after selecting '11:00', `getByRole('button',{name:'11:00',pressed:true})` resolves and the busy slot reports `aria-disabled`; axe has no 'aria-allowed-attr'/color-only violations on the picker.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — commerce.tsx SlotPicker day buttons (l.45-79) and slot buttons (l.88-112) carry no aria-pressed/role=radio/aria-checked (grep for aria states in the file returns nothing); selection is conveyed only by background color. The kit's own selectables expose state (chips.tsx:94 aria-pressed, radio-group.tsx:32-33 role=radio/aria-checked, segmented.tsx:82 aria-pressed), so this surface ships below the package's a11y bar.
- [x] **`PTN-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — SlotPicker selection never reconciles with slots/busy — controlled value silently points at an absent or now-busy slot
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/commerce.tsx:37-93`
  - **Evidence:** `const [slotVal, setSlotVal] = useControllable(slot, defaultSlot ?? "", onSlotChange)` (l.38); `onClick={() => setSlotVal(slotItem)}` with no guard, and busy is only checked for the disabled attribute (l.85). Real consumer relies on this: trailhead DateSlot.tsx:86 has to recompute `!busy.includes(cart.slot)` itself and resets slot on date change at l.148.
  - **Impact:** When the day changes (new slots array) the previously selected time stays 'selected' even if it no longer exists or is now sold out; the highlighted button can vanish while onSlotChange was never re-fired, so the consumer's parent state and the visual selection drift apart. Every consumer must defensively clear the slot on day change (as trailhead does), which is exactly the boilerplate a template should remove.
  - **Fix:** On day/slots change, if the current slotVal is absent from slots or present in busy, clear it (uncontrolled) or call onSlotChange(undefined) so the parent can react; document that selecting is blocked for busy slots. Consider a single `onChange({day,slot})` to keep them coherent.
  - **Done when:** Test: render controlled, select '12:00', change `days`/`slots` so '12:00' is gone or busy → component no longer shows it selected and (uncontrolled) emits a cleared value.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — commerce.tsx onClick={()=>setSlotVal(slotItem)} (l.93) has no guard and busy only gates the disabled attribute (l.85); no effect reconciles slotVal when slots/busy change, so a prior selection stays 'selected' (or silently absent) after a day change without re-firing onSlotChange. The real consumer confirms the missing logic: trailhead DateSlot.tsx resets slot:undefined on date change (l.148) and re-checks !busy.includes(cart.slot) itself (l.86).
- [x] **`PTN-003`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — TKXPHeader progress bar is a bare div — no role=progressbar / aria-valuenow, unannounceable
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/gamification.tsx:52-62`
  - **Evidence:** `<div style={{ … height: 7 … }}><div style={{ width: `${Math.min(100, Math.max(0, xp))}%` … }} /></div>` — no `role`, `aria-valuenow/min/max`, or label. The kit's file-input.tsx:177-180 uses `role="progressbar" aria-valuenow/min/max`, and a TKProgress primitive already exists.
  - **Impact:** Screen readers announce nothing for the user's level progress; the 'XP to next level' meaning is conveyed purely visually. A Mini App team using this as their profile header has no accessible progress indicator and must rebuild it.
  - **Fix:** Add `role="progressbar" aria-valuenow={clampedXp} aria-valuemin={0} aria-valuemax={100} aria-label={hint || locale.progress}` to the track div, or compose the existing TKProgress primitive instead of a hand-rolled bar.
  - **Done when:** `getByRole('progressbar')` resolves with `aria-valuenow` matching the clamped xp; axe passes on the header.
- [x] **`PTN-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — SlotPicker day index and xp accept out-of-range / NaN values with no clamping or empty-state
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/commerce.tsx:29-43`
  - **Evidence:** `defaultDay = 0` then `const on = index === dayIdx` (l.29,43) with no clamp to `days.length`; `days`/`slots` rendered directly with no empty guard (l.42,84). In gamification.tsx:56 `width: `${Math.min(100, Math.max(0, xp))}%`` clamps range but `Math.min/max(…, NaN)` returns NaN → `width:'NaN%'` is dropped, collapsing the bar.
  - **Impact:** A `day={5}` (or stale index after the days array shrinks) highlights nothing and leaves the picker with no active day; an empty `slots=[]` or `days=[]` renders an empty silent container with no empty-state. `xp={NaN}` (e.g. division-by-zero from API) silently renders a zero-width bar. These are reachable from ordinary API data and fail without warning.
  - **Fix:** Clamp `dayIdx` into `[0, days.length-1]`; render nothing or an empty-state when arrays are empty; coerce `xp` with `Number.isFinite(xp) ? clamp : 0`.
  - **Done when:** Tests: `day={99}` selects the last valid day (or none deliberately) without console errors; `xp={NaN}`/`xp={Infinity}` renders a 0–100% bar; empty `slots` does not throw.
- [ ] **`PTN-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — TKLeaderboard rows are non-interactive — no onRowClick/href, no key strategy, forcing a fork to make rows tappable
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/gamification.tsx:88-149`
  - **Evidence:** Each row is a plain `<div key={index} …>` (l.100-102) with no onClick, role, or href, and the row key is the array index. There is no `you` auto-scroll or stable id.
  - **Impact:** Leaderboards in real Mini Apps are almost always tappable (open a profile, view a player). Consumers can't add interaction without re-implementing the whole row, and index keys cause React reconciliation bugs (wrong-row highlight/animation) when the board re-ranks. The `you` row also can't be programmatically scrolled into view because rows carry no ref/id.
  - **Fix:** Add optional `id` per row used as the key, an optional `onRowClick(row)`/`href` that upgrades the row to a button/anchor with proper role and 44px target, and expose a ref or data-attr on the `you` row for scroll-into-view.
  - **Done when:** With `onRowClick` set, rows render as `role=button`, fire the handler on tap, and key by `row.id`; re-sorting the array does not mis-highlight the `you` row.
- [ ] **`PTN-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — Wallet adapters have no haptics/runtime feedback and become a silent disabled button during loading
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/wallet.tsx:29-49`
  - **Evidence:** `<button … onClick={onClick} disabled={loading} style={{ … opacity: loading ? 0.72 : 1 }}>` (l.29-49) — loading is shown only as 72% opacity, there is no spinner, no `aria-busy`, and no haptic hook. The icon chip uses `rgba(255,255,255,.18)` (l.59) instead of an on-accent token.
  - **Impact:** This is the package's TON-Connect entry point (consumed by trailhead Profile.tsx). During the connect round-trip the button just dims and stops responding with no progress affordance or `aria-busy`, so screen-reader and sighted users get no 'connecting…' feedback; the example app had to manage its own `connecting` state and haptics around it. Teams expect a Telegram template to wire haptics/MainButton, and this one delegates all of it.
  - **Fix:** Render a spinner + `aria-busy={loading}` and an accessible 'Connecting…' label while loading; replace the white-literal chip background with an on-accent-derived token; optionally accept an `onConnectingChange`/haptics hook so the adapter fires selection/notification haptics consistently.
  - **Done when:** With `loading`, button exposes `aria-busy="true"`, shows a spinner, and an axe/snapshot test confirms a visible busy affordance; chip background uses a token, not rgba(255,255,255,…).
- [x] **`PTN-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — TKPaymentSummary keys rows by index and abuses display:contents wrappers, breaking divider/styling integrity
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/commerce.tsx:149-174`
  - **Evidence:** `{rows.map((row, index) => (<span key={index} style={{ display: "contents" }}>{row.total ? <div … divider /> : null}<div …>…</div></span>))}` (l.149-174). The `display:contents` span carries the React key while the divider+row are its children.
  - **Impact:** Index keys make reordering/inserting promo rows reconcile incorrectly (values can animate/attach to the wrong row). `display:contents` is also stripped from the accessibility tree in some engines and disables the span as a styling/spacing hook, so consumers can't target rows. A summary is the surface most likely to be data-driven and reordered (add/remove discounts), so this is a live footgun.
  - **Fix:** Key by a stable `row.id` (add to TKSummaryRow) rather than index, and render the divider+row as a real fragment or a single grid row instead of a display:contents span; document the total/divider contract.
  - **Done when:** Test: reorder/insert a row and confirm the correct value+divider move together; type check that `id` is required-or-stable; no display:contents on the keyed node.
- [ ] **`PTN-009`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-RESP` — SlotPicker injects `columns` raw into gridTemplateColumns and assumes equal-width fixed columns — overflow on long slot labels / narrow WebView
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/commerce.tsx:20-83`
  - **Evidence:** `columns?: number` (l.20) → `gridTemplateColumns: `repeat(${columns}, 1fr)`` (l.83). Day labels also force `textTransform: "uppercase"`, `letterSpacing: 0` (l.72-73). No min-width:0 on day buttons, no responsive fallback.
  - **Impact:** With long locale slot strings (e.g. '10:00–10:30 утра') or RTL, fixed 3-column 1fr cells clip/overflow in a ~320px Telegram WebView; uppercasing day labels can blow out abbreviations in languages where the abbreviation is already long. A non-integer or negative `columns` produces an invalid grid template silently.
  - **Fix:** Use `repeat(${columns}, minmax(0, 1fr))`, clamp/validate columns to a positive integer, drop forced uppercase for non-Latin locales (or make it a knob), and allow auto-fit as a responsive default.
  - **Done when:** Snapshot at 320px with long/RTL slot labels shows no horizontal overflow; `columns={0}`/negative falls back gracefully.
- [ ] **`PTN-010`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-EDGE` — SlotPicker keys slots by their string value — duplicate times throw React key warnings / drop buttons
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/commerce.tsx:84-92`
  - **Evidence:** `{slots.map((slotItem) => (<button … key={slotItem} …>))}` (l.84-89) keys directly by the slot string, and selection compares `slotItem === slotVal`.
  - **Impact:** Real schedules can legitimately contain two distinct sessions at the same printed time (e.g. two '10:00' rooms). Duplicate keys produce React warnings and one button silently disappears, and selecting one ambiguously selects both (string equality), so the consumer can't represent two same-time slots without a workaround.
  - **Fix:** Accept slots as `{ id; label; busy? }[]` (or key by `${slot}-${index}`) and select by id, so duplicate display times remain distinct.
  - **Done when:** Render `slots=['10:00','10:00']` with no React key warning and independent selection of each.
- [ ] **`PTN-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-TYPES` — xp typed as bare number with a 0-100 contract that's only doc-commented, not enforced or named
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/patterns/gamification.tsx:5-16`
  - **Evidence:** `/** Progress to the next level, 0-100. */ xp?: number;` (l.8-9). The 0-100 percentage semantic lives only in a JSDoc comment; the prop name `xp` strongly implies raw experience points, not a percentage.
  - **Impact:** The name/type mismatch is a classic footgun: a consumer naturally passes raw XP (e.g. 3200) expecting the bar to scale, gets a clamped full bar, and only the runtime clamp hides the bug. There's no `value`/`max` pair and no discriminated contract, so the type system gives no help.
  - **Fix:** Rename to `progress` (0-100) and/or accept `value`+`max` and compute the percentage internally; if keeping `xp`, brand it or at minimum document it prominently and add a runtime dev warning when out of [0,100].
  - **Done when:** Type/usage review shows the percentage contract is expressed in the prop name or via value+max; a dev-mode warning fires for out-of-range input.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`PTN-DX-001`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKSlotPicker forces a flat `slots: string[]` + parallel `busy: string[]` model. Every real consumer owns a richer slot object and has to shred it into two arrays: examples/trailhead/src/features/discover/DateSlot.tsx L80-84 does `const slots = e.slots.map(s => s.time)` then a separate `reduce` to build `busy`; RescheduleSlot.tsx L83-84 repeats the exact same flatten + filter. The component then can't show per-slot metadata (price, '2 left') and consumers must re-join on the string `time` to look anything up.
  - **Proposal:** Accept structured items as the primary API: `interface TKSlot { time: string; id?: string; disabled?: boolean; label?: ReactNode; meta?: ReactNode }` and `slots: (string | TKSlot)[]`. Keep `string[]` working by normalizing internally (`typeof s === 'string' ? {time:s} : s`). This kills the parallel `busy[]` array (encode availability on the item, deprecate `busy`), lets the picker key on `id ?? time` (fixes PTN-010 duplicate-time keys for free), and lets `selected` reconcile against item identity (supports PTN-004). Common case stays one prop; rich case stops needing a second derived array.
- [ ] **`PTN-DX-002`** · defaults · effort S
  - **Pain:** Selection has no built-in haptic/runtime feedback, so every consumer hand-wires it on every change. DateSlot.tsx L162-165, RescheduleSlot.tsx L142-145 both do `onSlotChange={(s)=>{ haptics.selection(); ... }}` and the calendar callbacks repeat it. The repo already ships `useHaptics`/`useOptionalHaptics` (used internally by switch.tsx, tabbar.tsx, segmented.tsx, radio-group.tsx) — patterns are the odd family that doesn't fire it. Forgetting the call ships a dead-feeling Telegram surface.
  - **Proposal:** Make selection haptics the default inside TKSlotPicker (day + slot taps) and TKLeaderboard row taps, calling the foundation `useOptionalHaptics().selection()` before firing `onSlotChange`/`onDayChange`. Add an opt-out `haptics={false}` for the rare consumer driving its own. Consumers' callbacks collapse to `onSlotChange={(s)=>dispatch(...)}`. Mirrors how segmented.tsx/tabbar.tsx already behave, so the family becomes consistent.
- [ ] **`PTN-DX-003`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKXPHeader.xp is a raw 0-100 percent, so consumers convert their domain XP by hand. Train.tsx L43-44 derives `level = Math.floor(streak.xp/1000)+1` and `xpInLevel = streak.xp%1000`, then L91 passes `xp={Math.round(xpInLevel/10)}` purely to satisfy the percent contract, and L92 hand-builds the 'N to next level' hint. Every gamified app re-derives this same level/remainder/percent math, and an off-by-10 silently mis-fills the bar.
  - **Proposal:** Add a higher-level value API alongside the raw percent: `value?: number; max?: number` (e.g. `value={streak.xp % 1000} max={1000}`) from which the bar width and an auto `level`/`hint` can be computed. Optionally expose `levelSize` so `level` and the default hint ('120 XP to LVL 4') are derived internally. Keep `xp` (0-100) as the escape hatch. Train.tsx collapses to `<TKXPHeader value={xpInLevel} max={1000} levelSize={1000} />` with no manual division.
- [ ] **`PTN-DX-004`** · api · effort S
  - **Pain:** TKPaymentSummary makes the consumer assemble the total row and infer accent by hand. Checkout.tsx L87-94 spreads line items, then manually appends `{ label: t('checkout.total'), value: ..., total: true }`, and sets `accent: li.stars < 0` per row. The 'total' is conceptually distinct data (it's the sum + the styled divider row) yet it's smuggled into the same `rows` array with a magic `total: true` flag, so every checkout screen re-implements the same append.
  - **Proposal:** Promote total to its own prop: `<TKPaymentSummary rows={lineItems} total={{ label, value }} />`, rendering the divider + emphasized row internally (and dropping the per-row `total` flag, or keeping it deprecated). Consider a `formatSign`/`accentNegative` default so discounts auto-green without each caller writing `accent: stars < 0`. Checkout.tsx's `rows` builder loses its manual total push and the accent ternary.
- [ ] **`PTN-DX-005`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKLeaderboard rows are non-interactive (builds on red-team PTN-006). Train.tsx L133 renders a static board; to make a row open a profile the consumer must fork the component, even though the sibling TKCell already solves this with `TKPolymorphicProps`/`as`/`href` and keyboard handling (composites/lists/cell.tsx L51,L88-112,L191). The patterns family ignores an existing repo idiom.
  - **Proposal:** Add `onRowClick?: (row, index) => void` and per-row `href?`/`onClick?`, reusing the established `internal/polymorphic.ts` so a row renders as `<button>`/`<a>` with the same focus + `:active` press treatment TKCell uses. Provide a `getRowKey?: (row, i) => string` so consumers stop relying on the index key (also addresses PTN-006's key concern). For full control, an `asChild`/`renderRow` escape hatch (button.tsx already ships `asChild`) lets a consumer wrap each row in their router Link without forking.
- [ ] **`PTN-DX-006`** · api · effort L
  - **Pain:** Wallet is two disconnected presentational buttons with zero connection logic, so Profile.tsx re-implements the whole adapter by hand: L33-36 pulls TonConnect hooks, L47-63 a bespoke connect flow, L65-76 a 12-line effect syncing TonConnect state into the local store, L84 fallback walletName. Every Mini App that uses TKWalletConnectButton/TKWalletStatusCell copy-pastes this same TonConnect glue, and the button silently becomes a dead disabled control during `loading` (red-team PTN-007).
  - **Proposal:** Ship a thin headless adapter hook `useTKWallet({ address, connected, name, onConnect, onDisconnect, loading })` returning the exact props both wallet components want, plus a `<TKWalletConnect adapter={...} />` that wires connect/disconnect, fires success/error haptics, and renders a spinner instead of a silent disabled button. Provide a ready `tonConnectAdapter(tonConnectUI, tonWallet, tonAddress)` in an optional subpath so Profile.tsx drops its sync effect to `const wallet = useTKWallet(tonConnectAdapter(...))`.
- [ ] **`PTN-DX-007`** · types · effort S · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** `xp` (gamification.tsx L9-10) and the SlotPicker `day`/`xp` numbers carry their contracts only in JSDoc ('Progress to the next level, 0-100'), unenforced (red-team PTN-011/PTN-005). A caller passing `xp={87.5}` raw, `xp={870}` (forgot /10, exactly Train.tsx's risk), or `day={5}` for a 1-day list compiles clean and renders wrong. There's no named, self-documenting type to hover.
  - **Proposal:** Introduce a branded/named alias `type TKPercent = number & { __percent?: true }` (or at minimum a well-named `xpPercent` rename) and document the range on the type, plus runtime clamping already implied by PTN-005. Pair with PTN-DX-003's `value`/`max` so the percent path is opt-in for people who really have a percent. Even without branding, renaming `xp`→`progress`/`xpPercent` and `day`→`dayIndex` makes the unit obvious at the call site.
- [ ] **`PTN-DX-008`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** Storybook coverage for the whole family is three render-only single-variant stories with no `argTypes` and no controls: storybook/templates/commerce.stories.tsx is 44 lines showing one happy-path SlotPicker+Summary; gamification (34 lines) and wallet (29 lines) are similar. There are no stories for the states a consumer actually hits — selected/busy/empty slots, loading wallet, light-accent theme (where PTN-001's invisible text shows), interactive leaderboard, or a controlled-value example. Devs can't discover props or copy a real usage.
  - **Proposal:** Add controls + state-matrix stories per template: SlotPicker (uncontrolled / controlled `selected` / all-busy empty state / long-label overflow), TKXPHeader (xp=0/40/100, no-level, light-accent theme to prove contrast), TKLeaderboard (with `you` row + interactive rows), Wallet (disconnected / connecting / connected). Wire `argTypes` so each prop is a control, and paste one copy-ready snippet mirroring DateSlot.tsx into the docs block. Turns the family from 'read the source' into 'open the story, tweak, copy'.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

Family scope: the patterns directory contains only three implementation files (commerce.tsx, gamification.tsx, wallet.tsx) plus the re-export barrel templates/patterns.tsx; there are no co-located *.css, *.test.tsx, or *.stories.tsx in the directory. Stories live at packages/uikit/storybook/templates/{commerce,gamification,wallet}.stories.tsx and the only test is packages/uikit/test/templates-reorg.test.tsx.\n\nCoverage gaps observed (not findings themselves): the existing test (templates-reorg.test.tsx:37-65) only asserts export identity and a single happy-path render/click; there are NO tests for busy-slot disabling, controlled-value drift, empty arrays, NaN/out-of-range xp, theming under a light accent, or any a11y assertion. Stories render only one canonical state each with short Latin strings, so the visual/a11y Playwright gates never exercise long-locale/RTL, light-accent, loading, or empty states for this family.\n\nAssumptions: (1) PTN-001 severity assumes consumers run under the .tk-tg Telegram theme where --tk-on-accent resolves to tg-theme-button-text-color (confirmed in tokens.css:163 and dist/style.css); I did not render against a real light-button Telegram theme, but the token indirection is the documented purpose of --tk-on-accent and the rest of the kit uses it consistently (18 on-accent uses vs the 10 raw #fff literals, 6 of which are in this family). (2) PTN-004's drift is inferred from useControllable semantics (internal.ts:19-24, set only mirrors/echoes, never reconciles) plus the real consumer compensating in trailhead DateSlot.tsx:86,148; I did not author a failing repro test. (3) PTN-008's display:contents a11y-tree stripping is engine-dependent (observed historically in WebKit); the index-key reconciliation problem is unconditional. All other findings are directly grounded in the quoted lines.

---

### Onboarding + Confetti — templates/onboarding.tsx, templates/confetti.tsx

`ONB` · `packages/uikit/src/templates` · 12 findings (0🔴 4🟠 7🟡 1⚪) · 9 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-A11Y` Accessibility (focus, keyboard, ARIA, live regions, reduced-motion) · `D-TG` Telegram runtime (haptics, viewport/scroll, swipe-to-minimize, runtime-absent) · `D-API` API ergonomics (controlled state, dismissal, escape hatches, sane defaults) · `D-STATE` State model & edge values (empty steps, count edges, async storage, controlled drift) · `D-EDGE` Robustness (unmount-during-async, races, missing target, reduced-motion reactivity) · `D-THEME` Tokens & theming (raw hex, hardcoded px, dark/light correctness) · `D-PERF` Performance (canvas DPR, re-render on scroll, particle allocation) · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView / i18n (overflow, long text, RTL, viewport size)

#### Findings

- [x] **`ONB-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-API` — Coach-mark cannot be dismissed: no Escape, no scrim-tap, no onSkip — only the in-bubble buttons
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/onboarding.tsx:131`
  - **Evidence:** <TKPopper open anchorRef={step.target} placement={step.placement ?? "bottom"} arrow autoFlip testId={testId}> — `onClose` is never passed, so the popper's Escape/outside-pointer handlers (popper-tooltip.tsx:130-147) call `onClose?.()` into a no-op. The scrim div (lines 114-130) has `pointerEvents:"auto"` but no onClick.
  - **Impact:** A Mini App user who opens the tour by accident is trapped until they hunt for the small Skip/Next buttons; pressing Escape (hardware keyboard / Telegram Desktop) or tapping the dimmed backdrop — the universally expected dismiss gesture — does nothing. Consumers cannot programmatically close it either (no controlled `open`).
  - **Fix:** Add an `onSkip`/`onClose` prop and an optional controlled `index`. Wire the scrim div's onClick and forward `onClose={finish}` to TKPopper so Escape/outside-tap dismiss, gated by a `dismissable` prop (default true) so accidental backdrop taps can be opted out per design.
  - **Done when:** With keyboard focus anywhere, pressing Escape ends the tour and fires onFinish/onSkip; tapping the scrim outside the spotlight dismisses it; a controlled-index story can drive the step from the parent.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — onboarding.tsx:131 renders <TKPopper open ...> with no onClose, so popper's Escape (popper-tooltip.tsx:143) and outside-pointerdown (line 135) handlers call onClose?.() into undefined; the scrim div (lines 114-130) sets pointerEvents:"auto" but has no onClick, and no controlled `open` is exposed. Only the in-bubble Skip/Next buttons dismiss.
- [x] **`ONB-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-A11Y` — Coach-mark traps no focus and announces nothing — keyboard & screen-reader users get a dead overlay
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/onboarding.tsx:112-153`
  - **Evidence:** The root `<div>` has no role/aria-modal/aria-label; TKPopper is rendered with default `role="tooltip"` (popper-tooltip.tsx:63 `role = "tooltip"`) which is not focusable and not announced as a dialog. There is no focus move into the bubble, no focus trap, and no `aria-live` region for the `{index + 1} / {steps.length}` step counter (line 138-140).
  - **Impact:** On step change nothing is announced, so SR users don't know the tour advanced or what it points at; keyboard focus stays on whatever was focused before, so Tab can wander behind the scrim into the dimmed page. This fails the package's own a11y/Playwright gate intent for a modal coaching flow.
  - **Fix:** Render the popper with `role="dialog"`, give it an `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby` from `step.title`, move focus to the Next/Done button on mount and on index change, restore focus to the trigger on finish, and wrap the step counter + text in a `role="status" aria-live="polite"` region.
  - **Done when:** axe/Playwright a11y run reports a labelled dialog; Tab cycles only within the bubble; advancing a step emits a polite live-region announcement; closing returns focus to the originating element.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — Root div at onboarding.tsx:113 has no role/aria-modal/aria-label and TKPopper uses its default role="tooltip" (popper-tooltip.tsx:63); there is no focus move/trap into the bubble and no aria-live on the step counter (lines 138-140), so step changes are unannounced and Tab can wander behind the scrim.
- [x] **`ONB-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — Empty / shrinking `steps` array: index goes stale and tour silently shows nothing or wrong step
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/onboarding.tsx:106-110`
  - **Evidence:** const step = steps[index]; if (status !== "open" || !step) return null; — `index` lives in useState and is never reset when `steps` length changes. const last = index === steps.length - 1; computed off the live length.
  - **Impact:** A consumer that conditionally builds steps (feature flags, async-loaded targets) and passes a shorter array after the user advanced will hit `steps[index] === undefined` and the whole tour vanishes mid-flight with no onFinish; if it grows, `last` mis-fires so Done shows on a non-final step. Passing `steps={[]}` renders nothing but also never calls onFinish, so a gating flag never flips.
  - **Fix:** Clamp index into range with a guard (`const safeIndex = Math.min(index, steps.length - 1)`), reset index when the step set identity meaningfully changes, and if `steps.length === 0` call `finish()`/`onFinish` once so the consumer's seen-flag still advances.
  - **Done when:** Shrinking steps after advancing keeps a valid step or finishes cleanly; `steps={[]}` invokes onFinish exactly once; growing steps recomputes `last` correctly.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — index (useState, line 48) is never reset when `steps` changes — the only setIndex is the Next increment (line 147); a shrinking array makes step=steps[index] undefined and the early return at line 107 unmounts with no onFinish, and `last`=index===steps.length-1 (line 110) reads the live length. steps={[]} renders nothing and never calls onFinish.
- [x] **`ONB-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-EDGE` — Null target ref turns the spotlight scrim into an opaque full-screen blackout with no escape
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/onboarding.tsx:109-130`
  - **Evidence:** const rect = step.target.current?.getBoundingClientRect(); … rect ? {…cutout…} : { position: "fixed", inset: 0, zIndex: tkZ.popper, pointerEvents: "auto", background: "var(--tk-scrim)" } — when the target hasn't mounted/measured, the fallback is a full-bleed scrim covering everything.
  - **Impact:** Refs to lazily-rendered or off-screen elements (a tab not yet painted, a virtualized row) are commonly null on first render. The user sees a full-screen dim with the bubble floating with no visible anchor — and combined with ONB-001 there's no backdrop/Escape dismiss, so it reads as a frozen app. The trailhead example points step 3 at `trainTabRef` populated in an effect, exactly this hazard.
  - **Fix:** When `rect` is missing, skip the step (advance) or render the bubble centered without the blackout fallback; optionally retry on next animation frame via the existing bumpRect path before falling back, and never use a solid full-screen scrim as the 'measuring' state.
  - **Done when:** Rendering a step whose target ref is null does not produce a full-screen opaque scrim; the step either centers gracefully or auto-advances, and remains dismissable.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — onboarding.tsx:128 fallback when rect is falsy is {position:"fixed",inset:0,...background:"var(--tk-scrim)"} — a full-bleed scrim; combined with ONB-001 there is no dismiss. The trailhead demo (examples/trailhead/src/components/Onboarding.tsx:26-29,71) anchors step 3 to trainTabRef populated in an effect, reproducing the null-ref-on-first-render hazard.
- [ ] **`ONB-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — Capturing window scroll listener on every reflow fights Telegram's swipe-to-minimize guard and thrashes layout
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/onboarding.tsx:79-98`
  - **Evidence:** window.addEventListener("scroll", onReflow, { passive: true, capture: true }); … raf(() => { pending = false; bumpRect(); }); — every captured scroll frame forces a re-render, and TKPopper (popper-tooltip.tsx:117) independently registers the same capturing scroll listener, so each scroll frame triggers two getBoundingClientRect passes.
  - **Impact:** In a narrow Telegram WebView, top/bottom overscroll is what Telegram reads as swipe-to-minimize; a global capturing scroll handler plus per-frame full-subtree re-render adds jank exactly during the gesture the platform is most sensitive to, and double-measures the anchor. On low-end devices the coach mark visibly stutters while scrolling.
  - **Fix:** Observe the target with ResizeObserver/IntersectionObserver and only listen to scroll on the actual scroll container (e.g. the `[data-tk-page-scroll]` ancestor) rather than capturing on window; coalesce with TKPopper's own reflow rather than duplicating it. Add `overscroll-behavior: contain` on the scrim.
  - **Done when:** Scrolling the page during the tour fires one rect measurement per frame (not two) and does not register a window-capturing scroll listener; profiler shows no per-frame full re-render of the host tree.
- [ ] **`ONB-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — No haptics on step advance / finish despite useOptionalHaptics being the house pattern
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/onboarding.tsx:100-104`
  - **Evidence:** const finish = () => { setStatus("done"); if (storage && storageKey) void storage.set(storageKey, "1").catch(() => {}); onFinish?.(); }; — no haptics anywhere; meanwhile main-button.tsx:28/53 does `const haptics = useOptionalHaptics(); … haptics.notification("success")`.
  - **Impact:** Onboarding completion and step transitions feel flat next to the rest of the kit, where buttons/switches/pins all buzz. Mini App teams expecting the kit's consistent tactile feedback have to thread haptics manually around onFinish.
  - **Fix:** Call `useOptionalHaptics()` and emit `selection()` on Next and `notification("success")` on Done/finish, gated behind an opt-out (`haptics?: boolean`, default true) so it stays runtime-absent safe (the hook already no-ops off-platform).
  - **Done when:** Advancing a step triggers a selection haptic and finishing triggers a success haptic on a Telegram runtime; with no WebApp present nothing throws.
- [ ] **`ONB-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — storage.set failure on finish is swallowed — tour silently replays forever, with no onError
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/onboarding.tsx:102`
  - **Evidence:** if (storage && storageKey) void storage.set(storageKey, "1").catch(() => {}); — the write rejection (CloudStorage quota/offline/race) is dropped, and the `get` path (line 59) `.catch(() => alive && setStatus("open"))` defaults to showing the tour again.
  - **Impact:** With a real `useCloudStorage()` adapter, a failed write means the seen-flag never persists, so the coach mark re-fires on every launch — the single most annoying onboarding bug — and the consumer gets no signal to fall back to local persistence. The JSDoc explicitly advertises CloudStorage as the intended adapter (line 14).
  - **Fix:** Expose an `onError`/`onSeenWriteError` callback and/or optimistically mark seen in a local mirror; document the replay risk. At minimum surface the rejection so consumers can retry or fall back.
  - **Done when:** A storage adapter whose `set` rejects invokes a consumer-provided error callback; a test simulating a write failure proves the consumer can observe it instead of a silent forever-replay.
- [x] **`ONB-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-THEME` — Confetti default palette is six raw hex literals — no accent token, breaks brand/dark theming
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/confetti.tsx:16`
  - **Evidence:** const DEFAULT_COLORS = ["#3390ec", "#7c5cff", "#1fab66", "#ff7a45", "#e5484d", "#ffb224"]; — the JSDoc claims "defaults to the accent + festive palette" (line 9) but the first color is a hardcoded Telegram blue, not `var(--tk-accent)`.
  - **Impact:** A Mini App with a non-blue brand accent or a custom Telegram theme gets confetti that clashes with its palette, and the colors don't respond to the theme CSS vars the rest of the kit honors. The JSDoc overpromises token-awareness that the code doesn't deliver, so consumers trust a default that's actually off-brand.
  - **Fix:** Resolve defaults from semantic tokens at runtime (read `--tk-accent` plus a small `--tk-confetti-*` festive set off the element's computed style) or document plainly that colors are literal and must be passed for brand alignment. Drop the misleading 'accent' wording if not implemented.
  - **Done when:** With a custom `--tk-accent`, the default burst includes the brand accent (or the docs no longer claim it does); colors remain overridable via the `colors` prop.
- [ ] **`ONB-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-PERF` — Canvas ignores devicePixelRatio and never resizes — blurry confetti on every retina Telegram client
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/confetti.tsx:47-48`
  - **Evidence:** const width = (canvas.width = canvas.offsetWidth || 360); const height = (canvas.height = canvas.offsetHeight || 640); — backing store is set to CSS pixels with no `* devicePixelRatio` and no `ctx.scale`, and width/height are captured once with no resize/orientation handling.
  - **Impact:** On the 2x/3x screens that dominate Telegram mobile, the confetti renders soft/pixelated, undercutting the 'delight' purpose; if the container hasn't laid out yet it silently falls back to a fixed 360x640 that mismatches the real WebView, mis-placing the burst origin (`width/2`, `height*0.42`). Rotating the device mid-burst leaves a stale canvas.
  - **Fix:** Set `canvas.width = offsetWidth * dpr` / `canvas.height = offsetHeight * dpr` and `ctx.scale(dpr, dpr)`, measure after layout (or via ResizeObserver), and recompute the emitter origin from the live size.
  - **Done when:** On a 2x viewport the canvas backing store is 2x its CSS box and edges are crisp; the burst originates from the real container center at any WebView size.
- [x] **`ONB-010`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-STATE` — Confetti count/duration unclamped: 0/negative no-op, huge values allocate unbounded and starve the frame
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/confetti.tsx:26-72`
  - **Evidence:** export function TKConfetti({ count = 150, duration = 1800, … }) … const parts: Particle[] = Array.from({ length: count }, …) — `count` feeds Array.from length directly; negative throws RangeError, 0 renders an empty animation that still runs for `duration`, and a typo'd `count={100000}` allocates 100k particles in a per-frame loop.
  - **Impact:** A consumer computing `count` from a reward amount (e.g. points earned) can pass 0 (dead burst that still blocks `onDone` for the full duration), a negative (Array.from RangeError crashes the render), or a large number that pins the main thread of a low-end Telegram device. No defensive clamp protects against data-driven inputs.
  - **Fix:** Clamp: `const n = Math.max(0, Math.min(Math.floor(count) || 0, 600))` and `Math.max(0, duration)`; short-circuit to an immediate `onDone()` when `n === 0` instead of waiting the full timer.
  - **Done when:** `count={-5}` no longer throws; `count={0}` calls onDone promptly; `count={1e6}` is capped and the frame stays responsive; fractional counts are floored.
- [ ] **`ONB-011`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — Confetti is position:absolute with toast z-index — origin/coverage depend on an arbitrary positioned ancestor
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/confetti.tsx:99-105`
  - **Evidence:** style={{ position: "absolute", inset: 0, zIndex: tkZ.toast, pointerEvents: "none", ...style }} — the burst fills the nearest positioned ancestor, not the viewport. The kit's own story has to compensate: onboarding.stories.tsx:42 wraps it in `<Screen style={{ position: "relative" }}>`, and Checkout.tsx:359 drops it into `<TKPage>` whose root (page.tsx:50-66) is NOT position:relative.
  - **Impact:** In the real trailhead Checkout the confetti's containing block is whatever positioned ancestor happens to exist (the `.tk` root or none), so the burst may cover the wrong region or the whole viewport unexpectedly, and `zIndex: tkZ.toast` can render it under a higher overlay (sheet/dialog/popper). Consumers must just know to add a relative wrapper — an undocumented footgun.
  - **Fix:** Offer a `fixed` mode (default to `position: fixed; inset: 0` so it reliably blankets the WebView) or document the required `position: relative` ancestor and expose a `container`/portal target; reconsider z-index so a celebration over a success sheet isn't occluded.
  - **Done when:** Dropping <TKConfetti/> into TKPage without a relative wrapper bursts over the full visible area; docs/story state the positioning contract; the burst is not hidden behind the success sheet that triggered it.
