- [ ] **`ONB-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-EDGE` — Reduced-motion read once at mount and confetti is fully aria-hidden — toggling OS setting mid-session and SR users get no completion signal
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/templates/confetti.tsx:18-34`
  - **Evidence:** const [alive, setAlive] = useState(() => !prefersReducedMotion()); … useEffect(() => { if (!alive) doneRef.current?.(); }, []); and the wrapper is `aria-hidden="true"` (line 101) with no companion live-region. `prefersReducedMotion` is sampled only in the initial state and never re-subscribed.
  - **Impact:** If reduced-motion was off at mount but the burst is the only feedback for 'payment complete', screen-reader users (the canvas is aria-hidden) get no equivalent announcement — the success is invisible to them. The once-only read also means a user who flips the OS toggle during a session sees stale behavior. The reduced-motion path correctly skips visuals but provides no accessible substitute.
  - **Fix:** Keep the reduced-motion visual skip, but document that consumers must pair the burst with an accessible success message (or add an optional `announce`/`label` prop that renders a visually-hidden `role="status"`); subscribe to the media query via `matchMedia.addEventListener('change', …)` so it reacts.
  - **Done when:** With a screen reader and reduced-motion on, completing the rewarded action still produces a polite announcement (via prop or documented pairing); flipping the OS reduced-motion setting affects the next burst without a reload.

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`ONB-DX-001`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** The whole first-run flow — welcome gate, the tour, persistence, and the post-tour add-to-home prompt — has to be hand-wired by every consumer. examples/trailhead/src/components/Onboarding.tsx is 130 lines of phase state, a <dialog> with showModal/onCancel plumbing, a manual COMPLETE_ONBOARDING dispatch, querySelector to grab the third tab button, and a toast on finish — none of which the template helps with. The published 'seen once' machinery (storageKey/storage in onboarding.tsx:22-24,51-63) is the easy 10% while the consumer re-implements the hard 90% by hand.
  - **Proposal:** Ship a `useTKOnboarding({ storageKey, storage })` hook that owns the gate so the common case is a few lines: `const tour = useTKOnboarding({ storageKey: 'v1', storage })` returns `{ shouldShow, start(), TourProps }` plus a `tour.markSeen()`. Consumer renders `{tour.shouldShow && <TKOnboardingTooltip {...tour.tourProps} steps={steps} />}`. The hook does the async storage read, the stale-replay guard, and exposes a single boolean — collapsing trailhead's Onboarding.tsx phase reducer and the swallowed storage write into managed state.
- [ ] **`ONB-DX-002`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKOnboardingTooltip is monolithic: the bubble layout (title/text/counter/Next/Skip in onboarding.tsx:131-153) is hard-coded inline styles with no way to inject custom bubble content, a media slot, a 'Back' affordance, or branded chrome. A consumer who wants an illustration or a progress-dots row has to fork the file. There is also no `render`/children escape hatch — `step.title` and `step.text` are the only seams, both ReactNode but boxed into the fixed flex column.
  - **Proposal:** Add a per-step `render?: (ctx: { index: number; total: number; next(): void; back(): void; skip(): void; finish(): void }) => ReactNode` and a top-level `renderBubble` fallback. When provided, the template supplies only the spotlight + positioned TKPopper shell and hands all bubble content to the consumer. Keep the current default layout as the zero-config path. This is the asChild-style escape hatch the rest of the kit offers (TKPopper already takes arbitrary children) — it lets teams brand the coach mark without forking.
- [ ] **`ONB-DX-003`** · a11y · effort M
  - **Pain:** The template renders its own bare scrim+bubble (onboarding.tsx:113-154) and passes the default `role="tooltip"` to TKPopper, even though TKPopper *already supports* `role="dialog"` with focus-trap/outside-tap semantics (popper-tooltip.tsx:33-38, onClose at :28). So the kit ships the accessible primitive but the template opts out of it — every consumer inherits the dead, unfocusable, unannounced overlay (the root cause behind red-team ONB-002). a11y is not the consumer's job to bolt on; the template should be correct by construction.
  - **Proposal:** Make the template drive TKPopper with `role="dialog"`, wire `onClose` to the skip/dismiss path, and emit `aria-modal`, a labelled region (`aria-labelledby` the step title, `aria-describedby` the text), and an `aria-live="polite"` step-change announcement ('Step 2 of 3') by default — no new props required. Consumers literally cannot ship the inaccessible variant because there is no inaccessible variant to ship.
- [ ] **`ONB-DX-004`** · defaults · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKConfetti's DEFAULT_COLORS (confetti.tsx:16) is six raw hex literals, and the very first one (`#3390ec`) is a hand-copied duplicate of the `--tk-accent` token whose real source of truth is tokens.css:19. Theme the app — change `--tk-accent` — and the confetti stays Telegram-blue; switch to dark and the festive palette ignores it. The common 'celebrate in my brand color' case requires the consumer to pass a full `colors` array they have to author themselves (builds on ONB-008 but targets the default-derivation, not just the literals).
  - **Proposal:** Default `colors` to a token-derived palette read at render: `[ 'var(--tk-accent)', 'color-mix(in srgb, var(--tk-accent) 70%, #fff)', 'var(--tk-success)', ... ]`, resolving CSS vars via getComputedStyle on the canvas element before painting (canvas fillStyle can't take a var()). Add an `accent?: string` shorthand so `<TKConfetti accent="var(--tk-accent)" />` generates a tasteful tint ramp from one value. Common case becomes one prop and tracks the theme for free.
- [ ] **`ONB-DX-005`** · api · effort L
  - **Pain:** TKConfetti is mount-to-fire: you celebrate by conditionally rendering it and tearing it down in onDone, which every consumer reinvents as a boolean+setState dance (examples/trailhead/src/features/discover/Checkout.tsx:359 `{confetti ? <TKConfetti onDone={() => setConfetti(false)} /> : null}`). That fights React — re-firing requires a remount, and there is no imperative 'burst now' that matches the kit's own house pattern (useTKToast returns an imperative `show()`, toasts.tsx:164).
  - **Proposal:** Add a `useTKConfetti()` hook returning `{ fire(opts?) }` backed by a single persistent, pooled canvas mounted once near the provider — `const confetti = useTKConfetti(); confetti.fire()` on success. No mount/unmount, no boolean state, re-fires instantly, and it can portal to a top-level layer (fixing the positioned-ancestor coverage issue, ONB-011, by construction). Keep the declarative `<TKConfetti/>` for the simple render-once case.
- [ ] **`ONB-DX-006`** · perf · effort S
  - **Pain:** Good defaults shouldn't require the consumer to know the magic numbers. The Storybook story (onboarding.stories.tsx:47) already overrides to `count={24} duration={1200}` because the shipped default of 150 particles / 1800ms (confetti.tsx:26) is heavy for a phone — implying even the kit's own authors found the default wrong. Consumers have no guidance on sane ranges, and unclamped values silently no-op or starve the frame (ONB-010).
  - **Proposal:** Lower defaults to a phone-tuned `count: 80, duration: 1500`, internally clamp to `count ∈ [1, 400]` / `duration ∈ [200, 8000]` with a dev-only `console.warn` naming the clamp ('TKConfetti: count 5000 clamped to 400'), and expose a named `intensity?: 'subtle' | 'normal' | 'celebration'` preset so consumers pick a vibe instead of guessing two numbers. perf-by-default plus a self-documenting API.
- [ ] **`ONB-DX-007`** · types · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** TKOnboardingStep.target is `RefObject<HTMLElement | null>` (onboarding.tsx:8), so consumers who hold a `RefObject<HTMLButtonElement>` or `RefObject<HTMLDivElement>` must widen or cast, and the trailhead author resorted to `useRef<HTMLElement | null>(null)` + a querySelector assignment (Onboarding.tsx:26-30) to satisfy it. There is also no inference linking a step to its target element type, and `storageKey`/`storage` are independently optional so you can pass one without the other and get a silently no-persisting tour (the misuse behind ONB-007) with zero type feedback.
  - **Proposal:** Loosen the target to also accept a `() => HTMLElement | null` getter and a CSS selector string, and type it covariantly so any `RefObject<HTMLElement>` subtype assigns without a cast. Make persistence a single discriminated prop — `persist?: { key: string; storage: TKOnboardingStorage }` — so the type system enforces 'both or neither' and the no-op-persistence footgun becomes unrepresentable.
- [ ] **`ONB-DX-008`** · docs · effort S
  - **Pain:** There is exactly one onboarding story (onboarding.stories.tsx) covering the trivial single-step coach mark and a static confetti burst — and no standalone Confetti story, no docs for the storage adapter, no copy-paste recipe for the cloud-storage 'seen once' wiring (the headline feature in the TKOnboardingStorage doc comment, onboarding.tsx:14), no multi-step example, and nothing showing the post-tour handoff that trailhead actually needs. A developer adopting this has to read the source to learn the intended flow.
  - **Proposal:** Add stories/MDX: (1) a multi-step tour with skip/finish and an `onFinish` toast handoff; (2) the `storage={useCloudStorage()}` recipe shown as a copy-paste snippet; (3) a dedicated Confetti story with the imperative `useTKConfetti().fire()` button and an accent-token swap; (4) a reduced-motion note. Each story doubles as the canonical copy-paste source so the common integration is lifted verbatim, not reverse-engineered.
- [ ] **`ONB-DX-009`** · ergonomics · effort S
  - **Pain:** Haptics are the house pattern (useOptionalHaptics from @tg-mini-app/telegram exposes selection/impact/notification, provider.tsx:103-126; switch/pull-to-refresh fire them unconditionally), yet onboarding step-advance/finish and confetti completion are silent (ONB-006). Worse, there's no prop to opt in — a consumer who wants a celebratory `notification('success')` on the confetti's onDone has to wrap it themselves, and reduced-motion users (who get no visual burst, confetti.tsx:27) get no alternative signal at all.
  - **Proposal:** Fire `useOptionalHaptics()` by default — `selection()` on step advance, `notification('success')` on finish and on confetti burst start — since the hook is already no-op-safe when haptics are disabled. Add `haptics?: boolean` (default true) to both components to opt out. Under reduced motion, still fire the success notification so the completion is felt even when the confetti is suppressed — turning a silent dead-end into a multi-sensory confirmation with zero consumer code.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

COVERAGE GAPS: There are zero unit/component tests for either file (no onboarding.test.tsx / confetti.test.tsx anywhere under the repo; only one shared Storybook story onboarding.stories.tsx covering a single happy-path step each). The trailhead a11y spec (examples/trailhead/e2e/a11y.spec.ts:55) only asserts confetti renders nothing under reduced motion — it does not cover the coach-mark's focus/keyboard/dismiss behavior, so ONB-001/002/004 are entirely unguarded by the e2e gate. ASSUMPTIONS: (1) ONB-005's swipe-to-minimize interaction is inferred from page.tsx:82-84 comments and Telegram WebView behavior, not directly observed on a device; the double getBoundingClientRect (onboarding bumpRect + TKPopper sync) is confirmed from code. (2) ONB-011's containing-block claim assumes no other position:relative ancestor wraps TKPage in trailhead; verified that TKPage's own root (page.tsx:50-66) sets only display:flex, and the kit story compensates with an explicit relative wrapper. (3) ONB-008: I could not find a runtime that injects --tk-accent into DEFAULT_COLORS, so the JSDoc 'accent + festive palette' claim is treated as unimplemented based on the literal array. (4) useOptionalHaptics is re-exported from @tg-mini-app/telegram (foundation/telegram.tsx is a shim) and is the confirmed house pattern (main-button.tsx, switch.tsx, pin.tsx, etc.), so wiring it into these templates is consistent and runtime-absent-safe. NOT FILED (considered, low signal): confetti's per-particle ctx.save/translate/rotate/restore is acceptable for a one-shot ≤600-particle burst once count is clamped (ONB-010); the onboarding scrim's `boxShadow: 0 0 0 100vmax var(--tk-scrim)` cutout technique is a legitimate pattern and renders fine. The intentional exhaustive-deps omissions (onboarding.tsx:65-73) are well-justified in comments and not flagged. RTL/long-i18n bubble overflow (D-RESP) was reviewed but the popper's maxWidth clamp (popper-tooltip.tsx:198) largely mitigates it, so it was folded into context rather than filed as a standalone finding.

---

### Typography + tokens — tokens/typography.tsx, tokens/tokens.css (type scale, token surface)

`TYP` · `packages/uikit/src/tokens` · 12 findings (0🔴 4🟠 5🟡 3⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-THEME` Tokens / theming correctness · `D-RESP` Narrow WebView / i18n / dynamic scaling · `D-A11Y` Accessibility (headings, contrast, labelling) · `D-API` API ergonomics / footguns · `D-TYPES` TypeScript surface quality · `D-TG` Telegram runtime / SSR import safety · `D-PERF` Performance / inline-style churn

#### Findings

- [x] **`TYP-001`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-THEME` — Base `.tk` paints color/background from tokens that only exist under data-theme/.tk-tg — bare `.tk` renders unstyled/transparent
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/tokens.css:101-104`
  - **Evidence:** `.tk { ... color: var(--tk-text); background: var(--tk-bg); }` — but `--tk-text` and `--tk-bg` are declared ONLY inside `.tk[data-theme="light"]` (l.111,115), `.tk[data-theme="dark"]` (l.136,140) and `.tk.tk-tg` (l.164,166). The base block has no `var(--tk-text, #...)` fallback.
  - **Impact:** A consumer who slaps `className="tk"` on a node without going through TKThemeProvider (or forgets `data-theme`) gets `color: var(--tk-text)` = invalid → inherited/black text on a transparent background. TKText/TKTitle also resolve `var(--tk-fz-*)` and `var(--tk-text-2/3)` which still work, but the page background and base text color silently vanish. This is the single most common 'I imported the kit and it looks broken' trap.
  - **Fix:** Declare safe defaults on the base `.tk` block (treat light as the default theme): add `--tk-text:#131c26; --tk-bg:#eef1f6; --tk-surface:#fff; --tk-text-2:#57636f; --tk-text-3:#646e79;` etc. in `.tk {}` so `[data-theme]`/`.tk-tg` only override. Alternatively give every base `var()` an inline fallback.
  - **Done when:** Render `<div className="tk">x</div>` (no data-theme, no tk-tg) in a browser/JSDOM and assert computed `color` and `background-color` are concrete colors, not empty/transparent. Add a tokens-contract test asserting `--tk-text`/`--tk-bg`/`--tk-surface` resolve on a bare `.tk`.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — tokens.css base `.tk` block (l.103-104) sets `color: var(--tk-text); background: var(--tk-bg)` with no fallback, and both vars are declared only under `.tk[data-theme=light/dark]` (l.115/140,111/136) and `.tk.tk-tg` (l.164/166). A bare `className="tk"` without the provider's `data-theme` (theme.tsx l.148) leaves both invalid → unstyled text/transparent bg.
- [x] **`TYP-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-THEME` — TKTitle `level="large"` points at `--tk-fz-large`, but no token named that path is wrong — actually it resolves, yet `large` is unreachable type-vs-token mismatch with TKText
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/typography.tsx:89`
  - **Evidence:** `const size = level === "large" ? "large" : ...; ... fontSize: \`var(--tk-fz-${size})\``. Token `--tk-fz-large` exists (tokens.css l.81). BUT TKText's `size` union (l.20) is `caption2|caption|footnote|sub|body|title3` and excludes `title1/title2/large`, while TKTitle hardcodes the title sizes. There is no single source of truth: the `--tk-fz-*` token set and the two component size unions are maintained by hand and already drift (TKText cannot render title1/title2/large; TKTitle cannot render body/sub).
  - **Impact:** Mini App teams cannot pick `title1`/`large` body text via TKText, and adding a new scale step requires editing 3 places (css token, TKText union, TKTitle mapping) with no compile-time link. Easy to ship a `size`/`level` that interpolates to a non-existent `--tk-fz-foo` (e.g. a typo) and get `font-size: var(--tk-fz-foo)` = inherited, with zero error.
  - **Fix:** Derive a single `TKFontSize` union from the token names and share it; or validate `size`/`level` against a const map of declared tokens. At minimum, expand TKText `size` to include `title1|title2|large` and add a contract test that every interpolated `--tk-fz-${x}` is in the declared-token set.
  - **Done when:** A test enumerates all values of TKText.size and TKTitle.level, renders each, and asserts the resulting `--tk-fz-*` name is present in tokens.css declared tokens (reuse the declaredTokens set from tokens-contract.test).
  - **Verify:** ❌ refuted — The headline claim is self-refuting: `var(--tk-fz-large)` resolves to the existing token `--tk-fz-large` (tokens.css l.81) and `level="large"` (typography.tsx l.89) works correctly. The remaining point (TKText/TKTitle size unions don't share a source of truth with the token set) is an API-ergonomics opinion, not a defect — both components map only to valid hardcoded literals.
- [x] **`TYP-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-RESP` — `truncate` is single-line-only and hard-codes white-space:nowrap; no multi-line clamp for long i18n strings
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/typography.tsx:54-56`
  - **Evidence:** `overflow: truncate ? "hidden" : undefined, textOverflow: truncate ? "ellipsis" : undefined, whiteSpace: truncate ? "nowrap" : undefined`. Identical block in TKTitle (l.101-103). There is no `lines`/`clamp` prop.
  - **Impact:** In a 360px Telegram WebView, German/Russian/Arabic strings are far longer than English. The only escape is `truncate` which forces a single line — a 2-line guide name or title gets chopped to one line + ellipsis. Consumers (see GuideDirectory.tsx using `truncate` on names/footnotes) must drop down to raw `style={{ WebkitLineClamp }}` workarounds, defeating the token component.
  - **Fix:** Add a `lines?: number` prop: when `lines>1` apply `display:-webkit-box; -webkit-box-orient:vertical; -webkit-line-clamp:lines; overflow:hidden;` and keep `truncate` as the `lines:1` shorthand. Keep `white-space:normal` for the multi-line case.
  - **Done when:** Render `<TKText truncate lines={2}>` with overflowing text in JSDOM/browser and assert computed style has `-webkit-line-clamp:2` and `display:-webkit-box`; single-line `truncate` still yields `white-space:nowrap`.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — typography.tsx hardcodes `whiteSpace: truncate ? "nowrap"` in both TKText (l.56) and TKTitle (l.103) with no `lines`/`clamp` prop, so truncate is single-line only. Consumers like GuideDirectory.tsx (l.111,114,165) rely on it; note the claim's `WebkitLineClamp` workaround does not actually appear in the repo, but the core no-multiline-clamp defect holds.
- [x] **`TYP-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-API` — No id / aria-* / role / lang passthrough — typography can't be labelled, described, or marked-up for i18n
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/typography.tsx:16-28`
  - **Evidence:** `TKTextProps` exposes only `children, as, tone, size, weight, align, truncate, className, style, testId`. There is no `...rest`, no `id`, no `aria-*`, no `lang`, no `dir`, no `title`. Same for TKTitleProps (l.65-75) and TKCaptionProps (l.112-120). The JSX spreads none of them: `<Tag data-testid={testId} className={className} style={...}>`.
  - **Impact:** A consumer cannot give a TKTitle an `id` to wire `aria-labelledby`, cannot add `aria-hidden` to decorative text, cannot set `lang`/`dir` on a foreign-language string for correct hyphenation/RTL, and cannot add a native `title` tooltip on a truncated label. Every such case forces wrapping in an extra element — exactly what a heading/text primitive should prevent.
  - **Fix:** Add `...rest: Omit<JSX.IntrinsicElements["span"|"p"|"div"], 'style'|'className'|'color'>` (or an explicit `id?`, `lang?`, `dir?`, `aria-*`, `title?` set) and spread it onto `<Tag {...rest}>` before the controlled props.
  - **Done when:** `<TKTitle id="sec" aria-describedby="d" lang="de">` renders an element exposing `id="sec"`, `aria-describedby="d"`, `lang="de"` (assert via getByRole + getAttribute).
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — TKTextProps (l.16-28), TKTitleProps (l.65-75) and TKCaptionProps (l.112-120) expose no `...rest`, `id`, `aria-*`, `lang`, `dir`, `role`, or `title`; the JSX spreads only `data-testid`/`className`/`style` (e.g. l.43-46, 91-94). So consumers cannot wire `aria-labelledby` via id, mark decorative text `aria-hidden`, or set `lang`/`dir` without an extra wrapper element.
- [x] **`TYP-005`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — No ref forwarding on any of the three components
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/typography.tsx:30-41`
  - **Evidence:** `export function TKText({...}: TKTextProps) { return (<Tag ...>` — plain function components, no `forwardRef`. TKTitle (l.77) and TKCaption (l.122) likewise. No `ref` in any props interface.
  - **Impact:** Consumers can't measure a title for sticky-header collapse, can't scroll-into-view a section heading, can't attach an IntersectionObserver to a caption, and can't pass these to libraries (focus managers, tooltip anchors) that require a DOM ref. In a Telegram app where headers/anchors are common, this forces a wrapper div on every use.
  - **Fix:** Wrap each in `React.forwardRef<HTMLElement, Props>` and forward `ref` to `<Tag ref={ref}>`. Set `displayName`.
  - **Done when:** `const r = useRef(); render(<TKTitle ref={r}>x</TKTitle>)` then assert `r.current instanceof HTMLHeadingElement`.
- [x] **`TYP-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-A11Y` — TKTitle `as` decouples heading level from visual level — silent heading-order breakage; `as="div"` drops the heading role entirely
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/typography.tsx:88`
  - **Evidence:** `const Tag = as ?? (level === 1 || level === "large" ? "h1" : level === 2 ? "h2" : "h3"); const size = ...`. `as` and `level` are independent: `<TKTitle level={1} as="h3">` renders an `<h3>` styled as title1, and `<TKTitle level={2} as="div">` renders a non-heading. Nothing warns.
  - **Impact:** It's trivially easy to produce out-of-order or missing headings (a Playwright a11y/axe gate or a screen-reader rotor will flag 'heading levels skipped' or 'no h1'). Because the kit advertises real headings, teams trust it and ship inaccessible pages. `level="large"` collapsing to `h1` also means two h1s if a real h1 exists elsewhere.
  - **Fix:** Either drop `as` for TKTitle and derive the tag strictly from level (1→h1,2→h2,3→h3), or keep `as` but constrain its type to heading tags and dev-warn when `as` heading level disagrees with `level`. Document that visual size and semantic level are intentionally separable only via an explicit `headingLevel` prop.
  - **Done when:** axe/jsx-a11y rule on a story rendering nested TKTitles passes (no heading-order violation); a dev-mode console.warn fires when `as` heading number != `level`.
- [x] **`TYP-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-API` — TKTitle ignores weight entirely (hard-coded 700) and TKText line-height is non-overridable except via full style override
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/typography.tsx:98-99`
  - **Evidence:** TKTitle: `fontWeight: 700, lineHeight: 1.12` with no `weight` prop in TKTitleProps. TKText: `lineHeight: size.startsWith("caption") ? 1.25 : 1.35` with no `leading`/`lineHeight` prop. The only override is the spread `...style` (l.57/104), which is a blunt instrument.
  - **Impact:** A team that wants a 600-weight title (common iOS 'semibold' look) or tighter leading for a multi-line body block must hand-write `style={{ fontWeight: 600 }}` / `style={{ lineHeight: 1.2 }}`, bypassing the design system and risking inconsistency. The `weight` prop existing on TKText but not TKTitle is an inconsistency footgun.
  - **Fix:** Add `weight?: TKTextWeight` to TKTitleProps (default 700) and a `leading?: number|string` prop to TKText/TKTitle, or expose line-height as semantic tokens (--tk-lh-tight/normal). Keep the API symmetric.
  - **Done when:** `<TKTitle weight={600}>` renders with computed `font-weight:600`; `<TKText leading={1.2}>` yields `line-height:1.2`.
- [ ] **`TYP-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-PERF` — Per-render fresh inline-style object on every text node — no className path, defeats style sharing in long lists
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/typography.tsx:46-58`
  - **Evidence:** `style={{ display: ..., color: toneColor[tone], fontSize: \`var(--tk-fz-${size})\`, fontWeight: weight, lineHeight: ..., ...style }}` is rebuilt on every render of every TKText. Lists in trailhead (GuideDirectory/GuideProfile/Feed) render dozens of TKText per row.
  - **Impact:** In a feed/list of N items each row has several TKText/TKTitle; every parent re-render allocates fresh style objects and forces React to diff inline styles per node. There's no static class to lean on the CSS engine. On low-end Android Telegram clients this is measurable jank during scroll/re-render, and the components aren't memoized.
  - **Fix:** Provide static CSS classes for size/tone/weight (e.g. `.tk-fz-body`, `.tk-tone-secondary`) in tokens.css and have the components compose className + a small dynamic style only for truly dynamic bits (align/truncate). Optionally `React.memo` the components.
  - **Done when:** Profiling a 50-item list re-render shows TKText no longer allocating a new style object when props are unchanged (memo) and/or computed styling comes from classes; snapshot of generated className includes `tk-fz-*`/`tk-tone-*`.
- [x] **`TYP-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-THEME` — Whole token system relies on `color-mix()` with no fallback — older Telegram WebViews drop ink/surface/glass/scrim colors
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/tokens.css:84-98`
  - **Evidence:** `--tk-accent-06..35`, `--tk-accent-grad`, `--tk-accent-ink`, `--tk-red/green/orange-ink` and (l.176-179) `.tk-tg` `--tk-surface-2/3`, `--tk-glass`, `--tk-scrim` are ALL `color-mix(in srgb, ...)`. No `@supports` fallback or solid fallback value.
  - **Impact:** color-mix() shipped to stable webviews relatively late. On an older in-app Telegram WebView (still common on budget Android), every color-mix token resolves to its guaranteed-invalid value → the variable is unset → text using `--tk-*-ink` falls back to inherited color and tinted surfaces/scrims disappear (modals with no dim backdrop = unreadable). The whole `.tk-tg` surface derivation collapses to undefined.
  - **Fix:** Add `@supports not (color: color-mix(in srgb, red, blue))` overrides giving solid rgba fallbacks for the ink/surface/glass/scrim tokens, or precompute static rgba values and only use color-mix as progressive enhancement.
  - **Done when:** In a browser with color-mix disabled (or via @supports test), computed `--tk-scrim`/`--tk-accent-ink` resolve to a concrete color, not empty; visual a11y story shows a visible scrim.
- [ ] **`TYP-010`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-RESP` — `--tk-fz` is a fixed 16px px value, not rem — ignores OS / browser text-zoom for accessibility
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/tokens.css:22`
  - **Evidence:** `--tk-fz: 16px;` and the entire type scale multiplies it (l.73-81). All sizes are px-derived.
  - **Impact:** Users who bump system/browser font size for low vision get no scaling: the whole kit's type is locked to 16px-based px. The Tweaks panel can change it, but the end user's accessibility text-zoom is ignored. WCAG 1.4.4 (resize text) is effectively unmet for the default.
  - **Fix:** Default `--tk-fz` to `1rem` (and keep the px knob as an override), so root-font-size / user zoom propagates. Multipliers stay unitless.
  - **Done when:** Set root `font-size:200%` (browser zoom emulation) and assert TKText body computed font-size scales proportionally; tokens-contract test asserts `--tk-fz` default uses rem.
- [ ] **`TYP-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-TYPES` — `align` typed as raw CSSProperties['textAlign'] leaks logical/global values and isn't RTL-aware
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/typography.tsx:22`
  - **Evidence:** `align?: CSSProperties["textAlign"];` passed straight to `textAlign: align`. This accepts `left|right` (physical, breaks RTL), plus `match-parent|inherit|-webkit-*`, etc.
  - **Impact:** In an RTL Telegram locale, a consumer naturally writes `align="left"` and it stays physically left instead of start — a subtle i18n bug. The over-broad type also surfaces nonsense values in editor autocomplete with no guidance toward logical `start|end`.
  - **Fix:** Narrow to `align?: "start" | "center" | "end"` and map to logical `text-align: start|center|end` (or keep CSSProperties but document RTL guidance). Prefer logical values.
  - **Done when:** TKTextProps.align type rejects `"left"` at compile time (or maps it); an RTL story shows `align="start"` flipping to the right edge.
- [ ] **`TYP-012`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-A11Y` — Caption `uppercase` transforms visually but text-transform is announced literally / spacing tweak hard-coded; tone default 'secondary' on tinted surfaces is borderline contrast
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/tokens/typography.tsx:139-140`
  - **Evidence:** `letterSpacing: uppercase ? ".05em" : 0, textTransform: uppercase ? "uppercase" : undefined`. TKCaption default `tone="secondary"` → `--tk-text-2` (l.124). Text-2 dark is `#aebbc9` on surface-2 derived fills.
  - **Impact:** `text-transform:uppercase` keeps the accessible name lowercase (good) but some screen readers spell out all-caps; more importantly secondary/tertiary tones (`--tk-text-2`/`--tk-text-3`) used at the tiny caption/caption2 sizes are near the AA edge on `surface-2/3`, and there is no contrast guard or token note for caption-on-tinted. Tertiary on tinted fills can fail AA for small text.
  - **Fix:** Document/guarantee that tertiary tone meets AA only on the base surface; or add `--tk-text-3` that is verified AA at caption sizes; consider not auto-uppercasing the accessible content. Add a contrast unit check for text-2/text-3 against surface/surface-2/surface-3.
  - **Done when:** A contrast test computes WCAG ratio of --tk-text-2 and --tk-text-3 against --tk-surface/-2/-3 in both themes and asserts >=4.5:1 for the sizes captions use (or documents the exception).

#### DX proposals

- [ ] **`TYP-DX-001`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** Devs want non-semantic emphasis but TKTitle always emits a heading role, so they style TKText as a title instead: `as="div" weight={700}` appears in ~12 example sites (examples/trailhead/src/features/guide/GuideProfile.tsx:76, GuideDirectory.tsx:32/165, discover/Feed.tsx:164). The common case (a bold visual label) is 3 props + an `as` override instead of one. typography.tsx has no notion of 'emphasis level' separate from heading semantics.
  - **Proposal:** Add a `variant` prop to TKText that bundles the common recipes so the everyday case is one token: `variant?: "body" | "label" | "strong" | "caption" | "footnote"`. `label` => weight 600 + size sub, `strong` => weight 700, `caption` => size caption + weight 600 tertiary. `<TKText variant="strong">{title}</TKText>` replaces `<TKText as="div" weight={700}>`. Keep size/weight/tone as explicit overrides on top. Variant maps to a className (see TYP-DX-006) not inline style, so the 80% path needs zero per-prop config.
- [ ] **`TYP-DX-002`** · ergonomics · effort S
  - **Pain:** `truncate` is all-or-nothing: it forces nowrap + ellipsis + overflow:hidden. Devs who just want 'don't wrap this price/badge' must drop to `style={{ whiteSpace: "nowrap" }}` — 7 occurrences (examples/trailhead/src/features/discover/Feed.tsx:128, DateSlot.tsx:136, guide/GuideProfile.tsx:113). Multi-line clamp (TYP-003) is impossible. The single boolean can't express the three real intents (no-wrap, single-line ellipsis, N-line clamp).
  - **Proposal:** Replace the boolean with a union that covers all three intents and a numeric clamp: `truncate?: boolean | number` where `true` = 1-line ellipsis, a number = N-line line-clamp (-webkit-line-clamp), and add a separate `nowrap?: boolean` for 'don't wrap, no ellipsis'. `<TKText truncate={2}>` clamps long i18n strings to 2 lines; `<TKText nowrap>` replaces the manual whiteSpace style. Keep `truncate` boolean-compatible so existing call sites don't break.
- [ ] **`TYP-DX-003`** · ergonomics · effort S
  - **Pain:** Telegram Mini Apps are full of prices (Stars), ratings, counts and timers, so tabular figures are a constant need — but there is no prop, forcing `style={{ fontVariantNumeric: "tabular-nums" }}` (examples/trailhead/src/features/discover/Feed.tsx:147 and similar). Without it, numbers jitter as they update (live counters, stepper totals). This is exactly the kind of perf/quality default a kit should own, not leave to memory.
  - **Proposal:** Add `numeric?: "tabular" | "oldstyle"` (and/or a boolean `tabular`) to TKText/TKTitle that sets `fontVariantNumeric` via className. `<TKText weight={700} numeric="tabular">{priceStars}</TKText>`. Consider defaulting tabular automatically when content is detected as numeric-heavy in TKCaption used as metadata — at minimum expose the prop so price/rating call sites stop reaching into `style`.
- [ ] **`TYP-DX-004`** · a11y · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** TKTitle `as` lets the heading level (h1/h2/h3) drift from the visual `level`, and `as="div"` silently drops the heading role (typography.tsx:88). Heading order is the single biggest a11y footgun in a kit, and nothing guides or warns the dev. The example codebase already avoids `as` entirely (0 TKTitle `as=` usages) — meaning when someone finally needs it, there's no guardrail and they'll ship broken heading order or a roleless title.
  - **Proposal:** Make semantics a11y-by-default and the override loud: (1) decouple visual size (`level`) from semantic tag via an explicit `as` that is constrained to heading tags by default; (2) when a consumer wants visual-only emphasis without a heading, steer them to TKText (TYP-DX-001) instead of `as="div"` — drop or deprecate `as="div"` on TKTitle; (3) in dev builds, `console.warn` once when `as`'s implied level differs from visual `level` (e.g. `level={1}` rendered as `h3`). Signature: `as?: "h1"|"h2"|"h3"|"h4"|"h5"|"h6"`. Cheap to add, prevents silently inaccessible output.
- [ ] **`TYP-DX-005`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** Per-component pain leaks into `style` constantly: `style={{ fontSize: 20 }}` for an off-scale size (Train.tsx:100), `style={{ marginTop: 4 }}` (Checkout.tsx:297), `lineHeight`, `display:inline-flex`+gap to put an icon next to text (Feed.tsx:138-154). Because there is no asChild/polymorphic escape hatch and the prop API is narrow, every non-listed need becomes an inline-style override that fights the kit's own inline styles and is impossible to theme. There's no way to render typography styling onto an existing element (e.g. a link, a Trans/i18n wrapper) without an extra wrapper node.
  - **Proposal:** Add an `asChild` escape hatch (Radix-style Slot) so typography styling/semantics can be merged onto an arbitrary child: `<TKText asChild weight={700}><a href={url}>{label}</a></TKText>`. This kills wrapper-div boilerplate around links/i18n components and lets icon+text rows be a single styled element. Pair with a documented `style`/className precedence so consumer overrides reliably win (move base styles to a class layer per TYP-DX-006).
- [ ] **`TYP-DX-006`** · perf · effort L
  - **Pain:** Every text node builds a fresh inline-style object per render (typography.tsx:46-58, 94-105) — in long virtualized Telegram lists (Feed has many TKText per card) this defeats style sharing and bloats the DOM with duplicated inline style strings, and the inline styles also can't be overridden by a consumer className without `!important`. tokens.css already establishes a `tk` cascade layer but typography doesn't use it.
  - **Proposal:** Emit stable utility classes from tokens.css instead of inline styles: `.tk-text`, `.tk-fz-{size}`, `.tk-tone-{tone}`, `.tk-w-{weight}`, `.tk-truncate`, `.tk-clamp` (with `--tk-clamp-lines`), `.tk-nowrap`, all inside `@layer tk`. Components compose className strings; only truly dynamic values (clamp line count, custom align) use a CSS var or a tiny style object. Net: identical text shares one rule, consumer unlayered styles win without `!important`, and the render is allocation-free for the common case.
- [ ] **`TYP-DX-007`** · api · effort M
  - **Pain:** TKCaption is a near-duplicate of `<TKText size="caption" weight={600} tone="secondary" />` and has ZERO real usages in either example app (only the Storybook demo). The parallel third component splits the mental model ('which one do I import?'), doubles the a11y/ref/passthrough surface that must be fixed (TYP-004/005), and its `uppercase` prop is its only unique feature (typography.tsx:139-140) — which is itself an a11y smell (announced literally) better handled by a CSS class.
  - **Proposal:** Collapse TKCaption into TKText: expose `variant="caption"` (TYP-DX-001) and an `uppercase?: boolean` (or `caps`) prop on TKText that applies `.tk-caps` (text-transform + letter-spacing via class). Re-export `TKCaption` as a thin deprecated alias `(props) => <TKText variant="caption" {...props} />` with a JSDoc `@deprecated` so existing imports and the README table keep working while the surface shrinks to two components.
- [ ] **`TYP-DX-008`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** The one Storybook story (storybook/tokens/typography.stories.tsx) is a single static TypeScale panel with no Controls/argTypes, no copy-pasteable per-prop examples, and no coverage of truncation, multi-line clamp, tones-on-surfaces, RTL/align, or the as/level semantics — exactly the props that bite. README only lists the component names (README.md:165). A dev can't discover `tone`, valid `size`s, or the heading-semantics rules without reading source, which is why example code reinvents recipes in `style`.
  - **Proposal:** Ship an interactive, recipe-driven story set: (1) an `argTypes`-wired Playground for TKText/TKTitle with live tone/size/weight/truncate/align controls; (2) a 'Recipes' page with copy-paste snippets for the patterns the examples keep hand-rolling (price row w/ tabular nums, 2-line clamped i18n string, list item title+subtitle, label vs heading); (3) a contrast matrix rendering every tone on bg/surface/surface-2/surface-3 to surface the secondary-on-tint contrast risk (TYP-012). Add a short Typography section to README linking the Playground.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

Coverage: I read both source files in full (typography.tsx, tokens.css), the barrel (src/index.ts line 1 side-effect-imports tokens.css and line 5 re-exports typography), both existing tests (test/tokens-contract.test.ts, test/tokens-reorg.test.tsx), the Storybook story (storybook/tokens/typography.stories.tsx), and consumer usage in examples/trailhead (GuideDirectory/GuideProfile/Feed) and examples/surface-composer. Existing test gaps that let these slip: tokens-contract only checks that token NAMES are declared, never that they RESOLVE to a value on a bare `.tk` (TYP-001) and never that every interpolated `--tk-fz-${size}` is reachable from a component (TYP-002); tokens-reorg only checks identity/props shape and one inline render, not refs/a11y/truncation/i18n. ASSUMPTIONS: (a) TYP-001 severity assumes consumers sometimes apply `.tk` without TKThemeProvider — TKThemeProvider in foundation/theme.tsx l.148 always sets data-theme, so via the provider this is masked; the risk is the documented `className=\"tk tk-tg\"` manual path and any direct `.tk` usage. (b) TYP-009/TYP-010 (color-mix / px-vs-rem) reflect general WebView support and WCAG, not a reproduced failure on a specific Telegram client version. (c) TYP-012 contrast is asserted by inspection of the hex tokens, not a measured ratio — the acceptance check adds the missing measurement. No D-SEC issues found: typography uses no dangerouslySetInnerHTML, no src/file handling — it renders children only.

---

### Internal hooks — useControllable, useDragGesture, useOverlayLayer, useScrollLock, useVerticalSwipeGuard (shared primitives every composite depends on)

`INT` · `packages/uikit/src/internal` · 11 findings (1🔴 4🟠 4🟡 2⚪) · 8 DX

**Dimensions in scope:** `D-GESTURE` Touch/gesture correctness · `D-TG` Telegram runtime + SSR safety · `D-STATE` State model / controlled-value drift · `D-EDGE` Robustness / races / StrictMode / unmount-during-gesture · `D-PERF` Re-render & rAF behavior · `D-API` API ergonomics · `D-TYPES` TypeScript quality

#### Findings

- [x] **`INT-001`** · 🔴 CRITICAL · `D-GESTURE` — tkShouldCommit treats any flick >0.5px/ms as a commit regardless of distance OR size — micro-flicks close sheets and trigger destructive full-swipe
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/internal/useDragGesture.ts:33-36`
  - **Evidence:** export function tkShouldCommit(offset: number, velocity: number, size: number): boolean { if (offset <= 0) return false; return offset > size * 0.5 || velocity > 0.5; }
  - **Impact:** velocity is evaluated independently of size and independently of how far the gesture actually traveled. A 3px downward twitch with a fast release (common on a thumb tap that micro-slides) reports velocity>0.5 and commits: TKSheet closes, nav swipe-back pops the stack, and TKSwipeCell with fullSwipe fires the FIRST action (side[0].onAction(), swipe-cell.tsx:99-102) — a potentially destructive 'delete' — from what the user felt as a tap. The velocity branch ignores `size`, so the same flick over-commits identically on a 40px cell and a 600px sheet.
  - **Fix:** Require a minimum traveled distance before the velocity branch can commit (e.g. `return offset > size * 0.5 || (offset > Math.min(size * 0.15, 48) && velocity > 0.5);`), and gate fullSwipe destructive actions on a higher distance/velocity floor in swipe-cell.
  - **Done when:** Unit test tkShouldCommit(4, 0.6, 400) === false and tkShouldCommit(120, 0.6, 400) === true; e2e: a fast 4px flick on a fullSwipe cell does not fire the action; sheet stays open on a sub-threshold flick.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — useDragGesture.ts:34-35 `return offset > size * 0.5 || velocity > 0.5` does commit on velocity alone, ignoring size/distance — a fast flick just past the 6px activation threshold closes a TKSheet (sheet.tsx:130) or pops nav (nav.tsx:164). Note the swipe-cell destructive claim is wrong: side[0].onAction() at swipe-cell.tsx:99 is gated by `Math.abs(raw) > width*0.6`, not by tkShouldCommit, so a micro-flick cannot fire it; and the literal '3px twitch' never reaches onEnd because the drag needs ≥6px to activate (line 127/144).
- [x] **`INT-002`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-EDGE` — useDragGesture only checks `enabled` on pointerdown — disabling mid-gesture keeps firing onMove/onEnd and never releases pointer capture
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/internal/useDragGesture.ts:103-156`
  - **Evidence:** onPointerDown(e) { if (!enabled) return; ... } onPointerMove(e) { const d = drag.current; if (!d || d.canceled) return; ... } // no `enabled` check onPointerUp(e) { ... } // no `enabled` check, no releasePointerCapture
  - **Impact:** `enabled` is read once at pointerdown. If a consumer flips `enabled` to false during an active drag (e.g. TKSheet becomes non-dismissible, nav stack drops to length 1 mid-swipe — nav.tsx:157 `enabled: !!swipeBack && stack.length>1`), onMove/onEnd keep mutating state and can pop/close after the feature was disabled. Separately, setPointerCapture is acquired (line 129) but releasePointerCapture is never called on up/cancel, so capture lingers until the browser auto-releases — on some WebViews subsequent taps elsewhere are swallowed.
  - **Fix:** Snapshot enabled into an `enabledRef` and bail in onPointerMove/onPointerUp when it goes false (cancel the in-flight drag, call onEnd with zeroed state); call `e.currentTarget.releasePointerCapture?.(e.pointerId)` in onPointerUp/onPointerCancel.
  - **Done when:** Test: start drag, set enabled=false, fire pointermove — onMove not called and onEnd receives no-commit; assert releasePointerCapture invoked on pointerup.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — `enabled` is only read in onPointerDown (useDragGesture.ts:104); onPointerMove (116) and onPointerUp (141) have no enabled check, so flipping enabled false mid-drag still fires onEnd. setPointerCapture is called at line 129 but grep confirms releasePointerCapture is never called anywhere, so capture lingers until the browser auto-releases.
- [x] **`INT-003`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-GESTURE` — nav swipe-back (axis:x) ships no touch-action arbitration, so horizontal swipe-back fights native vertical scroll on narrow WebViews
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/composites/nav.tsx:187`
  - **Evidence:** <div ref={rootRef} {...handlers} ...> // axis:"x" drag handlers, no touchAction set // compare swipe-cell.tsx:170 `touchAction: "pan-y"` and sheet.tsx:204 `touchAction: "none"`
  - **Impact:** useDragGesture never sets touch-action or calls preventDefault — it relies entirely on each consumer adding the right `touch-action`. The sheet grab handle and swipe-cell/pull-to-refresh do; the nav stack does NOT. On a tall scrollable panel in a narrow Telegram WebView the browser may claim the vertical pan and the horizontal swipe-back drops samples or never activates, while a diagonal drag can scroll and pop simultaneously. The footgun is structural: nothing in the hook signals the requirement.
  - **Fix:** Add `touchAction: "pan-y"` to the nav root that carries the x-axis handlers, and document in useDragGesture's JSDoc that x-axis consumers must set `touch-action: pan-y` and y-axis consumers `touch-action: pan-x`/`none`.
  - **Done when:** Visual/e2e: on a scrollable nav panel, a horizontal drag pops the stack and a vertical drag scrolls without popping; computed style shows touch-action: pan-y on the swipe surface.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — The nav root div (nav.tsx:184-189) sets no touchAction while swipe-cell.tsx:170 uses `pan-y` and sheet.tsx:204 uses `none`; useDragGesture sets no touch-action and never calls preventDefault (grep found neither in the hook). The axis:'x' swipe-back thus has no touch-action arbitration against native vertical scroll, exactly as cited.
- [x] **`INT-004`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-STATE` — useControllable ignores a changing defaultValue and silently drops internal state when switching controlled→uncontrolled
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/internal/useControllable.ts:12-24`
  - **Evidence:** const [internal, setInternal] = useState(defaultValue); const isControlled = controlled !== undefined; ... return [isControlled ? controlled : internal, set];
  - **Impact:** Two real drift cases for the kit's controls (switch/checkbox/slider/input all funnel through this). (1) defaultValue is only honored on first render; if a consumer updates defaultValue (e.g. async-loaded form default) it's silently ignored with no warning. (2) If `controlled` transitions from a value to undefined (controlled→uncontrolled), the component snaps back to the stale first-render `internal` rather than the last controlled value — a classic React footgun, and unlike React's own inputs there is no dev warning about switching modes.
  - **Fix:** Add a dev-only warning when isControlled flips between renders; document that defaultValue is initial-only; optionally seed internal from the last controlled value on transition (track previous controlled in a ref).
  - **Done when:** Dev console warns on controlled↔uncontrolled flip; documented behavior test confirms defaultValue change after mount is a no-op (intended) and is called out in JSDoc.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — useControllable.ts:12 `useState(defaultValue)` honors defaultValue only on first render, and line 24 `isControlled ? controlled : internal` returns the stale first-render internal when controlled→undefined; there is no dev warning for changing defaultValue or switching modes. Code matches both claimed drift cases.
- [x] **`INT-005`** · 🟠 HIGH · `D-EDGE` — useOverlayLayer / useScrollLock / useVerticalSwipeGuard share unguarded module globals that desync across a duplicated bundle or a skipped cleanup
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/internal/useOverlayLayer.ts:22-23`
  - **Evidence:** let tkActiveLayers = 0; let tkTopLayer = 0; // also: useScrollLock tkScrollLockCount/tkPrevBody, useVerticalSwipeGuard tkSwipeGuardCount/tkSwipePrevEnabled
  - **Impact:** All three coordinators rely on a single module-scoped counter. The reset guard `if (tkActiveLayers <= 0)` is the only recovery path. If an unmount cleanup is skipped or two overlays of the same level race their effects, tkTopLayer drifts and z-indices collide; for scrollLock a missed release leaves body `position:fixed` permanently (page frozen). With duplicate React/bundle copies (common when a Mini App imports the kit AND a sub-dependency does) each copy gets its own counter and the scroll-lock/swipe-guard reference counts split, double-locking or prematurely restoring Telegram swipes.
  - **Fix:** Park the shared state on a `globalThis` symbol (`globalThis[Symbol.for('tk.overlay')]`) so duplicate module copies share one source of truth, and add a hard floor (never let counts go negative) plus a dev assert when a release finds count===0.
  - **Done when:** Test: import the module twice (separate registry entries) and confirm a single shared counter; simulate a skipped cleanup and assert the body is not left position:fixed.
  - **Verify:** ✅ confirmed — All three coordinators use unguarded module-scoped `let` counters (useOverlayLayer.ts:22-23 tkActiveLayers/tkTopLayer; useScrollLock.ts:12 tkScrollLockCount; useVerticalSwipeGuard.ts:23 tkSwipeGuardCount), so duplicate bundle copies each get an independent counter and a skipped cleanup leaves body position:fixed or drifts z-index. The reset guard (`tkActiveLayers<=0`) only protects the single-copy case; the structural shared-global risk is exactly as described.
- [x] **`INT-006`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-PERF` — useOverlayLayer assigns its z-slot in a post-paint effect — a stacked overlay paints one frame UNDER the overlay below it
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/internal/useOverlayLayer.ts:38-54`
  - **Evidence:** const [level, setLevel] = useState(0); useEffect(() => { if (!active) return; ... setLevel(assigned); ... }, [active]); const slot = Math.max(level, 1); // floors to 1 so first paint matches the FIRST layer
  - **Impact:** The layer slot is assigned in a post-paint useEffect, so the first committed frame always renders as layer 1 (base z 10/11). For a SECOND overlay opened on top of a first, that frame paints UNDER the existing overlay's panel until the effect runs and bumps z — a one-frame flash where the new sheet's scrim sits beneath the old panel, exactly the bug the file's comment block says it prevents. It also forces a guaranteed extra render per overlay open.
  - **Fix:** Assign the layer synchronously (useState initializer claiming the slot during render, or useLayoutEffect for the increment/cleanup) so the first paint already has the correct z-index.
  - **Done when:** Open overlay B over overlay A; capture the first committed frame and confirm B's scrim is above A's panel with no transitional frame.
- [x] **`INT-007`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-TG` — useVerticalSwipeGuard re-runs acquire/release on every useWebApp identity change, thrashing Telegram's enable/disableVerticalSwipes
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/internal/useVerticalSwipeGuard.ts:59-63`
  - **Evidence:** useEffect(() => { if (!active) return; tkAcquireSwipeGuard(wa); return () => tkReleaseSwipeGuard(wa); }, [active, wa]);
  - **Impact:** `wa` is in the dependency array. If the Telegram provider returns a new WebApp object reference on re-render (common when provider memoization is imperfect, or on theme/viewport events), the effect tears down and re-acquires: it calls enableVerticalSwipes() then disableVerticalSwipes() in quick succession. On real clients this briefly re-enables swipe-to-minimize mid-overlay (a downward drag during that window can collapse the app) and spams native bridge calls. The guard's correctness silently depends on `wa` being referentially stable, which the hook cannot guarantee.
  - **Fix:** Depend only on [active]; read the latest `wa` from a ref updated each render. Re-acquire only when `active` toggles, not when the WebApp reference changes.
  - **Done when:** Test: force useWebApp to return a new object each render while active stays true; assert disableVerticalSwipes is called once and enableVerticalSwipes is not called until active flips false.
- [x] **`INT-008`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-GESTURE` — useDragGesture pointerdown never cancels a prior drag, and onPointerCancel reports delta:0 — discarding a committed swipe
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/internal/useDragGesture.ts:103-156`
  - **Evidence:** onPointerDown(e) { if (!enabled) return; ... drag.current = {...}; } // overwrites any in-flight drag onPointerCancel() { ... onEnd?.({ delta: 0, velocity: 0 }); }
  - **Impact:** (1) A second pointerdown (multi-touch, or a synthetic re-fire) overwrites drag.current without flushing the previous gesture, dropping a pending rAF and leaking its onEnd. (2) onPointerCancel (fired by the browser when it steals the gesture for native scroll/vertical-swipe, very common on iOS Telegram) always hands onEnd `{delta:0, velocity:0}`, so consumers snap the sheet/cell back to origin even though the finger had clearly committed — the user's swipe is silently thrown away mid-motion, feeling like the app 'dropped' the gesture.
  - **Fix:** On pointerdown, if drag.current is active flush+null it first. On cancel, pass the last known pending delta/velocity (or expose a `canceled` flag) so consumers can decide to commit rather than forcibly reset to zero.
  - **Done when:** Test: dispatch pointercancel after a committed-distance move; onEnd receives the real delta (or a canceled flag), not {0,0}; multi-touch second down does not leak the first gesture's onEnd.
- [x] **`INT-009`** · 🟡 MEDIUM · `D-EDGE` — useDragGesture leaks a queued rAF when the component unmounts mid-drag (no cleanup effect)
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/internal/useDragGesture.ts:68-158`
  - **Evidence:** export function useDragGesture(...): TKDragHandlers { const drag = useRef<...>(null); ... return { onPointerDown, onPointerMove, ... }; // no useEffect cleanup that cancels drag.current.raf on unmount
  - **Impact:** If the host unmounts while a drag is active (overlay closed by an external event, route change, list virtualization recycling a TKSwipeCell), the scheduled requestAnimationFrame(flush) is never cancelled and fires after unmount, invoking the (now stale) onMove which calls setState on an unmounted tree. In React 18 this is a benign warning, but the onMove closure captures setters/refs from the dead render — wasted work and, for swipe-cell recycling during fast scrolling, repeated leaked frames.
  - **Fix:** Add `useEffect(() => () => { if (drag.current?.raf) cancelAnimationFrame(drag.current.raf); drag.current = null; }, [])` to cancel any pending frame and drop state on unmount.
  - **Done when:** Test: start a drag (queue an rAF), unmount the component, advance a frame; assert onMove is not invoked and no act() warning is logged.
- [x] **`INT-010`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-TYPES` — tkZ force-casts CSS-var strings to `Record<..., number>` via `as unknown as`, lying to every consumer about the value type
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/internal/dom.ts:19-33`
  - **Evidence:** export const tkZ = { base: "var(--tk-z-base)", ... } as unknown as Record<"base"|...|"popper", number>;
  - **Impact:** tkZ.overlay is typed `number` but is actually the string "var(--tk-z-overlay)". Any consumer that does arithmetic (tkZ.overlay + 1) typechecks but produces "var(--tk-z-overlay)1" at runtime — a silent z-index break. The double-cast also suppresses the legitimate error that would catch a typo'd token name. Because this is a shared primitive, the unsafe type propagates to all interactive components.
  - **Fix:** Type tkZ as `Record<..., string>` (it holds CSS var strings) or use a branded `CSSZIndex` type; reserve numeric z-indices for useOverlayLayer's computed slots only.
  - **Done when:** tkZ values are typed string; `tkZ.overlay + 1` is a type error; no `as unknown as` cast remains in dom.ts.
- [x] **`INT-011`** · ⚪ LOW · `D-API` — Entire internal family ships zero unit tests despite encoding pure commit/velocity/roving heuristics that gate destructive gestures
  - **Where:** `packages/uikit/src/internal/useDragGesture.ts:15-36`
  - **Evidence:** No *.test.ts under packages/uikit/src/internal (grep for useControllable|useDragGesture|tkShouldCommit|tkRovingNext across *.test.* returns nothing); tkDragVelocity, tkShouldCommit, tkRovingNext, tkTabbableIndex are pure functions with rich edge behavior.
  - **Impact:** tkShouldCommit (INT-001), tkDragVelocity's 100ms-window walk-back (off-by-one when ALL samples predate the window — `first` stays samples[0]), and tkRovingNext wrap/disabled-skip are exactly the logic where a regression silently fires the wrong action or traps keyboard focus, yet they have no characterization tests. A consumer app shipping this has no guardrail against a future refactor flipping a commit boundary.
  - **Fix:** Add a focused unit suite for the pure helpers (tkDragVelocity windows, tkShouldCommit boundaries incl. the INT-001 cases, tkRovingNext wrap + all-disabled + Home/End, tkTabbableIndex) before stabilizing the API per the plan's M3.1 note.
  - **Done when:** New test file covers each pure helper's boundary and edge cases; CI runs them; the INT-001 micro-flick case is encoded as a regression test.

#### DX proposals

- [x] **`INT-DX-001`** · composition · effort M
  - **Pain:** Every modal in the kit re-wires the SAME five mounted-keyed hooks by hand. TKSheet (packages/uikit/src/composites/overlays/sheet.tsx:83-92) calls useOverlayA11y, useScrollLock(mounted), useVerticalSwipeGuard(mounted), useOverlayLayer(mounted) and useBackIntercept individually; dialog.tsx and action-sheet.tsx repeat the identical block. A new overlay author must know all five exist, key them on the same flag, and order them correctly — miss one and the overlay leaks scroll, paints under its scrim, or collapses the Mini App. This is the single biggest source of copy-paste in the family and the surface where INT-005/006/007 desync bugs hide.
  - **Proposal:** Ship one composing hook so the common overlay is one line: `const overlay = useModalOverlay(mounted, { ref, onClose: dismissible ? requestClose : undefined, dismissible, scrollLock: true, swipeGuard: true });` returning `{ scrimZ, panelZ, scrimProps, panelProps }` (panelProps pre-spreads role="dialog" aria-modal aria-labelledby tabIndex={-1} and the z-index). Internally it sequences useOverlayLayer → useScrollLock → useVerticalSwipeGuard → useOverlayA11y → useBackIntercept against one active flag, so the five-hook dance becomes a single delightful call and the shared-global hooks can never be wired out of order. Keep the five primitives exported for the rare bespoke overlay.
- [x] **`INT-DX-002`** · defaults · effort M · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** useDragGesture (packages/uikit/src/internal/useDragGesture.ts:57-62) returns ONLY a bare pointer-handler bag and owns no touch-action. So every single consumer has to remember the correct CSS arbitration by hand and they all guess differently: sheet sets touchAction:'none' (sheet.tsx:204), swipe-cell and pull-to-refresh set 'pan-y', and nav swipe-back (nav.tsx:155, axis:'x') sets NOTHING — which is exactly why horizontal swipe-back fights native scroll (INT-003). Pull-to-refresh is forced to hand-roll an entire non-passive touchmove listener (pull-to-refresh.tsx:77-105) just to do the arbitration the hook should own.
  - **Proposal:** Make the hook return the correct touch-action for its axis instead of leaving it to memory: `const { bind, style } = useDragGesture({ axis: 'x' })` where `style.touchAction` is `'pan-y'` for axis:'x' and `'pan-x'` for axis:'y' (claim the cross-axis, release the drag axis to native). Consumers spread `<div {...bind} style={{ ...style, ...custom }}>` and the common case is correct by default with zero per-call thought. For the rare full-claim sheet, accept `touchAction: 'none'` as an explicit override. This kills the INT-003 footgun by construction and removes the need for consumer-side touchmove plumbing.
- [x] **`INT-DX-003`** · ergonomics · effort M
  - **Pain:** The roving-tabindex pattern is hand-assembled in EIGHT files (segmented.tsx, radio-group.tsx, chips.tsx, inline-buttons.tsx, tabbar.tsx, steps.tsx, category-tabs.tsx, page-dots.tsx). Each one repeats the exact same ~15 lines: a `refs.current[]` array, `tabIndex={index === tabbable ? 0 : -1}`, and an onKeyDown that calls tkRovingNext, then event.preventDefault(), setVal, and refs.current[next]?.focus() (see segmented.tsx:22,80,87-93). tkRovingNext/tkTabbableIndex are pure index math — they give you a number but make every consumer re-derive the ref wiring and focus-move themselves, so a new roving component is easy to ship subtly wrong (forgotten preventDefault, focus not moved).
  - **Proposal:** Add a stateful `useRovingFocus({ count, activeIndex, orientation, isDisabled, onActivate })` that owns the ref list and returns `getItemProps(index) => { ref, tabIndex, onKeyDown }` plus `focusIndex(i)`. A consumer becomes `{items.map((it,i)=> <button {...roving.getItemProps(i)} .../>)}` — the keyboard contract (arrow/Home/End, wrap, skip-disabled, preventDefault, move focus) is handled once and identically everywhere. Keep tkRovingNext/tkTabbableIndex as the pure primitives underneath. This collapses 8 copies of fragile a11y wiring into one tested hook.
- [x] **`INT-DX-004`** · api · effort S
  - **Pain:** The commit/velocity heuristic is hardcoded into tkShouldCommit's signature `(offset, velocity, size)` with the constants baked in: `offset > size * 0.5 || velocity > 0.5` (useDragGesture.ts:33-36). There is no way for a consumer to tune the distance ratio or the flick threshold, so the destructive micro-flick problem (INT-001) literally cannot be opted out of at the call site — and each consumer instead bolts ad-hoc guards on top (swipe-cell.tsx:105 does `tkShouldCommit(...) || Math.abs(raw) > max/2` because the raw heuristic doesn't fit). The thresholds 6 (drag activation) and the 100ms velocity window are equally invisible/unconfigurable.
  - **Proposal:** Promote the heuristic into a named, overridable policy object: `tkShouldCommit(offset, velocity, size, { distanceRatio = 0.5, velocity = 0.5, minDistance = 24 })` and let useDragGesture accept a `commit` policy in TKDragOptions so a sheet, a swipe-cell and a nav-back can each express their own commit feel (e.g. require minDistance before a flick counts — directly defusing INT-001). The default object documents the magic numbers in one place instead of scattering literals, and makes the destructive-gesture knobs discoverable in autocomplete.
- [x] **`INT-DX-005`** · composition · effort S · ⚠️ breaking
  - **Pain:** useDragGesture hands back a fixed handler bag (onPointerDown/Move/Up/Cancel) with no way to merge a consumer's own pointer handlers or apply it to an arbitrary element. nav.tsx:168-180 has to manually clone the bag and re-wrap onPointerDown to add the edge-zone gate, and TKDomProps already passes through onPointerDown/onPointerUp/onPointerCancel (dom.ts:43-45) — so any component that both takes consumer pointer props AND drags must compose them by hand or silently clobber one. There is no asChild/merge escape hatch.
  - **Proposal:** Return a `bind(userProps?)` merger instead of a static object: `bind({ onPointerDown: edgeGate })` chains the user handler before the drag handler (and respects defaultPrevented), so nav's edge-zone gate and any consumer-supplied pointer props compose cleanly. Pair it with the dom.ts mergeRefs convention so the dragged element can also forward a ref. This makes the hook a first-class composition citizen rather than something every caller has to defensively wrap.
- [x] **`INT-DX-006`** · types · effort S
  - **Pain:** tkZ (dom.ts:19-33) force-casts a map of CSS-var STRINGS to `Record<..., number>` via `as unknown as` (INT-010). Every consumer that reads tkZ.overlay (e.g. shared.tsx:9,180) is told by TypeScript it has a number, then assigns a `var(--tk-z-overlay)` string into CSSProperties.zIndex. The type is a lie: arithmetic like `tkZ.overlay + 1` typechecks but produces `"var(...)"+1` garbage, and there's no honest way to get a numeric z for the layering math that useOverlayLayer does in raw numbers.
  - **Proposal:** Split the concern honestly: keep `tkZ` typed as `Record<Layer, string>` (it returns CSS-var strings — say so), and add `tkZIndex(layer: Layer, offset = 0): number` that resolves the numeric scale for the rare case that needs math (the overlay-layer base computation). CSSProperties.zIndex accepts a string, so the cast is unnecessary; consumers get truthful inference and a real number when they actually need one. Mechanical change, but it stops the type system lying to every overlay.
- [x] **`INT-DX-007`** · api · effort S
  - **Pain:** useControllable's signature `(controlled, defaultValue, onChange)` is positional and untyped at the call site, so usages read as magic argument order: switch.tsx:21 `useControllable(checked, !!defaultChecked, onChange)` and segmented.tsx:19 `useControllable(value, defaultValue ?? firstEnabled?.value ?? '', onChange)`. Nothing names which arg is controlled vs default, the returned setter is unnamed `[val, setVal]`, and there is no dev-time warning when a consumer flips controlled↔uncontrolled (the INT-004 silent-drop trap) — the API actively hides the one mistake it's most prone to.
  - **Proposal:** Offer an options-object overload that self-documents and warns: `const [value, setValue] = useControllable({ value, defaultValue, onChange, name: 'TKSwitch.checked' })`. With `name` set, emit a one-time dev-only console.warn when `value` transitions between defined and undefined ("TKSwitch.checked is changing from controlled to uncontrolled") — the canonical React controlled-input guidance, which the kit currently ships zero of. Keep the positional form for back-compat. Pure DX: clearer call sites and the misuse that bites hardest finally produces a legible message.
- [ ] **`INT-DX-008`** · docs · effort M
  - **Pain:** The entire internal family ships with zero usage documentation or Storybook surface dedicated to the primitives. Storybook only has overlays.stories.tsx and gestures.stories.tsx for the finished composites; there is no copy-pasteable recipe showing how to build a NEW swipe surface with useDragGesture, a new roving group, or a new overlay. A consumer or contributor extending the kit has to reverse-engineer the contract from sheet.tsx and pull-to-refresh.tsx — including the non-obvious touch-action / non-passive-touchmove dance — with no canonical example. (INT-011 covers the test gap; this is the distinct docs gap.)
  - **Proposal:** Add a `storybook/internal/primitives.stories.tsx` (or an MDX recipe doc) with three minimal, runnable recipes: (1) 'Build a swipe-to-dismiss card' using useDragGesture + tkShouldCommit + the returned touch-action, (2) 'Build a roving toolbar' using useRovingFocus, (3) 'Build a stacking overlay' using useModalOverlay. Each ~20 lines, copy-paste-ready, doubling as the living contract for the hooks. This is the cheapest lever to make the family approachable and to stop every new gesture component re-deriving the arbitration footguns by hand.

#### Notes / coverage gaps

COVERAGE GAPS / ASSUMPTIONS: (1) No automated test exercises any of these five hooks — all findings are from source reading + consumer call-sites (sheet.tsx, swipe-cell.tsx, pull-to-refresh.tsx, nav.tsx, action-sheet.tsx, dialog.tsx), not from a running repro. (2) INT-005 (duplicate-module-copy desync) is an ASSUMPTION about bundler/dependency-dedup behavior; it manifests only when two non-deduped copies of @tg-mini-app-uikit load — verify against the actual published bundle/peer-dep setup. (3) INT-007 assumes useWebApp() can return an unstable reference; I read only packages/telegram/dist/provider.d.ts (not the src provider), so confirm provider memoization before sizing. (4) StrictMode is confirmed active in BOTH example apps (surface-composer/src/main.tsx:44, trailhead/src/main.tsx:94); I traced useOverlayLayer/useScrollLock as self-healing under the single-overlay mount→unmount→mount cycle, so no separate StrictMode finding was raised, but the shared-global pattern (INT-005) is what makes that fragile. (5) tkDragVelocity has a latent off-by-one (the loop breaks on the first out-of-window sample but `first` is only updated inside the window, leaving `first=samples[0]` when ALL samples predate the 100ms window) — folded into INT-011 rather than raised separately since the practical impact is bounded by the 24-sample cap. (6) roving.ts (tkRovingNext/tkTabbableIndex) and pageScroll.ts were read and found correct for the cases inspected; only their lack of tests is flagged (INT-011). No D-SEC issues found in this family (no innerHTML, file inputs, or PII handling here). Severity weighting favors consumer-facing breakage in a narrow Telegram WebView: INT-001 is critical because it can fire destructive swipe-cell actions from a tap.

- [x] **Checkpoint M10-K (list-cell MEDIUM) DONE & reviewed:** LST-006 (`TKCell` gained `wrap`/`lines` — title & subtitle stay single-line ellipsis by default, `wrap` flows multi-line, `lines={n}` clamps with `-webkit-line-clamp`), LST-007 (press feedback `tk-cell-tap` now only on genuinely actionable rows — onClick/link — not a decorative `chevron`). New tests `test/m10k-cell-medium.test.tsx` (6). Reviewer (no blockers): aligned `subtitle` render on `!= null` for LST-003 consistency (0/"" subtitles now render) + documented `lines` applies per title/subtitle. Suite 1083 green, `tsc` clean.
- [x] **Checkpoint M10-L (typography MEDIUM) DONE & reviewed:** TYP-005 (`TKText`/`TKTitle`/`TKCaption` now `forwardRef` to the DOM node — Tag cast to `ElementType` so one `HTMLElement` ref fits the element union), TYP-006 (`TKTitle as="div"` keeps heading semantics via `role="heading"` + `aria-level` from `level`; native h1/h2/h3 carry no redundant role), TYP-007 (`TKTitle` `weight` prop replaces the hard-coded 700; `TKText` `leading` overrides line-height without a style hack). New tests `test/m10l-typography-medium.test.tsx` (5). Reviewer (no blockers): confirmed `{...rest}`-before-our-attrs ordering is safe; documented the `large→aria-level=1` mapping. Suite 1088 green, `tsc` clean.
- [x] **Checkpoint M10-M (service/icons MEDIUM) DONE & reviewed:** SVC-004 (`TKIcon` optional `label` → labelled `role="img"` + `aria-label`, drops `aria-hidden`; decorative `aria-hidden` without it), SVC-005 (`TKTappable` `pressEffect={false}` omits the `tk-press` class; its reduced-motion is already global via CRD-004), SVC-007 (unknown icon name dev-warns + renders a visible placeholder `<rect>` instead of an empty svg). New tests `test/m10m-service-icons-medium.test.tsx` (5). Reviewer caught **SVC-004 double-naming** (aria-label + `<title>` under role=img) → dropped `<title>`, single `aria-label` source. Suite 1093 green, `tsc` clean.
- [x] **Checkpoint M10-N (CTL-010 group a11y) DONE & reviewed:** `TKRadioGroup` + `TKChipGroup` now `forwardRef` their group root and accept `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby`. RadioGroup combines consumer `aria-labelledby` with the field `labelId` (IDREF merge, no name divergence). ChipGroup is now a named `role="toolbar"` (matches its horizontal focus-roving + aria-pressed toggle chips, and announces its name reliably — vs a generic `group`). New tests `test/m10n-controls-aria.test.tsx` (4). Reviewer caught: `role="group"` name is unreliably announced → `role="toolbar"`; labelledby override could diverge from a visible label → IDREF merge. Suite 1097 green, `tsc` clean. **CTL-007 (discriminated-union types) + CTL-008 (RTL rating/slider geometry) remain.**
- [x] **Checkpoint M10-O (nav/pattern MEDIUM) DONE & reviewed:** NAV-003 (`TKCategoryTabs` + `TKPageDots` are named `role="group"` — CategoryTabs via new `ariaLabel`/`locale.categories`, PageDots via a positional `slidePosition` label "Slide 3 of 5"), NAV2-008 (`TKNavStack` dev-warns when a stack entry references an unregistered panel id, listing the available ids — was a silent blank screen), PTN-003 (`TKXPHeader` bar is now `role="progressbar"` with clamped `aria-valuenow`). New locale key `categories`. New tests `test/m10o-nav-pattern-medium.test.tsx` (5); reverted a per-dot `locale.page` change that broke 11 carousel tests (total now lives in the group label only). Reviewer caught the misleading `tabs` default → `categories`; flagged pre-existing nav.tsx limitations (NAV2-009 dedup window, controlled index-key) as documented, out of M10-O scope. Suite 1102 green, `tsc` clean.
- [x] **Checkpoint M10-P (button MEDIUM) DONE & reviewed:** BTN-005 (`TKIconButton` forwards native `type`/`name`/`value`/`form`/`title`/`onKeyDown`/`onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave` — `type="submit"` submits its form), BTN-006 (badge clamps to `99+`, hides for ≤0), BTN-009 (`TKButton` announces loading via an off-screen `role="status"` mounted only while loading; `TKSpinner` is `aria-hidden` by default with a `label` opt-in). New tests `test/m10p-button-medium.test.tsx` (5). Reviewer (no blockers): moved the status to mount only on `loading` (was always-present → future getByRole collision + matches AsyncBoundary); tied IconButton `position:relative` to the badge predicate. Suite 1107 green, `tsc` clean.
